# Volt Streaming - Design vs Build Reconciliation

*Single source of truth for "what did we decide" vs "what is actually built."*
*`volt_build_status.html` is generated from THIS file: `node scripts/build-status.mjs`.*
Generated 2026-08-02 by reconciling the 25 project-memory notes (decisions from
all chat sequences) against the code in `volt-app/`, `volt-encoder/`, `e2e/`,
and `deploy/terraform/`.

**Last reconciled 2026-09-05**, including a sweep of every 🧪 prototype row against the tree (see the *Prototype sweep* section at the end): five of six were resolved, four to built and one to partial, and the one that remains a prototype turned out to have no caller. Counted from the tree on that date, not
remembered: **24 migrations**, **83 check suites** (54 TypeScript, 29 shell),
**48 mounted routers**, **136 backend TypeScript files (30.0k lines)**,
**48 console files (18.6k lines)** across **25 pages**. Suite *results* are not
restated here because they need a running database and API; run
`node scripts/check.js` for those.

**Legend** - ✅ Built & verified · 🟡 Partial (some built, some stubbed) ·
📐 Designed only (decision + doc, no code) · 🧪 Prototype / test-harness only ·
❌ Not started

**MEDIA SCOPE.** A note beginning **Video.**, **Audio.** or **Video + audio.**
states which workflow a capability belongs to. Added 5 Sep because the two were
being read as one and the requirements were drifting together: the waveform and
level metering are an AUDIO surface (a station, or the audio track of a video
asset) and say nothing about video; timed in-stream metadata is a VIDEO delivery
concern first; graphics overlays are video-only by decision and radio is
explicitly out of their scope. A capability with no scope note applies to both.
Anything built for one and described as if it served both is a requirement
defect, not a documentation one — it is how a video feature ends up with an
audio acceptance test and neither works.

The headline as first written: the **control/production/orchestration core is
built and verified** (chat-1 work), with the API contract, EPG, source-provider,
geo/rights, analytics and app-keys designs documented but not implemented.

**That gap has largely closed.** Hypermedia, EPG, geo/rights, analytics and
app-keys are all built and covered. What remains 📐 is now mostly work that was
*designed after* the original reconciliation — graphics overlays, timed
in-stream metadata, schedule inference, the operating report — rather than a
backlog the build never caught up with. The honest current gap is different in
kind: **the ECS launcher path, archive/restore against real S3, and scale beyond
one box have code and tests but no production exercise.**

---

| **Hypermedia / HATEOAS - JSON-LD + Hydra typed links, collections, entrypoint** | ✅ | Built 2 Aug: content-negotiated (`Accept: application/ld+json`); `hypermedia.ts` + middleware + `/api` entrypoint, `/api/context.jsonld`, `/api/doc`. **Completed 20 Oct 2026** (closes `docs/API_NAVIGABILITY_REPORT.md`): resolution runs over the running app's own route table (`util/routeInventory.ts`) and the list registry, so EVERY list endpoint is a `hydra:Collection` (wrapped bodies such as `{ jobs }` too) and every other `/api` answer gets `@id`, `up` and a breadcrumb. Paging: `hydra:view` first / previous / next / last from the list standard's keyset cursor links, `totalItems` = full match count, `hydra:search` IriTemplate of each list's filters. Navigation: member `previousSibling` / `nextSibling` in view order, item `siblings`, `breadcrumb` (a recording's runs through its channel), one typed link per sub-resource route; parameterised sub-resources and scoped collections (`/api/epg`, `/api/publications`) are IriTemplates, never dead links. `/api/doc` declares every class's operations (method, target, expects, returns, from the routes) and properties (from the list definitions); `volt:` now resolves to `/api/doc#`; status links are `/api/status?channelId=` (200 before a stream has run). Plain JSON unchanged plus `Link: rel="alternate"; type="application/ld+json"`; `?format=jsonld` selects the view. `test/hypermedia.check.sh` 57/57, listStandard ok. Blind link walk from `/api` (demo seed): 70 GET route patterns reached (was 18), 0 failing links (was 6). Postman folders 3 and 4 now assert the fixes. Open: trimming the 65-column member representation (a contract change for plain JSON) |
| **List paging both ways + item routes for deep links (20 Oct 2026)** | ✅ | `util/listQuery.ts`: keyset cursors read after a row (next), before a row (prev) or the final page (last), so every list on the standard sends `Link` rel `next`, `prev`, `first` and `last` (plus `X-Prev-Cursor`); the hypermedia layer's `hydra:view` can follow them both ways. Old cursors keep working. **Bug fixed on the way:** cursors carry milliseconds while Postgres stores microseconds, so an ascending time sort repeated the last row of every page (a client following next looped for ever) and a descending one skipped rows in the anchor's millisecond; times now sort and page at millisecond precision with the id tiebreak (an expression index on `date_trunc('milliseconds', col AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')` serves it). New item routes so every member's `self` resolves: `GET /api/filler-assets/:id`, `/api/scheduled-clips/:id`, `/api/publish-targets/:id`, `/api/publications/:id`, `/api/epg/:id` (programme), tenant-scoped like their lists, a non-id falls through (`onlyIdParam`). Checks: `test/listQuery.check.ts` 53/53 (prev walks back over the same pages for four sorts, first, last, offset, microsecond rows both sorts), `test/itemRoutes.check.sh` 18/18, `listStandard` (29 lists, every sort and filter) and `hypermedia.check.sh` 55/55 pass on it. Extend target_kind to things we DESCRIBE, then wire the projection into the player session. **5 Sep review, and it is worse than this row implied.** `target_kind` is still `vod | event | channel | any` from `0001_init.sql:395` — no migration extended it, so the `SportsTeam` gap is exactly as described. The sharper finding: **`services/eventPromo.ts` has no caller at all.** `presentationFromEventStatus`, `inferTemplate`, `promoFromMetadata` and `sportsEventJsonLd` are referenced by **zero** files in `volt-app/src`, and only by `test/event-promo.check.ts`. So the 17/17 green suite proves the *functions* are correct while nothing in the running application emits any of it: no route, no player session, no JSON-LD on the wire. Correctly 🧪 — a tested library awaiting a caller, not a feature. Wiring it is small (one route plus the player session payload); the `target_kind` extension is the half with a migration in it |
| Timed in-stream metadata (EXT-X-DATERANGE / SCTE-35 / ID3 / emsg) | ✅ | **Corrected 20 Oct 2026: the September tags never reached production viewers.** They were added by the control plane when it served `/origin`, which only happens locally; in production playlists go S3 → CloudFront directly, and plain channels had no PROGRAM-DATE-TIME to place them by. Now the ENCODER writes them into the delivered copies (`timed_events.py`, polling `GET /api/streams/:id/timed-events`), the delivered copies (`*_cc`) are written for every video stream, and DASH gets an `EventStream` per Period. See the in-stream graphics row below and `volt-app/docs/DESIGN_stream_graphics_triggers.md`. **Video. Built 5 Sep — `EXT-X-DATERANGE` is now emitted.** `services/timedMetadata.ts` annotates media playlists on the way out of `/origin`, so the schedule stays in Postgres and a rename or an override corrects itself on the next fetch instead of being frozen into a segment at encode time. Tags land in the header (a DATERANGE after an `#EXTINF` is a parse error, not untidiness), are idempotent across refetches, and a multivariant master cannot receive them because it has no PROGRAM-DATE-TIME to derive a window from. Quotes in a title are stripped rather than escaped — the attribute grammar has no escape for them. Fails open: an unreadable schedule serves a plain playlist rather than no playlist. `test/timed-metadata.check.ts` 37/37. **SCTE-35: done 22 Oct 2026 for live channels and events (build queue 1.6, v0.6.0), FAST since 20 Oct; in-band `emsg` in DASH segments still to do (the MPD EventStream carries the cue).** Original note: Programme boundaries, ad markers and now/next carried in the DELIVERY stream, which is a video-asset concern: `EXT-X-DATERANGE` for programme and ad windows, SCTE-35 for signalling, `emsg` for the DASH equivalent. **Not the radio metadata path** — a station's now-playing arrives as ICY on the INGEST side and is already handled by `services/radio/icyMeta.ts` and `nowPlaying.ts`. The one genuine overlap is ID3-in-`emsg`, which can carry a track title on an HLS AUDIO rendition and is what would let an Icecast station's titles reach an HLS listener; scope that as a separate audio row when it is built, so the video work is not judged by it. **Built 23 Oct 2026 (build queue 1.7, v0.7.0): radio HLS and DASH segments carry the title as ID3 in emsg; see the 23 Oct section.** |
| **In-stream graphic triggers + player graphics (Up next)** | ✅ | **Built 20 Oct 2026.** Standard carriers only: HLS `EXT-X-DATERANGE` (`CLASS=com.boomlabs.volt.graphic`, `X-` data) and DASH `EventStream` (`urn:com:boomlabs:volt:graphic:2026`, JSON `messageData`). Control plane builds triggers from the EPG (`services/graphicTriggers.ts`; Up next at lead 120 s / 12 s by default, per channel or event in `graphics`, migration 0060), cached per channel and invalidated by the existing row triggers (20 polls = 0 reads); encoder places them (`timed_events.py`); player reads Shaka `metadataadded` / `timelineregionadded` and Safari DataCues (`sdk/triggers.ts`), drives the existing Up next card, `state.graphics`, `graphic` event, gated by `showGraphics` (Player setup, default on). **Verified end to end** in Chromium with Shaka on live HLS and DASH: card within 0.1-0.2 s of its scheduled moment at the viewer's playhead, 11.6/12 s on screen, nothing with graphics off. Found and fixed: Shaka drops DATERANGE `ID` (server repeats it as `X-ID`) and passes DASH attributes still XML-escaped (player unescapes). Checks: graphic-triggers 47/47, player-triggers 26/26, test_timed_events.py 34/34. Not yet run against the real Shaka packager + encoder on the Mac. Events have the API setting but no console control yet. |
| **Now-playing resolution for sources that carry no metadata** | ✅ | **Audio.**  Built 5 Sep: `services/radio/nowPlaying.ts` makes one four-way decision (source title → scheduled programme → station name + reason → real break), used by the station list, the detail payload and the now-playing poll, which previously each decided separately and only one had every case. `sourceCarriesTitles()` lets the URL override the protocol column, so an `.m3u8` is HLS whatever the row says (same rule as `looks_like_hls()` in `radio.py`). Fixes an HLS station reading permanently as `Untitled break`, which is a claim that the source is *between* titles and sent operators looking for a metadata fault that does not exist. `test/now-playing.check.ts` 32/32, verified negatively at 30/2. `docs/REQ_now_playing_resolution.md` |
| Listener count = the whole audience, not the MP3 clients | ✅ | **Audio.** Built 5 Sep: `listenerCounts()` sums the MP3 fan-out clients and live player concurrency from `stream_concurrency` (across asset kinds, 120s freshness, matching the fleet total in `platform.ts`). The console previously rendered `p.mp3Clients.size` as "0 MP3 listeners", so a station with an HLS audience and no MP3 clients reported zero. Breakdown kept on `listenerCounts` for the tooltip; null still renders as no line rather than as a zero |
| **Automatic now-playing / EPG inference (broadcaster feed · fingerprint · transcript)** | 📐 | **Audio first, video later.** Designed 5 Sep, not built. The commercial answer to the case above, where a broadcaster hands us a URL and no schedule. Three routes — (a) fetch the broadcaster's own XMLTV/JSON guide: exact, but one bespoke adapter per network; (b) acoustic fingerprinting: high accuracy on released music, near zero on sport, speech and local programming, which is exactly what these stations carry; (c) transcribe the segments we already cut and infer: the only one that fits sport and talk, and the only one with a per-station-hour running cost. Shape that matters: **propose, never publish** — inferred programmes land as `proposed` with source and confidence for accept/reject in the EPG editor, ranked below an operator's schedule and above the station name; **detect the boundary before naming it**, since "the programme changed at 14:03" is cheaper and more useful than a wrong title nobody caught; billed per station, not a platform default. `docs/REQ_now_playing_resolution.md` §4 |
| API keys / app registration (account+app keys, playback token, per-app restrictions) | ✅ | Built 2 Aug: `apps`+`api_keys` (sha256, prefix), `apiKeys.ts` (gen/verify/restrictions/token), `/api/apps` + public `/api/availability` gate. 8 tests (origin enforce, 401/403, metering) |
| On-the-fly image service (resize/crop/filter by URL, cached · sharp) | ✅ | Prototype 3 Aug in `volt-image-proto/`: sharp service, URL grammar `/{fit}/{WxH}/filter(...)/{path}` - fit-in/fill/stretch + focus (center/edges/smart) + sharpen/blur/grayscale/format(webp/avif)/quality, HMAC-signed URLs, disk derivative cache (HIT 1.4ms vs ~150ms render). 14/14 assertions + gallery. `docs/DESIGN_image_service.md`. **Productionised 5 Sep review: this is in the app and serving.** `services/imageResize.ts` + `services/imageDims.ts`, `sharp ^0.35.3`, route `GET /img/<WxH>[,opts]/<store-key>` in `server.ts`, derivatives cached under the store's `derived/` prefix with `Cache-Control: max-age=86400, immutable`, origin served from `GET /library/*`. Grammar carried over: `fit` (cover|inside|contain|fill), `focus=X-Y`, `gray`, `blur=N`, `sat=N`, `q=N`, `fmt` (webp|jpeg|png). The console calls it through `thumbUrl()` in `volt-web/src/api.ts` → `/img/{w}x{h},{fit}/{key}`, and station and channel artwork renders through it (confirmed in live screenshots 5 Sep). **Two prototype features did not carry**: AVIF output, and *smart* focus detection — focus is now an explicit percent. **Production release 15 Oct 2026:** moved to `src/modules/images.ts` + `src/services/images/` (`imageResize.ts` removed). Every render URL is signed with a per-account key derived from `IMAGE_SIGNING_SECRET` (no unsafe mode, rotation slot). Sources from the image store (local dir or the `{project}-{env}-images` S3 bucket, `library/{acct}/`). Derivatives: memory LRU, S3 `img-cache/` at the request path, CloudFront origin group (S3 first, render on miss), 1-year immutable. Guards refuse (not clamp) oversize, unknown options, disabled formats, big sources, pixel bombs; render limiter + single-flight. AVIF and smart/entropy focus restored, `contain`+`bg`, `up`, `v=`. Path-style URLs still accepted behind a signature. Signing: `POST /api/images/sign` (+batch), `GET /api/images/signing-key`, `@volt/image-url` SDK (console uses it). Terraform `images.tf`. Tests: `images.check.ts` 145, `images-api.check.sh` 22, `images-s3.check.ts`. Guide `docs/IMAGES.md` |

It lives in `patchEntity` rather than at each call site for the same reason the audit write does: one function changes a record, so one place can be certain what changed — a caller that had to remember would forget, as four call sites forgot `on_air`. **Only the SHAPE of a change travels** (columns, version, actor), never the values, so a frame cannot hand a viewer a field they may not read; the page re-reads through its own credential. Own-writes are suppressed by version, or every save would accuse the operator of conflicting with themselves. `test/live-record.check.ts` 24/24, including a structural assertion that fails if values ever reach the frame. Wired into the channel settings drawer; other editors are a one-line addition each. Public front-end live remains selective by design. Original note: Push committed field changes to other admins viewing the same record over the existing SSE hub + Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY; version-conflict warning. Public front-end live = selective only: EPG now/next served from CDN + client advances at the known programme boundary + cache-tag purge on override (no per-viewer socket, so 500k viewers hit cache not origin) |

## 2. Production plane (encoder / packager)

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| package_only + transcode → CMAF → HLS+DASH | ✅ | real ffmpeg→Shaka, decodable |
| **Local-storage mode (encoder writes CMAF to disk, no S3)** | ✅ | Built 6 Aug: `encoder.py` `output.mode=local` → writes the ladder + HLS/DASH straight to a local origin dir (served at `/origin/<id>`) and **skips the S3 uploader**; `s3` block no longer required; per-job `UDP_BASE_PORT` so concurrent channels don't collide. Origin dir IS the live edge + DVR (`time_shift_buffer_depth`) + recording (`preserved_segments=0`). Unit tests + real e2e (HLS pull → 720/480/360 ladder → CMAF, decodable, DASH `timeShiftBufferDepth` honoured) |
| **Local thumbnailer mode (poster/storyboard/VTT to disk)** | ✅ | Built 6 Aug: `thumbnailer.py` `out_dir` → writes `poster.jpg` + `sb_NNN.jpg` sheets + `thumbs.vtt` locally, no S3; `accurate` seek path for CMAF fMP4 (Shaka's live playlists carry no ENDLIST). Verified against a real local encode (poster + 5×5 sheet + VTT). 4 new builder tests |
| Input failover + filler-on-loss (package_only) | ✅ | `source_selector.py` for primary/backup. **9 Oct:** the filler is no longer pushed through the packager (Shaka stopped on its codec change and took the channel down); it is a pre-built, source-matched loop spliced into the delivered HLS. `dev/wftest.sh vod_loop package_only`: primary→backup→loop→primary delivered, packager stays up, 0 stalls |
| SPEKE / Axinom DRM cbcs (client) | 🟡 | **20 Oct 2026: the whole path is now exercised against a local SPEKE v2 stand-in and a real Shaka Packager** (`volt-encoder/dev/test_fairplay_drm.py` 26/26). Two production faults fixed: the control plane sends `auth_ref` (a Secrets Manager ARN) + `tenant_id` but the encoder only read `drm.auth`, which nothing sends, so every key request went out unauthenticated (now `resolve_drm_auth`: ARN, `ARN#field` of a JSON secret, or `env:NAME`, giving Axinom's `tenant:key`); and the credential was passed on the command line, readable by any process on the host (now the `SPEKE_AUTH` environment). Still 🟡 for one reason: it has never spoken to a real Axinom tenant, which needs a tenant id and management key (credentials only, no code decision left) |
| Keyscreen thumbnailer (CPU) | ✅ | `thumbnailer.py`, 22 assertions |
| Status / events → control plane | ✅ | `events.py`, 16 assertions |
| **Encoder health + metrics endpoint (20 Oct 2026)** | ✅ | `volt-encoder/bin/health_server.py`, on when `HEALTH_PORT` is set (Terraform sets 9090 on the encoder task). `/healthz` = liveness (supervisor loop turning, every critical process running), used by the new ECS container health check; `/readyz` = viewers get fresh segments (output available, newest segment within three segment durations), so a lost source with no filler fails readiness but not liveness and ECS does not restart a container that a restart would not fix; `/metrics` Prometheus text (up, ready, children, active source one-hot, source up, filler, segment age, CPU, ingest bps and bytes, uptime); `/status` the snapshot the control plane receives. Read from state the encoder already holds (`health_snapshot`). Checks: `dev/test_health_server.py` 27/27 (verdicts, exposition format, a real socket, the encoder's own snapshot); `terraform validate` passes |

Extended 3 Aug: Whisper transcribes ~100 langs + native X→English; **NLLB-200 MT stage** adds all other targets (en↔ms, en↔zh, en↔ta proven with real espeak→whisper→NLLB run). Account master toggle `accounts.captions_provider` (none\|whisper\|gladia) gates it; jobConfig pins engine to the account. 33 unit tests. Live Gladia websocket adapter still stubbed (needs key+network). Workflow + engine alternative: `docs/WORKFLOW_captions_translation.md` **8 Oct: the 19 Aug one-clock sync fix had been overwritten (2f29a5d, then 0f1a6cd and a 30 Aug sync) and is restored; median sync -19.8s → +0.02s, 94% of cues within 1s. On the Mac the encoder ran with Homebrew python3, which cannot import faster-whisper; run-on-mac.sh now uses volt-encoder/.venv. See CAPTIONS.md** |

| **Live captions: Gladia live adapter, Whisper tiny/base on CPU in the encoder, QC captions lane (20 Oct 2026)** | ✅ | **Gladia:** `GladiaAdapter` (`volt-encoder/bin/captions.py`) replaces the `NotImplementedError` stub: Gladia v2 live (`POST /v2/live` with `X-Gladia-Key`, then a websocket, PCM in, final transcripts and translations out), ONE session per run kept across caption windows, only each window's new audio sent (never the preroll), utterance stream times mapped onto each window's WAV so the encoder's preroll rule still applies, a dropped socket fails that window and the next opens a new session. Stdlib websocket client `bin/ws_client.py` (handshake accept check, masking, fragments, ping/pong, close) so the encoder image stays dependency-free. The encoder now builds one Gladia adapter per run instead of one per window. **Whisper on CPU:** tiny and base run inside the encoder when the image is built with `CAPTIONS_CPU=true` (`CAPTIONS_MT=true` adds NLLB on CPU) and the control plane is told by `CAPTIONS_CPU_IN_ENCODER=true`; `captionsPlan.ts` then plans no GPU task (`inEncoder`), the job carries `in_encoder` and `model_size`, `streamLoad.ts` reserves the CPU (measured: tiny 0.19, base 0.30 CPU-seconds per audio second, warm, arm64). Off by default. **Fixed:** with the GPU add-on the encoder ALSO started its own whisper worker (the job never said where the words are produced), which in the base image failed with an import error on every run; now `in_encoder: false` makes the encoder advertise the subtitle tracks only. **QC lane:** `captionsLane()` in `pipelineHealth.ts` from the encoder's `caption.status` / `caption.error` events for the current run (starting, healthy, degraded, failed with stage and hint, stopped), on `health.captions`, shown under the gates in the QC panel; never makes the channel red. The encoder now reports when captions recover after errors. Checks: `dev/test_gladia_live.py` 24/24 (local stand-in for the Gladia API, no key), `dev/test_whisper_cpu.py` 8/8 (espeak-ng speech, faster-whisper 1.0.3 tiny and base on CPU, warm window under half its length), `dev/test_captions.py` 58/58, `test/captions-lane.check.ts` 19/19, `captions-plan` 19/19, `captions-toggle` 6/6, `captions-orchestration`, `stream-load` 31/31, `orchestrator`, `qc-reset` 8/8, `startup-settling` 17/17. **Not verified here:** NLLB on CPU (the test machine has 3 GB of memory) and a Docker build with `CAPTIONS_CPU=true` (no Docker on the test machine); a real Gladia key |

| Transcode-workflow failover | ✅ | **Tested 8 Oct** with `dev/wftest.sh` (real encoder + Shaka, sources killed on cue): primary→backup→loop→primary and primary→backup→slate→primary both delivered segment by segment, 0 playback violations. Fixed on the way: the loop was read over HTTP and ffmpeg hung (no output during the whole outage), the slate filler ran unpaced at 6.7x realtime for the channel's whole life, loss detection 5s→3s and a 1s forwarder probe (output gap at a switch 16s→10s, inside the player's 12-14s live hold) |
| **Operator source control reaches a remote encoder: switch source, force slate or loop (20 Oct 2026)** | ✅ | The Failover panel (`POST /api/channels/:id/failover-test`, modes auto / primary_down / backup / slate / loop) wrote a control file into the origin directory on the CONTROL PLANE's disk, which the source selector and playout read. On ECS the encoder runs on another host, so in production the manual switch and the forced slate did nothing. Now `services/failoverControl.ts` records each choice as a `failover.control` stream event (with who made it, so the QC log shows it), and the encoder receives it on the timed-events poll it already makes (`control.failover` on `GET /api/streams/:id/timed-events`, ingest token); `apply_remote_control` in `encoder.py` writes the same local control file, so selector and playout are unchanged. A choice belongs to the run it was made in (newer than the latest `channel.start`), so a restart is automatic again. Latency is the poll interval (5 s). Checks: `test/failover-control.check.ts` 14/14 (real DB + HTTP: recorded with the actor, carried on the poll, read back by the panel, run-scoped, another account 404, stopped channel 409, bad mode 422, poll needs the token), `volt-encoder/dev/test_source_control.py` 11/11 (TriggerFeed hands it over, written only on change, selector and playout obey, unknown modes ignored), `bin/test_timed_events.py` 34/34, graphic-triggers 47/47, timed-metadata 37/37. **Soak test of switching continuity (item 5b): not run in this pass**, see the open items |
| Zero-gap primary↔backup splice | ✅ | **9 Oct: manifest-level splice built for HLS and DASH** (`bin/playout.py`): when the live edge is three quarters of a segment late the delivered playlists carry the pre-built loop with a discontinuity, and `manifest_cc.mpd` carries it as its own Period. DASH Period boundaries are placed per track (audio and video segments do not end together), the one segment crossing a boundary is served cut to it (`bin/mp4util.py truncate`), and a track whose clock jumps gets its own boundary. Played in real Chrome with the console's hls.js and dash.js (`dev/browsercheck.mjs`): 0s frozen on transcode and package_only, both formats (52s stalled without). A source switch that takes longer than the hold shows one loop segment. Open: gpac stops following the live MPD at a Period change; one hls.js run in five froze 40s on package_only |
| **Switching continuity soak, package_only over SRT (20 Oct 2026)** | 🟡 | `volt-encoder/dev/soak_switch.sh`: two aligned ladders from `e2e/source-ladder.sh` (now takes `BASE_PORT` and `PATTERN`, so primary 9000/9001 and backup 9010/9011 run side by side with different pictures), the real encoder and Shaka with failover and auto failback, a cycle repeated for `SOAK_SEC` (default 30 min, every `CYCLE_SEC` 90 s): kill primary, restore; every `FILLER_EVERY`-th cycle (3) also kill the backup so the slate goes on air. A player simulation follows the delivered `master_cc.m3u8` for the whole run and `playcheck.py` watches every variant; PASS needs no stall, no violation and a switch at every cue. **Fixed on the way:** `playcheck.py` judged a PDT gap against 3x the LAST segment's EXTINF, so one short spliced segment (0.12 s) at the end of the window flagged every normal 2 s gap (293 false violations in a 2 minute run); now each gap is measured against the segments listed in it. **Short runs on the test VM (4 arm64 cores, both ladders encoding live beside the encoder):** primary to backup and back, two cycles: 0 stalls, 0 violations, both switches each way. With the slate cycle: 0 playlist violations, but the player simulation saw short stalls on slate segments (18 stalls, 2.8 s total in one run; 6 stalls, 2.2 s in another), always on `loop`/slate segments after the second filler engagement. **Open:** run the full 30 minute soak on the Mac (`cd volt-encoder && PACKAGER_BIN=... dev/soak_switch.sh`) to tell CPU contention on the test VM from a real fault in the filler splice on package_only; nothing longer than three minutes can run in this session's shell |

| **Recording timeline: false gaps on burst sources, zoom focus, MP3 clips (11 Oct)** | ✅ | Reported on the ABC Jazz (HLS) station: a continuous recording showed a gap every 10 s (29 in a 5 minute clip) while playback was smooth. Cause, measured on the Mac's run of 10 Oct 17:28: the media clock was continuous but the station wrote segments in bursts (2 or 3 within 0.1 s, every 10 s, because the source delivers 10 s segments), and segments were placed at "written minus duration". `services/localRecordingIndex.ts placeSegments` now places by the media clock anchored to the wall clock (the smallest write lag, a suffix minimum, so the anchor only steps forward; a step beyond 1.5x the run's median delivery spacing is a real stall; missing sequence numbers and tfdt resets still break pieces). All 28 of that station's runs go from 9 to 46 false gaps each to none, nothing placed after it was written. The same bursts made the Icecast relay drop the mp3 after 10 s of nothing, the pipeline exit and auto-connect start a new run: 28 runs in an hour. HLS sources are now read at real time (`-re`, one segment in hand: `-live_start_index -2`) in `radio.py` and `radio/args.ts`; measured with ffmpeg, output segments come every 4 s instead of all within 0.1 s. Timeline: zoom buttons and +/- now zoom on a chosen focus (playhead, In, Out or middle, remembered), drawn as a line, and bring it into view when it is off screen; a hover line shows where the wheel will zoom. Audio clips: the Output choice reads Downloadable MP3 / Streaming + MP3 and builds MP3 + M4A, no audio-only MP4. `test/segment-placement.check.ts` 17/17 (24 real ABC segments plus holes that must still show), radio-args 51/51, radio-ingest-protocols 12/12. |

Measured: template DOM update 0.9 ms/frame (45× realtime), so the cost is the *capture* pipeline, not the browser — capture the graphic's bounding box on state change, never the full frame at frame rate. Needs `encoder.py` `start_transcode()` moved from per-rung `-vf` to one `-filter_complex` composite before the ladder splits, so the blend happens once rather than per rendition. Sequenced player-side first, burn-in last. `docs/REQ_graphics_overlays.md` (requirements + workflows), `docs/DESIGN_html_graphics_overlays.md` (cost, measured), `docs/prototypes/graphics-bench.html` (client-facing prototype) |

The HLS writers already emit `EXT-X-DISCONTINUITY`, so the markers themselves are a small addition to code that exists. Prerequisite for server-side ad insertion, and the correct answer to "insert banner ads" — a burned-in banner is identical for every viewer and sells for a fraction of a targeted one |

| FairPlay HLS | 🟡 | **Packaging built and verified 20 Oct 2026; Apple playback needs credentials.** `speke_client.py` now reads FairPlay's key URI from SPEKE v2 `HLSSignalingData` (and v1 `URIExtXKey`), the explicit IV and every PSSH box, and refuses FairPlay with no key URI (exit 3) rather than ship playlists no Apple player opens. `encoder.shaka_drm_flags` adds `--protection_systems` (so Shaka writes `#EXT-X-KEY:METHOD=SAMPLE-AES,URI="skd://...",KEYFORMAT="com.apple.streamingkeydelivery"`), `--hls_key_uri`, `--iv`, `--pssh`, and `--clear_lead 0`: Shaka leaves the first 5 s in the clear by default and writes no key tag until the first encrypted segment, so the start of every run and recording was unprotected (`DRM_CLEAR_LEAD` to change). Verified with Shaka v3.4.2: video and audio playlists carry the FairPlay key tag, DASH carries the default KID and Widevine ContentProtection, init segments are `encv`/`enca` cbcs with our KID, segments carry `senc`, decrypting with the SPEKE key gives back 100 of 100 frames and the wrong key does not. **Blocked:** playback in Safari needs the Apple FairPlay Streaming deployment package (certificate + ASk) and a license endpoint, i.e. the Axinom tenant above; the player side (`volt-player`) has no FairPlay certificate fetch yet |

## 3. Orchestration & infrastructure

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Orchestrator lifecycle → ECS (mock+ecs), recordings, retention, scheduled-clip firing | ✅ | `orchestrator/`, 30 assertions |
| **Local launcher - real encoder.py/thumbnailer.py as on-box child processes (no AWS)** | ✅ | Built 6 Aug: `orchestrator/localLauncher.ts` implements the `TaskLauncher` interface, so **all** orchestrator wiring (recordings open/close, thumbnailer-on-stop, retention, day-roll) is reused unchanged. `ORCH_LAUNCHER=local` selects it. `launchEncoder` injects `output.mode=local` + per-stream UDP port + `WORK_DIR=origin/<id>`; `stopTask` SIGTERM→SIGKILL; `launchThumbnailer` picks the hi-res rendition, synthesizes a VOD (ENDLIST) playlist over the on-disk segments, and runs the real thumbnailer. TS integration test drives the real encoder end-to-end (12/12): ingest→ladder→CMAF (HLS+DASH), clean stop, poster+sheet+VTT. **This closes the mock/ecs-only gap - the real encode/package/DVR/record/thumbnail pipeline now runs and is testable locally.** `run-on-mac.sh` auto-enables it when ffmpeg+python3+Shaka `packager` are present |
| **App surfacing: channel Start → real local encode, played back in ChannelDetail** | ✅ | Built 6 Aug: channel **Start** already calls `orchestrator.startStream`; with the local launcher it now spawns the real encoder to `/origin/<id>`. Status endpoint probes the origin on disk (fresh `master.m3u8`) so `testStream.running` fires for the real encoder too - the existing hls.js player + DVR scrubber play it with **no UI rewrite**; a `REAL ENCODER · x264 → CMAF` badge distinguishes it from the test card; `producer`/`poster`/`thumbsVtt` added to the status payload |
| Terraform Tier-2 cloud stack (VPC/ECS/RDS/S3/CloudFront/ECR/ALB/Secrets) | ✅ | `deploy/terraform/`, `terraform fmt -check` and `terraform validate` clean; ready to apply with `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` (27 Oct 2026, build queue 2.1: every control-plane setting set or explained, see the 27 Oct section; 2.1b: radio and captions task definitions and repositories, GPU pool for captions); apply needs AWS creds |
| Deploy and rollback script (`scripts/deploy/deploy.mjs`) | 🟡 | **Built 4 Nov 2026 (build queue D1, v0.43.0); run for real on the Docker staging stack, not yet on AWS.** One command per environment in `deploy/environments.json` (`compose` driver for `deploy/staging`, `ecs` driver for the Terraform stack): release checks, images tagged `v<version>-<sha>` (never latest), migrations once as a one-off task (`dist/migrateOnly.js`), rolling service moves with a steady-state wait, smoke test (ready, play gate, manifest), automatic rollback, `--rollback`, new encoder revisions for new streams only, `release/deployments.json` as the record. Terraform image tags must name a release; services ignore `task_definition`. `test/deploy-script.check.ts` 59/59. Not done: a real AWS deploy (Christian). The migration safety check is D2 (next row). |
| Migration safety check (`scripts/release/check-migrations.mjs`) | ✅ | **Built 4 Nov 2026 (build queue D2, v0.44.0).** Rolling deploys run old and new tasks on one schema and a rollback keeps it, so a migration may only add. `scripts/release/migrations.mjs` reads each unshipped migration (comments and strings ignored, DO blocks read, ALTER actions one by one) and refuses dropping or renaming a table or column, a type change, NOT NULL without a default, SET NOT NULL, a blocking CREATE INDEX on an existing table, CONCURRENTLY outside a `-- volt:no-transaction` file; a removal passes with `-- volt:contract <what> after v0.60.0` naming a release already out. New boot top-ups (`bootSchema.ts`) are judged too. Shipped migrations are hashed in `release/migrations-baseline.json` (111 on 4 Nov; `release.mjs` adds each release's): not judged again, edits and removals refused. Runs from `check.mjs` (pre-commit, release, deploy). The runner (`services/migrate.ts`) runs a `-- volt:no-transaction` file statement by statement. `test/migration-safety.check.ts` 60/60. `--all` over every existing migration: 79 findings in 43 files (75 blocking indexes, 4 type changes in 0027 and 0080). Not done: SQL built inside EXECUTE strings is not seen. |
| HTTPS, certificates and DNS in Terraform (`deploy/terraform/dns_https.tf`) | 🟡 | **Built 11 Nov 2026 (build queue D3, v0.87.0); planned against mocked providers, not yet applied on AWS.** The manual inputs are `domain_name` and, when Route 53 hosts it, `route53_zone_id`. From them: `api.`, `cdn.`, `img.` and `origin.<domain>` (`hostnames` changes the labels); a DNS-validated certificate in us-east-1 for the CloudFront names (only distributions that exist) and one in the stack's region for the ALB (`origin`, plus `api` when `enable_api_edge` is off); the ALB's HTTPS listener (TLS 1.2+ policy) with port 80 redirecting; the API edge, the delivery CDN and the image CDN on their aliases with TLS 1.2; CloudFront and the encoders (`INTERNAL_API_BASE_URL`) reaching the ALB as `origin.<domain>` over HTTPS (`alb_origin_protocol` and `image_edge_origin_protocol` now follow the domain when left empty); `PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, `CDN_BASE_URL` and `IMAGE_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` on the new names; Route 53 validation and A/AAAA alias records with a zone, or the `dns_validation_records` and `dns_host_records` outputs without one (APPLY.md step 3: a targeted apply of the certificates first). No domain leaves the stack as before; aliases set by hand still win; an HTTP origin with a domain is refused at plan (the ALB would redirect CloudFront to itself); `env = prod` without a domain plans with a warning. `deploy/terraform/tests/dns_https.tftest.hcl` 11/11 (fails at HEAD), all terraform tests 21/21, `terraform fmt -check -recursive` and `validate` clean (1.9.8). Not done: the first real apply (Christian), and HTTPS for SRT and RTMP ingest addresses (D4). |
| S3 retention cleanup (delete, cool, archive, restore objects in the bucket) | 🟡 | **Built 20 Oct 2026 for clips and extracts; recordings blocked on a design choice.** The row said "DB row deleted; S3 sweep is a TODO" but the ladder in `services/media/retention.ts` had long replaced that. The real gap was underneath it: `storageDriver()` returned the LOCAL driver whatever `MEDIA_STORE_DRIVER` said, so on S3 the sweep "deleted" `s3://` locations with rmSync (nothing removed, row marked deleted) and cold/archive rungs were refused. Now `services/media/s3Driver.ts`: S3 driver (stat, in-place storage-class change for cold/archive, RestoreObject + restore status from `x-amz-restore`, DeleteObjects in batches of 1000) behind a router (s3:// to S3, paths to local). A trailing slash marks a prefix, so `s3://b/clips/abc` can never reach `clips/abcdef/`, and the bucket root is refused. `clip.extract` on an S3 deployment uploads the clip to `s3://<bucket>/clips/<assetId>/` as a second location of the same media object (`CLIPS_S3_UPLOAD=false` to keep clips local), and `tools/extract-clip.ts` takes `upload: {bucket, prefix}` and prints the URI to register. Fixed on the way: a cold or archive rung on an object with a local and an S3 copy moved the LOCAL one, which the local driver refuses, so such objects failed every sweep; the sweep now moves the object-storage copy. Checks: `test/s3Retention.check.ts` 39/39 against moto with the real AWS SDK (upload, content types, stat, cool to GLACIER_IR, archive to GLACIER, refusal without restore, restore, delete, sibling prefix untouched, sweep cools by rule, deletes local and S3 copies by rule leaving a tombstone, reclaims ownerless S3 media); offline it runs the pure part (8). media-lifecycle 120/120, media-library 43/43. **Open question (not built):** live recordings in S3 sit under the stream prefix (`channel/<id>/`, one prefix for every day and run), so a recording has no prefix of its own to delete and registering it as an S3 object would let a delete rung remove the live channel. Needs per-run S3 prefixes (the same change `CONSOLIDATION.md` asks for) or per-segment-range deletion by time. Until then S3 recordings are not registered and are not swept. Also pre-existing and unrelated: `clip-extract.check.ts` 31/33, two smart-render planning assertions expect full-render |

Manifest trim + multi-recording concat + progressive MP4 (ffmpeg -c copy). e2e/extractor-test.sh: MP4 decodes h264, exact 8s. S3-upload of outputs + ECS launch = wiring follow-up |

See the 27 Oct section "Chaos and scaling harness". The real run needs the AWS deployment (needs input) |

| **Relay tier in the fleet — `relays` + relay accruals (migration 0021)** | 🗑️ | The Icecast relay was a single `ICECAST_HOST:PORT` in the environment, which left three questions unanswerable: which relay is this station pointed at, how is it doing, and what does it cost — so the fleet page implied it was free. Everything needed already existed for encoders and was simply not pointed at the relay: `compute_hosts` for identity and first/last seen, `host_samples` for cpu, memory and network in/out, and the rates table whose prices stay flagged `indicative` until contracted ones replace them. **Two gaps remain**: `max_listeners` is stored and read by nothing, and there is no relay UI (the API exists behind `requirePlatform`) **Removed 3 Nov 2026 (2.1c-2, v0.32.0): no relay.** |
| Relay capacity and load balancing across relays | 🗑️ | Measured, decided, not built. Icecast package defaults are `<clients>100</clients>` and `<sources>2</sources>`; at 128 kbps a listener is 16 KB/s, so 1 Gbps ≈ 6,200 concurrent at 80% utilisation. The finding that settles the design: **a CDN cannot cache an endless stream**, so every edge holds an origin connection per listener and MP3 does not fan out the way HLS does. Self-served MP3 on inclusive bandwidth is ~6× cheaper per listener-hour than CDN-delivered HLS; AWS egress is the expensive combination. `docs/DECISION_mp3_delivery.md` **Dropped 3 Nov 2026 (2.1c-2): no relay.** |
| **Stream sizing + placement (migration 0045)** | ✅ | Built 13 Oct. Every start estimates the stream's CPU, GPU, memory and network from the encoder job it will run (`services/fleet/streamLoad.ts`: transcode vs package, ladder, fps, watermark, captions, backup source, DRM), reserves that plus 30% as a task-level ECS override, and places it on the fullest running server that passes `capacity.fits()` (reservation, task slots, and CPU and network under 65% after), else on a new server from the smallest pool that holds it (`placementPlanner.ts`). Every RunTask now binpacks on CPU, and a chosen server is pinned with memberOf, retried unpinned if it filled up. Channels stay on on-demand, events prefer Spot. Measured history for the same settings replaces the estimate (`calibrate`). Never blocks a start. Checks: stream-load 31, placement-planner 26, server-status 10, placement-e2e 21 against Postgres. **27 Oct (build queue 2.4a):** the coefficients are now learned from every run (multiplier per component and instance family, bounded 0.5x to 2x, outliers set aside), storage is projected from the bitrates, and placement can refuse a server whose task disk would run short (off until `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` is set, 2.4c); see the 27 Oct section. **Found while building:** a full 1080p x264 ladder needs about 4.6 vCPU and was being given 2 |
| Small + large server pools, termination protection | ✅ | Terraform: `ecs_capacity_large.tf` adds Spot and on-demand 2xlarge pools (zero servers until needed); managed termination protection is now ENABLED with `protect_from_scale_in`, so scale-in can only remove an empty server. Previously DISABLED, which let scale-in pick a server still carrying a live stream. `terraform validate` passes; not yet applied |
| Consolidation of under-used servers | 🟡 | Planner built (`consolidation.ts`, 28 checks), on the fleet pass in ADVISE mode: plan written to `fleet_plans`, readable at `GET /api/platform/fleet-plan`. Moves nothing. Acting on it needs the make-before-break handoff in `CONSOLIDATION.md` (per-run S3 prefix, playlist-writer handoff, stable push-ingest address) |

Measured: a 13 s join took **0.109 s** and produced exactly 75 + 250 = 325 packets, so a one-hour asset is effectively free and the body is never re-encoded. The trap, also measured: a parameter mismatch joins anyway, **exits 0**, and yields a file reporting 90.9 s and the wrong resolution — so the publish step must verify packet count against the sum of the parts before anything ships. Social cut-downs re-encode regardless, so burned graphics are free there. `docs/DESIGN_html_graphics_overlays.md` |

Standards-based design-around (no per-frame storage; open formats + prior art). `docs/DESIGN_frame_access.md`. **5 Sep review — half of this shipped and the other half was REPLACED rather than ported, which is worth being explicit about.** **Built and in daily use:** frame-exact cutting. `services/clips/resolve.ts` holds the segment index with each segment's *measured* true duration (the encoder targets 4s and lands where the keyframe lands), and `services/clips/extract.ts` puts `-ss` **after** `-i` — decode-and-discard output seeking, which is precisely what makes the cut frame-exact rather than keyframe-exact; input seeking would be faster and would land on the preceding keyframe. Driven by `modules/timeline.ts` (three-lane coverage API) and the clipping workbench, covered by `clip-extract.check.ts` and `clip-provenance.check.ts`. **Not built, and replaced by a different approach:** the prototype's JIT random access. There is no frame-index table in any migration, no single-frame pull endpoint, and no `EXT-X-I-FRAME-STREAM-INF` trick-play playlist anywhere in `volt-app` or `volt-encoder`. Scrubbing is served instead by `services/waveform.ts` and `services/thumbs.ts` (waveform buckets + filmstrip), which is cheaper and answers the editor's actual question. **Still genuinely open:** smart render — every cut needing a trim is a `full-render` today, and `extract.ts` already carries the `copy` vs `full-render` strategy split plus a note naming which segments a smart render would re-encode, so the seam exists. | Watchfolder ingest (assign a folder or S3 bucket/prefix as an auto ingest source) | ✅ | **Complete for local folders 6 Sep: schema, rules, quarantine, scanner, ingest, API and console.** Migration `0029_watchfolders.sql` adds `watchfolders`, `watchfolder_rules` (ordered, first match wins) and `watchfolder_items` — deliberately ONE table for the idempotency log and the QC queue, because they are the same fact seen twice, and splitting them is how a quarantined file gets re-quarantined on every scan. **The requirement that shaped it: a file that matches nothing is neither ingested as `other` nor deleted.** It is moved to a review path, the reason is STORED (not recomputed against rules somebody has since edited), and a person corrects, reclassifies or removes it. `services/watchfolder/rules.ts` is pure, so the same function answers "what would happen to this file" for the console's dry run and "what happens" for the scanner — a dry run with its own copy of the logic would be worse than no dry run, because it would be confidently wrong. Hygiene runs before the rules: `.DS_Store`, `~$` locks, `.part` uploads still copying, and the quiet one, a correctly-named **zero-byte** file from a failed copy. `services/watchfolder/scanner.ts` walks the folder, never descends into the quarantine path, only considers files whose mtime has been QUIET for `min_age_ms` (a file copied under its final name is briefly a truncated file with a perfectly good name), dedupes on **name + content** so a corrected re-drop is a new item rather than a silent no-op, and moves a refused file BEFORE writing its row. `services/watchfolder/ingest.ts` turns an accepted item into a `media_files` record: it **re-hashes the file first**, because the row's checksum is a claim about the past and an operator can drop a corrected version over the top between passes; a file that has moved is closed as `failed`, not left at `seen` to be re-read on every pass forever. Bytes go to the store BEFORE the row, so the failure mode is an orphaned object rather than a library record pointing at nothing; a name collision is suffixed rather than failed, since `media_files` is UNIQUE on (account_id, path) and refusing the second is a file that can never ingest; and a target the image library cannot hold (`vod`, `loop`) is **deferred to its own workflow, never coerced into `other`**. The item is also **claimed atomically** before any bytes move, because the 60s tick and the operator’s Scan button are two callers over one queue: guarding only the closing UPDATE is too late, since by then both have written a library record and the second is suffixed, reading as though the file was dropped twice. `/api/watchfolders` gives folders and rules CRUD, a dry run, scan-and-ingest, the item queue, and reprocess/remove. Console **Watchfolders** page (Media): numbered movable rules with the winning rule highlighted, the filename dry run, Scan now, and the review queue where Dismiss says on the button that the file is left where it is. Scanned and ingested every 60s by the orchestrator, on its own error boundary. `test/watchfolder-rules.check.ts` 43/43, `test/watchfolder-scan.check.ts` 33/33 against real files, `test/watchfolder-api.check.ts` 26/26 against a real Postgres, `test/watchfolder-ingest.check.ts` 70/70 — each verified negatively by reverting the guard and confirming the suite fails. **Remaining: S3 event-driven acquisition (EventBridge/SQS ObjectCreated + reconcile scan).** *(23 Oct 2026, build queue 1.13: VOD and loop targets checked end to end and three faults fixed; see the 23 Oct section.)* `docs/DESIGN_file_manager_watchfolder.md` |

| File-based video encoding (upload or watchfolder → encode job on the live ladder) | ✅ | **Complete 6 Sep: engine, both ingest paths, and the console.** The requirement was that a file encode MATCH the live transcoder, and the way that is achieved is the whole design. Copying the live rungs into a VOD ladder is correct on the day it is written and wrong for the rest of the system's life: somebody re-tunes the live 720p rung, both encodes keep succeeding, both outputs still look plausible, and the only symptom is that the two disagree. So `0030_encode_profiles.sql` adds `encode_profiles` (one rendition) and `encode_ladder_groups` (a named ordered set), and a group can **track** a live `output_profile` rather than copy it: it holds no rungs, reads them at encode time, and takes the tracked profile's segment length and fps too, because GOP = segment × fps and a different GOP puts the keyframes somewhere else. Verified against a real Postgres: change the live profile, the file ladder follows with no write of its own. The per-rung ENCODER settings (preset, maxrate 1.1×, bufsize 2×, GOP, sc_threshold, forced keyframes, AAC 128k) were inline in `encoder.py:start_transcode`; they are now `volt-encoder/bin/ladder_args.py`, imported by the live path and the file path alike, and the extraction was proved byte-identical against a captured golden for both x264 and NVENC. `dev/test_ladder_args.py` reads the source and fails if either caller starts building them by hand — verified by putting back a hand-rolled copy that produces IDENTICAL arguments today and confirming it still fails. `bin/vod_encoder.py` probes, encodes every rendition in ONE ffmpeg pass (N passes decode the source N times) and packages CMAF HLS+DASH; proved end to end on a real 720p source: three renditions with keyframes at identical timestamps, `EXT-X-PLAYLIST-TYPE:VOD`, `mediaPresentationDuration=PT12S`. **A real bug caught in that run:** the HLS playlist was VOD and the MPD was `type="dynamic"` with a 30-minute `timeShiftBufferDepth` over a 12-second file, because Shaka takes two different flags and only one was set — checking the HLS playlist and concluding the package was right is being correct about the wrong question. `services/encode/ladder.ts` fits the ladder to the source: never upscale, and a **height that was not measured is not a height of zero** (zero drops every rung and produces an asset with no renditions while the log claims the source was tiny). A source below every rung adapts the lowest rung to the source's own height rather than encoding nothing and reporting success. `vod.encode` runs on the durable jobs table; the watchfolder's `vod`/`loop` targets stop being deferred, with `loop` registered as a filler and NOT laddered because its consumer re-encodes it anyway, and the source **copied out of the drop folder before the job is queued** so an operator tidying the folder cannot break a job that was accepted. `test/encode-ladder.check.ts` 50/50, `test/watchfolder-video.check.ts` 53/53, `dev/test_ladder_args.py` + `dev/test_vod_encoder.py` 30/30, each verified negatively. Console: **Media → Encoding Profiles** manages renditions and ladders, shows a tracking ladder's resolved rungs with where they come from, and previews which rungs a given source height would get through the SAME `fitToSource()` the encode runs. Ladder pickers on the watchfolder detail and the Media Library upload panel; the library row shows the encode's live state and, once done, the rungs kept and dropped. `0031_vod_uploads.sql` links an upload session to its VOD asset so a two-hour upload is visible and can be watched to fail while its bytes are in flight, with a CHECK that a session points at one asset, not two. **A bug caught while wiring the library:** `vod_assets` ownership was an INNER JOIN through the channel or event, correct while every asset came from a recording and silent death for one that does not — uploaded assets would have encoded successfully and then never appeared in any list. Now a LEFT JOIN with `COALESCE(g.account_id, v.account_id)`, verified against a real Postgres: the old join found 0 uploaded assets, the new one finds them, recording-derived assets are unchanged, and another account still sees none. `test/encode-store.check.ts` pins the assertion the feature rests on — re-tune the live profile, re-read the ladder, and it has changed with no write of its own. **Remaining: S3 watchfolders, and a re-encode action on an asset whose ladder later changes.** |
| TAMS - Time Addressable Media Store API (BBC/AMWA open standard, Apache 2.0) | 📐 | Strong fit: our CMAF-segments-in-S3 + Postgres segment index already = TAMS essence + metadata planes; recordings/renditions/segments → Sources/Flows/Flow Segments/Media Objects; non-destructive clip = TAMS edit-by-reference (copy-on-write). Plan: read-side API first (`GET /flows` + `/flows/:id/segments?timerange=` → presigned S3 URLs) over the frame-access segment store, then write-side (storage + segment register); add TAMS nanosecond-TAI timerange layer + account scoping. Interop: AWS MediaConvert accepts TAMS input. Complements frame-access (TAMS = store/API; frame index = frame-level read) |

## 5. Domain model & taxonomy

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Two-plane composition (production vs distribution) | ✅ | **Built 20 Oct 2026 as a read model over the existing tables** (`domain_model.md`: conceptual separation first, sub-documents rather than services): `GET /api/live-streams/:kind/:id` (`services/streamModel.ts composeStream`, feature `monitoring: view`, tenant-scoped) returns the PRODUCTION plane (origin: live inputs with roles and failover, or the FAST lineup; processing: profile, workflow, ladder, packaging, DRM, captions, watermark burned in only on transcode; placement: the running task) and the DISTRIBUTION plane (VideoObject + BroadcastEvent, metadata schema and values, availability: on air, public URL, share links, geo). Writes still go through each resource's own API. `test/stream-model.check.ts` 25/25 |
| Unified spine + origin×lifecycle presets (Channel/Event/FAST) | ✅ | **Presets built 20 Oct 2026, derived not stored** (`product_positioning.md`): `PRESETS` in `services/streamModel.ts` (live channel = live ingest + continuous, live event = live ingest + bounded, FAST channel = scheduled playout + continuous, premiere = scheduled playout + bounded), `presetOf()` from the row, on every aggregate and at `GET /api/live-streams/presets`; a new preset is a row, not a code path. **One list endpoint BUILT 23 Oct 2026 (build queue 1.12, v0.12.0):** `GET /api/live-streams` over channels, stations, events and FAST channels with the list standard; the Live Streams List view reads it; see the 23 Oct section. **Loop preset removed 23 Oct:** a loop is a filler asset in Loops & Fillers, not a kind of stream (Christian, 22 Oct), and `channels.origin` never allowed it |
| Metadata schemas assignable to events/channels (not just VOD) + schema compose/extends + `reference` field type | ✅ | Built 2 Aug: `target_kind`/`domain`/`extends_id` on schemas, `schema_id`+`metadata` on channels/events, `mergeFields` composition, `reference` type, `resolveEffectiveFields`. 9 tests; caught+fixed a forward-FK bug |

## 6. EPG & scheduling

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Scheduled clips (timeline → auto-VOD at end) | ✅ | `scheduledClips.ts` + orchestrator fires it |
| EPG programme store, RRULE recurrence, materialized instances, conflict detection | ✅ | Built 2 Aug: `programmes`+`programme_series` (tstzrange span, GiST/GIN idx), `epg.ts` (RRULE expand, effective-end), `/api/epg` (guide/now-next/conflicts/series). 7 pure + 6 HTTP tests |
| **EPG repeats, Copy or repeat day, undo (19 Oct)** | ✅ | Replaces the 19 Oct correction (recurrence was API-only, never topped up, no edit/delete, channels only). **One repeat engine for a programme or a whole day** (`services/epgRepeat.ts` dates + wall-clock in a zone, DST-safe; `services/epgPlace.ts` placement): a day repeat is a series with one item per programme of the source day, a programme repeat has one item. Rules: daily / every N days, weekdays, weekly on chosen days (every N weeks), ending after N weeks, on a date, or open-ended (kept 60 days ahead by the scheduler tick, idempotent via `source_ref`). **Same rule for anything already there**, for Copy day, repeats and the top-up: replace (default) / only fill empty time / keep both; never removes a programme that has started or aired, has a VOD, or came from an imported feed or the radio log (that slot is skipped, with the reason); never places anything in the past; an identical programme is taken over, not "replaced". **Preview** (`dryRun`) before every apply; **Undo** for copy, repeat and Clear day (`epg_batches`). A removed or replaced occurrence is written to the series' exdates so the top-up never refills it. Copy day keeps independent copies to dates, skips a repeat occurrence its own repeat already covers, and now takes only programmes that START on the day (the 23:00 programme from the night before was copied twice). Edits and deletes of an occurrence: this one (remembered as individually edited), this and following (splits the repeat), all repeats; Stop repeating after this. Events repeat too. Migration 0058. UI: Copy or repeat day modal, Repeat section in the programme panel, Undo in the toast, Repeats badge. `test/epg-repeat-pure.check.ts` 40/40 (incl. Sydney DST), `test/epg-repeat.check.sh` 45/45; epg-extended 18, epg 7, epg-vod 32, xmltv 49, xmltv-import 35+42 unchanged and passing. Still not built from the concept: week view, FAST slot rounding/pad, extend-live with re-place-next. |
| **EPG timeline view (19 Oct)** | ✅ | Guide page has a List / Timeline switch (remembered per viewer). `pages/EpgTimeline.tsx`: vertical 24h axis, three zoom levels, blocks sized by real duration, overlaps drawn side by side in lanes, gaps shown and clickable, now line, programmes crossing midnight clipped and labelled, click empty time to add a programme at that time (15 min snap), block opens the same edit drawer. Typechecked; rendered with sample data. |
| **Plan vs as-run for channel guides (20 Oct 2026)** | ✅ | Nothing wrote as-run for TV channels (only radio, from stream titles). `services/asRun.ts` decides it from the encoder's own events: a run is a `channel.start` to the next `channel.stop` (or now), and `playout.splice` to loop/slate and back marks filler. Each programme gets `actual_start` (first moment on air, e.g. later than planned when the channel came up late), `actual_end` once it has ended, status `live` while airing and `aired` after; one that never aired keeps `scheduled` with no actual times. The orchestrator stamps it every minute for TV channels that ran in the last 6 h, writing only rows that changed (so guide caches are not invalidated for nothing). `GET /api/epg/as-run?channelId=&from=&to=` (max 7 days, tenant-scoped) compares plan and as-run per programme: deltas, on-air, source and filler seconds, outcome aired / partial / missed / airing / upcoming / cancelled, and totals. XMLTV export and the timeline already prefer actual times. Checks: `test/as-run.check.ts` 26/26 (pure: outage, late start, loop, live now, missed; real rows; the HTTP report; another account 404; 7 day cap), epg 7/7, epg-extended 18/18, epg-pure, epg-repeat-pure 40/40, epg-vod 32/32, orchestrator. `epg-repeat.check.sh` cannot sign in on the test VM (it hashes a password with the Mac's argon2 native module); unrelated to this change |
| **XMLTV export (console download · API pull · partner feeds · per-channel XMLTV ids)** | ✅ | **Video + audio.** Built 10 Oct. `services/xmltv.ts` (pure builder: DTD element order, offsets per instant so DST is right either side of a change, zero-based `xmltv_ns` plus `onscreen`, escaping and XML 1.0 control-character stripping, radio programmes marked `<video><present>no</present>`), `modules/xmltv.ts` (streamed per channel, optional gzip, ETag/Last-Modified over a digest of exactly what would be emitted so an unchanged guide is a 304). Three ways in: `GET /api/epg/xmltv` (session or secret account key, scope in the query: channels, pastDays/futureDays or from/to up to 93 days, `times=scheduled|asrun` stated rather than defaulted silently, `tz=local|utc`, includeEvents), `GET /xmltv/<token>.xml[.gz]` partner feeds (public, token = credential + scope, revocable/rotatable per partner, disabled or suspended account answers the same 404, polls metered) and `GET /api/epg/channels` (the JSON channel list this table already claimed existed; it did not until now). `channels.xmltv_id` (unique per account, defaults to the UUID). Excludes cancelled programmes and guide-disabled channels. Console: **EPG → XMLTV export** drawer (download, partner feeds, channel ids). A channel with `origin = playlist` (migration 0065). **Builder:** a run order of slots that loops (pass after pass, not a day): title slots with a rotation pool (one per pass), "skip this turn", every Nth pass; series slots (N episodes each time, in order or shuffled with no repeats, loop or stop, encore of another slot); free-running (HBO model: a 14 h pass airs each film ~12 times a week at 12 clock times) or daily restart; rotate per pass; grid rounding with pad; rights-window backfill; plays a week per slot and "want N / week"; repeat report; week view (`services/fast/lineup.ts`, `volt-web/src/pages/Fast.tsx`). Deterministic; versions carry the run order and every series on, no hole after the programme on air. **Guide:** activating writes programmes + `stream_breaks`, topped up every 10 min. **Playout:** `volt-encoder/bin/fast_playout.py`, no encoder: live HLS (`v<height>.m3u8`, `audio.m3u8`) and multi-Period DASH from packaged VOD, discontinuity per part, elastic slate, audio cut by media time, state carried over restarts; run per channel by the control plane with the local launcher (`services/fast/playoutRunner.ts`). `vod_encoder.py` now starts media time at zero so audio and video segments line up and any new asset can go on a FAST channel without re-encoding. **Measured** in Chromium/Shaka: channel HLS 0 stalls, channel DASH 0 stalls, a stitched viewer session 0 stalls. ## 7. Geo / rights / delivery

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Geo/rights evaluation in the play gate (blackout per current programme) | ✅ | Built 3 Aug: `rights.ts` + wired into `/availability` + `/access/check`; block/allow-list by country. 7 tests. (Edge CloudFront-Function switching still prototype) |
| Program replacement (blackout → replacement asset) | ✅ | Built 3 Aug: blocked region gets the programme's replacement asset + a playback token for it. Tested |
| **Edge rights: CloudFront Function + KeyValueStore on every manifest request (20 Oct 2026)** | 🟡 | **23 Oct (build queue 1.14a): the single `_rp` replacement playlist is replaced by per-audience variants and a signed rights class; see the 23 Oct section "Edge rights variants". What follows is the 20 Oct state.** **Built and tested offline; not deployed.** `deploy/edge/rights-viewer-request.js` (viewer-request, cloudfront-js-2.0) on the `*.m3u8` and `*.mpd` behaviours only: reads `CloudFront-Viewer-Country` and the stream's rules from the KeyValueStore (key `s:<id>`), then allow, 451 (not cached), or rewrite to the replacement playlists (`*_cc` to `*_rp`, same URL for the viewer). Segments never run it. No key = open; an unreadable store or a broken value fails OPEN (an outage of the rules must not take channels off air; the play gate still enforces). Viewer-specific access rules stay in the play gate. `volt-app/src/services/edgeRights.ts`: compiles account geo (`accounts.geo_access`), the channel's geo and programme blackout windows for the next 24 h into a compact value inside the store's 1 KB (nearest windows first, `u` = complete until, rewritten an hour before), syncs every minute from the orchestrator when `EDGE_RIGHTS_KVS_ARN` is set, writing only what changed. Terraform `edge_rights.tf`: KeyValueStore, function (templatefile injects the store id), association on both manifest behaviours, control plane IAM, `EDGE_RIGHTS_KVS_ARN` env, output; `edge_rights_enabled` (default true) turns it all off. `terraform validate` passes. Checks: `test/edge-rights.check.ts` 37/37 (the real function file with a stand-in KeyValueStore: account block, channel allow list incl. unknown country, programme window before/during/after on the same URL, replace to `_rp` for master, media and DASH, fail-open cases, 1 KB trimming, database loader, sync put/unchanged/delete/memo/refresh). **Open, in order:** (1) install `@aws-sdk/client-cloudfront-keyvaluestore` and `@aws-sdk/signature-v4-crt` in volt-app (the KeyValueStore API signs with SigV4A; not installed here because `node_modules` is shared with the Mac); (2) the encoder does not yet write the `*_rp` replacement playlists (a second playout timeline with the slate spliced over the replace windows, sent on the timed-events poll), so a replace window is enforced as a block at the edge and the app still gets the replacement asset from `/api/availability`; (3) `terraform apply` |
| CloudFront native geo-restriction | 🟡 | **Placeholder removed 5 Sep.** Was a hardcoded `restriction_type = "none"`; now driven by `var.cdn_geo_restriction_type` (validated `none|whitelist|blacklist`) and `var.cdn_geo_countries`, defaulting to `none` so behaviour is unchanged until somebody sets it. **Stays 🟡 on purpose, and the reason matters before anyone treats this as the geo feature.** CloudFront applies ONE country list to the WHOLE distribution, so it cannot express "this programme is blacked out in these markets" — that is what `rights.ts` already does per-programme in the play gate, with a replacement asset for a blocked viewer. What this buys is a hard edge stop for a country we must not serve at all (a sanctions list, a licence held nowhere): enforced before a request reaches the origin and not dependent on our token logic being right. It cannot go ✅ until a `terraform apply` has happened, and nothing has ever been applied |

## 8. Analytics & reporting

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Server-side audience/QoS/realtime from CDN logs; by-app lead; no-referrer VPN classification | ✅ | Built 2 Aug: Postgres serving layer (`play_sessions`, `analytics_rollups`, `stream_concurrency`), UA/referrer parsing, beacon ingest (playback-token), reports (realtime/asset/audience/usage). CloudFront→Kinesis firehose = scale path. 6 tests |
| WAF enforcement (paid add-on) | 📐 | designed; not built |
| **Air ledger — `air_sessions` (migration 0023)** | ✅ | Built 5 Sep. "Total hours on air" had no defensible source: a channel going on air ran a bare `UPDATE channels SET on_air` and left no trace, radio wrote a `stream_events` row an operator can now clear with a button, and an event's `public_start`/`public_end` are a PLAN written at creation. One row per window of public delivery; `to_at IS NULL` means on air now, so one table answers the live question and the historical one. Identity is snapshotted and FK-free (as `usage_accruals` does) so the record outlives the stream, and a rename does not rewrite history. A partial unique index `(stream_id) WHERE to_at IS NULL` is the invariant: overlapping windows double-count and every row still looks correct. `services/airSessions.ts` owns the column AND the ledger together, because four call sites used to write `on_air` and already disagreed about whether to record anything; `test/air-sessions.check.ts` reads the source and fails if a fifth appears. 37/37. `docs/REQ_stream_reporting.md` |
| Per-stream operating report (runs, hours run vs hours on air, peak concurrent) + CSV | ✅ | **Built 19 Oct 2026.** `services/streamReport.ts` + `modules/reports.ts`: `GET /api/reports/stream/:id[.csv]` and `GET /api/reports/streams[.csv]` (period summary, kind/q/sort), gated by `analytics`. Runs from `usage_accruals`, on air from `air_sessions` (events: run time inside the public window, labelled `air_basis`), why each run ended from the new `run_stops` (migration 0059, written by `stopStream`), exact per-run peak by sweeping `play_sessions` while raw is retained, otherwise `not_retained` (null, never 0) with the day's rollup peak. Availability, planned/unplanned stops, MTTR, MP3 listeners for stations, cost basis for platform readers only. **Found and fixed:** `usage_accruals.instance_id` is ON DELETE SET NULL, so deleting a channel collapsed all its runs into one; 0059 adds FK-free `instance_ref` (backfilled, meter writes it). Console: Analytics → Operating (summary, per-stream page with run strip, CSV). `test/stream-report.check.ts` 77/77 covers all six acceptance criteria. Open: availability treats every channel as 24x7 (a part-time channel reads low until channels carry an expected schedule); quality block (dead air, degraded output, failover time) not yet included. |
| **Listener sessions — measured MP3 egress (migration 0022)** | ✅ | The only place egress is a fact rather than an estimate. Every earlier figure was bitrate × assumed duration, or a host's `net_out_bps` integrated and divided among whichever stations shared the box — an estimate of an estimate. The on-demand gateway terminates the listener connection itself, so it WROTE every byte it reports: one row per listener, closed on leave, with the measured count. Makes per-station and per-account relay cost answerable for pricing. No IP stored; `ip_hash` is salted and truncated |
| Listener count = the whole audience, not the MP3 clients | ✅ | Built 5 Sep: `listenerCounts()` sums MP3 fan-out clients and live player concurrency from `stream_concurrency` (across asset kinds, 120s freshness, matching the fleet total in `platform.ts`). The console rendered `p.mp3Clients.size` as "0 MP3 listeners", so a station with an HLS audience and no MP3 clients reported zero. Breakdown kept for the tooltip; null renders as no line rather than a zero |

## 9. Frontend

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Site-wide search (channels · events · recordings) | ✅ | Built 16 Aug: `GET /api/search?q=` account-scoped across the three entities (defensive per entity), top-panel field with a debounced results dropdown that navigates on click. |
| Radio section (audio-only scope) | ✅ | Built 16 Aug: `/radio` + `/radio/:id` reuse the channel list + detail at the audio scope, station wording, audio-ladder column and editor, create form with artwork + AAC rungs. |
| **Auto connect is armed by a healthy run, not by pressing Start (migration 0024)** | ✅ | Built 4 Sep. `desired_state='running'` was written the moment Start was pressed and the resume pass read that as licence to relaunch, so a channel whose **first** start failed (wrong source URL, relay never running) was relaunched five times twenty seconds apart before anyone could read why — each relaunch overwriting the log with a fresh identical failure, burying the one reason the operator needed under four copies of itself. `resume_armed_at` is now stamped by the health pass the first time SERVER, INPUT, ENCODER and OUTPUT are all healthy at once. Until then a fault is a failed start and is left exactly where it fell |
| Operator can put down a give-up notice, and clear the event log | ✅ | Built 4 Sep: `POST /radio/:id/clear-recovery` and `DELETE /radio/:id/events`. The trap in the first: `resume_gave_up_at IS NULL` is one of four conditions for auto connect and a FAILED stream still carries `desired_state='running'` deliberately, so clearing the give-up alone hands the crash loop straight back. Dismissing is an acknowledgement, not a retry — it clears the give-up **and** stands the intent down. The trap in the second: a cleared log must not read as a log of nothing, so the delete is followed by one `log.cleared` entry naming the actor and the count, with its own audit row because the event log cannot be its own witness. `test/station-clear.check.ts` 25/25 |
| Recovery notice styling and placement | ✅ | Built 5 Sep: full transparent-red panel matching the primary-error banner rather than a card with an offset left rule, and moved from above the breadcrumb to under the channel header, where it sits with the other failure banners on both the channel and station pages |
| Monitoring: fleet activity reads as events, not as a wall | ✅ | Built 4 Sep: `activityLog.ts` collapses runs of the same owner+type+detail into one row with a `×9 over 1m` badge, and shows an absolute clock as the primary with the relative time underneath. One station appearing nine times in a feed was a repeated heartbeat rendered as nine incidents. Root cause fixed too: `packager()` in `volt-encoder/bin/events.py` emitted an event on **every** heartbeat call — a level reported as an edge — and now only fires on transition. 24 + 13 assertions |
| Recordings counts computed before filtering | ✅ | Built 4 Sep: the tab counts were derived from the already-filtered list, so "Recording now" read 0 while a station was demonstrably capturing.
