## 10. SaaS platform layer

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Multi-tenant isolation (every API filtered by account_id) | ✅ | Built + audited 2 Aug; cross-tenant isolation test (account A can't see B's data). `docs/SAAS_ARCHITECTURE.md` §2 |
| Analytics data store (clearly defined) | ✅ | Postgres serving + S3/Athena raw + Redis/Timestream realtime; documented in `SAAS_ARCHITECTURE.md` §1 |
| SaaS-admin vs client-admin roles | ✅ | `platform_role` on users + `requirePlatform` middleware (saas_admin/support/read_only). 4 auth tests |
| SaaS-admin dashboard (servers/perf, upcoming events, projected instances, active channels, viewers, admins, API status, tickets) | ✅ | `/api/platform/overview` + `/accounts`. Cross-tenant aggregation. Tested |
| Support tickets (client file + SaaS triage) | ✅ | `support_tickets` + `/api/tickets` (client) + `/api/platform/tickets` (triage). Tested |
| Account impersonation ("view as") | 🟡 | **Backend built and audit-logged; the hold is a decision, not a gap.** `POST /api/platform/impersonate/:accountId` in `modules/platform.ts:202`, behind `requirePlatform` + TOTP, writing a `platform.impersonate` row to `audit_log` on every use. What is missing is only the console affordance — no "view as" control exists in `volt-web`, deliberately, **deferred past initial deploy** per your call. Related and worth keeping in view: `auth/jwt.ts` records that the pre-MFA challenge token once reached this route, which is why `typ` is now checked on every access token. Stays 🟡 because a feature an operator cannot reach is not shipped, but nothing here is unfinished code |
| SSO - Microsoft / Google (OIDC) | 📐 | `identity_providers` schema + flow plan in `SAAS_ARCHITECTURE.md` §4; OIDC exchange built next |

## 11. API access control (public vs admin, keyed)

| Capability | Status | Notes / source |
|---|---|---|
| Public vs admin API classification (map + enforced) | ✅ | `docs/API_ACCESS_MAP.md`; every route tagged public/admin/platform/ingest. Enforcement test 17/17 |
| Content filtered by account key - no cross-tenant access | ✅ | admin credential → one `account_id`; every query filtered; isolation + ACL tests |
| Dual-credential admin auth (session OR account secret key) | ✅ | `requireAuth` accepts JWT or `sk_live` (mgmt scope); publishable keys refused for admin. Tested |
| Public play surface: get-play-URL + check-access | ✅ | `/api/availability` (mints token) + `/api/access/check` (booleans); publishable-key auth |
| Share page - secure time-boxed standalone preview link | ✅ | Built 3 Aug: `share_links` table + `shareLinks.ts` + `/api/share-links` (create/list/revoke, account-scoped) + public `/share/:token` isolated page (`sharePage.ts`) + `/api/share/:token/resolve`+`/download`. Time-boxed, revoke, max-views, optional PIN, watermarked preview, allow-download (hi-res/metadata) + notify-on-open, geo/rights enforced, cross-tenant isolated. Renders to the supplied mockup. 20 e2e assertions. Workflow: `docs/WORKFLOW_share_page.md` |
| Share preview drawer + link management in the React app | ✅ | Built 16 Aug: two-step drawer on channel detail (also on radio station and event pages since; not on Media Library assets) (set up → created link). Recipients + **email the one-time URL** at create/rotate, expiry preset **or an exact date/time**, PIN (generate/clear, min 3), view cap, watermark + notify-on-open toggles, note. Created step shows copy, open, **QR** (`volt-web/src/qr.ts`, dependency-free, verified against a real reader), expiry countdown, PIN reminder, delivery count. Link list per channel: view stats, last opened, recipients, **extend**, **new URL (rotate)**, revoke. API gained `PATCH /api/share-links/:id`, `POST /:id/rotate`, `?resourceKind&resourceId` filtering, `has_pin`. 42 assertions in `share-links.check.sh`. `docs/WORKFLOW_share_page.md` |
| Sub-accounts (parent → child subtree) | 🟡 | `accounts.parent_account_id` + `accountSubtree()` helper (8 tests); per-query subtree wiring = follow-up. **5 Sep review: still true, and the file admits it.** `services/tenancy.ts` exports `accountSubtree()` (recursive CTE) and `isInSubtree()`, and its own header notes that auth still resolves a single `account_id`. Neither export is called from any module — the same no-caller shape as `eventPromo`. **Blocked on a decision, not on effort**: wiring the subtree into every query means answering what a parent should actually see (all children's channels? their usage and billing? their users? read-only or operable?), and each answer is a different set of query changes and a different RBAC surface. Cheap once decided; wrong to guess |
| SaaS-admin MFA (2-step login) | ✅ | Built 3 Aug: `totp.ts` (RFC 6238, no dep) + `/api/platform/auth/login`→enrol→`/mfa`→session. 9 TOTP-vector + 8 flow tests |
| **Sessions that last as long as the work (silent token refresh)** | ✅ | Built 5 Sep. The refresh token, the hashed store and `POST /auth/refresh` all existed and **nothing ever called them**: the sign-in screen read `access` and dropped `refresh`, and the only 401 handling in the console was a bounce to the login form. So every session was capped at `ACCESS_TTL` of wall clock — 15 minutes, mid-broadcast, whatever the operator was doing. The console now renews proactively a minute before expiry, recovers reactively on a 401 with one retry, and does both through a **single-flight** promise, because this console fires many concurrent requests and a refresh per failure would stampede an endpoint that rotates the credential — the first response would invalidate the token the others still hold and sign the operator out via the fix. Presence is tracked from pointer/key/wheel/touch/visibility events only, **never from API traffic**, so a tab left open overnight is not mistaken for someone working. Refresh token stays in memory; persisting it survives a reload and is a separate decision with a different risk |
| Refresh endpoint honours user standing, and rotates | ✅ | Two holes that had to close before a long session was safe. **A disabled user was never signed out**: the query joined `users` for `account_id` and `role` and never read `u.status`, so a disabled account minted fresh access tokens for the full 14 days — while the Users page states "Disabling signs the user out (at the next token refresh)", an interface making a claim the code did not implement. Both user status and account suspension are now checked. **Rotation would have removed the cap**: the replacement inherits the original row's `expires_at` rather than getting a fresh 14 days, or an absolute cap silently becomes a sliding window that never closes. Logout revokes server-side; `expiresIn` comes from `config.jwt.accessTtl` instead of a `900` literal that appeared twice. `test/session-refresh.check.ts` |

## How to keep design and build converged
1. **This file is the checklist.** Update the status column whenever something
   is built or decided, in the same session. A decision recorded in memory
   without a status change here = a gap that will resurface (this is exactly what
   happened with Hydra).
2. **Design docs point back here.** The chat-2/3 docs (metadata/source-provider,
   EPG, geo-rights, analytics, api-keys) are specs; each maps to one or more rows
   above. "Delivered a doc" ≠ "built."
3. **Suggested convergence order** (implementation catching up to design):
   API hypermedia layer (closes the most-cited gap) → metadata-on-events +
   two-plane model → EPG/scheduling core → geo/rights edge → analytics ingest →
   real React app. Encoder functional gaps (captions, vod-extractor) slot in
   wherever they unblock a demo.

## 2026-08-13 update - streaming works end to end + detail page de-faked

- LIVE STREAM END TO END (working, verified): create a Transcode channel with an
  HLS/RTMP/SRT pull source, Send source, and the real encoder ingests, builds the ABR
  ladder (x264), packages CMAF (HLS+DASH) to the origin, and the app plays it. Verified
  in a full local stack against the JR HLS source (1080/720/480 ABR served + playable).
- CHANNEL DETAIL wired to the REAL encoder (was driving the synthetic test-card). Source
  + output players load /origin/<id>/master.m3u8; Send source / Drop source call the
  production start/stop.
- DETAIL PAGE DE-FABRICATED: EPG (was Sunrise/Morning News...), viewers (was ~18k), status
  board and event log were all invented in channelDetail.ts. Now real only: status from
  stream_status, event log from stream_events PLUS the real encoder.log error lines (see
  WHY a source/output failed), EPG from the programmes table (empty state when none),
  viewers empty until real analytics.

  reset); removed the stale volt-update.bundle step that aborted startup.

  runtime). README updated with setup / test / local build / cloud deploy.

  create form + detail; cap output rungs to source. ffprobe proven; UI + endpoint next.
- Settings fields read-only (copyable) while running, editable when stopped. Next.

- Real analytics wiring for the viewers card.

## Recording, clipping, VOD and the media lifecycle

*Added 23–24 Aug. Every row below is verified by an assertion in `volt-app/test/`,
against real media on disk rather than fixtures. 481 assertions across 13 suites.*

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Migration runner** | ✅ | Numbered `NNNN_name.sql`, one transaction each, advisory-locked, adopts pre-runner databases, refuses `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE`. Boot refuses to serve on a failed migration |
| **Durable job system** | ✅ | `jobs` rows claimed `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` against a LEASE, not a flag; reaper for expired leases; exponential backoff; `PermanentError` skips retries; `dedupe_key` for idempotent enqueue; `job_attempts` history. `test/jobs.check.ts` 43 |
| **Frame-accurate clips** | ✅ | Output seeking (`-ss` after `-i`), per-block render then concat demuxer. A 20,000ms request produces 20,023ms of media. `test/clip-extract.check.ts` 36 |
| **Broadcast-day containers** | ✅ | 06:00→06:00 in channel-local time; a clock-change day is 23 or 25 hours. tz and anchor FROZEN on the container so editing the channel cannot re-file history. Verified across both UK changes, New York and Sydney. `test/broadcast-day.check.ts` 35 |
| **Dayparts** | ✅ | Anchors only — a start time per channel, each running to the next, so gaps and overlaps are unrepresentable. UK presets default. `test/containers-dayparts.check.sh` 23 |
| **Three-lane timeline** | ✅ | Media, time, on-air on one wall-clock axis from ONE response, so the lanes cannot disagree about a boundary. Spans containers: a capture boundary is a mark on the axis, never the end of it |
| **Audio waveform in the media lane** | ✅ | True peak on a LINEAR amplitude scale, per-run cache, progressive fill. A flat block says a recording exists; the waveform says where to cut. `test/waveform.check.sh` 10 |
| **Video thumbnails in the media lane** | ✅ | Keyframe extraction, free because the encoder forces an IDR on every segment boundary. Cold strip of six: 263ms. A slot with no media stays empty rather than borrowing a neighbour's frame. `test/thumbs.check.ts` 27 |
| **Clip provenance chain** | ✅ | `clip_segments`: one row per contiguous block, back to run, rendition, segment range, recording and broadcast day. ON DELETE SET NULL so the chain OUTLIVES its sources. `GET /api/vod/:id/provenance`, and `GET /api/recordings/:id/derived` in reverse. `test/clip-provenance.check.ts` 57 |
| **Media lifecycle** | ✅ | `media_objects` (what it is, can it be read now) separate from `media_locations` (where the bytes are, what reading them costs). Nine states. Deletion leaves a tombstone with its logical key, size and history |
| **Retention ladder** | ✅ | Ordered rungs: cool, archive, delete. Walked oldest-first, so year-old media is deleted directly rather than cooled, archived and deleted across three sweeps. Holds outrank every rule. Dry run before it fires |
| **Feature-gated archive** | ✅ | Cold and archive actions offered ONLY on S3 (or compatible) with archiving explicitly enabled. On local storage a rule naming them is refused at WRITE time with the reason. Restore records the tier's published window and says "expected by", never "ready at"; a restored copy lapses back on its own timer. `test/media-lifecycle.check.ts` 97 |
| **QC** | ✅ | Duration against the request, coverage from the chain, silence, black frames, loudness and true peak — one ffmpeg pass. Findings with a time range and a measurement, not verdicts. Checks that could not run are recorded, because "we did not look" and "we looked and it was fine" are different answers |
| **Review** | ✅ | Decisions kept as events with a name and a note. The publish gate is SOFT by default: publishing over a QC warning is allowed and is written down. Two hard blocks per account, both off by default. `test/review-qc.check.ts` 51 |
| **Analytics retention** | ✅ | Reports outlive the events they were computed from. Recompute → seal → prune, with the seal check inside the DELETE's own predicate. Raw window per account, default 90 days. `test/analytics-retention.check.ts` 33 |
| **Publication provenance** | ✅ | A publication stamps the build time, review state, QC state and who published. A rebuild marks existing publications stale |
| Smart render (re-encode only the two partial end segments) | 📐 | `plan()` already reports the saving. Must be validated frame-for-frame against the full-render output before it replaces it |
| Segment duration per profile | 📐 | Frame accuracy made 4s a performance question rather than an accuracy one |

### Defects found and fixed while building this

Each of these was live, and each was found by a test written to prove something
else:

* **Negative segment durations.** `scanRun` read a duration from the tfdt delta
  between consecutive segments. An encoder rebasing its timeline inside a run
  makes that delta negative — one real run measured -1,264,001 ms — and start is
  mtime minus duration, so the segment was placed twenty-one minutes into the
  future.
* **Durations measured across a hole.** With a segment missing, the delta spans
  the gap: the segment before a five-segment hole took a 24s duration and was
  placed 20s early, on top of its own predecessors. Media that looks present, in
  the wrong place, with no gap reported.
* **A run treated as one contiguous span.** Segments either side of a hole were
  byte-joined, so a clip came out shorter than asked for, with the missing
  minutes silently absent and every trim point after the hole wrong, at 100%
  reported coverage.
* **The waveform plotted loudness, not amplitude.** Real programme audio came
  out pinned near full scale with no shape to trim against.
* **Recordings filed only at boot.** A recording opened at 09:00 stayed unfiled
  until the next restart, invisible to every question asked by broadcast day.
* **Media objects duplicating.** Two identity bugs, both producing the same
  media described twice with two histories and two separate retention futures.
* **Unique views were not unique.** The rollup added one to total_views and one
  to unique_views per session, so the columns were always equal.
* **The account-scope rollup had no uniqueness at all.** scope_id is NULL for
  that scope and Postgres treats NULLs as distinct in a unique constraint, so
  `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` never fired and every write inserted instead of
  accumulating.
* **Every audit trail wrote the actor as "unknown."** The code read `req.user`;
  the access claim is `req.auth`.

## 2026-09-05 update - reporting foundations, session length, and three "correct about the wrong question" bugs

Five days of work whose through-line is worth naming, because the same shape
turned up four times: **things individually well built, failing in the wiring
between them, and several numbers that were correct about the wrong definition.**

**Built and covered**

* **The air ledger** (migration 0023 + `services/airSessions.ts`). "Hours on air"
  now has a source that survives a channel deletion, a rename, and an operator
  clearing the event log. One function owns the boolean and the history together;
  a check suite reads the source and fails if a fifth call site writes `on_air`.
* **Sessions that survive a shift.** The refresh token, its hashed store and
  `/auth/refresh` all existed and were never called, so every session ended after
  15 minutes of wall clock. Silent single-flight refresh in the console, plus two
  server holes closed: a disabled user could refresh for 14 days (while the Users
  page claimed otherwise), and naive rotation would have turned the absolute cap
  into a window that never closes.
* **Now-playing resolution** for HLS and other sources that carry no track
  metadata, replacing a permanent "Untitled break" that read as a broken pipeline.
* **A listener count that means the whole audience**, not the MP3 clients.
* **Auto connect armed by a healthy run** (migration 0024), ending the five-relaunch
  crash loop that buried the one error message an operator needed.
* **Operator control over the give-up notice and the event log**, with the
  stand-down that stops dismissing from re-arming the loop.

**Measured, not assumed**

* **MP3 delivery economics.** A CDN cannot cache an endless stream, so every edge
  holds an origin connection per listener. Self-served MP3 is ~6× cheaper per
  listener-hour than CDN-delivered HLS; AWS egress is the expensive combination.
  `docs/DECISION_mp3_delivery.md`
* **Graphics overlay cost.** Template DOM update 0.9 ms/frame (45× realtime); the
  bottleneck is the capture pipeline, not the browser. Intro/outro concat with
  matched parameters: 0.109 s, no re-encode — and a parameter mismatch joins
  anyway, **exits 0**, and yields a corrupt file, so the publish step must verify
  packet count.
* **API navigability.** The Hydra/JSON-LD layer is content-negotiated and only
  activates on `Accept: application/ld+json`; a blind walk with the right header
  reached 39 URLs three levels deep. `docs/API_NAVIGABILITY_REPORT.md`

**Known open, carried deliberately**

* `/radio/:id/stream.mp3` still 302s to a dead relay instead of returning the
  degraded reason.
* `resolveOutputs` defaults `mp3:false` while `outputUrls`/`publicUrls` default
  `mp3:true` — three defaults, two answers.
* `ownedRecording()` still infers kind from `radio_config IS NOT NULL` rather than
  reading the column that already knows.
* `stream_events` has no retention sweep.
* `max_listeners` on relays is stored and read by nothing; there is no relay UI.
* Events are not wired to the air ledger — no `on_air` column and no air endpoint,
  pending a decision on whether an event goes on air by explicit action or by its
  scheduled window.

Console signs via `thumbUrl()`. `docs/IMAGES.md`. |
| Frame-accurate media access | 🧪 | 🟡 | Frame-exact *cutting* is in daily use (`-ss` after `-i`, decode-and-discard seek). **2 Nov (build queue 3.2, v0.27.0):** a frame index per asset (`services/frames/`, `asset_frame_indexes`) and exact frames on request for assets and recordings through the signed image service (`GET /api/vod/{id}/frame`, `GET /api/recordings/{id}/frame`). **3 Nov (3.2-2, v0.29.0):** smart render on by default, every picture of six test clips checked against the source and the full render (`test/smart-render-frames.check.ts`). **3 Nov (3.2-3, v0.30.0):** frame stepping, timecode and exact In and Out frames in the Media Library preview and the recordings workbench. **3 Nov (3.2b, v0.31.0):** optional I-frame trick-play for on-demand HLS (switch per output profile, off by default). Still open: DASH, live and FAST trick-play and the iOS SDK (3.2b-2). |
| Admin prototype (single-file HTML) | 🧪 | ✅ | The file is gone from the tree. Superseded by `volt-web` (25 pages). |

| Test stream + playback + subtitles | 🧪 | ✅ | Still wired and surfaced. A shipped operator feature, not a mock — though the local launcher running the *real* encoder has largely taken over its job. |
| `BroadcastEvent` / `SportsEvent` + SportsML-G2 | 🧪 | 🧪 | **Stays, and for a worse reason than the row gave.** `eventPromo.ts` has **zero callers** outside its own test. The 17/17 suite proves the functions; nothing emits them. |

### The lesson worth keeping

Two of these had drifted in *opposite* directions, and both drifts came from the
same habit of writing the status when the work was done and never re-reading it:

* **The image service and the timeline were undersold.** They had been built into
  the app and the row still said prototype, so real capability was invisible on
  the status page and in the app-surfacing note, which still listed the image
  service as "not in app yet" while the console had been serving artwork through
  it for weeks.
* **`eventPromo` was oversold.** A green test suite reads as evidence of a working
  feature, and here it is evidence of a working *library*. A passing suite says
  the code does what its test asks. It says nothing about whether anything calls
  it. Worth checking callers, not just coverage, before a row claims output is
  being produced.

### Follow-ups this sweep created

1. ~~**Sign the image URLs** before the service faces the internet.~~ **Done 15 Oct 2026.**
   Every render URL is signed with a per-account key (no unsigned mode), and the
   service is production-ready: `src/modules/images.ts`, S3 + CloudFront cache,
   guards, SDK. See `docs/IMAGES.md`.
2. **Give `eventPromo` a caller**, or move it out of the build status until it
   has one. One route plus the player-session payload.
3. **Extend `metadata_schemas.target_kind`** past things we STREAM to things we
   DESCRIBE, so a `SportsTeam` can be typed and SportsML-G2 ingest stops
   discarding the identifiers it was fetched for. This one has a migration in it.

  and validate the join frame-for-frame against a full render of the same range.

## 2026-09-05 - media scope, and the smart render that is built but not on

### Media scope is now a stated property, not an assumption

Video and audio workflows had started to be described as one thing, and the
requirements were drifting into each other. Three that were actually being
confused:

* **The waveform, level metering and the signal verdict are AUDIO.** They read a
  station's broadcast output, or the audio track of a video asset. They say
  nothing about picture, and no video capability should be specified in terms of
  them.
* **Timed in-stream metadata is VIDEO first.** `EXT-X-DATERANGE`, SCTE-35 and
  `emsg` carry programme boundaries and ad markers in the DELIVERY stream. A
  radio station's now-playing is a different thing on a different side of the
  pipeline: ICY on INGEST, already handled. The one real overlap is ID3-in-`emsg`
  on an HLS audio rendition, and that is its own row when it is built, so the
  video work is never judged by an audio acceptance test.
* **Graphics overlays are video only**, by decision, and radio is explicitly out
  of their scope — which the design doc says and the tracker did not.

The legend now carries the convention. A capability with no scope note applies to
both; anything built for one and written up as if it served both is a
requirements defect rather than a wording one.

### Smart render: built, measured, and deliberately off

`services/clips/extract.ts` gained the whole path — an eligibility gate, an
Annex-B TS intermediate so each part carries its own parameter sets through the
join, a single audio pass to remove AAC priming seams, a runtime verify and a
fallback to the full render. Measured **1,059ms against 2,077ms** on the test
fixture, with the saving growing as the clip lengthens because the middle is
never decoded.

It is **opt-in and off by default**, and the reason is a measurement:

> A 15,082ms render against a 15,000ms request passed the runtime duration check
> **and was six frames short.** A duration check cannot see a seam.

The middle currently starts ~74ms late, because the concat demuxer offsets the
next part by the previous file's container duration (1.541s) while its picture
ends at 1.520s. `test/clip-smart-render.check.ts` is the acceptance test — 20
passing, 4 failing, and the four failing ones are the frame-for-frame
comparisons that define done. It is deliberately **not** wired into
`npm run check` yet: it currently fails on purpose, and smart render stays off
until it does not.

Four real defects were found and fixed on the way there, each by the frame
comparison rather than by reasoning: the TS 1.4s start offset accumulating
across parts; AAC priming making the muxer shift the picture a frame late;
`-shortest` truncating the picture because audio was the shorter stream; and a
hardcoded 80ms tolerance that rejected a correct 25fps render by 17ms.

### Live updates: the server half is done

`patchEntity()` now publishes a `record` frame to the account's SSE hub after a
committed change, and the console's shared `EventSource` dispatches it. It sits
in `patchEntity` rather than at each call site for the same reason the audit
write does: one function changes a record, so one place can be certain a change
happened and what changed. A caller that had to remember would forget, exactly
as four call sites forgot `on_air`.

**Only the SHAPE of the change travels** — which columns moved, the new version,
and who — never the values. The receiving page re-reads through its own
credential, so a frame cannot hand a viewer a field they are not allowed to see.

Remaining: the console-side affordance. A detail page holding an older `version`
than the frame reports knows its copy is stale, which is what turns a silent
overwrite into a warning.

## 2026-09-05 (overnight) - three off the queue

Worked in priority order while Christian slept. Everything committed; the tree is
working at every commit.

**Live updates — done, both halves.** Server publishes, console listens, the
channel settings drawer warns while the form is still open rather than after
Save is refused. 24/24.

**Timed in-stream metadata (video) — DATERANGE now emitted.** Annotated on the
way out of `/origin` rather than baked in by the encoder, because the schedule
keeps changing after the segment was written. 37/37.

**Watchfolder — complete for local folders.** Schema, ordered rules and the
quarantine model that was the actual requirement, then the scanner, the ingest
step, the API and the console page. 43 + 33 + 26 + 63 assertions, and the
migration was applied to a real Postgres on top of the existing ones rather than
eyeballed.

The ingest step is the one worth reading. The scanner writes a row saying what a
file is, and that row carries the checksum it had at that moment. Sixty seconds
later the ingest pass reads the bytes — and in that gap an operator can drop a
corrected version over the top under the same name. Trusting the row would put
content in the library that was never classified, so the file is re-hashed and a
file that has moved is refused. The half that is easy to miss is that refusing
is not enough: a row left at `seen` over a file that will never match is re-read
and re-hashed on every pass for the life of the folder, so it is CLOSED, and the
new content arrives as a new item because the unique index is keyed on content.

The same shape appears three more times in that file. Bytes go to the store
before the row, because an orphaned object costs disk while a row pointing at
nothing is a broken asset that looks authoritative. A library path collision is
suffixed rather than failed, because `media_files` is UNIQUE on
(account_id, path) and refusing the second is a file that can never ingest
however often it is retried. And a target the image library cannot hold, `vod`
or `loop`, is deferred to its own workflow rather than coerced into `other` —
coercion is exactly the unidentifiable-junk outcome the quarantine exists to
prevent, and marking it failed would hide real failures in the noise.

### Two things caught that would have cost the morning

* **A migration number collision.** The new migration was written as `0025_` and
  `0025_audio_export_keys.sql` already existed. The runner tracks by full
  filename so both would have applied, but it is wrong and fragile — renumbered
  to `0026_`, and the mis-numbered file moved to `migrations/_to_delete/`.
* **The smart-render verify was not sufficient**, found the same way: a render
  that passed a duration check was six frames short. Smart render is therefore
  built, measured (~2x faster) and **off**, with a failing acceptance test that
  defines when it may be turned on.

### Still queued

| Build | Scope | State |
|---|---|---|
| Watchfolder: S3 acquisition | Both | Done 27 Oct 2026 (build queue 2.6): S3 folders are taken in from the bucket's ObjectCreated notifications on an SQS queue, the walk is the hourly backstop; see the 27 Oct section |
| Smart render seam | Video | Concat demuxer offsets by container duration while the picture ends earlier. Byte-concat the TS parts and regenerate timestamps |
| SCTE-35 / `emsg` | Video | Done 22 Oct 2026 (build queue 1.6, v0.6.0) except in-band `emsg` in the DASH segments; see the 22 Oct section |
| ID3-in-`emsg` now-playing | **Audio** | Done 23 Oct 2026 (build queue 1.7, v0.7.0); see the 23 Oct section |
| Social publishing | Both | Blocked on developer app registrations, not on code |

## 2026-10-26 - On-demand ads: VMAP from the ad server (build queue 1.14k-4, not yet released)

What: SSAI.md section 5.1 (the second source of the schedule) and the row marked 1.14k-4 in section 9.

- **The rule** (`services/vodAds/plan.ts` `VodAdPolicy`): `schedule: rules | vmap` and `vmapUrl` in the rule's policy (no migration: the policy is JSONB). Empty `vmapUrl` means the ad server's own tag is the VMAP request. `PUT /api/vod/ads/rules/...` refuses VMAP without an ad server and a VMAP address that is not http(s) (`modules/vodAds.ts`).
- **VMAP** (new `services/vodAds/vmap.ts`): `parseVmap` (namespace prefixes ignored, linear breaks only; AdTagURI with CDATA, VASTAdData kept as the VAST document, custom ad data kept as a break with no source; breakStart, breakEnd and error trackers; non-linear breaks and unreadable offsets left out and listed in `problems`), `parseOffset` (`start`, `end`, `HH:MM:SS(.mmm)`, `n%`, `#n`), `offsetSec`, `fetchVmap` (one request inside the ad server's `timeout_ms`, never throws; 204 or an empty body is an empty schedule), `sourceFetcher` (an inline VAST answered in place so `fast/vast.ts` `resolve` handles both kinds of source with its wrappers, budget and error codes unchanged).
- **The plan** (`plan.ts` `planVmapBreaks`): each offset on the nearest segment start, as Volt's own mid-rolls; a moment on 0 is the pre-roll, on the end the post-roll; AdBreaks on the same moment become one break with their sources in order; the title's `adRule` still wins (`none` nothing, `no_mid` pre and post only); `source: 'vmap'`. A break's planned length is the rule's for its position, used only when the ad server returns nothing and house spots fill it.
- **Asking** (`index.ts` `vmapFor`, `breaksForRule`): per playback with the viewer's macros (the VAST ones plus `[CONTENT_DURATION]` and `[CUE_POINTS]`: the title's cue points and a catch-up title's replaced breaks); without a viewer for the shared guided playlist and the console plan, cached five minutes (a failure one minute). When the ad server is slow or fails, the rule's own breaks are planned and marked `vmapFailed`, filled with house spots without asking the server again (`vod_ad_breaks.fallback` `vmap-timeout` / `vmap-error`). An empty schedule: the gate gives no ads.
- **Deciding** (`decideVodBreak`): a VMAP break resolves its sources in parallel (each within the server's budget) and plays their ads in the listed order; the pod is as long as its ads. VMAP breakStart and breakEnd ride on the pod's first and last paid ad, sent by `eventsDueAt` with the first and last segment requests (only when the ad server gave some, so no empty event rows).
- **Guided**: the shared playlist from the title's schedule, its moments part of the guided version; each asset list opens the viewer's session (their own schedule) and fills the shared break by moment, empty when the viewer's schedule has no break there.
- **API**: the plan route and the session timeline carry `schedule` and `vmap` (`state`, `message`, `problems`, `elapsedMs`); plan breaks carry `vmapBreakIds`, `vmapOffsets` or `vmapFailed`.
- **Console** (`volt-web/src/pages/VodAds.tsx`): **Where the breaks go** (Volt places them, or the ad server places them (VMAP)), **VMAP address**, hints that say what the pre, mid and post settings mean under VMAP, the ad server choice worded for VMAP.
- **Docs**: `guides/on-demand-ads.md` (a VMAP section, the plan under VMAP, two troubleshooting rows, a curl example), `api/play-gate.md` (the timeline's `schedule` and `vmap`), the On-demand ads screenshot retaken with the screenshot script, SSAI.md sections 7 and 9.

## 2026-10-26 - On-demand ads: catch-up dynamic ad replacement (build queue 1.14k-3, not yet released)

What: SSAI.md section 5.2 and the row marked 1.14k-3 in section 9.

- **Where the broadcast breaks were** (`services/vodAds/index.ts` `recordedStartMs`, `broadcastBreaks`, `catchupCuts`): a recording-derived asset of a channel or event, a full replay or a clip of one range (an auto-VOD programme), is dated on the wall clock from its playlist's first PROGRAM-DATE-TIME, else from its recording's start (a full replay; `deriveEventVod` stores 0 in the asset) or its clip range. Its stream's `stream_breaks` that are not cancelled and overlap the title (operator breaks and SCTE-35 ones alike) are the cuts. Uploads, watch-folder files, exports and highlights joined from several ranges get none.
- **The cut** (`plan.ts` `catchupWindows`, `replaceBreaks`, `recordedToCatchup`; `hls.ts` `cutMedia`): each break moved to the nearest segment starts (a cut can be off by half a segment at most), clipped to the recording, overlapping ones merged, one shorter than half a segment or one that would leave nothing dropped. Every rendition leaves out the segments whose middle falls in a cut (audio off the video's boundaries follows the picture, captions too); the first segment after a cut is marked and written after a DISCONTINUITY even when no ad fills the gap. Nothing is re-encoded. `parseMedia` now reads the playlist's first PROGRAM-DATE-TIME.
- **The plan** (`plan.ts` `planVodBreaks` `replace`): the cuts are the title's mid-rolls (`source: replace`), each as long as the broadcast break it replaces, at its place in catch-up time; no interval or cue mid-rolls are added and the opening, end-guard and gap rules do not apply (the broadcaster already chose the places); a cut at the very start is covered by the pre-roll. Content time of a catch-up title is the programme without the broadcast's ads. Cuts are made only when the rule has mid-rolls and the title allows them: with mid-rolls off, `no_mid`, `none`, or an ad-free viewer, the title plays as recorded.
- **Delivery** (`index.ts`): a stitched session keeps its cuts in its plan, so every rendition and every playlist request cut the same; guided playlists are cut the same way, with PROGRAM-DATE-TIME restated after each cut so the interstitials sit where the plan puts them, and the guided version includes the cuts (a changed break means a new CDN address). A session with cuts offers no DASH (`delivery.dash` null, its MPD and caption routes 404): the DASH cut does not skip stretches yet.
- **API**: `GET /api/vod/ads/assets/:id/plan` and the session timeline gain `replaced` (`contentSec`, `recordedFromSec`, `recordedToSec`).
- **Docs**: `guides/on-demand-ads.md` (what it is for, a Catch-up section, a troubleshooting row, the plan's `replaced`), `api/play-gate.md` (the timeline's `replaced`), SSAI.md section 9. No console change, so no screenshot to retake.

Checked: new `volt-app/test/vod-ads-catchup.check.ts` **47/47** on Postgres 16 with a local VAST server and HLS on disk. Pure: cuts on segment starts exactly and moved to the nearest (600.9 s for 61.3 s becomes 600 to 664 s), a break begun before the recording and one after it, one running past the end, overlapping breaks merged, a tiny cut gone, a cut of everything dropped; catch-up positions (1200 s and 2280 s) and recording time to catch-up time; PROGRAM-DATE-TIME dating; the cut playlist (segments left out, audio by its middle, a DISCONTINUITY with the init again when nothing fills the cut, the pod in the cut when filled); guided with the clock restated; the plan (replaced breaks as mid-rolls, kept in the opening minutes and close together, no second break at 0, `no_mid`). Real API: a recorded hour (19:00 to 20:00, dated playlists, captions) with an operator break at 20:00 for 120 s and a SCTE-35 break at 40:00 for 90 s, plus a cancelled break, another channel's break and one after the hour: the plan has a pre-roll and the two replaced breaks only, `replaced` lists exactly those two; the gate opens a stitched session with no DASH; the 720p playlist holds 848 of the 900 content segments in order, the pod between segment 300 and 331 and between 600 and 623, audio cut the same, captions cut with a blank WebVTT per pod; timeline content 3392 s with breaks at 0, 1200 and 2280 (stitched 0, 1215, 2320) and `replaced`; decisions recorded as 120000 and 88000 ms breaks filled by the VAST ad and a house spot; the MPD 404. Guided: the same 848 segments, interstitials at 20:00 for 120 s and 38:00 for 88 s, a DISCONTINUITY and the clock restated after each cut; cancelling the SCTE-35 break changes the guided version and leaves one cut. An event replay as `deriveEventVod` makes it (0-based, undated playlist) dated from its recording; a clip of the second half dated from its range (the 40:00 break at 10:00 in it); highlights from two ranges and an upload keep ordinary mid-rolls; mid-rolls off, `no_mid` (3600 s, pre-roll only) and a paying viewer play as recorded. Would fail without the change: with this change's service files put back to HEAD (the new pure functions kept so the suite loads), 8 failures before it stops at the timeline's `replaced`. ## 2026-10-26 - On-demand ads, second slice: guided in the SDK's engine, stitched DASH, keys and captions (build queue 1.14k-2, not yet released)

What: SSAI.md section 9, the rows marked 1.14k-2.

- **Guided in the SDK's own engine** (`volt-player/src/sdk/engine.ts` `guided`, `startInterstitials`, `guidedCapable`; `player.ts`; `ads.ts` `guidedAd`, `guidedRef`): the player now says `hls-interstitials` wherever Shaka can play them (MSE and Shaka 4.16's interstitial manager), as well as natively on Apple. Shaka plays the ads in the same video element (`supportsMultipleMediaElements: false`: TVs have one decoder, the skin's controls stay on what plays) and resumes the content where it stopped. Shaka does not send `_HLS_primary_id`, so the engine adds it to each asset list request from the play attempt's sid (one Volt session per playback). The engine reports the ad on screen (place, count, time left, its address) twice a second; `VodAds.guidedAd` shows it in the same overlay, reads the session's timeline once for Learn more, and sends pause and mute to the session named in the ad's address.
- **Interstitial attributes** (`services/vodAds/hls.ts` `guidedMedia`): `CUE="PRE"` and `CUE="POST"` (the playlist attribute Shaka and hls.js read; it was `X-CUE`), and `CUE="ONCE"` on mid-rolls: a watched break is not replayed on a seek back, and Shaka resuming the content at the break's moment no longer starts the same ad again (it looped without it).
- **Stitched DASH** (`services/vodAds/dash.ts`, new): `parseMpd` takes the asset's static single-Period MPD on SegmentTemplate (Representation or AdaptationSet level, SegmentTimeline with `r`, or `@duration`) and refuses SegmentBase, live and multi-Period MPDs; `stitchMpd` cuts it into content Periods at each break (startNumber and presentationTimeOffset moved, a segment goes to the Period its middle falls in so audio frames off the video's boundaries are neither lost nor doubled) with a Period per ad between them: the ad's fMP4 HLS renditions as a SegmentList (one S per segment: dash.js 4 counts a repeated S in a SegmentList as one segment), init straight from storage, segments through the session's segment route so impressions and quartiles are sent as in HLS. AdaptationSet ids, Roles and Labels are kept across Periods; protection stays on content Periods and ads are clear; each text AdaptationSet has a blank WebVTT twin in ad Periods; a sidecar WebVTT is cut per content Period onto that Period's clock (`cutVtt`, served at `/vod-ads/s/<session>/text/<k>/<rep>.vtt`).
- **Service, API, gate** (`services/vodAds/index.ts` `assetMpd`, `sessionMpd`, `sessionText`; `modules/vodAds.ts`; `modules/apps.ts`): the MPD is the one beside `master.m3u8` when the asset's formats include DASH, read once and kept ten minutes; `GET /vod-ads/s/:sid/manifest.mpd` (404 for a guided session, or when an ad decided for the viewer is TS only: the viewer plays HLS); the play gate's `delivery.dash` is the session's MPD for a stitched viewer of a DASH-encoded title, still `null` for guided and for titles without DASH. `masterEntries` now also reads codecs (split video and audio), bandwidth, width and language.
- **Keys across a pod** (`hls.ts` `writeSeg`, `stitchMedia`): a stitched HLS playlist closes the content's EXT-X-KEY lines (METHOD=NONE) before a clear pod and states them again where the content resumes, before the init segment; an ad with its own key keeps it. Before this an EXT-X-KEY stated once at the top applied to every ad after it.
- **Docs**: `guides/on-demand-ads.md` (how ads reach the player, captions and DRM across breaks, a troubleshooting row for the DASH 404), `api/play-gate.md` (section 6: `delivery.dash` with ads, `_HLS_primary_id`, captions and DRM), `guides/player-sdk.md`, SSAI.md section 9; overlay entries for the two new routes, API reference regenerated.

## 2026-10-26 - X off by default, on per account (build queue 1.14h, not yet released)

What: X bills its API per post and per read (pay-per-use since 6 Feb 2026), so X is no longer offered to every account. Two ways in: the platform switch, off by default, set by a system administrator (X then runs on the deployment's X app), or the account's own X developer app (X bills the customer, no switch needed). With neither, X is hidden and refused; linked X accounts are kept and listed as off. The X adapter itself (`services/social/platforms.ts`) is unchanged apart from taking the account's app.

- **Data** (`volt-app/migrations/0085_x_access.sql`): `accounts.x_enabled` (default false), `x_client_id`, `x_client_secret_enc` (sealed with the social token key, AES-GCM), `x_app_updated_at`.
- **Service** (`services/social/index.ts`): `xAccess`, `assertPlatformAllowed`, `appFor` (the account's own app first, else the deployment's, else refused), `setOwnXApp`. Sign-in (`startConnect`, `finishConnect`) and token renewal (`validToken`) use `appFor`, so an own app signs in and renews with its own client id and secret (`platforms.ts`: `exchange` and `refresh` take the app). Refused while X is off: sandbox X, the publish job (a post queued before X went off fails at once), take-downs; `syncViews` reads nothing from X for such an account. `platformStatus` leaves X out for an account X is off for and marks it ready when the account has its own app.
- **API** (`modules/social.ts`): `GET /api/social/x` (on, how, `canSwitch`, the callback URL, the client id but never the secret), `PATCH /api/social/x` (the switch: system administrators, or the owner on a single-client Volt, as for the plan; audited), `PUT` and `DELETE /api/social/x/app` (settings manage; audited by client id). Connect, sandbox and retry for X answer 403 while off; publishing to an X account answers 422 with the reason; `GET /api/social/accounts` adds `off`. `POST /api/publish-targets` refuses an X target (`modules/publishing.ts`). Publishing rules (`services/publishingRules.ts`): an X account is listed with `off: true` and `enabled: false`, and a rule naming one cannot be saved while X is off; `services/availability.ts` skips an X target while off, so an old rule publishes everywhere else. System Admin API (`modules/platform.ts`): `PATCH /api/platform/accounts/:id` takes `xEnabled`, the account list shows `x_enabled` and `x_own_app`.
- **Console** (`volt-web/src/pages/SocialAccounts.tsx`): no X card while off; an X panel at the bottom says Off, On for this account, or On with your own X app, with Use your own X app (callback URL, permissions, client id and secret), Change X app and Remove X app, and the switch for whoever may set it; X accounts kept while off are listed as Off. Someone who can neither switch X on nor add an app sees no X panel either. `SocialPublish.tsx` does not offer an off X account; `RuleEditor.tsx` marks one as off. Also fixed "Publishes to a account" on the X and TikTok cards.
- **Docs**: `docs-site/docs/guides/social-accounts.md` (X off by default, the two ways in, step by step for your own app, when X is switched off, access, troubleshooting, the four routes), `guides/publishing-rules.md` (an X account marked off), screenshot retaken with `scripts/screenshots.mjs` in the customer view (saas mode), overlay entries for the four routes and the API reference regenerated.

Checked: new `volt-app/test/social-x.check.ts` **46/46** through the HTTP API and the local X stand-in: no X card and every X path refused for an account with neither (connect 403, sandbox 403, a hand-made target 403, publish 422, a rule refused, an old rule posting only to YouTube, a queued post failed with nothing sent to X, retry 403, no view reads, take-down refused), the old X account kept and listed as off; the owner of a customer account cannot set the switch and a system administrator can (audited); with it on X shows, links on the deployment's app (Basic auth checked on the wire) and sandbox X publishes; System Admin's account API needs a platform session and switches it off again; with its own app X shows without the switch, the authorize URL carries the account's client id, the token exchange and the token renewal authenticate as the account's app, the secret is in no answer, stored sealed, and not in the audit log; removing it turns X off and keeps the accounts; a viewer cannot set the app. `social.check.ts` 53/53 and `social-api.check.sh` 20/20 (both now switch X on for their account, as X is off by default), `publishing-rules` 28/28, `platform-groups` 11/11, `tenancy` 8/8, `access-control` 17/17, `access-rules` 28/28, `fast-access` 29/29, `geo-access` 15/15, `docs-coverage` 279/279, `docs-samples` 116/116, `docs-seed-fast` 20/20, `docs.check.sh` ok once the API reference is regenerated, `vod-library-pure` 155/155. `reframe` and one `vod-library` assertion fail on the copy only because it has no `volt-encoder` folder (the same on HEAD). ## 2026-10-26 - Journey to play (build queue 1.14g, not yet released)

What: QOE_REPORTING.md section 13. How a viewer got to the play button, joined to what happened after it: a fixed set of about ten app events in the web SDK, the journey carried into the play attempt, reports 28 (journey to play) and 29 (discovery attribution), and entry point and session depth as filters on reports 1 to 10. Not a general analytics SDK.

- **SDK** (`volt-player/src/sdk/journey.ts`, exported in `sdk/index.ts` and on `window.VoltPlayer`): `VoltJourney` with `app_open` (launch source from `utm_medium=push` or an outside referrer, or the app's `launch`), `screen` (from pushState, replaceState, popstate and hashchange, named by a route map with `:id`, or the path), `content_select` (`select(contentId, { entry: 'row:Trending', position })`), `search` (results count; query only as SHA-256 with `searchQuery: 'hash'`), `paywall_show`, `purchase`, `play_intent`, `background`/`foreground` (a new app session after 30 min away), `app_close`, `track` (20 names a session, ten props). Consent: `consent: false` sends, keeps and forwards nothing until `setConsent(true)`. Transport is CmcdSender (batches, back-off, 410 stops, keepalive at pagehide) with a `contentType` option (`cmcd.ts`). `ga4Forward()` maps the events to gtag (`screen_view`, `select_content`, `search`, `volt_play`, ...).
- **Into the play** (`cmcd.ts` `onPlay`, `player.ts` openAttempt, `types.ts` `journey` option): the attempt's first CMCD report carries `com.volt-jid`, `-asid`, `-entry`, `-depth`; `play_intent` with the attempt's sid is sent when play is pressed (or autoplay begins).
- **Collector** (`volt-app/src/modules/journey.ts`, `POST /api/journey` and `/api/public/journey`, publishable key, NDJSON or a JSON array): each event checked against the set and stripped to its allowed fields (dropped fields counted in `x-journey-dropped-fields`, never stored), kept raw first in the raw store's `journey/` stream (`rawStore.ts` `stream` argument, same local and S3 layout under `journey/`), per app session rate limit, the edge's country, never the IP. Public prefix and ALB rule (`scale/config.ts`, `deploy/terraform/scaling_alb.tf`).
- **Derived** (`services/qoe/journeys.ts`): one builder for the live path (10 s flush, upsert by id) and the replay (`replay.ts` rebuilds a day's journeys from their raw stream too), `qoe_app_sessions` and `qoe_journeys` in the analytics database (`analyticsDb.ts`, schema 3). Definitions version 3 (`views.ts`, `store.ts`): `journey_id`, `app_session_id`, `entry_point`, `session_depth` on each view; `reports.ts` takes `entry_point` and `session_depth` (`2`, `3-5`, `6+`) as filters.
- **Reports**: 28 `GET /api/analytics/qoe/journeys` (funnel open, browse, detail, play, entitled, paid, first frame over app sessions; a session counts at a step when it got there or further; drop-off, share of the step before, ms after open p50 and p95; app open to first frame; paywall shown, bought, abandoned; refused by the gate), 29 `GET /api/analytics/qoe/discovery` (`by=entry` or `by=detail`: journeys, plays, started, watch time, completion, startup failure, startup p50, rebuffer ratio, depth and position; viewed, never played). Both take the app's own dimensions and `entry_point`.
- **Console** (`volt-web/src/pages/PlaybackReports.tsx`): Journey to play (stats, step bars, step table) and Where plays come from (by entry point or by row or campaign, viewed never played), filters Came from and Screens before play.
- **Docs**: `docs-site/docs/guides/analytics.md`, `guides/player-sdk.md` (Journey to play, sample `samples/player/journey.html`), `api/playback-reporting.md` (step 5, samples `send-journey.sh` and `journeys.sh`), overlay entries and a Journey tag, API reference regenerated.

Checked: new `volt-app/test/journey.check.ts` **68/68** (stops at its first SDK check on the code before): consent off sends and forwards nothing; the attempt keys and the first CMCD report carry the journey; play_intent once per attempt with the sid; GA4 gets every event; eleven planted app sessions through the real collector and their plays through `POST /api/cmcd`: funnel 11, 11, 10, 9, 9, 6, 6 with the planted paywall drop-off (3 of 9 lost at paid), app open to first frame p50 7000 ms and p95 9500 ms, step times; rows 8 journeys, 7 played, 4 started, completion 0.5, watch time 240 s, deep link 30 s, search 20 s; by row Trending 5 and Premium 3; film-x viewed and never played; view rows with journey id, app session, entry point and depth 2 (definitions 3); reports 1 to 10 filtered by entry point and depth; an e-mail field and a plain search query dropped and absent from the raw files; 415, 410, 400; another account sees nothing; replay rebuilds 11 sessions and 10 journeys with identical reports. `docs-samples` **116/116** (107 before; the two shell samples and `player/journey.html` in Chromium). Neighbours: qoe-reports 71/71, qoe-reports-more 70/70, cmcd 73/73, player-sdk 47/47, docs ok, docs-coverage 279/279, hypermedia 57/57, auth-boundary 23/23. ## 2026-10-26 - Playback reports 5, 7, 8, 9 and 11 (build queue 1.14f, not yet released)

What: the five QoE reports 1.14e left (QOE_REPORTING.md section 5): rendering and device health, DRM and access, live-stream health, advertising, and one viewer's session timeline from the raw files, in the API and in the console.

- **Definitions version 2** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/views.ts`, new columns in `analyticsDb.ts`, `store.ts`): ad is its own state (abs to abe: playing, buffering and stalls inside a break are the ad's, not the programme's; ad startup delay, return to content, left during an ad); dropped frames (largest `dfa`), decoder errors (errors at the decode step), hardware decoding and DRM system from the SDK's identity keys; licence requests, failures and time from `rr` reports marked `com.volt-req=licence`; token errors (401 or 403 on a manifest or segment after the first frame, `com.volt-http`); latency first, last, lowest and highest per view (drift = last minus first), catch-up and slow-down time from `pr`. Existing rows keep their defaults until their day is replayed.
- **Play gate log** (`services/qoe/gateLog.ts`, `modules/apps.ts` `timedDecide`): every decision the API makes is timed and classified (allowed, replacement, geo, rights, payment, sign in, group, rules, availability, app restriction, unknown asset, error) with its sid, buffered in memory (50 000 at most) and written to `qoe_gate` in the analytics database with the collector's 10 s flush. Never on the gate's path; held through an analytics outage.
- **Reports** (`services/qoe/reports.ts`): `rendering`, `access` (failed views joined to the gate's reason by sid), `liveHealth` (per device), `advertising` (with the stitcher's `ad_beacons` summary, read from the control plane by `modules/qoe.ts`), `listViews`; all in `GET /api/analytics/qoe/overview`.
- **Report 11** (`services/qoe/timeline.ts`, `GET /api/analytics/qoe/sessions/:sid`, list at `GET /api/analytics/qoe/sessions?problem=`): every raw report of one sid in the player's order and in plain words, the states as spans, missing sequence numbers, duplicates counted once, the view rows, the gate's decisions and the beacons for that viewer. Reads only the days the sid's views cover.
- **Ad beacons join** (`services/fast/delivery.ts`, `modules/publicLive.ts`): a stitched FAST session asked for with a sid is named `<sid>_<random>`, so `ad_beacons.session_id` joins the player's reports. Without a sid it stays random.
- **SDK** (`volt-player/src/sdk/cmcd.ts` `adBreak()`, `engine.ts` `httpStatus()`, one line in `player.ts`): `abs` and `abe` with `com.volt-brk` when the stream's break at the playhead changes; `com.volt-http` on errors from a refused request.
- **Console** (`volt-web/src/pages/PlaybackReports.tsx`): Rendering and devices, Access and DRM, Live streams (when live views are in the period), Advertising (when there are breaks), and Sessions with a Timeline per view (state strip, every report, gate answers, beacons).

## 2026-10-25 - Docs seed: a FAST channel that can air, and the week it shows (build queue 1.14-G5c, not yet released)

- **Docs seed** (`volt-app/scripts/seed-docs.ts` `seedFast`): four short titles generated with ffmpeg (a test pattern and a tone, 48 to 64 s, 2 to 3 MB each, made only when missing) and laid out as a video upload leaves them: the file under `clips/vod-src/<id>.mp4`, `source_key` pointing at it, nothing packaged. - **Fix found doing it** (`volt-app/src/services/fast/lineup.ts` `nominalLength`): on a run order with **Round programmes up to** set, What this run order does to a week counted only the content, so this run order read "one pass 3m 34s, 2826 passes this week" while its Week showed two-hour passes. - **FAST** (`modules/fast.ts`, `volt-web/src/pages/Fast.tsx`): choosing Stitched with no ad server holds the choice, opens the ad server form (or the Ask list when the account already has servers) and saves stitched with the server on Add and use; Cancel drops the held choice. ## 2026-10-25 - FAST access follows the TV channels levels (build queue 1.14-G1b, not yet released)

What: `/api/fast` was outside the access catalogue, so any signed-in user could read FAST channels and any operator-tier role could create, change, activate and put them on air whatever their TV channels level, while the console showed FAST to TV channels: View.

- **Gate** (`volt-app/src/auth/roles.ts`): `/api/fast` is a TV channels prefix. View: every read, and `POST /lineups/:lid/preview` (it plans a window and writes nothing; new `readWrites` list on a feature). Operate: `POST /channels/:id/on-air`, `POST /lineups/:lid/activate`, `POST /channels/:id/breaks/:bid/decide`. Manage: everything else (create, lineups, ads, ad servers and their test, prepare, apply). `/api/fast-playout` (ingest token) is not caught by the prefix.
- **Breaks on any stream** (`/api/fast/streams/<kind>/<id>/breaks`): follow the stream, so an event is Events, a station is Radio stations, a TV or FAST channel is TV channels; View to list, Operate to add (Go to break) or cancel.
- **Docs**: FAST guide and the FAST-from-the-library workflow guide access tables, `volt-app/docs/PERMISSIONS_MAP.md`, regenerated `API_ROUTES.md`, `api-routes.json`, `openapi.json` (only the FAST rows change).

Inline paths are from the repository root; bash blocks start at the root and follow their own `cd`; it checks scripts run with node, tsx, bash, sh or python3, `npm run` scripts against the folder's `package.json`, `check.js` filters against `volt-app/test`, relative links, the index, secrets (AWS keys, private keys, live keys, JWTs, the local development secrets and demo password, credentials in URLs other than the local throwaway database) and em dashes. Folders Volt makes at run time are accepted by name (`volt-app/origin`, `volt-encoder/_local_out`; the suite holds that `volt-clean.sh` names them).

## 2026-10-24 - QoE collector and the first playback reports (build queue 1.14e, not yet released)

What: QOE_REPORTING.md sections 3 to 9a. The CMCD reports the Volt player sends (1.14d) become one row per view and minute aggregates in a separate analytics database, and six of the eleven reports are in the console.

- **Live path** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/collector.ts`): `modules/cmcd.ts` writes each accepted batch raw, then calls `ingestReports`; session state in memory, closed views (quit, fatal, 60 s silence; resumed within 60 minutes keeps its id), minute aggregates and the collector's accepted and refused counts written in one batch each every 10 s. Writes that fail are held (200 000 views at most, then dropped and counted for a replay) and retried; the player never sees it. `QOE_DERIVE=off` turns it off.
- **Definitions** (`services/qoe/views.ts`, version 1, stamped on every row): view opened at `s`, preload-only attempts not counted; states playing, rebuffering, seeking, paused, backgrounded; outcome started, failed or exited; stalls after the first frame; time-weighted bitrate; switches and oscillations (reverse within 10 s); exit after stall or error (30 s); telemetry: sn gaps, no sn, late (60 s), clock skew, unknown keys; duplicate sn ignored.
- **Analytics database** (`services/qoe/analyticsDb.ts`, `store.ts`): `ANALYTICS_DATABASE_URL` or `<DATABASE_URL db>_qoe`, created on first use; pool `ANALYTICS_POOL_MAX` (4), `ANALYTICS_QUERY_MS` (10 000). Tables `qoe_views`, `qoe_minutes` (DDSketch bins in JSONB, merged with `qoe_jsonb_add`), `qoe_collector_minutes`. Batched with `jsonb_populate_recordset`.
- **Sketch** (`services/qoe/sketch.ts`): DDSketch, 1 % relative error, nearest-rank quantiles.
- **Raw store** (`services/qoe/rawStore.ts`): `ctx` on each session's first row per POST (user agent, `sec-ch-ua-platform` and version, country, region, ASN from `cloudfront-viewer-*` when `TRUST_EDGE_GEO`); `readDay`, `accountsOnDay`; S3 path with `QOE_RAW_BUCKET`, `QOE_RAW_PREFIX`, `QOE_RAW_REGION` (gzipped NDJSON, 10 s or 64 MB).
- **Replay** (`services/qoe/replay.ts`, `src/tools/qoe-replay.ts`, `POST /api/analytics/qoe/replay`): day plus neighbours read, day replaced in one transaction.
- **Reports** (`services/qoe/reports.ts`, `modules/qoe.ts`, mounted in `modules/analytics.ts` at `/api/analytics/qoe`): overview, series, breakdown, dimensions, definitions, collector, replay; 18 filters; experience counted by end, engagement by start.
- **Console** (`volt-web/src/pages/PlaybackReports.tsx`, a Playback tab beside Audience and Operating): filter bar, trend, the six reports, worst segments, definitions.
- **Terraform** (`deploy/terraform/qoe_raw.tf`, `variables.tf` `qoe_raw_retention_days` 760 and `qoe_raw_lock_days` 90, `local.qoe_env` in `ecs_services.tf` and `scaling_services.tf`).

Browser check in Chromium on the real API and Vite with 160 seeded viewers: all panels, filter and breakdown follow, no sideways scroll at 1440 and 390 px, no console errors. `terraform validate` ok (1.9.8), `terraform fmt -check` ok on the changed files. ## 2026-10-24 - CMCD v2 in the Volt SDK (build queue 1.14d, not yet released)

What: QOE_REPORTING.md sections 2 to 4 and 10. Every Volt player reports what its viewer saw in the CMCD v2 standard, from the moment play is pressed, and the CDN can be joined to it.

- **Play attempt** (`volt-player/src/sdk/cmcd.ts` `PlayAttempt`): opened in `player.ts` `load()` before `resolveAsset()`; `sid` a UUID (`player.sessionId`), sent to the gate (`apps.ts` `availSchema.sid`, a `sid` claim in `mintPlaybackToken`) and used by `Beacons` as its session. States s, d, p, k, r, a, e, f, q; first frame from `requestVideoFrameCallback`; startup spans entitlement, manifest, licence, first segment, decode and other, adding up to `msd`.
- **Request mode** (`engine.ts` request filter): `CMCD=` on playlists, init and media segments, built with `@svta/cml-cmcd` 2.8.0 (`appendCmcdQuery`, version 2, request mode); none on licence and key requests. Native HLS and progressive: the first URL only.
- **Event mode** (`CmcdSender`): `application/cmcd` lines to `POST /api/cmcd?appKey=`; ps, e (with `ec` and `com.volt-step`), bc, t (30 s), b, pr, m, um, rr (startup, 1 in 50, failed, slow), each with v, ts, e, sid, sn; 429, 410, 5xx and offline handled as the spec says.
- **Collector** (`volt-app/src/modules/cmcd.ts`, `services/qoe/cmcdParse.ts`, `services/qoe/rawStore.ts`): publishable key, raw first (`QOE_RAW_DIR`, default `volt-app/qoe-raw`, ignored by git), 204/400/410/413/415/429. Public tier prefix `/api/cmcd`.
- **CDN** (`deploy/terraform/s3_cloudfront.tf`, `scaling_alb.tf`): delivery response headers policy exposing `x-amz-cf-id` and the CMSD headers, Timing-Allow-Origin; cache policies left ignoring query strings; `/api/cmcd` to the public tier.
- **Player options** (`types.ts`): `cmcd` (off, `app`, `appVersion`, `deviceModel`, `requestMode`, `eventMode`), `drm` (`servers`, `headers`).

Checked: `volt-app/test/cmcd.check.ts` **73/73** and `volt-app/test/cmcd-browser.check.ts` **34/34** (Chromium, real gate, collector and Postgres, a stand-in CDN on its own origin; 15/34 on the SDK before this change). Every request and report validated with the library's CMCD v2 validator; a refused entitlement (gate-403, entitlement step) and a failed licence (shaka-6007, DRM step, encrypted DASH, licence server 500) both reported as views; two viewers with different CMCD share one cached segment; the startup `rr` reports carry the CDN request id. ## 2026-10-24 - Rights preview on the admin side (build queue 1.14c, not yet released)

What: EDGE_RIGHTS_VARIANTS.md, "Testing it from the admin side". An operator picks a country, a rights class and a time and is told what the CDN edge gives that viewer, why, and can watch exactly that version, before it airs.

- **Routes** (`modules/channels.ts`, `modules/events.ts`): `GET /api/channels/:id/rights-preview` and `GET /api/events/:id/rights-preview`, account-scoped through `groups.account_id`, `?country=` (two letters, `XX` unknown), `&rightsClass=`, `&at=` (ISO, epoch seconds or now; within 31 days). 400 on nonsense, never a silent default.
- **Answer** (`services/rightsPreview.ts`): `decision`, `edge` (the edge's own string), `variant`, `rule` (layer and reason in words; for a window the programme, its times, countries, exempt classes and replacement), `until`, `loopReady`, `variantService`, the guide `windows`, and `strip`: the next 24 hours per audience (every country the rules name and anywhere else, each with every exempt class, at most 40). Each strip piece is decided on the rules as the sync publishes them at that piece's start, because a variant key is a hash of the audience's whole timeline and changes once an earlier window ends.
- **Same code as the edge** (`services/edgeRights.ts`): `explainDecision()` walks the steps of `decideFor()` and returns the layer; `loadEdgeDetail()` gives the published windows with the programme each came from (now also selecting `id, title`).
- **Play path** (`modules/publicLive.ts`): `GET /origin/:id/v/:key/:file?pt=<token>` runs the variant manifest service as the token's viewer (country, class and time fixed by the token, the stand-ins ignored), with no on-air wait and `no-store`. The token is HMAC signed, for one account, stream, audience and time, two hours. 401 without a valid one, 404 when the stream has moved account. OpenAPI: hidden `Origin` tag; the two routes are in the Admin API reference.
- **Console** (`volt-web/src/RightsPreview.tsx`, `index.css` `.rp-*`): a Rights preview panel on channel and event pages: country, class and time, quick chips for the countries with rules and the guide times, the answer with its rule, Play as this viewer (hls.js), and the strip; a strip piece asks for that audience at that time.

Checked: `volt-app/test/rights-preview.check.ts` **50/50** (query and token, 195 300 randomised cases where `explainDecision()` and the CloudFront Function agree, channel and event routes over real HTTP on Postgres, tenancy 404 and 401, replace, allow, the channel's block, a blackout, an exempt class, a guide time, the strip agreeing with the edge function on all 20 pieces, the play path off air, byte for byte the AU viewer's playlist once on air, refusals of a missing, re-aimed or forged token, origin files byte-identical). `volt-web/test/rights-preview.browser.mjs` **13/13** in Chromium on a database seeded by `rights-preview.seed.ts` with VP9 test media (blue programme, red loop): AU now plays red through `/origin/<id>/v/<key>/`, MY plays blue, FJ blocked with no play button, the Late AU guide time normal for premium and replaced without it, a click on NZ's strip piece, the event page at 390 px with no sideways scroll; screenshots looked at. Full run 191/197 suites, 6136 assertions: agent, images, live-degraded, xmltv-import fail the same on the tree before the change, fast is the known clock-dependent one, recordings passes alone (35/35).

Not yet: a programme naming its own replacement asset still plays the stream's loop (as 1.14b); Multiview view-as on the variants; nothing issues a rights class yet (needs input, see 1.14a). Not released: other sessions' uncommitted CHANGELOG and change notes are in the tree.

## 2026-10-23 - Edge rights variants: the manifest service (build queue 1.14b, not yet released)

What: EDGE_RIGHTS_VARIANTS.md. Until now a replace window was enforced as a block because nothing could make a variant; now the delivery side makes it from the playlist the encoder already wrote. Encoder, segments, the origin's own playlists and recordings untouched.

- **Service** (`volt-app/src/services/variantManifest.ts`): reads the delivered playlist (`master_cc`, the `_cc` media playlists, `manifest_cc.mpd`; disk on the Mac, S3 `<kind>/<id>/` in the cloud) through a 1 s cache shared by every variant (`VARIANT_SOURCE_CACHE_MS`), and the loop from `filler/filler.json` beside the output (else fillerPrep's `filler/prebuilt`).
- **HLS** (`renderVariantMedia`, `renderVariantMaster`): a segment whose start is inside a window becomes the matching rendition's loop segment, keeping the slot's EXTINF and PROGRAM-DATE-TIME. Loop index from the slot's own time (k = floor((t - from) / D + 0.5), pass k / N), so it is the same in every reload; discontinuity and EXT-X-MAP at the start, every wrap and the end; EXT-X-DISCONTINUITY-SEQUENCE = the source's plus the window points already passed. A pass that would begin less than a segment before the window end is not started (those seconds show live; a following window takes them). Subtitles: `empty.vtt`. Encrypted: `METHOD=NONE` over the loop, the key again after. Low-latency parts dropped, I-frame playlists dropped from the variant master, the tester's stand-ins passed on to each playlist.
- **DASH** (`renderVariantMpd`): live Periods cut at the window edges (`presentationTimeOffset` and `startNumber` moved), one Period per loop pass (`<key>-<ms>`, loop time 0, its own init and `$Number$` template), text tracks and event streams kept out of the loop, `Location` removed, only available segments listed, only the last Period open.
- **Routes** (`modules/publicLive.ts`): `GET /live/:id/v/:key/:file`, `GET /channel/:id/v/:key/:file`, `GET /event/:id/v/:key/:file`. Each viewer gets their own variant from the published audience map whatever the key says; 451 for the account or channel geo or a blackout; 404 off air (the same check as the public route); 503 `variant_unavailable` when the loop or a rendition is missing; `X-Volt-Variant`, `X-Volt-Rights`; shared for a segment (`s-maxage`) unless a tester's stand-ins make it one viewer's answer. The public route's redirect now carries `as`, `rc`, `at` and a query `vrc`.
- **Switch** (`services/edgeRights.ts`): `variantServiceOn()` is on outside production unless `EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE=0`; in production only with `EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE=1`. `loadEdgeState()` gives the rules and the variants from exactly the published windows.
- **Edge** (`deploy/edge/rights-viewer-request.js`, 6.9 KB): a rewrite to a variant calls `cf.selectRequestOriginById('variants')`.
- **Terraform**: `s3_cloudfront.tf` origin `variants` (the control plane's ALB, Origin Shield in `var.region`, the X-Volt-Edge header) and `/channel/*/v/*`, `/event/*/v/*` behaviours with the rights function; `edge_rights.tf` origin request policy `variants` (country header, `vrc` cookie and query); `ecs_services.tf` `EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE`; `scaling_alb.tf` the two paths to api-public (also in `PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES`). `terraform validate` passes; `fmt -check` flags only `ecs_cluster.tf`, which was already so (item 2.1).

Checked: new `volt-app/test/edge-variant-manifests.check.ts` **62/62** (the four loop passes, PDT continuity, stable discontinuity numbers over 61 slides, two variants at once over real HTTP on Postgres, blocks, refusals, DASH Periods, the edge's origin pick, a load run: 20 viewers 12 service requests and 4 origin reads in 6 s, 1000 viewers 12 and 8, origin files unchanged). Also rendered against the real delivered files of the channel running on the Mac: valid HLS and DASH. Full run 197/201 suites, 6133 assertions; `fast`, `live-degraded` and `live-record` fail the same on the tree before this change, `recordings` passes alone (35/35).

Not yet: a programme naming its own replacement asset still gets the stream's loop (needs a package per asset); confirm `cf.selectRequestOriginById` at the first apply; the two-tab player check on the Mac; Multiview view-as on the variants (1.14c). Not released: another session's uncommitted CHANGELOG and change notes are in the tree.

## 2026-10-23 - Edge rights variants: the reducer and the edge decision (build queue 1.14a, not yet released)

What: EDGE_RIGHTS_VARIANTS.md (Christian, 22 Oct). One `_rp` playlist could not serve two audiences replaced at different times (AU 20:00 to 21:00, NZ 21:00 to 22:00). Delivery side only: encoder, segments and recordings untouched.

- **Reducer** (`volt-app/src/services/edgeRights.ts`, `reduceVariants`, `streamVariants`): per audience (each country the windows name, `*` for any other or unknown country, each also with every rights class a window exempts) the replace timeline, worked out interval by interval the same way the edge decides (first window that applies wins). Audiences with the same timeline share one variant, keyed `v` + 8 hex of the timeline's hash, so the key is the same on every edge. Work grows with distinct outcomes, not viewers.
- **Store value**: `vm` (audience to key, only where the more general entry would give a different key), an optional 6th window element listing exempt rights classes (from `programmes.metadata.rights_exempt`, no migration), `vs` (variant service running; `EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE=1`, off until 1.14b). The map is made from exactly the windows that fit the 1 KB value. `rp` is gone.
- **Decision**: `decideFor`, `variantFor`, `edgeDecision` (allow, block, replace:<key>; a replace with no variant or no variant service is a block, never the content).
- **Rights-class claim**: `signRightsClaim` / `verifyRightsClaim`, `<class>.<exp>.<sig>` (first 32 hex of HMAC-SHA256 over stream id, class and expiry), key `EDGE_RIGHTS_CLAIM_KEY` or derived from the JWT secret; the orchestrator's KeyValueStore sync publishes it at `k:rc` (`syncClaimKey`).
- **CloudFront Function** (`deploy/edge/rights-viewer-request.js`, 6.4 KB of the 10 KB): verifies `vrc` from the query or a cookie, rewrites to `/<kind>/<id>/v/<key>/<file>`; a request that names a variant itself is served only to the audience the function would send there, so a variant (which carries the main stream outside its windows) is no way round a block. Fail-open on a missing key, unreadable store or broken value, as before.
- **On the Mac** (`volt-app/src/modules/publicLive.ts`, `publicRightsDecision`): the public `/live/<id>/master.m3u8|manifest.mpd` route makes the same decision after the account and channel geo gate. Outside production `?as=AU`, `&rc=premium`, `&at=<ISO>` and the `X-Volt-Test-Country` / `X-Volt-Test-Rights` headers; production ignores them and uses the CDN country header and the signed claim. Answers carry `X-Volt-Rights`, `Vary: CloudFront-Viewer-Country` when the stream has rules, and are not shared when per viewer. A replace answers 451 naming the variant until the variant service exists, then 302 to `/live/<id>/v/<key>/<file>`.

Checked: new `volt-app/test/edge-variants.check.ts` **62/62**: the reducer (AU then NZ gives two variants and at 21:30 AU normal, NZ the loop; the same window from two programmes or one shares one key; overlapping AU and NZ give two with the first rule winning; allow lists map every other country to `*`; an exempt class watches through, a class with its own timeline gets its own entry), 408 replace decisions each landing on a variant whose timeline covers that moment with the right replacement, 60 windows cut to 1 KB with the map from the published windows only; the real function file run beside `edgeRights.ts` over **8840** fixture cases (countries, classes, times, windows), all equal; claims signed by the app verified by the function, and forged, other class, expired, other stream and other key all rejected; the handler's rewrites, cookie claims, no claim key, direct variant requests and fail-open; and on Postgres over HTTP the Mac route (dev `?as=AU` 451 with the AU variant, MY through to the on-air check, `&at=` 90 minutes on replaces NZ, `&rc=premium` exempt, 302 with the variant service on; production ignores `as`, `rc` and the test header, honours a signed query or cookie claim, refuses a forged one). `edge-rights` 38/38 (the `_rp` checks now check the variant rewrite), `edge-viewas` 5/5. Full `node scripts/check.js` (cloud container, Postgres 16): 176/199 in the first pass with only volt-app in the copy; with volt-web, volt-player, volt-encoder, volt-agent and packages added, all 23 pass alone (fast 45/45, radio-id3 36/36, recordings 35/35, docs ok, the player suites) except `live-degraded` 12/17, which fails identically with this item's four files put back to HEAD. volt-app typecheck clean on the device.

Not yet: nothing issues a rights class (needs input in BUILD_QUEUE.md: from a group, an entitlement product or the app, and the class names); no guide field for exempt classes; the Multiview view-as path in `server.ts` still looks for `_rp` files (1.14c moves it to the variants); variant manifests are 1.14b (built 23 Oct, section above). Not released: another session's uncommitted CHANGELOG and change notes are in the tree.

## 2026-10-23 - Watch folder video drops land where they belong (build queue 1.13, not yet released)

What: the `vod` and `loop` targets were wired on 6 Sep and unit tested with an in-memory store. Run end to end against Postgres and the real API, a drop showed three faults.

- **Loops** (`volt-app/src/services/watchfolder/videoIngest.ts`): the filler row was written with `source_key` '' and no filename or type. Loops & Fillers listed it, but the orchestrator hands the encoder the source key as the loop's address, so no channel could fall back to it. It is now written as an upload writes one: file at `fillerPath()`, `source_key` `filler/<account>/<id><ext>`, filename, content type from the extension (`videoTypeOf()`), `uploaded_at`. The id is picked before the copy so the file and the key agree; a failed row removes the copy.
- **No second asset** (`pgVideoIngestStore.markLanded`): the item was left at `seen` once the asset existed, and the pending query takes `seen` items whose claim is over ten minutes old, so each drop became a new asset and a new encode every ten minutes. Items now close as `ingested` with the note "in the Media Library as ..., queued for encoding" or "in Loops & Fillers as ...". The Media Library row shows the encode's own state.
- **Ingest rules for VOD drops** (`runIngest`): uploads run `ingestLanded()` (naming, real type, duplicate check, metadata, sidecar, webhooks) before the encode; drops did not. They now do, with the folder id as the source (so a source-rung ingest rule for a folder applies), the drop's checksum and its path in the folder. Only an `ingested` outcome is encoded, from the path the rules leave it at; duplicate, rejected or awaiting metadata are a new `held` outcome. If the rules cannot run, the file still encodes, as for an upload.
- **Counts** (`store.ts`, `ingest.ts`, `volt-web/src/pages/Watchfolders.tsx`, `api.ts`): the scan summary had one `queued` count for encodes and loops; it now says "N queued for encoding, N added to Loops & Fillers, N held by the ingest rules". The In library status help names the three libraries.

## 2026-10-23 - One list over every stream (build queue 1.12, v0.12.0)
