## 2026-10-23 - Server-side ad beacons for stitched breaks (build queue 1.11, v0.11.0)

What: in a stitched FAST break the player never sees the VAST, so the ad server was never told an ad played. The stitcher now sends each paid ad's impression and tracking URLs itself, as each viewer's player reaches them, and records what it sent per break.

- **When** (`volt-app/src/services/fast/beacons.ts`, pure): `adSchedule()` lays a decided pod's paid ads on the break clock (house spots and slate counted for position, never beaconed; an ad cut by the break end completes at the end) with impression (inline and every wrapper), creativeView, start, firstQuartile, midpoint, thirdQuartile, complete and VAST 4 `progress@` offsets (time or percent); player-only events (pause, mute, skip, click) are left to players. `playheadOf()` places the viewer: segments are shared CDN files, so the stitcher sees only the session's playlist refreshes, and a live player sits the hold back behind the newest segment (`HOLD-BACK`, else 3 target durations). `dueBeacons()` returns what the playhead passed since the last refresh, in order (time, then VAST order, then pod position) and skips what fell due during a gap of more than 30 s or before the session was first seen; the playhead never moves back.
- **Sending** (`beaconSender.ts`): each URL is claimed in `ad_beacons` (migration 0078, unique per session, break, pod position, event and URL index) before the request, so refreshes of a session's video and audio playlists, or two API tasks, never send one twice; one promise chain per session keeps the order, an event's URLs go together; GET with `User-Agent: Volt-SSAI/1.0`, `X-Device-IP`, `X-Device-User-Agent` (VAST 4.1 server-side reporting), macros `[TIMESTAMP]`, `[CACHEBUSTING]`, `[ADPLAYHEAD]`, `[MEDIAPLAYHEAD]`, `[BREAKPOSITION]` in plain and `%5B..%5D` form; 5 s timeout; redirects followed by hand (3), every hop checked against the private ranges (loopback, RFC 1918, link-local and metadata, CGNAT, IPv6 local; `AD_BEACONS_ALLOW_PRIVATE=1` for a local stub only); status, error and time recorded; a failure is not retried.
- **Stitcher** (`modules/fast.ts`, the public session route): after the playlist is answered, for stitched channels, the breaks this session was actually given (eligible with a pod) are scheduled and the due beacons queued. Decision scope `channel` still beacons per viewer.
- **Report:** `GET /api/fast/channels/:id/ad-report` gains `beacons` (per event: ads that reached it, URLs sent, failed, pending; impressions, completes, completion rate, failed); new `GET /api/fast/channels/:id/breaks/:bid/beacons` (account-scoped; per ad in the pod and event: viewer sessions, sent, failed; the latest 100 beacons with tracker host, status and error, never the full URL). API reference overlay for both; `docs:routes` and `docs:openapi` regenerated. Console: the FAST channel's Ads tab, Last 7 days card, section Reported to the ad server (impressions, reached halfway, played to the end, completion, a notice for failed pixels, an empty state).

Checked: new `volt-app/test/fast-beacons.check.ts` **51/51**. Pure: schedule (order at one moment, quartile and progress times, wrapper URLs, a cut ad, house spot skipped), playhead (3 targets, HOLD-BACK, no PDT), due and skipped (first sight, repeat refresh, backwards playhead, a 31 s gap, forgetting old breaks, a session first seen mid-ad), macros, address guard. End to end through the real API and Postgres: a stand-in playout writing a live HLS window (2 s segments, a 16 s break), a VAST stub (a wrapper around ad A with its own impression and start, ad B inline, ad A's first quartile redirecting, ad B's complete answering 500), the channel set to stitched per viewer through the API, three viewers refreshing every second: S1 on video and audio sends all 16 beacons exactly once, in order, 6.7 to 6.8 s after each moment in the stream (one hold back), with the device headers and filled macros, and the redirect followed; S2 leaving at the first quartile sends impression, start and first quartile only; S3 joining mid-break gets the house fill and sends nothing; 22 rows, the 500 recorded as failed with its status, nothing half sent; ad report 3 impressions, 2 completes, 0.667, 1 failed; per break both ads (A: 2 viewers, 1 complete; B: complete failed); another account and an unknown break 404. Against the route before this change, with the new modules present so the suite imports: 32 passed, 4 failed, and the run stops. Neighbours: fast-ads 48/48, fast-lineup 56/56, fast-graphics 15/15, fast.check.sh 44/45 (clock dependent, fails the same before), docs ok, hypermedia 57/57, auth-boundary 23/23, custom-role 31/31, player-api 32/32, viewer-reporting 14/14. Full `node scripts/check.js`: **188/191 suites, 5889 assertions, 18 failed**: fast 1 (above), images 1 (the container copy had no `packages/`; with it 145/145), recordings 16 in the full run (35/35 alone, the known shared path). Typechecks clean on the device. Browser (real API, Postgres, Chromium): the Ads tab card with beacons and without; no console errors.

Not yet: VAST `<Error>` pixels for a creative we skip (not conditioned, too long) are not sent; a viewer paused or scrubbed back in the DVR window is not followed (only the player can report that); order is guaranteed per session within one API task, and across tasks each pixel is still sent once but two tasks may interleave; the stitcher is HLS only, so DASH sessions have no beacons until DASH stitching (3.1). On the Mac: `DATABASE_URL=... npx tsx test/fast-beacons.check.ts` (about 50 s).

## 2026-10-23 - Graphics burned into the encode (build queue 1.10, v0.10.0)

What: graphics assigned "Burned into the video" (targets `burn` or `both`) on a transcode channel or event are now drawn into the encoded picture, so every viewer, recording, clip and third-party player gets them. This is the production cut of the 20 Oct spike (`volt-app/docs/SPIKE_live_graphics_burnin.md`): render on change in Chromium, a box-sized PNG with alpha handed over by temp file and rename, composited once before the ladder splits.

- **Control plane** (`volt-app/src/services/graphics/burnFeed.ts`): `burnFeed()` behind `GET /api/streams/:id/burn-graphics?kind=` (ingest token, like timed-events, in `modules/status.ts`): the stream's burned graphics at published versions only, their package, placement and declared box, and the state now (the same values and on/off as live control and the player, through `liveFor`; off-air graphics carry no values; an ad break that hides graphics takes them off, as the player does). `placedBox()` is the player overlay's placement maths (anchor, offset, scale, template canvas fitted to 1920x1080) and `burnRegion()` the union of the placed boxes, padded 8 px, on even pixels. `burnPlan()` at start; the orchestrator puts it on the job context and `jobConfig.ts` writes `graphics_burn` (feed URL, token, API base, region, fps) for our own encoder on the transcode path only (never package-only, never a vendor engine). `streamLoad.ts` reserves the renderer (100 CPU units, 320 MiB, from the spike) and gives such streams their own cost signature (`|gfx` appended; every existing signature unchanged).
- **Renderer** (`volt-encoder/gfx/burn_renderer.js`, playwright-core 1.63.0 pinned in `gfx/package.json`): one headless Chromium page with each graphic in its own `sandbox=allow-scripts` iframe from `/gfx/<version>/`, laid out exactly as `volt-player/src/sdk/overlay.ts` lays it out, driven through the same bridge (update, play, stop). Polls the feed every 500 ms; on a change it captures the region frame by frame until the transition settles (3 identical frames), otherwise nothing; re-writes the current frame every 2 s as a heartbeat. A dead feed holds the last state.
- **Encoder** (`volt-encoder/bin/gfx_burn.py`, `encoder.py prepare_graphics_burn`, `ladder_args.video_rung_args(vmap=)`): when the job has `graphics_burn` and the host can run the renderer, the PNG is seeded (transparent, region-sized) before the transcoder starts, the renderer is started under the same supervisor as every other child, a dead-man clears the graphic if the file is not refreshed for 10 s, and `start_transcode` builds one `-filter_complex`: the picture scaled once to the top rung's height, the watermark and the graphic overlaid there, then `split` and a scale per rung, each rung mapping its label with the same encoder settings as before. If the host cannot run the renderer (no Node, Chromium or playwright-core), the log says so and the command is exactly the one without graphics. `Dockerfile` gains `--build-arg GRAPHICS_BURN=true` (Node 22, playwright-core, Playwright's Chromium, about 400 MB; off by default); `run-on-mac.sh` installs the renderer's one dependency and it drives the Mac's own Chrome.
- **Fault found and fixed while testing:** ffmpeg probes up to 5 s of every input before it starts, and on a looping image that is 125 frames of the PNG as it was at start, played first: every graphics change in the first 5 s after a transcoder start or respawn was never drawn (measured on ffmpeg 4.4 and 6.1). The graphic input is now not probed (`-analyzeduration 0 -probesize 4096`); a change 3 s after start shows within a second.

Checked: new `volt-app/test/graphics-burn.check.ts` **59/59**: placement maths and region; the feed through the real API and Postgres (401 without or with a wrong token, 404 for no such stream, only the burned graphic at a published version, not player-only, not a draft, no values while off, on with values once taken, off during a hiding ad break and back when it is cancelled, burn refused on a package-only channel); burnPlan, the job (with it the rest of the job byte-identical), never on package-only or a vendor engine, the load line and signature; then the real renderer in Chromium following the running API: transparent while off, heartbeat every 2 s, drawn 195 ms after the take-on call at exactly the player's position (bottom right, offset, scale 0.8: canvas 504,282 400x112), a value change redrawn in 189 ms, killed with SIGKILL the file stays a valid PNG, restarted it draws the current state again; then the encoder's own graph and rung settings (from `gfx_burn.py` and `ladder_args.py`) encoding a live 720p source to a 720p and 360p ladder while the operator API takes it on, changes it and takes it off: in the encoded frames 199 ms after take on, 200 ms after the change, 700 ms after take off (sampled every 200 ms), edges where the canvas box scales to at 720p, the 360p rung the same at its scale, nothing elsewhere, 350 of 350 frames, no renderer errors. New `volt-encoder/bin/test_gfx_burn.py` **54/54** (PNG, region, graph shape: no `-re`, no `format=auto`, not probed, composite once; rung arguments identical after the map; seed; dead-man clears once and recovers; real ffmpeg pixels on a live source with the file replaced 25 times a second, 225 of 225 frames, watermark still burned; `start_transcode` with the spawn caught: renderer and transcoder supervised, token not on the command line, and byte-identical to the old command when the host cannot render), on ffmpeg 4.4 (device) and 6.1 (container). Neither suite can import against the code before this change. Neighbours: `dev/test_ladder_args.py` 14/14, test_timed_events.py 65/65, test_id3_emsg.py 51/51, graphics 120/120, graphics-pure 152/152, graphics-live-push 32/32, graphic-triggers 47/47, fast-graphics 15/15, player-overlay 14/14, player-overlay-browser 31/31, stream-load 31/31, stream-model 25/25, encode-ladder 50/50, orchestrator ok, captions-toggle 6/6, auth-boundary 23/23, hypermedia 57/57. Full `node scripts/check.js`: **185/188 suites, 5743 assertions, 17 failed**: docs (the container copy had the route list from before `docs:routes` was rerun; with it, ok), recordings 19/35 in the full run and 35/35 alone (the known shared path), fast 44/45 (`the programme on air is left alone`, clock dependent, fails the same before this change). Typechecks clean on the device. `docs:routes` regenerated (the machine route; not in the public OpenAPI).

Not yet: worked out at start, so a graphic assigned to burn while the stream is running is drawn from its next start, and a running stream composites the region it started with (a new graphic outside it is cut at its edge until a restart); positions assume a 16:9 picture; one renderer per stream (the spike's shared pool per host is the next step for hosts with many such streams); tickers and running clocks are drawn by Chromium frame by frame while they move, not by native ffmpeg drawtext; the phase 3 hardening (a never-blocking shared-memory frame source) is not built; NVENC and a Docker build with `GRAPHICS_BURN=true` not run here (no GPU, no Docker). On the Mac: start a transcode channel with a graphic set to Burned into the video, take it on in live control and check the output in Safari, and `python3 volt-encoder/bin/test_gfx_burn.py`.

## 2026-10-23 - Graphics live updates over SSE (build queue 1.8, v0.8.0)

What: graphics live control, the running event rundown and a channel's graphics tab follow changes as they happen instead of asking every second.

- **The gap.** Live control read the whole graphics state of its stream every second (the rundown every 2 s, the channel tab every 5 s), whether anything had changed or not: one request a second per open panel, and still up to a second late.
- **Frames** (`volt-app/src/services/graphics/livePush.ts`). `publishGfx` sends `event: gfx` on the existing status hub with `{kind, id, reason, assignmentId?}`. No values, as with `record` frames: the stream reaches every admin of the account and pages re-read through their own credential. Through `publish()`, so several API processes fan out by Postgres NOTIFY like status frames.
- **Write paths.** `runtime.ts`: `setValues` and `setAir` (after the commit, only when something changed; the off timer goes through `setAir`), `takeItem`, `takeOffRundown`, `acceptPayload` (every stream with a field bound to the source, only when the payload changed). `modules/graphics.ts`: assignment create, edit, delete; setup edit (every stream using it); data source stale rule; every rundown edit (`rundownChanged`).
- **Time path.** `watchLive` runs in the leader's one-second graphics tick: resolves each live stream's graphics (`liveFor`) and announces the streams whose on/off, values, staleness or off time moved since the last tick. Catches a rule graphic at a programme boundary and a feed going stale, which have no write.
- **Who receives them.** `statusHub.subscribe` takes a per-viewer filter. `GET /api/status/stream` now also opens for a role with Graphics alone (only `gfx` frames, no status snapshots); a role without Graphics gets no `gfx` frames; neither is still 403.
- **Console.** `volt-web/src/liveStatus.ts` routes `gfx` frames to `gfx:channel:<id>`, `gfx:event:<id>` and `gfx:*` (not the plain stream keys, so pages that refetch a channel on any status news are not woken by a score change) and exposes `isLiveUp()`. `graphics/useGfxLive.ts`: re-read on a frame or reconnect, frames within 120 ms folded into one read, every 3 s only while the stream is down, every 30 s as a safety net. Used by `LiveControl.tsx`, `EventRundown.tsx` (run mode) and `ChannelGraphics.tsx`.

Checked: `volt-app/test/graphics-live-push.check.ts` 32/32 (new; real HTTP app, Postgres, an SSE client on the stream): frames 5 to 8 ms after the API call for values, take on and off, a feed push and take next (bar 1000 ms); the off timer, rundown add and take off, a setup edit on a channel and an event, a rule graphic going off when the next programme starts through the watch; nothing for an unchanged value or payload; no values in a frame; another account, a Live-only role and a Graphics-only role each get only what they should; the three consoles no longer poll. Against the code before: 13/32. Full `node scripts/check.js`: 185/186 suites, 5654 assertions, 16 failed, all recordings in the full run (35/35 alone). Typechecks clean on the device. Chromium against the real API: live control opens one status stream, makes 0 graphics requests in 6 s idle, shows a value set through the API in 181 ms, a take on in 249 ms, its own TAKE OFF in 154 ms; no console errors.

Not yet: the watch covers live streams only (a rule graphic on a channel that is not live shows at the 30 s safety read); it resolves each live stream every second beside the player snapshot pass, which could be merged if hundreds of streams carry graphics; not run on the Mac.

## 2026-10-23 - Now-playing as ID3 in the radio audio segments (build queue 1.7, v0.7.0)

What: a station's title changes travel inside its HLS and DASH audio as standard ID3 timed metadata, so any player shows the song and artist at the moment they are heard, without the Volt now-playing poll.

- **The gap.** Now-playing arrived as ICY on ingest and reached Icecast listeners as ICY again. HLS and DASH listeners got nothing in the stream: the delivered copies carried our DATERANGE and EventStream triggers, which no standard player shows as a title.
- **The list** (`volt-app/src/services/radio/nowPlayingTimeline.ts`). The timed-events poll (`GET /api/streams/:id/timed-events`) carries `now_playing` for radio stations: each change in the ICY content log (programmes rows, `metadata.source = 'icy'`), plus the one in force when the window opens; the schedule when a station sends no titles. TIT2 and TPE1 come from the ICY reader's class: a song is split "Artist - Title", anything else goes out whole, an empty title shows the station name.
- **The tags** (`volt-encoder/bin/id3_emsg.py`, from `radio.py` `refresh_delivered`). emsg version 1, scheme `https://aomedia.org/emsg/ID3`, presentation time on the track's own timeline (tfdt plus the offset from the segment's PROGRAM-DATE-TIME, or availabilityStartTime plus the segment time in DASH), ID3v2.4 TIT2 and TPE1 in UTF-8, a distinct id per tag. A segment gets a tag when a change falls in it, when the title in force differs from the last one sent, or every 30 s with the same title for listeners who join mid-song. The emsg goes just before the moof; ffmpeg's HLS segments have a sidx, whose first_offset grows by the same amount; sample offsets need nothing (default-base-is-moof).
- **Where.** Copies, never ffmpeg's files: `audio/id3/seg_N.m4s` only for tagged segments, with `index_cc.m3u8` pointing at them; `dash/id3/` for the whole rolling window (hard links where untagged) because the SegmentTemplate names segments by number, with `manifest_cc.mpd` naming `id3/` and declaring the InbandEventStream. The recording index skips both (no init.mp4). Anything unexpected fails open to ffmpeg's file. `Dockerfile.radio` copies `id3_emsg.py`.
- **Timing.** The change time is the content log's. A change learnt after its segment went out is carried at the start of the next segment: never early, at most a segment plus a poll late.

Checked: `volt-app/test/radio-id3.check.ts` 36/36 (new): synthetic ICY source, the real ICY reader, the endpoint, the real `radio.py` for 40 s; 7 tags in HLS and DASH, each a logged title, 0.3 to 1.1 s after its change, all 5 changes in the window carried, ffmpeg's files untouched, audio in the copies identical; 8 of 36 fail against the code before. `volt-encoder/bin/test_id3_emsg.py` 51/51 (new). Full `node scripts/check.js`: 184/185 suites, 5646 assertions passed, 16 failed, all recordings in the full run (35/35 alone). Typechecks clean on the device. Players in Chromium on the suite's own output: hls.js 1.6.17 and dash.js 4.7.4 read all 7 tags; Shaka 4.16 reads them on DASH, and on HLS with `mediaSource.dispatchAllEmsgBoxes` on; all play through without errors.

Not yet: the Volt player SDK still shows now-playing from its poll, not from the tags (Shaka would need `dispatchAllEmsgBoxes` for HLS); a run on the Mac with a real Icecast station and Safari.

## 2026-10-22 - SCTE-35 on live channels and events (build queue 1.6, v0.6.0)

What: every ad break on a live channel or event (Go to break, the API, a FAST break) now carries SCTE-35 in the delivered HLS and DASH, as FAST playout already did.

- **The gap.** FAST playout writes its own SCTE-35. Live streams' breaks reached the encoder through the timed-events feed only as our own ad trigger (`com.boomlabs.volt.ad`, `urn:com:boomlabs:volt:ad:2026`), which a downstream ad inserter or distributor does not read.
- **The cue** (`volt-app/src/services/graphicTriggers.ts`, `breakCue`). A splice_insert out with break duration and auto return, and the matching in, from the FAST builder (`services/fast/scte35.ts`). Splice immediate: a live stream's PTS comes from the source and the control plane never sees it, so the carrier says when (START-DATE, presentationTime, both on the stream's wall clock). Event id: the break's `scte_event_id`, else the hash FAST uses. The DATERANGE gains `PLANNED-DURATION` and `SCTE35-OUT`; the encoder poll carries the pair and the DASH event (`wireFormat`, `dashScte35`). The Mac's local `/origin` path gets the same DATERANGE.
- **The placement** (`volt-encoder/bin/timed_events.py`). `place_cues()`: `EXT-X-CUE-OUT:DURATION`, `CUE-OUT-CONT:ElapsedTime,Duration`, `CUE-IN` on the segments whose midpoint is in the break (a live source is not cut at the break, so the edge falls inside a segment); a window that opens mid-break starts with CUE-OUT-CONT. DASH: an EventStream with the scte35 scheme, one Event at the break start with the cue base64 in Signal/Binary, Event@id the splice_event_id; its `@value` marks it as ours so a rewrite never removes a SCTE-35 stream the packager passed through. Fails open as before.

Checked: `test/live-scte35.check.ts` 43/43 (new; stops at its first assertion on the code before), `test_timed_events.py` 65/65 (was 34; 8 fail on the old encoder code before the suite stops). Full `node scripts/check.js`: 181/184 suites, 5546 assertions passed, 19 failed in one run (live-record: container lacked volt-web node_modules, 24/24 with them; recordings 35/35 alone; fast 44/45, fails the same without this change). Typechecks clean on the device. Players in Chromium on the encoder's own output with VP9 test segments: hls.js 1.6.17, Shaka 4.16 (HLS and DASH) and dash.js 4.7.4 parse the cues, surface them at 20 to 50 s, and play across the break with no errors.

Not yet: in-band `emsg` SCTE-35 in the DASH segments (needs the packager); CUE lines on the local `/origin` path; reading SCTE-35 from a live source; a run with the real packager and encoder on the Mac.

## 2026-10-22 - Smart render seam fixed (build queue 1.5, v0.5.1)

What: the smart render join (re-encoded head, copied middle, re-encoded tail) is now seamless; it matches the full render frame for frame.

- **The cause.** The concat demuxer starts each part at the previous file's container duration, which runs to whichever stream ends last. The copied middle is 12.000 s of picture in a 12.022 s file (its AAC runs on), so the tail started 23 ms late: frame 338 of the fixture at 13,566 ms against the full render's 13,543 ms. At 50 fps with B-frames the gap was 30 ms and the clip came out one frame short.
- **The fix** (`volt-app/src/services/clips/extract.ts`). `concatListByPicture()` writes the join list with a `duration` per part, measured from the picture: where this part's last frame ends relative to its file start, less where the next part's first frame starts relative to its own. The picture length is cross-checked against frame count times frame time, and a part that does not add up throws, which sends the block to the full render. The demuxer's `duration` directive was chosen over a raw Annex-B byte concat with regenerated timestamps: the same continuity, without losing B-frame presentation order in a raw H.264 stream.
- **A seam check at runtime.** `seamFault()` reads the output's video packet timestamps (no decode), sorts them and requires every step to be one frame time (1 ms either way). It runs after the duration check in the smart render's verify, because a duration check cannot see a seam. Any fault falls back to the full render and the reason is on the result (`smartRenderSkipped`).

Checked: `test/clip-smart-render.check.ts` 35/35 (was 23/24): the original fixture now matches the full render on every presentation timestamp; new assertions for no gap or overlap in either render, one frame step across the head seam (frames 37 to 38) and tail seam (337 to 338), a planted 23 ms gap being caught, and a second source at 50 fps with B-frames (preset veryfast, 3 B-frames) cut 1.3 s to 14.7 s: 670 frames in both renders, every timestamp equal, no seam fault. Against the code before the fix the suite gives 29/35. Full `node scripts/check.js`: 183/183 suites, 5559 assertions, 0 failed (Postgres 16, own database per suite, cloud container). Typechecks clean on the device.

Not yet: smart render stays opt-in (`ExtractRequest.smartRender`); no caller sets it, so clips still take the full render. Turning it on for clip extraction is a decision for Christian. Not measured on real recordings with a mid-run encoder restart (a range spanning runs is joined per run, so each run's smart render is checked separately).

## 2026-10-22 - Encode tab facts read from the files (build queue 1.4, v0.5.0)

What: the Encode tab's five "not recorded" gaps (audio tracks and languages, video profile, caption tracks of file encodes, DRM on file encodes, a package-only source's codec and audio) are now stored facts.

- **Where it is kept.** `vod_assets.media_facts` (migration `0077_vod_media_facts.sql`), written by `services/media/mediaFacts.ts`: ffprobe of each delivered rendition's `init.mp4` joined (concat protocol) with its first `seg_N.m4s`, because Shaka writes the H.264 parameter sets in band and the init alone has no profile, level or frame rate. Per rendition codec, profile, level, size, frame rate; every audio track's codec, channels, sample rate, language (`und` is no language); subtitle tracks. NULL means not read yet.
- **File encodes** (`services/jobs/handlers/vodEncode.ts`): read before the S3 upload, written with the encode. `encode_result` also gains the source's full probe (`vod_encoder.py` now lists every audio and subtitle track with language, and the video profile; cover art is no longer taken for the picture), `audio` (track 0 to AAC 128 kb/s stereo 48 kHz, with the source track's language), `captions: []` and `drm: 'clear'`.
- **Recording-derived assets** (`modules/vod.ts`, `GET /api/vod/:id/encode`): read once, the first time the tab opens, from the first run overlapping the asset's window (`initFilesInRange`, `services/localRecordingIndex.ts`), then kept. Making a clip never waits on ffprobe and older assets get it too. For a package-only stream it is the only record of the contributor's codec and audio, and also gives the Source panel its picture size and frame rate.
- **Reading it** (`services/vodLibrary.ts` `encodeDetails`): profile and level per rendition over the settings, audio from the files (bitrate from the encode's own record, else measured), file captions and DRM stored, source codec and tracks, and `notes` for recorded facts that are not gaps (only the first of several audio tracks encoded, subtitle tracks not carried, in the clear). Console (`volt-web/src/pages/vod/AssetPanels.tsx`): Profile column shows level, Source panel lists video codec, audio and subtitle tracks, notes under the tables.

Checked: `test/vod-library.check.ts` 79/79 (19 new: a package-only recording made with ffmpeg, read once and kept, tenancy; `vod_encoder.py` probe of an MKV with eng and fra audio and eng SRT; a real file encode through the job handler and Shaka Packager, then the route; 16 of them fail on the code before this change and the suite stops), `test/vod-library-pure.check.ts` 155/155 (19 new, including `parseStreams` and the pre-1.4 gaps still stated). Full `node scripts/check.js` 180/183 suites, 5523 assertions, 20 failed: clip-smart-render 23/24 (item 1.5), fast 44/45 (`the programme on air is left alone`, fails the same on the code before this change, clock dependent), recordings 17/35 in the full run and 35/35 alone. Typechecks clean on the device. Browser (real API, Postgres, Chromium) on a package-only clip and an uploaded two-language MKV: values as above, no product console errors.

Not yet: assets encoded before this keep their gaps until re-encoded; recordings on the S3 origin are not read (needs an S3 reader of the init and first segment); package-only rendition bitrates are not in the files. On the Mac, set `PACKAGER_BIN` for the file-encode assertions in vod-library.check.ts, or they skip and say so.

## 2026-10-22 - check suite baseline (build queue 1.1)

`node scripts/check.js`, every suite on its own copy of the template database,
run in the cloud container on a copy of the whole repo (Postgres 16, ffmpeg,
icecast2; no GPU, no faster-whisper, no H.264 in its Chromium).

**Result: 178/179 suites pass, 5374 assertions, 1 failed.** The first run was
152/179 with 69 failed assertions. Of the 27 failing suites, 12 failed only
because the first copy held volt-app alone; the other 15 were real and are
fixed below, except the smart render seam, which is queue item 1.5.

Product faults found and fixed:

- **FAST on-air bypassed the air-session ledger.** `POST /api/fast/channels/:id/on-air`
  (`volt-app/src/modules/fast.ts`) did a bare `UPDATE channels SET on_air`, so a
  FAST channel's public hours never reached the on-air totals or the as-run
  report. Now `setOnAir('channel', ...)` like channels and stations. Caught by
  the structural check in `air-sessions.check.ts` (37/37), which was also
  narrowed to `channels.on_air` because `gfx_state.on_air` is a graphic, not a stream.
- **Radio list made its own now-playing decision.** `volt-web/src/pages/Radio.tsx`
  painted `r.nowPlaying` with its own Untitled break fallback; it now paints the
  server's `nowPlayingView` (dash for a source that cannot send titles, as the
  station page). `now-playing.check.ts` 32/32. Checked in a browser against the
  real API with the station list stubbed to four cases (ICY title, gap, HLS
  with no titles, schedule): each shows the expected line.

Suites brought up to date with the product (the product had moved, the suite had not):
admin (10 character passwords, roles model, platform session), platform-groups,
clip-extract, metadata-events, player-sdk-api, live-record. Fixture faults:
`scripts/seed-check.ts` now creates `admin@volt.test` (docs-session,
listStandard, vodOwnership), epg-repeat ids no longer collide with the seed,
workflows' user is active, and clip-smart-render and ingest-extract make their
own media. New helpers: `test/lib/platform-session.sh` (step-up with a real
TOTP code, for suites that call `/api/platform`) and `test/lib/react-global.ts`.

**Not yet / to run on the Mac:** clip-smart-render 23/24 (item 1.5).
captions-e2e runs with 0 assertions without faster-whisper (`pip install
faster-whisper`). player-sdk-api skips its bundle check until `cd volt-player &&
npm run build`. radio-container needs icecast2 (`brew install icecast`); here it
ran 23/23 once installed. Run `node scripts/check.js` on the Mac once to confirm
the same 178/179 there.

## 2026-10-27 - Mac end-to-end run sheet (build queue 1.15, not yet released)

What: one command that runs the whole product path on the Mac and says PASS, FAIL or SKIP per step.

- **Script** (`scripts/e2e-mac.sh`, `scripts/e2e-mac.mjs`, Node only, no packages): talks to the running Volt through its API (`VOLT_API`, the demo sign-in by default). Makes two synthetic live HLS sources with ffmpeg (colour RED primary, BLUE backup, spoken audio from `say` or espeak, else a tone) and serves them itself on port 8790; the radio source is `volt-app/test/icecast-sim.js` on the next port. Creates an output profile (x264 720p and 360p) and group of its own; when the plan has no room it uses an existing transcode group as it is and never edits its shared profile. Reads delivered output only: the newest frame of a playlist decoded to one pixel for the colour, `/filler/` segments in a rendition for the loop, the subtitle playlist and cue text, the recording's timeline and range playlist, the clip's own master with ffprobe, the FAST playlist's segments.
- **Steps**: preflight, setup, sources, channel, start, air, captions, rights, failover, stop, record, clip, vod, publish, radio, event, fast (table in `TESTING_WORKFLOWS.md` section 8). A step skips when a tool is missing (packager, faster-whisper; it says what to install) or a step it needs did not pass; skips never count as passes; the exit code is 1 on any FAIL. `--plan`, `--only`, `--cleanup` (removes only what the run made, the profile only after its group), `E2E_TIME_SCALE` for a slow machine, `E2E_ENCODER=docker` and `E2E_SOURCE_HOST` for the Docker encoder. Ctrl-C stops what it started.
- **Docs**: `TESTING_WORKFLOWS.md` section 8 rewritten as the run sheet; `docs/engineering/running-on-the-mac.md` gains a short section.

Checked (cloud container, Postgres 16, HEAD 2e8bde65 plus this change): new `volt-app/test/e2e-runsheet.check.ts` **14/14**: `--plan` and its order, a row per step in section 8, all 46 API calls and 6 cleanup routes found in `docs/api-routes.json` (a renamed route fails it), then a real API (local launcher) on a fresh account: setup with its own group and profile, sources, channel, radio end to end (ICY title in now playing, public address 200), the encoder steps skipped with the packager made unusable, `--cleanup` leaving no stream or group. ## 2026-10-26 - Threads publishing (build queue 1.14j, not yet released)

What: Threads profiles as publish targets, a clip as a video post and an Image Library picture as an image post, through the Threads container flow.

- **Data** (`volt-app/migrations/0097_threads.sql`): `threads` in the platform checks of `publications` and `publish_targets`. Everything else (the container id in `platform_state`, picture publications by `image_key`, likes and followers) came with Instagram in 0087.
- **Adapter** (`services/social/platforms.ts`): `threads` on graph.threads.net v1.0. Sign-in on threads.net with `threads_basic`, `threads_content_publish` and `threads_manage_insights` (the last is needed for views, likes and followers); code exchange, `th_exchange_token` for the 60 day token, renewed with `th_refresh_token` in its last week. Container flow: `POST /{user}/threads` (`media_type` VIDEO or IMAGE, `video_url` or `image_url`, `text`), status until FINISHED, `threads_publish`, permalink; the container kept so a retry carries on, an errored or expired container started again. Limits: 500 characters, one hashtag (the topic tag), 250 posts in 24 hours from `threads_publishing_limit`. `stats` reads views and likes in one insights call, `followers` from `threads_insights`. New `fetchesVideo` flag on a platform.
- **Media Threads fetches** (`services/social/index.ts`, `crypto.ts`, `modules/social.ts`): Threads takes no upload, so the job places the file (hard link, else copy) in `<clips>/.social-fetch/<publication>.mp4` and gives Threads `GET /api/social/media/<publication>.mp4?exp=&sig=`: public, HMAC signed with a key derived from the token key under its own label, six hours, range requests answered, `no-store`. Removed once published or failed for good; kept while a retry may still need it. A Volt with no public address fails the post at once, before Threads is asked. Pictures use the signed JPEG, as for Instagram.
- **Service**: the default post gives Threads the asset's first tag only; `fitPost` refuses a second hashtag in Threads' words; `limitReached` names the platform (Instagram's wording unchanged); sandbox Threads (`@...`, 300 followers, 250 a day, `SOCIAL_SANDBOX_TH_LIMIT` for tests).
- **Console**: Threads card on Social accounts (one hashtag, posts with their text, fetched from the public address), Threads in the composer (500 and 1 counters, first tag only, the 24 hour limit per profile), pictures offered to Threads too.
- **Docs**: `guides/social-accounts.md` (Threads, linking, pictures, five troubleshooting rows, API rows), `guides/publishing-rules.md`, `guides/images-and-thumbnails.md`, the Social accounts screenshot retaken, the overlay (new route, Threads in the Social and publish-image texts) and the API reference regenerated.

## 2026-10-27 - Watch folders: S3 pickup by event (build queue 2.6, not yet released)

What: an S3 watch folder is told about a drop by the bucket instead of listing its prefix every minute, and S3 folders now ingest at all.

- **Event path** (new `volt-app/src/services/watchfolder/s3events.ts`): reads S3's own ObjectCreated notification (URL-encoded keys, `+` for a space), the same inside an SNS envelope, and EventBridge's Object Created; removals and S3's test event are acknowledged and ignored. Each drop is matched to every enabled S3 folder whose bucket and prefix hold it (the review prefix is never a drop) and fetched alone (`mirrorS3Object` in `s3walk.ts`: head, the walk's junk and already-known checks, a `.part` file renamed into the staging mirror, its time set past the folder's quiet time because S3 only notifies a complete object). The touched folders are then scanned, their review decisions mirrored to the bucket and ingested at once (`takeInS3Folder` in `store.ts`). A message is deleted only when its objects are in the mirror (or there was nothing to fetch); a failed fetch leaves it on the queue for redelivery and, after five, the dead-letter queue.
- **Queue client** (new `volt-app/src/services/ingest/sqs.ts`): receive and delete over SQS's JSON protocol with a Signature Version 4 signer on `node:crypto` and the S3 client's own credentials (task role, environment, profile), so no new package in `package.json`; an in-memory queue with visibility for tests.
- **Scheduler** (`services/orchestrator/index.ts`): with `WATCH_S3_QUEUE_URL` set, the leader long-polls it (20 s, one batch at a time, a pause after an error and after an empty answer that came back at once). The minute pass walks each S3 folder at the first pass after a start and then every `WATCH_S3_BACKSTOP_MIN` (60) instead of every minute (`walkDue` in `store.ts`); Scan now still walks. Without the queue (the Mac) nothing changes.
- **Fixed** (found by the new suite): both ingest loaders in `store.ts` asked for kind `local` only, so files an S3 folder accepted were never ingested and stayed `seen`. They now use the staging mirror as the folder's path, and an S3 folder deletes the mirrored copy once the library has it (the bucket keeps the original; the walk and the queue both skip an ingested object).
- **Terraform** (new `deploy/terraform/watchfolders.tf`): `watch-drops` queue (SSE, 15 minute visibility, 4 days, 20 s long poll) with a dead-letter queue after five receives, a queue policy for S3 from the origin bucket (plus `watch_s3_extra_bucket_arns` for customer buckets), `aws_s3_bucket_notification` on the origin bucket for `s3:ObjectCreated:*` under `watch_s3_prefix` (default `watch/`, so live segment uploads never reach the queue), the control plane's receive and delete permission, outputs for both queue URLs; `WATCH_S3_QUEUE_URL` set in `app_settings.tf`; `WATCH_S3_BACKSTOP_MIN` (default) and `WATCH_S3_QUEUE_ENDPOINT` (local) in `env-coverage.json`; both variables in `terraform.tfvars.example`.
- **Docs**: the Watchfolders guide (bucket folders, the `watch/` prefix, the hourly look, the API sample), `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` smoke test step, `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` cost table (2.6 built).
- **Checked**: new `volt-app/test/watchfolder-s3-events.check.ts` **50/50** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container (message shapes, folder matching, the signer against AWS's get-vanilla vector and against `@smithy/signature-v4` on a signed POST, the client against a local stand-in, then real Postgres, the real HTTP app, store, scanner and ingest with an in-memory bucket and queue: a slate lands in the Image Library and an unmatched file in review in the bucket with the bucket never listed, a repeated notice makes nothing twice, a failed fetch keeps its message and lands on redelivery, the backstop walk timing, the loop). Before the ingest fix 7 of its assertions failed (files stayed `seen`). Neighbouring suites green: watchfolder-api 26/26, watchfolder-ingest 70/70, watchfolder-rules 43/43, watchfolder-scan 33/33, watchfolder-video 70/70, watchfolder-video-landing 45/45, ingest-s3 16/16, ingest-core 97/97 and the other ingest suites (14/14 suites, 500 assertions). `terraform-env.check.ts` 47/47 and `check-terraform-env.mjs` no problems (on the device); `terraform fmt -check`, `terraform init -backend=false` and `terraform validate` pass (Terraform 1.9.8 in the container); `tsc --noEmit` clean on the device; engineering-guides 35/35. docs-coverage 280/281: the one failure is three console sections (Encoding Queue, Genres, Single sign-on) from another session's uncommitted work, not this item.
- **Not done**: not run against real AWS (needs the account): the bucket notification's test message, a real drop. A folder in a customer's own bucket needs that bucket's notification set up on their side (to the `watch_drops_queue_url` output) and its ARN in `watch_s3_extra_bucket_arns`. S3 folders are created through the API only; the console's New watchfolder takes a path.

## 2026-10-27 - Projected load learns from what runs, and counts storage (build queue 2.4a, not yet released)

What: a stream's first estimate now starts from what its parts cost on every stream that ran, storage is projected before allocation, the projection error is reported per class, and placement can refuse a server whose task disk would run short.

- **Learning** (new `volt-app/src/services/fleet/loadFit.ts`, pure; `loadLearning.ts` reads the runs): every estimate line carries a component (`encode:x264`, `encode:nvenc`, `decode:h264`, `decode:hevc`, `decode:gpu`, `ingest`, `audio`, `package`, `drm`, `graphics`, captions); older stored lines are recognised from their wording. Runs of the last 30 days measured alone on their server (`tasks_on_host = 1`, as `calibrate()` already did) are set against their unfitted lines, and a multiplier per component is fitted by least squares pulled towards 1 with the weight of three runs, per instance family with five runs or more, all families together otherwise; network gets one multiplier. Guards: runs more than 4 MADs off on the log ratio set aside and the fit repeated until stable, multipliers clamped 0.5x to 2x, a run counts at most 24 hours. `assign.ts` applies the fit for the family placement prefers before the per-signature calibration (basis `fitted`), cached ten minutes, `FLEET_LEARN=false` turns it off. `recordAssignment` keeps the unfitted lines (`baseLines`) so learning never feeds on its own output.
- **Storage** (new `storageProjection.ts`): bytes per hour to the task disk and to S3 from the output bitrates (renditions, audio tracks, container overhead), and S3 held at steady state under the recording retention (null when kept until deleted). On every estimate, stored at launch in `placement_decision.storage`.
- **Task disk** (`capacity.ts`, `fleetModel.ts`, `placementPlanner.ts`, `snapshot.ts`): a server is refused when what its streams will have written by the end of the horizon (`FLEET_DISK_HORIZON_HOURS`, 24, or a running event's window) plus the new stream's share passes 85 % of `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB`; a stream bigger than an empty server's disk is unplaceable and says so. **Off until `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` is set.** Finding: on AWS the packager keeps the whole run on the task disk (`--preserved_segments_outside_live_window 0`) and the launch template sets no root volume, so the AMI's 30 GiB is shared by every encoder on a server and a 4-rung 1080p ladder (about 4.6 GiB an hour) fills it in about six hours wherever it runs. Turned on, the check would refuse every full HD ladder, so it waits on 2.4c (queued).
- **Report** (new `projectionReport.ts`): per class (transcode, package-only, radio, captions) the median actual/projected ratio, median absolute error and share under-projected by more than 10 %, for CPU, network and storage (bytes the host sent per hour; on an encoder host the uploader is the only sender). `GET /api/platform/fleet-plan` returns it as `projection`, with the fit as `loadModel` and the new settings under `policy`.
- **Settings**: `FLEET_LEARN`, `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB`, `FLEET_DISK_HORIZON_HOURS` in `PLACEMENT.md` and `deploy/terraform/env-coverage.json` (class default).
- **Checked**: new `volt-app/test/load-fit.check.ts` **47/47** (60 synthetic runs with known true costs: x264 within 3 %, decode within 5 %, network within 2 %, every run's total within 5 % (worst 1.0 %); two wild runs set aside and x264 moves under 1 %; clamp at 2x and 0.5x; five runs move part way; per-family fallback; a never-run ladder learned 7816 against a real 7858, constants 6106; storage 4.93 GB/h against 4.69 GB/h by the bitrates, within 10 %; the disk check in `fits` and `choosePlacement`; the report). New `volt-app/test/load-learning.check.ts` **22/22** on Postgres 16 (30 runs seeded in instances and host_samples read back, a run on a shared server ignored, the fit from the database, a never-run ladder through `assignStream` within 5 % of its real cost where the constants were over 20 % off, `FLEET_LEARN=false`, the stored `baseLines` and storage, the report per class, a server passed over because its disk would run short, a full HD ladder unplaceable for its disk, no disk set leaves placement as before). Neighbouring suites through `scripts/check.js`: stream-load 31/31, placement-planner 26/26, placement-e2e 21/21, consolidation-plan 28/28, server-status 10/10, segment-placement 17/17, relay-fleet 38/38, meter 80/80, fleet-api 23/23, orchestrator and captions-orchestration ok (13/13 suites, 320 assertions, plus fleet-api rerun after a stray server from an earlier suite held its port). `tsc --noEmit` clean on the device; `check-terraform-env.mjs` no problems; engineering-guides 35/35. volt-web `tsc -b` shows three errors, all in another session's uncommitted files (`ImagePicker.tsx`, `ChannelDetail.tsx`, `EncodeJobs.tsx`), none touched here.
- **Not done**: no real runs yet (the fit is tested on synthetic histories and starts on the Tier-2 cluster). A new event's window is not passed to the disk check (the orchestrator's start path is in another session's uncommitted `service.ts`), so new streams use the horizon. The platform route's new fields have no HTTP test (no suite signs in as a platform admin); the functions behind them are tested. No console view of the report.

## 2026-10-27 - CDN edge logs into reporting (build queue 2.9, not yet released)

What: the CDN's own record of every request reaches playback reporting. On AWS, CloudFront real-time logs of the delivery distribution go to a Kinesis stream; the scheduler's leader reads it into the analytics database. That gives report 6 (Delivery: is the CDN or the origin to blame?), report 12's reporting coverage, and a count of viewers whose player sends no reports at all, which nothing could count before.

- **Terraform** (`deploy/terraform/cdn_logs.tf`, on by default with `cdn_realtime_logs_enabled`): an on-demand Kinesis stream (24 hours, encrypted), the role CloudFront writes with, `aws_cloudfront_realtime_log_config` with 18 fields (timestamp, address, first byte, status, bytes, path, edge location, edge request id, time taken, protocol, user agent, the query with its CMCD, the cache result and its detail, country, origin first and last byte, ASN) and `realtime_log_config_arn` on all four behaviours of the delivery distribution (`s3_cloudfront.tf`). Terraform starts the rate at `cdn_log_sampling_rate` (100) and then leaves it alone (`ignore_changes`). The control-plane role may read the stream, update the log config and pass its role. Settings reach the control plane in `qoe_env`. Fixed cost about $29 a month (the stream hour); the per-line cost is capped by the sampling.
- **Reader** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/cdnStream.ts`): ListShards, GetShardIterator and GetRecords over Kinesis's JSON protocol, signed with the SigV4 signer from 2.6 (`ingest/sqs.ts`, `s3Credentials` now exported), no new SDK package. One GetRecords per shard per pass, passes at least 250 ms apart (inside Kinesis's 5 reads a second per shard), iterators kept between passes, shards listed once a minute (on-demand streams split and merge). The position per shard is in `qoe_cdn_cursor` and moves only after the batch is kept raw: at least once, never skipped. Started by the scheduler with `CDN_LOG_STREAM` (orchestrator `index.ts`); off on the Mac.
- **Adaptive sampling** (`nextSamplingRate`, `realLogConfig`): every 5 minutes the lines a second that arrived give the full request rate; over the target (`CDN_LOG_TARGET_LPS`, 1,000) by a fifth the rate drops at once to what the target needs (1 % floor); under 40 % of it the rate rises to 80 % of what would meet it. The change goes to CloudFront with UpdateRealtimeLogConfig (REST-XML, the stream, role and every field restated), is kept in `qoe_meta`, and weights the following lines. A refused change keeps the old rate, so the weights stay right.
- **Lines** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/cdnLogs.ts`): each line read in the configured order (the suite holds Terraform's list and the code's to the same order); CMCD from the query however CloudFront encoded it; the content from the first id in the path, its account through channels, events or assets (cached); a weight of 100 / the rate. Kept raw first in a new `cdn` stream of the raw store with the viewer's address blanked; sessions without a CMCD `sid` get an anonymous key (HMAC of the day, address, user agent and content with `QOE_ANON_SALT`, the access-token secret when unset). Lines nobody owns, and unreadable ones, are counted and dropped. Derived: `qoe_cdn_minutes` (requests, 4xx, 5xx, bytes, Origin Shield hits, starved requests, CMCD and sid shares, and DDSketches of edge first byte, time taken, origin first byte on a miss and segment delivery rate, all weighted) and `qoe_cdn_sessions`; analytics schema version 4.
- **Replay** (`replayCdnDay`, part of `replayDay` and run by the reader each night after 00:30 UTC for the day before): rebuilds a day's minutes and sessions from the raw lines (that day's and the next day's partitions, keeping the day's own requests), and joins each player `rr` report carrying `com.volt-cfid` to the edge's line with the same request id: the last mile, what the player waited beyond the edge's own first byte.
- **Reports** (`services/qoe/reports.ts`): `delivery` in `GET /api/analytics/qoe/overview` (requests, error rates, cache hit ratio, Origin Shield's share of misses, edge first byte, time taken, origin first byte, segment delivery rate with its slow end, starved share, last mile, by object type, worst content and country, and the audience: sessions at the CDN, with and without telemetry, without a session id); `telemetry.coverage`; the reader's state in `/collector`. Only content and country filter it (the edge does not know the device or app). Console: Analytics > Playback has a Delivery card and Reporting coverage in Telemetry health (`PlaybackReports.tsx`); the page no longer says "No playback reports" when only the CDN saw viewers.
- **Docs**: Analytics guide (Playback list, the reports table, two troubleshooting rows), developer guide Playback reporting (`delivery`, `coverage`, `com.volt-cfid` on `rr`), `volt-app/docs/REPORTING_TESTING.md` (the CDN path is built). No new screenshot: the guide has none of the Playback view. Fixed on the way: the docs site did not build (`{number}` in the Player SDK guide read as an expression since 1.14k-5).
- **Tooling**: `scripts/deploy/check-terraform-env.mjs` follows locals that take other locals (`qoe_env` takes `cdn_logs_env`); `aws-radio-captions.check.ts` looks at every PassRole statement, not the first one in the files.
- **Checked**: new `volt-app/test/cdn-logs.check.ts` **68/68** on Postgres 16 (the module does not exist before this change): Terraform and code agree on fields, order, behaviours and settings; sample CloudFront records read (times, user agent, CMCD encoded twice or whole, dashes, Origin Shield); no address in the raw files or the session key; a 5 % sample weighted by 20 against 1,000,000 requests: requests within 2 %, edge first byte p50 19.9 against 20.1 ms, p95 76.0 against 75.0, p99 130.3 against 129.7 (all within 2 % of the exact 100 % run); the rate rule (5,000 lines/s at 100 % to 20 %, the 1 % floor, held near the target, raised when well under); end to end through an in-memory Kinesis and the real API: 24 lines kept, the unknown content and a junk line left out, report 6's figures exact, 3 sessions at the CDN with 1 reporting and 2 not, coverage 1/3, the country filter, another account sees nothing; resume after a restart from the stored position, a closed shard marked and left, a rate change weighting the next line by 5; replay identical and idempotent, the last mile 75 ms from a 95 ms player first byte and a 20 ms edge one; the Kinesis and CloudFront calls signed and shaped right against a stand-in. `terraform test` 4/4 (new `tests/cdn_logs.tftest.hcl` 2/2 mocked, chaos 2/2), `terraform validate` and `fmt` clean. Neighbours: qoe-reports 71/71, qoe-reports-more 70/70 (one run 69/70 on its known clock-dependent gate check, 70/70 twice after), cmcd 73/73, journey 68/68, docs-samples 116/116, viewer-reporting 14/14, analytics-retention 33/33, watchfolder-s3-events 50/50, aws-radio-captions 54/54, chaos-harness 77/77, encoder-task-disk 42/42, terraform-env 45/47 (the two known, another session's `FAST_LAUNCHER`), docs-coverage 280/281 (another session's new console sections, as before), docs build ok and scrub clean. Typechecks on the device: volt-app clean; volt-web only the three known errors in other sessions' files. Browser (real API, Postgres, Vite, Chromium) with 600 seeded lines at 25 % and three SDK viewers: the Delivery card (2,400 requests, 93.5 % hits, 5 sessions, 2 without reports) and Reporting coverage 60 % render, no page errors, no sideways scroll at 390 px; screenshots looked at.
