## 2026-10-27 - Chaos and scaling harness (build queue 2.8, not yet released)

What: Tier 3 is ready to run against the Tier-2 staging deployment. One command per fault, a dry run that calls nothing and prints the exact commands, and a verdict judged on time budgets taken from the code rather than guessed.

- **Harness** (`deploy/loadtest/chaos.mjs`, `deploy/loadtest/chaos-lib.mjs`): five scenarios. `kill-encoder` stops a live channel's ECS task (the ARN the API reports) and expects the tick to notice within 45 s, auto connect to put a new task on air with the public playlist advancing within 240 s (480 s with `--new-server`), and one replacement only. `lose-source` stops the primary feed (a given command, or by hand) and expects the active source off primary within 14 s, no playlist stall over three segments, and failback within 29 s of the source returning. `fail-az` starts the zone's FIS template, stops the ECS servers in that zone and fails a Multi-AZ database over; expects the API never unreachable over 120 s and every channel back within 480 s. `spot-interrupt` starts the Spot template; every channel back within 600 s, one replacement each. `scale-out` runs `viewer-start.js` while sampling api-public's tasks; expects a scale-out, p95 at or under 300 ms and errors at or under 1 %. Every AWS action is an `aws` CLI argv shown in the dry run; nothing imports the SDK. A real run refuses without `--confirm <stack name>`, against anything named prod, or without the Terraform outputs. Exit 0 pass, 1 an expectation failed, 2 could not start; `--out` keeps every sample
- **Terraform** (`deploy/terraform/chaos.tf`): `enable_chaos_experiments` (default false, staging only) creates the FIS role (AWS-managed network and EC2 policies), one template per zone (`aws:network:disrupt-connectivity` on the private subnet, restored by FIS after `chaos_fault_seconds`, 600) and one Spot interruption template (two-minute notice, one running `<name>-ecs-node`). Outputs `chaos_stack` (stack name, region, database) and `chaos_experiment_templates`. Both variables in `terraform.tfvars.example`. Mocked `deploy/terraform/tests/chaos.tftest.hcl`
- **Run sheet** (`deploy/loadtest/CHAOS.md`): set-up (stack settings, CLI permissions, test channels armed for auto connect, one with a backup and one with only a filler), each scenario's command and budgets with the reason for each, the order to run them, a results table and the cost (FIS about $0.10 an action-minute).
- **Docs**: `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md`, `deploy/terraform/README.md`, `deploy/loadtest/README.md`. No customer guide: nothing a customer sees changes. Change note `--scope internal`.
- **Checked**: new `volt-app/test/chaos-harness.check.ts` **77/77** (tsx on the device; it needs no database): the stack read from Terraform outputs, all five dry runs make no call and print the stack's real commands (the zone's own template, the servers in that zone, the database failover only when Multi-AZ), the refusals, the playlist parsing, the judge passing and failing made-up timelines for every expectation (late, never back, a relaunch storm, a stalled playlist, no failback, filler counting as a switch, an API outage, no k6 summary), whole runs of kill-encoder and fail-az against a fake AWS and a fake Volt (the reported task stopped on the right cluster, exactly the listed servers stopped), and the Terraform gated and documented. `terraform fmt -check` clean, `terraform validate` clean, `terraform test` 2/2 with mocked providers (off: nothing created; on with two zones: two zone templates, one Spot, the output keys), Terraform 1.9.8 in the container. Neighbours: aws-radio-captions 54/54, terraform-env 45/47 (the two failures are `FAST_LAUNCHER`, read by another session's uncommitted `fast/playoutRunner.ts`, as at 2.4c). `tsc --noEmit` clean in volt-app; volt-web `tsc -b` stops on three errors in another session's uncommitted files (`ImagePicker.tsx`, `ChannelDetail.tsx`, `EncodeJobs.tsx`), none touched here.
- **Not done**: not run on AWS (needs the account, item "AWS first apply and Tier-3 real run"). The budgets are the design's; the first real run will show whether a relaunch on a server with room really lands inside 240 s. Losing the staging NAT gateway's zone cuts every zone's egress (one NAT to save money); the run sheet says to pick another zone. A zone cut only by the network (servers still running) would leave encoders RUNNING to ECS and so not relaunched; the scenario also stops the servers, as a real zone loss would, and that case is worth watching on the first run.

## 2026-10-27 - The encoder's task disk on AWS (build queue 2.4c, not yet released)

What: on AWS an encoder no longer keeps its whole run on its server's disk. It keeps a window once the segments are in S3, the servers get a sized root volume, and placement's disk check is on and counts that window. The Mac is unchanged: a local output never starts the uploader and the local run is the recording.

- **Uploader** (`volt-encoder/bin/s3-uploader.sh`): with `LOCAL_KEEP_SECONDS` set, the window is that or the job's `dvr_seconds` plus 120, whichever is longer (read from `JOB_CONFIG_JSON` or `/job/config.json` as `encoder.py` does). After each segment sync that exits cleanly it removes numbered media segments (`(seg|sub)_N.(m4s|ts|aac|vtt)`) last written more than the window before that sync started, so each one existed unchanged when a successful sync began and is in S3. A failed sync removes nothing; init segments, manifests and the end-of-run caption file stay; no sync ever deletes in S3. Unset, 0 or not a number: every segment stays. `encoder.py` (another session's uncommitted work) is not changed: its `--preserved_segments_outside_live_window 0` stays, and the uploader does the removing.
- **Terraform**: `block_device_mappings` on both launch templates (gp3, encrypted, `/dev/xvda`): `encoder_root_volume_gib` (30) for the small and large CPU pools, `gpu_root_volume_gib` (60) for the GPU pool. `ECS_ENGINE_TASK_CLEANUP_WAIT_DURATION=15m` on both, so a stopped task's container disk is not held for the default 3 hours. `LOCAL_KEEP_SECONDS` on the encoder task (`encoder_local_keep_seconds`, 1800); `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` and `ENCODER_LOCAL_KEEP_SECONDS` on the control plane; the variables in `terraform.tfvars.example`; `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` out of `env-coverage.json`.
- **Projection** (`services/fleet/storageProjection.ts`, `streamLoad.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `assign.ts`): `localWindowSeconds(job)` mirrors the script (off for a local output or without `ENCODER_LOCAL_KEEP_SECONDS`); `projectStorage` adds `scratchCapMib` (rate over the window plus 2 minutes); `scratchMibAt` caps at it, at placement and when the snapshot reads a running stream's stored decision. Records from before keep counting the whole run. A 4-rung 1080p ladder now counts about 2.5 GiB instead of about 110 GiB over the 24 hour horizon, so it places on a 30 GiB server.
- **Docs**: `PLACEMENT.md` (storage section, settings table, still to do), `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md`, `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md`. No customer guide: nothing a customer sees changes. Change note `--scope internal`.
- **Checked** (container, Postgres 16, on HEAD plus only this change): new `volt-app/test/encoder-task-disk.check.ts` **42/42**: the real script with a stand-in `aws` (a local folder as the bucket): old segments gone and each in S3 with its size, recent, init, manifests and caption file kept, a failed sync removes nothing, no window keeps all, a long DVR widens the window to 3120 s, the config file path, a bad value is off; `encoder.py` starts the uploader only for a non-local output; the projection and placement (the ladder unplaceable without the window, a new server with it); the Terraform. On HEAD's files 9 uploader cases fail and the projection part stops. `load-learning` **23/23** (new: a stored cap bounds the snapshot), `load-fit` 47/47, `aws-radio-captions` 54/54, `placement-planner` 26/26, `placement-e2e` 21/21, `consolidation-plan` 28/28, `fleet-api` 23/23, `relay-fleet` 38/38, `segment-placement` 17/17, `captions-plan` 19/19. `terraform fmt -check` and `terraform validate` (1.9.8) clean. `terraform-env` 47/47 on the working tree with `playoutRunner.ts` as committed; with another session's edit of it (11:56 UTC, reads `FAST_LAUNCHER`) it fails on that one name, before and after this change. Device typecheck of `volt-app` clean; `volt-web` has 3 errors in files with another session's uncommitted work (not touched here).
- **Pre-existing, not changed**: `captions-gpu-sharing` stops at its database part on a fresh check template (its instance rows have neither channel nor event, which `instances_check` refuses), the same on HEAD.
- **Not done**: not run on AWS. A real long 1080p run on the Tier-2 cluster should show the root staying near the OS, the images and the window; the 60 GiB GPU root is a judgement for the CUDA images, not measured. Radio's task keeps whole runs (no window set; a few hundred MB a day).

## 2026-10-27 - Several captions streams on one GPU (build queue 2.4b, not yet released)

What: with `captions_gpu_sharing` on, a Whisper captions task no longer takes a T4 to itself. Two small-model streams run on one g4dn.xlarge instead of two, and placement protects the GPU by its memory and its time.

- **Model** (`volt-app/src/services/fleet/streamLoad.ts`): `estimateWhisperLoad` now carries GPU memory (`vramMib`, from `captionsPlan.sizeWhisper`) and GPU time per model (`K.whisperGpuShare`: small 0.2, medium 0.35, large-v3 0.55, plus 0.1 for translation; was a flat 0.5). These are starting estimates from published faster-whisper throughput, not measured. `shareGpu()` gives the same estimate reserving no GPU (cost and signature unchanged), `unshareGpu()` the way back.
- **Fit test** (`capacity.ts`): a sharer needs a GPU server with no whole-GPU reservation, total GPU memory within 90 % of the card (T4 15 GiB, A10G 22.5 GiB, new `gpuMemoryMib` in the instance table) and GPU time, newcomer times 1.25, at or under `FLEET_SHARED_GPU_CEILING` (0.8); a whole-GPU task is refused on a server with sharers. CPU, memory, network and task slots as before: on a g4dn.xlarge the two task network interfaces stop a third small stream.
- **Placement** (`assign.ts`, `placementPlanner.ts`, `fleetModel.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `placementConfig.ts`): captions share only when `FLEET_CAPTIONS_GPU_SHARING` and `CAPTIONS_SHARED_TASK_DEF` are both set. A sharer planned on a new server carries the running GPU servers to avoid. The record keeps `reserved_gpus = 0` and the GPU memory in `placement_decision`, which the snapshot reads back. Placement off, unplaceable or failed: the estimate goes back to a whole GPU, so the record matches what launches.
- **Launcher** (`orchestrator/launcher.ts`): a sharing launch uses the shared task definition, no GPU override, pinned with `memberOf` or kept off the avoided servers with `not(ec2InstanceId in [...])`. When ECS will not start it, captions launch on the whole-GPU task definition (which makes the pool scale out) and the hints are updated so the record says the GPU is held.
- **Terraform**: `aws_ecs_task_definition.captions_shared` (`<name>-captions-shared`, no `resourceRequirements`, `NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=all`), `CAPTIONS_SHARED_TASK_DEF` and `FLEET_CAPTIONS_GPU_SHARING` in the control-plane env, variable `captions_gpu_sharing` (default false, in the example), and the GPU launch template makes NVIDIA Docker's default runtime when sharing is on (rendered and checked both ways). `FLEET_SHARED_GPU_CEILING` in `env-coverage.json`.
- **Docs**: `PLACEMENT.md` section "Captions on a shared GPU" with the real-T4 check, settings and suites tables; `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md`; `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md`. No customer guide: nothing a customer sees changes.
- **Checked**: new `volt-app/test/captions-gpu-sharing.check.ts` **43/43** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container (the model; the fit on a g4dn.xlarge for two small, task slots, GPU memory, GPU time and both neighbour rules; through the database with `assignStream` and `recordAssignment`: two small streams share the running g4dn.xlarge, the third is planned on a new one that avoids it, sharing off gives the second its own server, placement off reserves whole; the launcher against a mocked ECS client including the fall back; the Terraform). Against the code before this change it fails 2 and stops (`shareGpu` missing). Neighbours: aws-radio-captions 54/54, terraform-env 47/47, stream-load 31/31, placement-planner 26/26, placement-e2e 21/21, load-fit 47/47, load-learning 22/22, consolidation-plan 28/28, fleet-api 23/23, meter 80/80, relay-fleet 38/38, captions-plan 19/19, captions-lane 19/19, captions-toggle 6/6, captions-orchestration and orchestrator ok, engineering-guides 35/35 (on the device). `terraform fmt -check` and `validate` clean (1.13.4), `check-terraform-env.mjs` no problems, volt-app tsc clean. volt-web tsc shows three errors, all in files with another session's uncommitted work, none touched here.
- **Not done**: not run on a real T4 (needs the AWS account): two streams side by side, the NVIDIA runtime giving a container without a GPU reservation the GPU, and the real GPU time per model. Whether ECS scales the pool out for a task behind the `not(...)` constraint is not known; the fall back to a whole GPU covers it either way. NVENC encoders still reserve their GPU whole. The start path in `service.ts` was not changed (it carries another session's uncommitted work); the captions launch already passes its hints through.


## 2026-10-28 - Server-guided quality (CMSD) and content steering (build queue 2.10, not yet released)

What: when the origin's uplink is nearly full, live playlists tell players a fair highest bitrate (CMSD), and a stream delivered over more than one path lets the player be steered between them (HLS `EXT-X-CONTENT-STEERING`, DASH `ContentSteering`) per viewer country, from the players' own quality reports. Delivery stays standard HLS and DASH; the media never goes through Volt.

- **CMSD** (`volt-app/src/services/delivery/cmsd.ts`): `CMSD-Static: ot=m,sf=h|d,st=l` and `CMSD-Dynamic: "volt-origin"[;mb=<kb/s>][;du]` on every live playlist the Mac origin serves (the `/origin` middleware in `server.ts`, one dynamic import), on the variant manifest service's playlists and on the steered masters. The header is always sent: players keep the last CMSD-Dynamic they saw, so guidance is lifted by a header without `mb`. A meter counts every `/origin` response's bytes per stream and per viewer (CMCD `sid`, else the address) over a short window. Duress starts above `DELIVERY_DURESS_HI` (0.9) of `DELIVERY_ALLOWANCE_MBPS` (off when unset) and lifts only when what the viewers would take unguided (their streams' top rungs) fits under `DELIVERY_DURESS_LO` (0.7) for `DELIVERY_DURESS_HOLD_SEC` (20); lifting on the measured load made the lab cycle (guided players pull less, the guidance lifted, everyone went back to the top). Under duress each viewer gets a max-min fair share of `DELIVERY_DURESS_TARGET` (0.8) of the allowance, using the top rung as demand (a ceiling from the guided rate ratcheted every stream to its lowest rung), floored to a rung of the stream's own ladder (read from its delivered master), never below the lowest. `DELIVERY_CMSD=off` sends none. Shaka (the SDK's engine) follows `mb` by default, so the player needed no change; the SDK already reports `cmsds`/`cmsdd` (1.14d).
- **Steering** (`volt-app/src/services/delivery/steering.ts`, routes in `modules/publicLive.ts`): `DELIVERY_PATHWAYS` (JSON: pathways with id and base, optional per-country order, TTL) with two or more paths makes `/live/<id>/master.m3u8` and `manifest.mpd` answer the master here instead of a redirect: HLS with every rendition group and variant once per pathway (group ids suffixed, `PATHWAY-ID`, absolute addresses), DASH with a `BaseURL` per `serviceLocation` and `ContentSteering` (an MPD with its own MPD-level BaseURL is served as before). `GET /live/<id>/steering` (public, never cached) answers `VERSION 1`, `TTL`, a `RELOAD-URI` without the player's own parameters (Shaka otherwise added them again on every reload) and `PATHWAY-PRIORITY`: the configured order, a drained path (`DELIVERY_PATHWAYS_DRAIN`) last, and a path clearly worse for viewers in that country over ten minutes last (from `qoe_minutes` by country and serving host: rebuffer share plus failed-attempt share at least twice the best path's and at least 2 %, with at least 5 minutes of playing time; two paths on one host are not judged). Not for per-viewer addresses (FAST stitched) or the rights variants.
- **API docs**: overlay entry for `GET /live/:id/steering`; `api-routes.json`, `API_ROUTES.md` and `openapi.json` regenerated on HEAD plus this change and only this route's lines added to the working copies (they carry another session's routes not yet regenerated).
- **Guides**: `docs-site/docs/guides/player-sdk.md` (new section "Server-guided quality and delivery paths"), `docs/engineering/running-on-the-mac.md` (the settings), `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` (cost functions table). Scrub check clean, engineering-guides 35/35.
- **Checked** (container, Postgres 16): new `volt-app/test/delivery-guidance.check.ts` **72/72** (values read back as Shaka's own parser reads them, rung floor, meter, duress and hold, fair share and the two cycles it prevents, steered HLS and DASH, priority rules; through `createServer()`: CMSD on `/origin` playlists and not segments, metering by sid, duress from other viewers' load and its lifting, `DELIVERY_CMSD=off`, the redirect unchanged without pathways, the steered master and MPD, a player that ignores steering following a listed address, the steering service ordering by planted reports for AU and not NZ, drain). New `volt-app/test/delivery-browser.check.ts` **10/10** (four runs): Shaka 4.16 in Chromium with the SDK's streaming settings and CMCD, three VP9 rungs (400, 1200, 3000 kb/s), live playlists advancing every second. CMSD lab: four viewers on the top rung (11.6 Mb/s) behind one 10 Mb/s uplink (a proxy sharing 10 Mb/s between responses in flight), allowance 10 Mb/s, 40 s. Recorded: following CMSD **0 stalls** (0 ms), uplink full **0 s of 31**, all four at 1200 at the end; ignoring CMSD **2 stalls (194 ms)**, uplink full **15 s of 30**, least buffer 0.7 s; an earlier run 0 against 1 stall (60 ms), full 0 against 12 s. The suite asserts no more stalls and no more saturation with CMSD, the ceiling held, everyone still playing; the strict "fewer stalls" is recorded, not asserted, because two single-viewer labs (a throttle to 1.6 and then 1 Mb/s after a load rise) showed Shaka adapting with no stall either way. Steering lab: a viewer in AU starts on path a (127.0.0.1), asks the service every 2 s saying its path once, and moves to path b (localhost) within 0.1 to 2.1 s of the reports changing, with no stall after the move and the picture advancing 12 s in 12 s. Neighbours through `scripts/check.js`: cmcd-browser 34/34, edge-variant-manifests 62/62, edge-variants 62/62, rights-preview 50/50, live-streams-list 55/55, monitoring 32/32, qoe-reports-more 70/70, player-stats 49/49, radio-outputs 18/18, stream-model 25/25, timed-metadata 37/37, hls-master-pure 20/20, hls-master 12/12, geo-access 15/15, geo-rights 7/7. Not this change: cmcd.check and live-scte35 need `deploy/terraform` and `volt-encoder` beside the copy; fast-access 26/29 and player-stats-browser 23/25 fail on another session's uncommitted FAST routes and player menu. Repeated on HEAD plus only this change (a `git archive` copy): delivery-guidance 72/72, delivery-browser 10/10, edge-variant-manifests 62/62, edge-variants 62/62, live-streams-list 55/55, rights-preview 50/50 (once 49/50 on a one-second boundary in its strip times, 50/50 on the rerun); volt-app typecheck clean there too. Typecheck: volt-app clean; volt-web has three errors in another session's uncommitted files (`ImagePicker.tsx`, `ChannelDetail.tsx`, `EncodeJobs.tsx`), none touched here.
- **Not done**: CMSD on media playlists CloudFront serves from S3 (needs a viewer-response function on every playlist request: a cost decision, queued as 2.10a). The duress signal on AWS (the origin there is S3, so the Mac's uplink meter has no counterpart; the 2.9 edge logs could feed one). A second CDN (a contract, needs input). Steering for rights-replaced viewers (the variant service serves one path). The report that splits guided from network-driven drops (QOE_REPORTING.md 2.10 row): the SDK sends what it received, the report is not built. Not run on the Mac with real devices.

## 2026-10-28 - ClickHouse analytics store and the move to it (build queue 2.11, not yet released)

What: the derived playback data (view rows, minute aggregates, the play gate's log, the collector's counts, journeys and app sessions, CDN minutes and sessions) can live in ClickHouse instead of the Postgres analytics database, and with `QOE_STORE=auto` Volt moves there by itself once the audience passes the size QOE_REPORTING.md section 9 gives (1 M viewer hours a month). The raw reports stay the record; everything in ClickHouse is rebuildable by the replay.

- **Store** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/clickhouse.ts`): ClickHouse's HTTP interface through fetch, no client library, so the same code talks to a self-run cluster or ClickHouse Cloud (decision 4 in QOE_REPORTING.md section 11 stays a URL). `CLICKHOUSE_URL` may list both replicas: one that does not answer is skipped. Tables made on first use (ON CLUSTER and Replicated engines with `CLICKHOUSE_CLUSTER`): upserted tables ReplacingMergeTree on a write version, read FINAL; added tables (minutes, collector counts, CDN minutes and sessions) AggregatingMergeTree with SimpleAggregateFunction columns (sum, min, max, any, sumMap for sketch bins) so flushes from several processes merge as ON CONFLICT did, inserted with `insert_deduplicate=0`; the gate log MergeTree; month partitions; skip indexes on ended_at, started_at and sid. Settings make aggregates behave as in Postgres (sum of nothing is NULL, outer joins leave NULLs).
- **The report SQL** (`toClickHouse`): reports stay written once in Postgres SQL. Parameters become escaped literals, `= ANY($n)` becomes has(), `percentile_disc` becomes Postgres's own nearest rank (ceil(p x n) over the sorted non-null values, FILTER honoured), casts go, FINAL is added where rows are versioned, `jsonb_agg` of sketches becomes one merged sketch per group, and an alias that is also a column is renamed so ORDER BY and WHERE read as Postgres reads them. Four queries carry ClickHouse text beside the Postgres one (`aquery(text, params, chText)`): unknown keys and collector reasons (jsonb_each_text), the gate reason per failed view (LATERAL, now argMax), and the two correlated EXISTS (CDN coverage, journey entry filter). Tie-breakers added to five ORDER BYs that were not deterministic in Postgres either.
- **The move** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/storeSwitch.ts`, started by the leader in `services/orchestrator/index.ts`): postgres, moving, clickhouse, kept in qoe_meta so every process agrees. Past the size: both stores written from the next whole minute (ClickHouse first; a failed Postgres write while moving is counted, not retried, so a retry never adds minutes into ClickHouse twice), the gate log and collector counts copied up to the switch minute, up to 400 days (`QOE_BACKFILL_DAYS`) replayed from the raw archive into ClickHouse a day at a time (20 minutes an hour, resumable), the switch day replayed once it is over, then the reports read ClickHouse. Never moves back by itself; Postgres tables untouched. State on `GET /api/analytics/qoe/collector` (`store`).
- **Terraform** (`deploy/terraform/clickhouse.tf`, off by default): two r7g.large replicas at fixed addresses in two zones, Keeper on each and a t4g.small third, nightly backup to S3 at 03:17 UTC (full Sundays, incremental other nights, replica 2 only when replica 1 is down, kept 35 days), AL2023 arm64, IMDSv2, encrypted gp3, SSM and no SSH, port 8123 from the control plane only, the volt user's password generated and the URL in its own secret. The control plane gets `QOE_STORE=auto` (else `postgres`), `CLICKHOUSE_CLUSTER`, `CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE`, `QOE_CLICKHOUSE_ABOVE_VH`. About $200 a month on. `tests/clickhouse.tftest.hcl`, tfvars lines, env-coverage entries. Also four settings 2.10 added without coverage entries (DELIVERY_CMSD, DELIVERY_PATHWAYS, DELIVERY_PATHWAYS_DRAIN, STEERING_MIN_PLAYING_MS) now covered, so `check-terraform-env.mjs` is down to FAST_LAUNCHER (another session's uncommitted FAST work).
- **Tools**: `volt-app/scripts/chdb-http.py` (a stand-in ClickHouse server for the checks: the ClickHouse engine through chDB, the HTTP subset the store uses; no replication), `scripts/qoe-ch-bench.ts` (13 months of views generated inside ClickHouse, every report timed through the product's code), `scripts/qoe-load.ts` (the real collector under N simulated viewers), `scripts/clickhouse-drill.ts` (lose a replica, keep reporting, check it refilled; for a real cluster).
- **Guides**: `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` section 8a (turning it on, checks after apply, the drill, restoring a backup), `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` and `docs/engineering/check-suites.md`. No customer guide change: nothing a customer sets or sees changes, apart from reports keeping up at size (change note).
- **Checked** (container, Postgres 16; ClickHouse through the chDB stand-in, ClickHouse 26.9 engine; a ClickHouse server could not be installed there): new `volt-app/test/clickhouse-store.check.ts` **40/40** (literals nothing breaks out of; percentile_disc identical to Postgres in 120/120 cases over sizes, levels, NULLs and FILTER; 2 000 random views, 800 gate decisions and 600 minute rows in both stores, 24/24 reports identical and the filter values the same; a view written twice counts once, an identical minute flush adds, a CDN session widens and adds, a replay replaces one account's day only; the move end to end with raw reports for three past days: stays below the size, moves at the next whole minute, a view after the switch in both stores, early collector counts only copied, history replayed, reads move only after the switch day, then ClickHouse says exactly what Postgres said with 37/37 views; two replicas, one killed: same report from the other, health names the dead one, writes go through; the cluster DDL; the Terraform). The QoE suites on BOTH stores: qoe-reports 71/71, qoe-reports-more 70/70, cdn-logs 68/68, journey 68/68, cmcd 73/73, delivery-guidance 72/72, stream-report 77/77 (the outage drills now take down whichever store is in use). On Postgres also viewer-reporting 14/14. Typecheck clean. `terraform fmt -check`, `validate` and `test` (6/6) clean (Terraform 1.13.4).
- **Load** (`scripts/qoe-load.ts`, real API in one process, viewers over HTTP from another, 2 vCPU container): 100 000 concurrent viewers at the modelled 220 reports per viewer hour: **6 111 reports a second for 241 s, 1 466 650 accepted, 0 refused, 0 flush failures, 105 microseconds of CPU a report, 0.64 vCPU** at that rate, so two vCPUs of collector keep it near 32 % (the model in QOE_REPORTING.md section 9 assumed 50 microseconds, so its collector line roughly doubles: about $50 a month of Fargate ARM at 100 k concurrent). 100 000 live sessions held 1.0 GB of heap. Same on ClickHouse (105.4 microseconds, 0 flush failures).
- **Dashboards** (`scripts/qoe-ch-bench.ts`, 1 M viewer hours a month: 20.15 M views and 6.8 M minute rows over 13 months, 1.5 GB on disk, two vCPUs as on r7g.large, median of 3): last day 20 to 102 ms per report, last 31 days 94 to 488 ms, last 90 days 195 ms to 1.6 s, **13 months 0.7 to 6.1 s** (startup 6.1 s, buffering 5.9 s, rendering 5.5 s, playback success 3.0 s), filtered 13 months 0.7 to 3.8 s. The whole console page at once 1.3 s for a day (the stand-in runs one query at a time). Exact percentiles and exact distinct sessions over 16 M rows are the cost; DDSketch estimates measured 1.4 s for the startup report. So "under 1 s over 13 months" is **not met**: 2.11a asks which way to take it.
- **Not done**: the replica rebuild with a real Keeper (the drill script and APPLY.md 8a are ready; run on the AWS deployment). Session routing by sid across collector tasks (2.11b). Under 1 s over 13 months (2.11a, needs input). `openapi-overlay.json` not updated for the new `store` field of `GET /api/analytics/qoe/collector` (the file carries another session's uncommitted work). Not run on the Mac (needs a ClickHouse or `pip install chdb`).

## 2026-10-28 - Collector sessions routed by sid across tasks (build queue 2.11b, not yet released)

What: with two or more collector tasks behind the load balancer, each viewer session is now derived by exactly one task, so its live view row is the same row a replay gives and a task holds only its share of the audience; yesterday is replayed from the raw archive every night, so whatever a task change left provisional is exact by morning.

- **Routing** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/sidRoute.ts`): the tasks that take reports list themselves in the analytics database (`qoe_collectors`: id, address, heartbeat every 5 s, a member while seen within three heartbeats; a task joins with its first report). The owner of a session is picked by rendezvous hashing of account and sid over the member ids, the same on every task without asking anyone; a task joining or leaving moves only the sessions it gains or loses (4 tasks to 3: 998 of 4 000 moved, all the leaving task's). The receiving task keeps the batch raw first as before, derives the rows it owns, and queues the rest per owner; queued forwards go every 200 ms, one request per peer, to `POST /api/cmcd/_peer` (in `modules/cmcd.ts`), signed with HMAC-SHA256 over time and body with a key derived from `JWT_ACCESS_SECRET` (or `QOE_PEER_SECRET`), refused after 60 s or with any change, never forwarded again by the receiver. A forward that fails is derived where it arrived and counted (`collectorState().route.forwardFailed`). The player's 204 never waits on any of it. `QOE_ROUTE=auto` (default) routes when the task knows its own address: `QOE_PEER_URL`, or the task IP from the ECS metadata endpoint; on the Mac (one process, neither set) nothing changes. If the member list cannot be read, everything is derived locally.
- **Nightly replay** (`volt-app/src/services/qoe/nightly.ts`, started with the collector's flush timer): every 10 minutes each task that takes reports checks whether yesterday is due (after `QOE_NIGHTLY_REPLAY_AT`, default 02:30 UTC); the first takes an advisory lock in the analytics database, checks the `nightly_replay` marker in qoe_meta, replays every account's yesterday (`replay.ts`) and moves the marker. `QOE_NIGHTLY_REPLAY=off` turns it off. This is the scheduled nightly replay listed as not done under 1.14e.
- **Terraform**: the control-plane security group lets its own tasks reach each other on 8080 (inline ingress with `self = true`, so the group's inline rules are not overwritten by a separate rule resource); `QOE_ROUTE=auto` in `qoe_raw.tf`'s environment for every control-plane task.
- **Cost**: per task one upsert and one select every 5 s on the analytics database, and at most one forward request per peer every 200 ms however large the audience. Memory for live sessions falls to about 1/n per task. The CPU of forwarding (JSON out and in for (n-1)/n of the reports) was not measured under load.
- **Guides**: `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` (several collector tasks) and `docs/engineering/check-suites.md` (the new suite and why its fixture runs under `node --import tsx`). No customer guide change: nothing a customer sets or sees changes, apart from live reports being exact on a multi-task deployment.
- **Checked** (container, Postgres 16, staged copy): new `volt-app/test/qoe-sid-route.check.ts` **29/29**, three runs, about 9 s each: two real collector processes (`test/fixtures/qoe-collector-node.ts`) on one analytics database and raw folder, 40 sessions with every batch sent to both in turn. Without routing (the old behaviour) each process held 40 of 40 sessions and 40 of 40 live rows differed from a replay; with routing both listed the same two members, 20 and 20 sessions held, forwards both ways with none failed, every one of 290 reports derived exactly once, one row per session and every live row identical to the replay. Unsigned, stale and altered forwards 401. An owner killed: the player still got 204, the forward failed and was derived on the survivor, the dead task dropped from the list within three heartbeats, then its sessions were local. Nightly replay: nothing before 02:30, at 03:00 yesterday replayed (83 views) and the deliberately wrong rows exact again, nothing a second time, two callers at once gave one replay. Around it on Postgres: qoe-reports 71/71, qoe-reports-more 70/70, cmcd 73/73, journey 68/68, cdn-logs 68/68, delivery-guidance 72/72, stream-report 77/77; 9/9 suites, 499 assertions, 0 failed (clickhouse-store skipped itself: no chDB in this container). Terraform fmt and validate clean, terraform test 6/6. volt-app typecheck clean on the device; engineering-guides 35/35.
- **Not done**: not run with real ECS tasks (the metadata endpoint address and the security group rule are from AWS's documentation; check after the first apply that `collectorState().route.members` lists every api-public task). The suites were not rerun on ClickHouse (the change touches no store code; the member list stays in the Postgres analytics database either way). `docs/api-routes.json` and `openapi.json` not regenerated for the internal `/api/cmcd/_peer` route: both carry another session's uncommitted work. The volt-web typecheck fails on three files with another session's uncommitted work (`ImagePicker.tsx`, `ChannelDetail.tsx`, `EncodeJobs.tsx`), none touched here. No release: other sessions' unreleased notes are in the tree, as for the runs before.

## 2026-10-29 - Watchfolders, share link views and Send to add-on (build queue 1.14-G3a-3, v0.16.0)

What: (d), (i) and (j) of the media guides' fixes. (e) to (h) were split to 1.14-G3a-4 to keep this to one run.

- **Failed watchfolder items** (`volt-app/src/modules/watchfolders.ts`, resolve route): Failed rows are resolvable. Reprocess on a failed item is a retry: status back to `seen` with `resolved_at` cleared (the claim marker), so the next ingest pass takes it; the reason becomes "retrying after: <old reason>". Remove closes it and leaves the file in the drop folder. Any other status answers 400 "nothing to resolve". The console shows **Retry** and **Dismiss** on Failed rows, **Reprocess** and **Dismiss** on review rows.
- **Found on the way: resolve always answered 400.** The route audited under `entity_type 'watchfolder_item'` with the numeric item id, and `audit_log.entity_id` is a UUID, so the insert failed after the row had changed: the console showed an error for every Reprocess and Dismiss while the queue had moved on. The resolve is now audited under the folder (`entity_id` = folder id, the item id in the snapshot). `watchfolder-api` never went through HTTP, so it did not see it.
- **Target kinds** (`targetKinds()`, `assertTargetKind()` in the same module, reading `ingestableTargetKinds()` and `videoTargetKinds()`): creating or editing a rule that accepts files, switching a refusing rule to accept, and a folder's default target kind must name a kind the image or video ingest takes in (case-insensitive); refusing rules are not checked. `GET /api/watchfolders/:id` returns `targetKinds` (kind and library), and the rule form is a grouped list instead of free text. Stored rules with another kind are not changed; the console marks them "never taken in".
- **Folder page** (`volt-web/src/pages/Watchfolders.tsx`): Rename (inline), Switch off and on (PATCH `enabled`, versioned), Delete (ConfirmDialog, type the name; files kept, rules and queue deleted by the existing cascade); a switched-off folder says so and Scan now is greyed out; the list says "switched off". Rule delete asks first. Write controls (new folder, rules, ladder, scan, queue actions) follow Files, uploads & storage: Manage; Try a filename is greyed out with the reason without Operate, and an error from the dry run is shown instead of swallowed.
- **Share link views** (migration `0118_share_link_visits.sql`, `services/shareLinks.ts` `openShare`, `modules/shareLinks.ts`, `modules/sharePage.ts`): the page keeps a visit id in session storage (per link, per tab; a fresh one per load without storage) and sends it with every resolve. The first open of a visit inserts a row and counts the view; a visit that has counted is let back in under the view limit it used and only updates `last_viewed_at`. Revoked, expiry, PIN and region still apply. A malformed or missing visit id counts every time, as before. Revoke in the Share window asks on the row (Keep / Revoke link).
- **Send to add-on** (`volt-app/src/auth/roles.ts` `requirementFor`, `enforceAccess`): `POST /api/workflows/runs` for a `recording` needs Recordings: Operate or Account settings: Operate, for a `vod_asset` Media library: Operate or Account settings: Operate; other subjects stay Account settings. `GET /api/workflows/runs?subjectType=..&subjectId=..` is View on the subject's feature (the query is passed for this one path only). Cancel and retry stay Account settings. The button (`SendToAddon` in `pages/Workflows.tsx`) shows by the same rule; Track it and the workflow map link only for Account settings: View.
- **Docs**: `guides/watchfolders.md` (the kind list, Retry, rename/switch off/delete, who can, troubleshooting, API), `guides/share-links.md` (one view per visit, Revoke confirm), `guides/recordings.md` and `guides/workflows.md` (who can send). `watchfolders.png` retaken from the docs seed and looked at. API routes and openapi regenerated, no change.
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `test/watchfolder-share-addon.check.ts` **58/58** on Postgres 16 (**32/58** with the five server files at HEAD: 26 fail; it also counts a throw part way as a failure). Neighbours: 29/31 green (access-control 17/17, access-rules 28/28, custom-role 31/31, fast-access 50/50, geo-access 15/15, hypermedia 57/57, ingest-core 97/97, ingest-extract 7/7, ingest-providers 21/21, ingest-s3 16/16, ingest-sources 22/22, listStandard, media-internals 31/31, review-access 22/22, rights-preview 50/50, share-links 34/34, source-preview 11/11, watchfolder-api 26/26, watchfolder-ingest 70/70, watchfolder-rules 43/43, watchfolder-s3-events 50/50, watchfolder-scan 33/33, watchfolder-video-landing 45/45, watchfolder-video 70/70, workflow-fixes 27/27, workflow-policy, workflows 72/72); known and unchanged: review-qc 49/51 (same at HEAD), docs-coverage (the four sections queued as 1.14-G8; `check-coverage.mjs` 4 errors, scrub clean). ## 2026-10-29 - API uploads keep their name, a channel's kind the same in list and item (build queue 1.14-G6a, v0.21.1)

What: the two fixes found writing the developer guides.

- **(a) Uploads through the API keep their name** (`volt-app/src/services/ingest/pipeline.ts` title and `givenTitle()`, `volt-app/src/services/uploads.ts`): the ingest set the title from the content type's fields, a title in the file, or the file name read as a title, and wrote it over the title the upload was opened with; with no content type, `metadata.title` and `metadata.name` were dropped by the merge too. The title is now, best first: the content type's title field; a `title` or `name` sent in `metadata` (with or without a content type); the upload's own `name` when it is not just the file name, with or without its extension; a title in the file; the file name read as a title. The console opens uploads with the file name minus its extension, so console uploads are titled as before. `uploads.ts` reads the name from the placeholder asset before a retry's reattach swaps the record. Watch folders and remote ingest pass no name and are unchanged.
- **(b) A channel's `kind`** (`volt-app/src/modules/channels.ts` `GET /:id`): not JSON-LD as the queue said. The item answer spread the detail builder's stream `kind` (`channel`, shared with events) over the row's, so the list said `tv` or `radio` and the item `channel` in both forms. The row's kind now wins in the item answer. The console reads the detail kind only to tell events apart, so nothing there changes.
- **Docs**: `docs-site/samples/uploads/upload.sh` loses step 4 (the rename); `docs-site/docs/api/uploads.md` shows the title from the name, says what `name` and `metadata` do for the title, and its troubleshooting row says why a title can still come from the file. `volt-app/test/docs-samples.check.ts` expects the upload sample's title from its name and no longer looks for the rename. No new routes, so `docs:routes` and `docs:openapi` were not rerun.

Checked: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). ## 2026-10-30 - Slates and loops in the bucket on AWS (build queue 2.1a, first piece; v0.22.0)

What: on AWS an uploaded slate or loop now lives in the origin bucket instead of on the control-plane task that received it, so every task serves it and a replaced task loses nothing. The Mac is unchanged (files under `FILLER_DIR`). Per Christian's 27 Oct decision for 2.1a (option B, S3). The rest of 2.1a is split into 2.1a-2 (clip, encode and download files and the trash), 2.1a-3 (prebuilt loop segments) and 2.1a-4 (ffmpeg, packager and Python jobs as their own ECS tasks), one run's worth each.

- **Filler store** (new `volt-app/src/services/media/fillerStore.ts`): `FILLER_STORE_DRIVER` `local` (default) or `s3`. The object key is the asset's existing source key, `filler/<account>/<asset id><ext>`, in `S3_BUCKET` (or `FILLER_S3_BUCKET`, optional `FILLER_S3_PREFIX`). `putFiller` for direct uploads, `landFile` for files assembled on disk (resumable uploads, watch folder loops: uploaded then the local copy removed), `serveFiller` for `/filler/...` (immutable cache headers kept), `localCopy` for ffmpeg (scratch under `FILLER_CACHE_DIR`), `deleteFiller`.
- **Callers**: `modules/fillerAssets.ts` (direct upload), `services/uploads.ts` `completeLanded` (bytes in the store before the asset says ready), `services/watchfolder/videoIngest.ts` (loop drops; the object removed again if the asset row cannot be created), `server.ts` (`/filler` mount), `services/fast/fillerBridge.ts` (`fillerSource` is async and takes a scratch copy on S3; an asset with no object says upload it again).
- **Terraform and settings**: `ecs_services.tf` sets `FILLER_STORE_DRIVER=s3` on the control plane; `env-coverage.json` moves `FILLER_DIR` to default and adds `FILLER_S3_BUCKET`, `FILLER_S3_PREFIX` and `FILLER_CACHE_DIR`, relabels the remaining not-on-aws-yet settings to 2.1a-2, 2.1a-3 and 2.1a-4, and explains seven settings added since 2.1 (`FAST_LAUNCHER`, the QoE nightly replay and collector peer settings, `ECS_CONTAINER_METADATA_URI_V4`); `check-terraform-env.mjs` and `terraform-env.check.ts` follow.
- **Docs**: `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` section 9 and `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` cost table say what moved and what is left. No customer guide changes: nothing in the console or API changes.
- **Checked**: device typecheck clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`). New `volt-app/test/filler-store.check.ts` **36/36** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container (an in-test S3 API: direct upload, resumable upload, watch folder loop, `/filler` served from the bucket by a second app instance, the FAST render's scratch copy, nothing left on the task's disk); with HEAD's five callers **29/36**. The 29 Oct 22:47 run also passed it against moto. Neighbours: filler-library 33/33, filler-prep 15/15, uploads 49/49, upload-retry 20/20, tus 21/21, server-status 10/10, terraform-env 54/54, eight watchfolder suites 395/395, fast-ads 48/48, fast-lineup 62/62, rights-preview 50/50. Terraform 1.9.8 in the container: `fmt -check` clean, `init -backend=false` and `validate` pass. Validated on HEAD plus this item's files only, without another session's uncommitted FAST and player work.
- **Not done**: 2.1a-2, 2.1a-3, 2.1a-4 (queued). Not run against real S3 (needs the AWS account). A chunked upload whose chunks reach two control-plane tasks cannot be assembled yet (queued in 2.1a-2).

## 2026-10-30 - Resumable uploads spread over several tasks (build queue 2.1a-2; v0.23.0)

What: on AWS the load balancer sends one upload's requests to any control-plane task, and each task kept the chunks it received on its own disk, so a chunked upload split over two tasks failed at finish ("chunk N is missing from disk") although every chunk had been acknowledged; tus had the same fault. The parts now wait in the origin bucket, so any task takes any part and any task assembles. The Mac is unchanged. Found on the way: `POST /api/tus` answered 500 on Postgres for every upload (0012 allowed only `chunk_count > 0`), so the tus endpoint had never worked outside its memory-store protocol test; fixed by migration 0119. The old 2.1a-2 was split: clip, encode and download files are 2.1a-2b, the trash 2.1a-2c.

- **Upload staging** (new `volt-app/src/services/uploadStaging.ts`): `UPLOAD_STAGING_DRIVER` `local` (default, parts under `UPLOAD_STAGING_DIR/<session>/` as before) or `s3` (chunks at `upload-staging/<session>/c/<index>.part`, tus parts at `upload-staging/<session>/t/<start>-<end>.part`; `UPLOAD_STAGING_S3_BUCKET`, `UPLOAD_STAGING_S3_PREFIX`). The database stays the authority for what arrived. The assembled file is in-flight scratch on the assembling task, as decided for 2.1a.
- **Chunk protocol** (`services/uploads.ts`): a chunk is kept in the store before it is counted; finish reads the chunks from the store; one assembler per upload (a claim on the session row; a second finish waits and answers the same result; a claim older than 15 minutes is taken over), so two finishes on two tasks no longer write one file twice; abort, the expiry sweep and completion remove the parts from the bucket too; on S3 the task that opened an upload keeps no folder for it.
- **tus** (`services/ingest/tus.ts`, `modules/tus.ts`): an optional `parts` hook on the store. With it each PATCH body lands in a scratch file, is checked (checksum extension), kept as its byte range and only then counted with a compare-and-set on `received_bytes`; a PATCH at an offset another task already took answers 409 with the current offset; at the end the parts are joined from 0 to the length, leaving out a losing request's part. Without it (the Mac) nothing changes.
- **Migration 0119**: `upload_sessions.chunk_count >= 0`, so tus, ingest rule uploads and server-side transfers (all `chunk_count = 0`) can open a session.
- **Terraform and settings**: `ecs_services.tf` sets `UPLOAD_STAGING_DRIVER=s3`; `scripts/deploy/check-terraform-env.mjs` requires that value on AWS; the origin bucket's lifecycle configuration (`s3_cloudfront.tf`) gains an `upload-staging` rule expiring forgotten parts after 8 days; `env-coverage.json` explains the two new settings, `UPLOAD_STAGING_DIR` as scratch only, and relabels `CLIPS_DIR` to 2.1a-2b and `VOLT_TRASH_DIR` to 2.1a-2c.
- **Docs**: developer guide `docs-site/docs/api/uploads.md` (chunks in any order on any server, the tus extensions and the 409 on a shared offset, the 409 while another finish assembles); `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` and `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` say what moved and what is left.
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `volt-app/test/upload-staging.check.ts` **42/42** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container, with two real API processes sharing the database and an in-test S3 API but each with its own disks: the fault shown with the local driver (finish on B refused), the Mac path unchanged, chunks to A, B, A assembled on B and landed whole in the bucket, a chunk retried on the other task counted once, two finishes at once (both 200, one file), abort and expiry clearing the bucket, tus creating on Postgres, tus PATCHes alternating between A and B with a bad checksum refused and a race at one offset (one 204, one 409), the joined file whole, termination on the other task. Same suite against moto (`moto_server`) **42/42**. On HEAD's code: **12 passed, 21 failed**. Neighbours: uploads 49/49, upload-retry 20/20, tus 21/21, filler-store 36/36, filler-library 33/33, filler-prep 15/15, server-status 10/10, terraform-env 57/57, ingest-core 97/97, ingest-extract 7/7, ingest-providers 21/21, ingest-s3 16/16, ingest-sources 22/22, watchfolder-ingest 70/70 (15/15 suites, 516 assertions); engineering-guides 35/35 and docs-coverage 311/311 on the device; `terraform fmt -check` clean, `terraform validate` clean, `terraform test` 6/6.
- **Not done**: 2.1a-2b, 2.1a-2c, 2.1a-3, 2.1a-4 (queued). An upload that finishes on one task still leaves its VOD source on that task's disk (`CLIPS_DIR/vod-src`), so its encode must run there until 2.1a-2b. Not run against real S3 (needs the AWS account).

## 2026-10-31 - Originals of uploads and watch folder drops in the bucket (build queue 2.1a-2b; v0.24.0)

What: on AWS the control plane is several tasks sharing one job queue. An upload's original stayed on the disk of the task that finished it, and `vod_assets.source_key` was that path, so when another task's worker took the encode it failed with "the source file is not there", and a replaced task lost every original it held. Originals now land in the origin bucket once the ingest rules have named them, and any task fetches a private scratch copy for the length of a job. The Mac is unchanged. The old 2.1a-2b was split: clip, encode, ad conditioning and download files are now 2.1a-2b-2. Found on the way: the CDN's default behaviour could read any key in the origin bucket, so an original (an ingest rule can give it a readable, guessable name) or an upload part in flight was fetchable through CloudFront.

- **The store** (new `volt-app/src/services/media/vodSource.ts`): `VOD_SOURCE_DRIVER` `local` (default, the original stays under `CLIPS_DIR/vod-src/` as before) or `s3` (the original and its sidecar at `vod-src/<rule's name>` in the origin bucket; `VOD_SOURCE_S3_BUCKET`, `VOD_SOURCE_S3_PREFIX`). `fetchVodSource` gives a local file for a key: a local path as it is, a bucket original copied into `VOD_SOURCE_CACHE_DIR/use-*/` (written beside and renamed) and removed on release; copies are per job, so two jobs on one task never share one. Only keys under `vod-src/` with plain segments are fetched or deleted.
- **Where it lands**: one place, the end of `ingestLanded` (`services/ingest/pipeline.ts`), so the chunk protocol, tus and watch folder drops all land; held duplicates too (they can be kept and encoded later), rejected files stay quarantined where they are. If the ingest itself throws, `services/uploads.ts` still lands the file. If the bucket refuses, the file stays on the task with a warning and its encode still runs there.
- **Readers**: the VOD encode (`jobs/handlers/vodEncode.ts`), the FAST ladder job (`vodLadder.ts`) and frame and sprite builds (`media/assetImage.ts` `prepareFileFrames`) take a scratch copy per job; FAST readiness (`fast/prepare.ts`) counts a bucket original as present. Deleting: skipping a held duplicate and replacing a failed record's original remove the object and its sidecar.
- **Terraform and settings**: `ecs_services.tf` sets `VOD_SOURCE_DRIVER=s3`; `scripts/deploy/check-terraform-env.mjs` requires it on AWS; `env-coverage.json` adds the three new settings and relabels `CLIPS_DIR` to 2.1a-2b-2. `s3_cloudfront.tf`: the origin bucket policy keeps the CloudFront read and adds a deny for `vod-src/*` and `upload-staging/*`.
- **Docs**: `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` section 9 and `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` say what moved and what is left. No customer guide changes: nothing in the console or API changes.
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `volt-app/test/vod-source-store.check.ts` **30/30** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container, with a real API process (task A) and this process as task B, each with its own disks, an in-test S3 API and real ffmpeg videos: the fault with the local driver (A replaced, B's encode: "the source file is not there"), the s3 driver (source_key is the s3:// address, the object is the whole upload, the queued encode names it, A keeps nothing, B fetches a whole scratch copy and gives it back, B's encode gets past the source, no scratch left), a held duplicate landed and its skip removing object and sidecar, frames built on B from the bucket, the Mac path unchanged. Same suite against moto (`moto_server`) **30/30**. On HEAD's code: **21 passed, 9 failed**. Neighbours: uploads 49/49, upload-staging 42/42, upload-retry 20/20, tus 21/21, filler-store 36/36, filler-library 33/33, filler-prep 15/15, fast-ladder 54/54, asset-readiness 25/25, asset-image-types 51/51, sprite-store 31/31, vod-library 70/70, vod-markers 40/40, ingest-core 97/97, ingest-extract 7/7, ingest-providers 21/21, ingest-s3 16/16, ingest-sources 22/22, eight watchfolder suites 395/395, server-status 10/10, terraform-env 59/59, engineering-guides 35/35, docs-coverage 311/311. asset-images 106/114 and vod-library-pure 169/170 fail identically on HEAD's versions of this item's files (a song cover approval, and content types that another session is changing). Terraform 1.9.8 in the container: `fmt -check` clean, `init -backend=false` and `validate` pass, `terraform test` 6/6. The copy validated included another session's uncommitted work in other files; none of it is committed here.
- **Not done**: the full encode on a second task was not run here (no packager in the container); the suite runs it when `PACKAGER_BIN` works, so on the Mac: `cd volt-app && PACKAGER_BIN=../packager-osx-arm64/packager DATABASE_URL=... node scripts/check.js vod-source-store`. `services/fast/store.ts` `sourceOnServer` still reads a bucket original as not on the server (that file carries another session's uncommitted work; queued in 2.1a-2b-2). A deleted asset's bucket original stays in the bucket until the bin moves there (2.1a-2c). Not run against real S3 (needs the AWS account).

## 2026-11-02 - Clip and encode files and downloads served from any task (build queue 2.1a-2b-2; v0.25.0)

What: on AWS a clip is cut and a VOD encode packaged on whichever control-plane task ran the job, and both are copied to the origin bucket as they finish (`clips/<id>/` by clip.extract, `vod/<id>/` by `services/fast/vodStore.ts`). But `/clips` and the downloads were answered from the task's own disk, so a clip played only when the load balancer happened to send the viewer to the task that cut it, and a replaced task took the only servable copy with it. Now a task that does not hold a file reads it from the bucket copy. The Mac is unchanged (nothing is copied to a bucket there, so nothing is looked up). The old 2.1a-2b-2 was split: ad conditioning work, the FAST render of a slate or loop, caption track files and FAST's `sourceOnServer` are now 2.1a-2b-3. Found on the way: every encode's MP4 download on AWS answered "That file is no longer here" (on S3 its key is the bucket key `vod/<id>/<file>`, which the download route did not recognise); and `express.static` on `CLIPS_DIR` served `/clips/vod-src/<asset>.<ext>`, the original of any upload or watch folder drop, to anyone who knew the asset id (on the Mac too), and the encoder's probe scratch under `/clips/vod/_probe`.

- **The read side** (new `volt-app/src/services/media/clipsStore.ts`): `/clips/<asset>/<file>` maps to `clips/<asset>/<file>` when clips go to the bucket (`MEDIA_STORE_DRIVER=s3`, not `CLIPS_S3_UPLOAD=false`); `/clips/vod/<asset>/<file>` maps to `vod/<asset>/<file>` when `VOD_STORE` resolves to s3. Only plain path segments under an asset uuid are mapped, so nothing else in the bucket (originals, upload parts) can be reached.
- **Serving** (`volt-app/src/server.ts`): the playlist route reads a playlist from the bucket when it is not on disk and still writes in the skip markers and caption tracks; after `express.static`, a read-through streams any other file from the bucket with byte ranges passed through (206 and Content-Range, so MP4 seeking works) and the same long cache as before. A guard in front answers 404 for `vod-src/` and `vod/_*` under `/clips`.
- **Downloads** (`volt-app/src/modules/downloads.ts`): `locate` knows an encode's bucket key; `resolveBytes` prefers the task's disk and falls back to the bucket copy; the list shows sizes from the bucket (HeadObject); the file route streams from the bucket with the asset's title as the file name.
- **Settings and docs**: `env-coverage.json` keeps `CLIPS_DIR` as not-on-aws-yet with item 2.1a-2b-3 and says what moved; `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` section 9 and `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` updated. No customer guide changes: nothing in the console or API changes shape; the visible effects are fixes (downloads and clips on AWS, originals no longer reachable).
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `volt-app/test/clips-store.check.ts` **28/28** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container: this process is task A, which writes a clip and an encode and uploads them with the real `uploadTree` and `uploadVodDir`; task B, an API process with an empty disk on an S3 deployment, serves the clip's playlist, segment and an MP4 byte range, the encode's master, MPD and segments, lists and downloads the clip's MP4 and MP3 and the encode's MP4; task A serves its own copy from disk without touching the bucket; task C (the Mac, local stores) does not serve an original or the probe scratch and looks nothing up in a bucket. On HEAD's server and download code the same suite is **15/28** (every read-through and download fails, and the originals answer 200). Neighbours: clip-extract 33/33, clip-provenance ok, clip-smart-render 35/35, companion-files 64/64, downloads 15/15, scheduled-clips 15/15, server-status 10/10, share-links 34/34, vod-markers 40/40, vod-source-store 30/30, terraform-env 59/59. vod-library 69/70 (its `vod_encoder.py` probe check needs volt-encoder, not in the container copy) and vod-library-pure 169/170 (on-demand content types, under another session's uncommitted work, the same on HEAD).
- **Not done**: 2.1a-2b-3 (queued). Segments and MP4s read through stream via the task; for heavy clip playback the CDN would be cheaper (encodes on S3 already play from the CDN because their master key is a bucket key). On AWS the jobs that make these files still fail until they run as their own tasks (2.1a-4: no ffmpeg in the control-plane image). Not run against real S3 (needs the AWS account).

## 2026-11-02 - Ad creatives, FAST renders and caption clocks off the task's disk (build queue 2.1a-2b-3; v0.26.0)

What: on AWS the control plane is several tasks sharing one job queue, and three things still lived only on the disk of the task that started them. An ad creative was downloaded to `CLIPS_DIR/ads/<creative>/source.mp4` and the vod.encode job was given that path, so an encode taken by another task's worker failed with "the source file is not there". The FAST render of a slate or loop was written to `FILLER_DIR/<account>/<id>.fast.mp4` and became the library asset's `source_key`, so the channel's prepare failed the same way on any other task. And a caption track's X-TIMESTAMP-MAP was read from the asset's master, variant, init and first segment in `CLIPS_DIR` only, so on a task that did not hold them the cues went out without the map and drifted from the picture. The Mac is unchanged. `sourceOnServer` in `services/fast/store.ts` was left (that file carries another session's uncommitted work) and moved to the new 2.1a-2b-4.

- **Ad creatives** (`volt-app/src/services/jobs/handlers/adCondition.ts`): with `VOD_SOURCE_DRIVER=s3` the download is assembled under `CLIPS_DIR/vod-src/ads/<creative>/` and landed with `landVodSource` at `vod-src/ads/<creative>/source.mp4` (its own key per creative; a bare basename would have given every creative the same key), the local copy removed. The asset's `source_key` is the s3:// key, so removing the asset removes it, and the encode job gets the key and takes a scratch copy (`fetchVodSource`).
- **FAST renders** (`volt-app/src/services/fast/fillerBridge.ts`): on S3 the render goes to `vod-src/fast-filler/<account>/<id>.fast.mp4` the same way; `vodSourceAvailable` counts it for the prepare.
- **Caption clocks** (`volt-app/src/services/companions/serve.ts`): `readClipsFile` reads a path under /clips from the task's disk, then from the bucket copy through `clipsStore.readThroughKey` (clip and encode prefixes only, never `vod-src/`, paths normalised and kept inside `CLIPS_DIR`); `clipsPathOfMaster` understands an encode's bare bucket master key `vod/<id>/master.m3u8`; `timestampMapFor` is the old reader on top of it. `clipsStore.readObjectBuffer` added for binary reads.
- **Settings and docs**: `env-coverage.json` makes `CLIPS_DIR` class default (working space only); `terraform-env.check.ts` checks that; `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` section 9 and `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` updated. No customer guide changes: nothing in the console or API changes shape; the visible effects are fixes on a cloud deployment (change notes for FAST and VOD).
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `volt-app/test/clips-off-disk.check.ts` **29/29** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container, with real ffmpeg and the real AWS SDK against an in-test S3 server: two creatives downloaded over HTTP land under their own bucket keys with the right bytes, nothing left on disk, the encode job carries the key and a scratch copy has the same bytes and is removed after; a slate rendered by ffmpeg lands in the bucket as an MP4, is counted as available and shared by a second use; on the local drivers both behave as before and nothing goes to a bucket; an encode and a clip held only in the bucket give the right X-TIMESTAMP-MAP, originals and paths outside `CLIPS_DIR` are never read; a second API process with an empty disk serves the track with the map from the bucket (and the DASH form without one). On HEAD's code the suite fails 10 of its first 15 checks and stops at the caption checks. Neighbours: clips-store 28/28, companion-files 64/64, terraform-env 58/58, vod-source-store 30/30, fast-ads 48/48, filler-prep 15/15, filler-store 36/36, filler-library 33/33, downloads 15/15, vod-ads-vmap 56/56, edge-variant-manifests 62/62. vod-ads did not run here (it imports volt-player, which was not in the container copy).
- **Not done**: 2.1a-2b-4 (queued): FAST's `sourceOnServer`, and a gap found here: on S3 encodes play from the CDN and caption tracks are only written into masters by the API's /clips route, so CDN-played encodes have no caption tracks on AWS. The suite ran against the in-test bucket, not moto (not installed in the container): `UPLOAD_S3_TEST_ENDPOINT=<moto url> node scripts/check.js clips-off-disk` runs it there. On AWS the jobs themselves still need ffmpeg (2.1a-4). Not run against real S3 (needs the AWS account).

## 2026-11-02 - Caption tracks on CDN-played encodes (build queue 2.1a-2b-4; v0.26.1)

What: on an S3 deployment an encode's `master_key` is the bucket key `vod/<id>/master.m3u8` and players fetch it from the CDN, so the API's /clips playlist route, which writes caption tracks in as it answers, was never asked: encodes played on AWS had no subtitle tracks. Part (a) of the item, FAST's `sourceOnServer`, was split to 2.1a-2b-5 (`services/fast/store.ts` still carries another session's uncommitted work, last edited 30 Oct).

- **Choice**: write the tracks into the bucket copy rather than answer masters through the API. Tracks change rarely while masters are asked for on every play, so every request stays on the CDN with no control-plane load or latency per viewer start; the cost is up to a minute (the playlists' `max-age=60`) before a change reaches viewers.
- **Sync** (`volt-app/src/services/companions/bucketTracks.ts`): `syncBucketTracks(assetId)` acts only when the asset's `master_key` is its own `vod/<id>/master.m3u8`. It reads the bucket master and MPD, puts `subs/<file>.dash.vtt`, `subs/<file>.vtt` (with the X-TIMESTAMP-MAP of the encode's first segment, read through the bucket by `timestampMapFor`) and `subs/<file>.m3u8` for each enabled track, then the master and MPD with the tracks (or without any), and removes the three copies of every disabled or deleted track. One sync at a time per asset in a task, each reading the tracks fresh; fails open (logged, never thrown at the caller); invalidates the asset's cached tracks.
- **Wired** in `companions/store.ts` after every add, change and delete of a caption file, and in `jobs/handlers/vodEncode.ts` after an encode lands in the bucket and `master_key` names it (its new master has no tracks). The Mac and clips (`/clips` masters) are untouched.
- **The /clips route** (`companions/serve.ts` `withTracks`): with no track left it now strips a stale listing (`delivery.ts` `withoutHlsSubtitles`, leaving other SUBTITLES groups), so a bucket master whose last sync failed is still served right through the API. `activeTracks` is the uncached query both paths use.
- **Docs**: `volt-app/docs/COMPANION_FILES.md` section 4 says how CDN-served encodes get their tracks and why. No customer guide covers caption files on assets, and nothing in the console or API changes shape; the visible effect is a fix on a cloud deployment (change note for VOD).
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). ## 2026-11-02 - Frame index and exact frames (build queue 3.2 (a) and (b); v0.27.0)

What: frame-accurate access in the product, first half. Until now an exact frame meant cutting a clip; the console's stills came from sprites and keyframes. The item allowed two runs; (c) frame stepping and timecode in the console, (d) smart render on and the rest of (e) are 3.2-2.

- **Frame counts from the boxes** (`volt-app/src/services/frames/fmp4.ts`): every segment Volt writes is CMAF, and its moof already lists every sample with its duration, composition offset and sync flag. Reading tfhd, tfdt and trun counts a segment's frames and gives their presentation times with no ffprobe and no decode (well under a millisecond, against about 40 ms for an ffprobe). MPEG-TS segments (older clips) fall back to one ffprobe packet scan (`exactFrame.ts` `probeTsSegment`).
- **The index** (`frameIndex.ts`, pure): the tallest video variant of the asset's master (frame timing is the same in every rung; audio-only variants skipped), per segment its first frame and frame count, the rate from the commonest sample duration reduced to a fraction, `locateFrame` by binary search, `frameAtMs`, and `HH:MM:SS:FF` timecode, drop-frame (`;`) at 29.97 and 59.94 by SMPTE 12M, with the inverse refusing dropped numbers. Counts come from the segments, never from EXTINF times the rate, which drifts by a frame.
- **Stored per asset** in `asset_frame_indexes` (migration `0121_frame_index.sql`; a table of its own because library lists select `v.*`, cascades with the asset), states ready, none (no HLS yet, or audio only) and failed. Made by the new `frames.index` job (`jobs/handlers/frameIndex.ts`), queued after every `clip.extract` and `vod.encode`, by the API when an asset is asked for and has none, and by a leader sweep every 10 minutes for older media (`index.ts`).
- **Recordings have no stored index, by choice:** the per-run scan (`localRecordingIndex.ts`) already places each segment on the wall clock from its tfdt, so the frame at a moment is that one segment's boxes, read on request. A second, stored index of a 24x7 channel (21,600 segments a day) would be writes for nothing.
- **Exact frame** (`exactFrame.ts` `extractFrame`): only the segment holding the frame is read (with its init), decoded forward from its keyframe, and the k-th picture in presentation order kept; a segment that does not open on a keyframe is refused rather than answered with a neighbour. The JPEG (q2, source size) is kept in the image store under `derived/<account>/frames/` (`store.ts`): asset stills under the index build time, so a re-encode never serves a frame of the old file; made once even when several requests arrive together; recorded in `frame_stills` and pruned by the sweep after `FRAME_STILL_TTL_DAYS` (default 7). Every width comes from the signed image service and is cached there.
- **API** (`modules/frames.ts`): `GET /api/vod/{id}/frame-index` (rate, frame count, first and last timecode; `build=true`), `GET /api/vod/{id}/frame` (`n`, `tc` or `ms`; `w`; `redirect=true`), `GET /api/recordings/{id}/frame` (`n` or `ms` from the start, or wall clock `at`). Under the vod and recordings prefixes so the permission map gives Media library: view and Recordings: view. 409 while an index is being made or when an asset has no video, 404 outside the media or in a capture gap.
- **Docs**: API reference wording in `openapi-overlay.json` (routes and OpenAPI regenerated), a developer recipe "Show the exact frame at a timecode" in `docs-site/docs/api/recipes.md`. No console change in this half, so no user guide change; 3.2-2 brings the console and its guides.
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `volt-app/test/frames.check.ts` **71/71** through `scripts/check.js` on Postgres 16 in the container, on media made by ffmpeg in the test: a B-frame CMAF asset indexes to 500 frames at 25/1 in 10 segments, frame 137 is segment 2 frame 37 at 5480 ms (prototype assertions 1 to 5); frames 0, 49, 50, 137 and 499 equal a ground-truth decode of the source (MAD 0.16) and differ from their neighbours (6 to 8); through the image service at 320 px still frame 137; MPEG-TS; a 29.97 asset with segments of uneven length counted exactly, both sides of three boundaries matching; audio only gives none; drop-frame timecode cases and round trips; the API (409 while indexing, 404 outside, 400s, 302, another account 404, no sign-in 401, permission map); the job and the sweep; a recording's frame across a segment boundary on the wall clock, matching ground truth; stills made once under concurrency, pruned after the TTL, and made again after a rebuild. Neighbours: clip-extract 33/33, clip-provenance ok, clip-smart-render 35/35 (34/35 on a second pass; the same case fails the same on HEAD), clips-off-disk 29/29, clips-store 28/28, companion-files 64/64, docs.check ok, encode-store 24/24, images-api 22/22, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, recordings 35/35, recordings-counts 29/29. asset-images 106/114, images 144/145, docs-coverage and engineering-guides fail the same on HEAD in the container (volt-web and the other sibling folders were not copied).
- **Not done**: 3.2-2 (console frame stepping and timecode, smart render on, the exact-clip assertions). Recording stills read the capture from the task's disk, as clipping does, until 2.2 moves runs to per-run S3 prefixes. Not run on the Mac with a real encode: `node scripts/check.js frames` there needs only ffmpeg and Postgres.

## 2026-11-02 - Frame stills: where they are kept, and the clean-up (build queue 3.2 follow-up; v0.28.0)

What: Christian asked that frame stills keep a record of the S3 bucket and folder they are stored in, so the clean-up picks them up, and that they come under retention rules. Before this a still was only a key in `frame_stills` with a TTL; nothing else in Volt knew the folders existed.

- **Recorded twice** (`volt-app/src/services/frames/folders.ts`, migration `0122_frame_still_locations.sql`): each `frame_stills` row now has `bucket` (the image store's bucket, NULL on a local store), `folder`, `bytes` and `media_object_id`; and each folder (one per asset index version, one per capture run and rendition) is a media object of kind `frame_stills` whose location is the folder's full address, `s3://<bucket>/<IMAGE_S3_PREFIX>/derived/<account>/frames/.../` (trailing slash, so a delete takes that tree only) or the directory on disk. Registered before the first still is written; sized as the sum of its stills; listed on the Storage page.
- **Retention**: (1) `FRAME_STILL_TTL_DAYS` (7) per still, as before; (2) the owner's rule, delete rung only (`retention.ts` skips cold and archive rungs for stills: moving them costs more than making them again), with the still rows removed with the folder; (3) a run removed by retention, a date set by hand or `prune-recordings.ts` takes its frame folders at once, scoped to its stream (`prune.ts`); (4) an asset's folder is owned by the asset alone, so deleting the asset leaves it ownerless and the sweep reclaims it after the 24 h grace; (5) rebuilding an asset's index retires older versions' folders. A still for a folder retention already removed is refused (410), so nothing is written outside the clean-up.
- **Docs**: `volt-app/docs/RETENTION.md` (new section: folders, records, the five rules), the Storage guide (stills follow the Delete rung, the 7 days, listed as frames/...), the `kind` filter of `GET /api/storage/objects`.
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean. `frames.check.ts` **81/81** (10 new): a still's row has its folder, size and object, bucket empty locally; the folder object is kind frame_stills, owned by the asset alone, located at the folder and sized as its stills; on S3 the address is the bucket and prefix with the trailing slash; a recording still's folder is owned by the channel and keyed by run and rendition; removing a second run takes its stills (folder, object, rows) and leaves the first run's; the channel's rule (delete after a day) deletes a two-day-old folder; a later still there is refused 410; a deleted asset's stills are reclaimed as ownerless; a rebuild leaves one live folder. Neighbours: media-lifecycle 121/121, s3Retention 8/8, clip-extract 33/33, clips-store 28/28, recordings 35/35, docs.check ok. retention-bin 39/41 fails the same two sprite-sheet assertions on the copy without this change.
- **Found, not changed**: uploaded and watch folder assets have no channel, so the sweep finds no account for their media objects and no account rule reaches them (their clip outputs included, not only stills); their stills still go by the TTL, a rebuild and the asset's deletion. Worth a queue item if upload media should follow the account's ladder. Not run against real S3: the S3 path is the existing S3 driver's prefix delete.

## 2026-11-03 - Smart render on, every frame exact (build queue 3.2-2 (d) and (e); v0.29.0)

What: Christian's rule for smart render was "make it the default only if every frame matches". A new suite now decodes every picture of six clips and checks each against the source; it found off-by-one frames in both renders, which are fixed, and smart render is on.

- **Default** (`volt-app/src/services/clips/extract.ts`): `req.smartRender !== false`. Clips copy the whole middle segments and re-encode only the head and tail. The full render is the fallback for every refusal or failure; `strategyPerBlock` and `smartRenderSkipped` say which ran.
- **Fixes, both renders:** the Out frame is counted (`-frames:v` from `frameAt`, `-to` half a frame late only as a bound), since `-to` judges a frame by where it ends; the In cut is half a frame early (`frameCutMs`), right at 29.97 where frames start at fractions of a ms; `Probe.videoOffsetMs` measures cuts from the picture start when audio is muxed ahead of it; full render `-fps_mode passthrough` (it repeated the In frame); smart join `apad` so `-shortest` no longer drops the last two frames; one probe per run shared by both paths.
- **Suite** `test/smart-render-frames.check.ts` (no database, ffmpeg only): 25, 50 and 29.97 fps, B-frames, a head on a segment boundary, the prototype's 76-frame clip (full render as planned) and a muxed audio and video source.
- **Docs**: Recordings guide, Cut a clip: clips start and end on the marked frames, only the ends re-encoded. Three change notes.
- **Checked**: device typechecks clean (volt-app, volt-web). Container, Postgres 16: smart-render-frames 62/62, clip-smart-render 35/35, clip-extract 33/33, clips-off-disk 29/29, clips-store 28/28, frames 81/81, scheduled-clips 15/15, clip-provenance ok, recordings 35/35, recordings-counts 29/29, recordings-sources 14/14, jobs 43/43. Real recordings on the Mac: the 1080p rendition is video-only, 25/1 with B-frames, the shape the main sources use.
- **Not done**: (c) frame stepping and timecode in the console, now 3.2-3. Not run on the Mac against a real recording clip end to end: `node scripts/check.js smart-render-frames` there and one clip from the workbench would confirm it.

- **Shared** (`volt-web/src/frames.ts`, `FrameStill.tsx`, `index.css` .frame-*): timecode, frame and time maths copied from `services/frames/frameIndex.ts` (drop-frame at 29.97 and 59.94), the frame calls, the still overlay (clicks pass through) and the small mark stills.
- **Media Library** (`MediaPreview.tsx`, given `assetId` by `Vod.tsx`): timecode and frame of total under the video; paused, the arrows step a frame (capture phase, ahead of the player's 10 s jump). The index is asked for again every 5 s while it is queued. Loops' previews pass no asset and stay plain.
- **Recordings workbench** (`RecordingDetail.tsx`): paused arrows step a frame, `<` `>` pause and step, TC beside Position (from the recording's start), I and O on the exact frame (Out frame included), exact stills of the first and last frame under the marks (debounced). Keys moved to the capture phase so the player's own space and arrow handling no longer runs a second time.

In the container on a fresh docs seed, browser check 15/15 (steps, segment boundary, fast presses, In and Out stills, TC, play clears the still, queued index); screenshots 4/4, looked at; docs-coverage 315/315; coverage and scrub clean; openapi up to date on the device. The container's Chromium has no H.264, so the player itself shows a decode error there; the stills come from the server and are unaffected.
