## 2026-11-05 - Hybrid Sites 3a: the job lease (build queue H3, first part; v0.53.0)

What: work can now be sent to a Hybrid Site and run there. A job queued with a site's pool waits for that site; an approved server of the site leases it over HTTPS, reports progress, completes or fails it, and moves its files through URLs made for one job and one file. This is the remote half of the job queue that Mirror and Archive (H3-2) and site encoding (H4) run on. H3 was split: Mirror and Archive is H3-2.

- **Where**: migration `0132_job_pools.sql` (`jobs.pool`, add only); `volt-app/src/services/jobs/queue.ts` (`sitePool`, `enqueue({ pool })`, `claim` now `pool IS NULL`, new `claimInPool`, and `heartbeat`, `succeed`, `fail` optionally conditional on the lease holder, answering whether they applied); `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteJobs.ts` (new: lease, progress, complete, fail, transfer URLs, signed transfers, S3 presigning); routes in `modules/sites.ts` (`/api/site-agent/jobs/...`, `/api/site-agent/transfers/:token` mounted before the JSON parser in `server.ts`); `siteConfig` lists the host's pool.
- **How it works**: each lease id is `site-host:<host id>:<random>`, stored as `claimed_by`; every later call names it and only the current holder is heard (409 `lease_lost` otherwise, also for a cancelled or finished job). Another site's or account's job answers 404 like an unknown id. Expiry is the queue's own (120 s without progress, then the reaper). A job lists its transfers in the payload (`name`, `direction`, `uri`, optional `bytes` and `sha256`); the host sees names and sizes only. `s3://` objects get a SigV4 presigned GET or PUT (15 min, one key, a prefix refused), so copies never pass through the control plane; files on the control plane's disk get an HMAC-signed API URL checked against the lease on every use, uploads written to a part file and renamed in only when whole and matching the expected SHA-256.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/site-jobs.check.ts` 69/69 in the container (fails at HEAD: the lease service and routes are new), including an S3 round trip through moto (presigned GET and PUT). Around it: jobs 43/43, sites 88/88, site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, workflows 72/72, workflow-report 43/43, workflow-fixes 27/27, workflow-policy ok, migration-safety 60/60; docs-coverage 323/323 on the Mac.
- **Not here**: no site job type exists yet, so nothing in the product enqueues site work; the suite uses a test type. The agent does not lease yet (H3-2 adds it with the copy jobs).
- **Not done**: Mirror and Archive (H3-2). The agent's credential still needs the `hybrid_sites` licence; Mirror and Archive is sold separately, so H3-2 lets `mirror_archive` alone open the agent routes it needs.


### R1 Rights profiles (6 Nov 2026, v0.54.0)

Every asset carries one rights profile: territories, apps, uses, access types and window. Rights are the cap: collection access and publishing policies decide inside them, and the play gate, publishing and FAST refuse anything outside them. Design: Publishing Policies doc, "Rights"; screens: design link `rights-screens` (design-links.local.md).

- **Where**: migrations `0133_rights_profiles.sql` and `0134_rights_profile_index.sql`; `volt-app/src/services/rightsProfiles.ts` (pure evaluation, narrowing, cached reads); `modules/rights.ts` (`/api/rights`); the cap in `services/availability.ts` (`onDemandAccess`, `publish`), `services/rights.ts` (layer `rights`), `services/fast/store.ts` (`libraryFor`), `modules/apps.ts` (app type and `surface`); assignment in `services/uploads.ts`, `modules/tus.ts`, `services/watchfolder/videoIngest.ts`; permission `rights` in `auth/roles.ts`. Console: `volt-web/src/pages/Rights.tsx`, `src/rights/` (API client, icons, asset Rights tab), upload picker in `IngestPanel.tsx`, Used for in `pages/ApiKeys.tsx`. Guide `docs-site/docs/guides/rights.md`; docs seed adds three provider profiles.
- **How it works**: a BEFORE INSERT trigger fills `vod_assets.rights_profile_id` from the account default, so every insert path (and the previous release during a rolling deploy) gets a profile; existing assets were backfilled to No restrictions. A title's narrowing only takes away (the API refuses wider with the reasons). The gate reads the asset row and the profile from cache (tags `asset:<id>`, `rights:<id>`), so a warm gate still makes no database reads; saving a profile drops its tag.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device. `test/rights-profiles.check.ts` 75/75 (pure evaluation, built-ins, trigger, backfill, CRUD and refusals, narrowing, gate 451 by territory, app type, embed and venue surface, unknown country, access type and paywall products, publish refused and clamped, FAST library, bulk, agreement privacy and audit, permissions, watchfolder and upload assignment). Full run 263/302 in the container; the other 39 fail the same on HEAD. Browser check of the Rights list, a profile, the asset Rights tab and the app form; screenshots retaken by `scripts/screenshots.mjs` on the docs seed.
- **Not done**: filename rules and ingest rules naming a profile; enforcing clips and ads uses where clips are cut and ads inserted; catalogue and search filtering by territory and app (P1 decision records); territory groups; expiry warnings to rights admins.

## 2026-11-06 - Mirror and Archive, first part (build queue H3-2, first part; v0.55.0)

What: the Mirror and Archive add-on can keep a verified copy of an asset's original on storage the customer owns at a site. An administrator names a storage target (a folder on the site's server or a mounted NAS share, with an optional bandwidth cap) and asks for a copy; an approved server of the site leases the job, downloads the original through a URL scoped to that one file, writes it into the folder, reads it back and reports that checksum, and the cloud counts the copy only when it matches the original's. A re-check reads the copy again and finds it verified, corrupt or missing. H3-2 was split: copy rules, object-store targets, consolidated recordings, the copy-before-delete rung, scheduled re-checks, restore and the console are H3-3.

- **Where**: migration `0135_mirror_archive.sql` (two new tables, add only); `volt-app/src/services/archive/archive.ts` (targets, copies, enqueue, `judge` and `attemptFailed`) and `naming.ts` (names, shared with the agent); `volt-app/src/modules/archive.ts` (routes, mounted at `/api/archive` in `server.ts`); `services/sites/licences.ts` (`hasSiteLicence`, `requireSiteLicence`), `sites.ts` (either licence for enrolment and the credential, principal carries licences, config `addOns`, watchfolders only with Hybrid Sites), `siteJobs.ts` (licence-aware lease, archive results judged before `succeed`, failures recorded on the copy), `jobs/queue.ts` (`claimInPool` exclude list), `modules/sites.ts` and `siteTus.ts` (watchfolders and uploads need `hybrid_sites`); `volt-agent/src/siteArchive.ts` (new) and `site.ts` (the archive loop; agent 0.5.0); API docs regenerated (overlay entries and an Archive tag); guide `docs-site/docs/guides/hybrid-sites.md` (Keep archive copies at the site, troubleshooting, API); `HYBRID_SITES.md` Phase 3b.
- **How it works**: one copy per target, asset and `what` (the original for now); asking again returns a queued or verified copy and copies a failed, corrupt or missing one afresh on the same record. The job carries the target folder and one download transfer with the original's size and the checksum its upload was checked against. The agent writes `<name>.part-<random>`, keeps the cap, reports progress every 10 s, renames in under a free name (`-2`, `-3`), reads the file back, and removes it if the cloud refuses it. The cloud refuses a result whose read-back checksum differs from the original's or the download's, whose size differs, or whose path leaves the folder; a refusal is a failed attempt, retried with the queue's backoff and `failed` when spent.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web, volt-agent with volt-app's Node types). New `test/mirror-archive.check.ts` 72/72 in the container (Postgres 16), running the real agent pass against the real API with a temporary folder as the NAS: names for EPG and hourly recording files in the channel's zone, licences (Mirror and Archive alone opens sites and the lease but not watchfolders or uploads; Hybrid Sites alone never gets archive work and cannot open `/api/archive`; neither: 403), targets (bad paths, other accounts, duplicates, audit), a copy end to end, a clash to `-2`, the 4 Mbit/s cap measured, a wrong checksum refused, removed at the target, retried and then failed, re-checks finding verified, corrupt and missing, a fresh copy written beside a damaged one, an unmounted folder retried without making one, a path outside the target refused, lists by asset and state, a disabled target, the licence withdrawn. Around it: site-jobs 67/67 (69 on the Mac; the 2 moto assertions need MOTO_URL), sites 88/88, site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, agent 19/19, access-rules 28/28, retention-bin 41/41, s3Retention 8/8, watchfolder-api 26/26, watchfolder-scan 33/33, listQuery ok, listStandard 35 list endpoints / 3197 requests (the two new lists among them), migration-safety 60/60; docs-coverage 327/327 on the device.
- **Not here**: the run on the Mac with MinIO in Docker and a real agent process (no Docker or second machine in the scheduled run). Christian: start the API (`PORT=8080 npx tsx src/index.ts`), give an account `mirror_archive`, enrol `npx tsx ../volt-agent/src/cli.ts site enrol` and `site run`, make a target on a folder or mounted share, and `POST /api/archive/copies` for an uploaded asset; the folder should get the file and the copy show `verified`.
- **Not done (H3-3)**: copy rules (what, when, mirror or archive, off-peak window), on-prem S3-compatible targets (MinIO), consolidated recordings from the top rendition with a sidecar (the names are built), the copy-before-delete retention rung, scheduled re-checks, restore to the cloud, and the console (Sites for a Mirror and Archive-only account, its switch in System Admin, targets and copies on the Storage page).

## 2026-11-06 - Mirror and Archive: copy rules and scheduled re-checks (build queue H3-3, first part; v0.56.0)

What: an account with Mirror and Archive can now say in its publishing rules which storage targets an asset's original is copied to, and when: as soon as it is ingested, when it is published, or both, optionally only inside an off-peak window in a time zone of its choice. The most specific level of the ladder that says anything about copies decides (account, content type, channel or event, the asset itself). Every storage target also re-checks its verified copies on a cadence (30 days unless set otherwise, or never), so a copy edited or removed on the NAS is found without anyone asking. H3-3 was split: more copy rules, S3-compatible targets, recordings and the console are H3-4; the copy-before-delete rung and restore are H3-5, which needs a decision (below).

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/publishingRules.ts` (`archive` in `RuleSettings`, `RULE_KEYS` and the baseline); `volt-app/src/modules/publishingRules.ts` (schema and `cleanArchive`: licence, the account's own targets, each once, a real zone, equal ends mean no window); `volt-app/src/services/archive/archive.ts` (`recheckDays` on targets, `requestCopy` with `runAfter`, `recheckDue`, `offPeakStart`, `copyOnRule`); `services/ingest/pipeline.ts` (copy rules after the original has landed, not for rejected files or skipped duplicates); `services/availability.ts` (copy rules after a publish); `services/orchestrator/index.ts` (the re-check pass every 30 minutes); migration `0136_archive_rechecks.sql` (one column with a default; add only); API docs (overlay for targets, publishing rules and the Archive tag; `openapi.json` regenerated); guides `hybrid-sites.md` (Copy by rule, Re-checks on a schedule, API) and `publishing-rules.md` (API).
- **How it works**: a rule asks `requestCopy` for each target named for the moment, so an original already copied or waiting is not copied again, and a failed, corrupt or missing one is copied afresh. Outside the window the job is queued with `run_after` at the window's next opening, and the lease does not hand it out before then. A disabled target or site, or an asset with no original, is skipped with the reason in the log; nothing fails the ingest or the publish. The re-check pass takes verified copies whose last check is older than their target's cadence (licensed accounts, active targets and sites, no check already waiting), at most 200 a pass, and queues one `archive.verify` each; corrupt and missing copies are not re-checked until copied again.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web); docs-coverage 327/327 on the device. New `test/mirror-archive-rules.check.ts` 45/45 in the container (Postgres 16), with the real agent pass against the real API and a temporary folder as the NAS: the window (inside, outside, over midnight, summer time in London, equal ends), rule validation (no licence 403, an empty list allowed without it, another account's target, the same target twice, no moment, an unknown zone), ingest copy made and verified by the agent, no second copy, publish-only target untouched on ingest, the asset's own rung switching copies off and checked the same way, a disabled target skipped with why, no licence no copy, publishing queues and the agent makes the publish copy, the window holding the lease back and then released, the cadence on targets (default, null, 0 refused, changed), re-checks queued once, read back and confirmed, then left alone when fresh, never-recheck, disabled, unlicensed or corrupt, and a NAS edit found corrupt by the scheduled check. It fails at HEAD (the functions and the column are new). Around it, 30/30 suites and 1090 assertions (mirror-archive, site-jobs, sites, jobs, job-log, agent, retention-bin, publishing-rules, publishing-stream-rules, the ingest and watchfolder suites), plus access-rules 28/28, rights-profiles 75/75, listStandard ok, listQuery ok, s3Retention 8/8, uploads 49/49, upload-staging 42/42, vod-library 70/70, vod-audio-asset 30/30, review-access 22/22. edge-rights, rights-preview, review-qc (2 assertions), vod-ads, vod-ads-skins, vod-library-pure (1 assertion) and migration-safety fail the same way with the HEAD files in this container (parts of the tree it does not have, such as `scripts/deploy`); the release checks run migration safety on the device.
- **Not here**: the run on the Mac with a real agent process (as H3-2): put `"archive": {"copies": [{"targetId": "<target>", "on": ["ingest"]}]}` on the account rule, upload a file, and the target folder should get the original and the copy show `verified`.
- **Not done**: H3-4 (recordings, clips and packaged outputs as copy sources, on recording close and on a schedule, mirror mode, S3-compatible targets, consolidated recordings, the console). H3-5 needs Christian's input: no retention rung removes an asset's original today (asset ladders delete clip outputs and frame stills only, and uploads with no channel or event are not reached by an account-level asset rule), so the copy-before-delete rung needs to know whether it removes only the original from the cloud (renditions stay playable) or the whole asset, whether account asset rules should start covering uploads, and whether a recording's delete waits for its consolidated archive file.

## 2026-11-06 - Mirror and Archive: on-prem S3-compatible targets (build queue H3-4, part (b); v0.57.0)

What: a storage target can now be an S3-compatible object store at the customer's site (MinIO, NetApp StorageGRID and similar) as well as a folder or NAS share. The site agent writes the copies through the store's S3 API, reads each one back and reports that checksum, so a copy counts only once the store holds exactly the original. H3-4 was split: copy rules beyond originals, consolidated recordings and the console are H3-6.

- **Where**: `volt-app/migrations/0137_archive_store_targets.sql` (one nullable jsonb column; add only); `volt-app/src/services/archive/archive.ts` (`TARGET_KINDS` with `s3`, `cleanStore`, `storePath`, `targetForAgent`, create and change rules); `volt-app/src/modules/archive.ts` (docs, audit snapshot); `volt-agent/src/s3Target.ts` (new: signer, key loading, PUT, multipart, LIST, GET read-back, DELETE of its own refused copy); `volt-agent/src/siteArchive.ts` (`pump` shared by both kinds, `runCopyToStore`, store verify); `volt-agent/src/site.ts` (key file beside the state file; agent 0.6.0); API overlay and regenerated `openapi.json`; guide `hybrid-sites.md` (Copies to an object store, troubleshooting, API); `volt-agent/README.md`; `HYBRID_SITES.md` Phase 3d.
- **Decision taken in the build (the item left it open): the keys live on the agent host, never in the cloud.** The cloud knows only `credentialRef`; the API refuses requests carrying keys, so a database dump, an audit export or a job payload never holds the customer's store keys. A key file others can read is refused, as ssh does. Christian: if a customer wants keys entered in the console instead, that is a change to make deliberately (a secrets-store reference, as Payments does), not a default.
- **How it works**: the agent picks a free name with one LIST of the name's stem, streams the download into one PUT (up to a part) or a multipart upload (parts of 8 MiB or more, under 10 000 parts, one part in memory), with `If-None-Match: *` on the PUT and on completion so a name taken meanwhile is refused by the store (412) rather than overwritten; it then GETs the object and hashes what the store holds. The cloud judges that result exactly as for a folder. An object the cloud refuses, or that does not read back as sent, is deleted by the agent (only its own just-written copy). Store errors are kept on the copy with the store's own code.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web) and in the container (volt-agent); docs-coverage 327/327, coverage 62/62 and scrub clean on the device. New `test/mirror-archive-s3.check.ts` 50/50 in the container against moto 5.2.5 with IAM authentication on (the server recomputes every signature; a wrong secret answers SignatureDoesNotMatch), 29/29 with no store (the signer and API parts): the signer gives the AWS SDK's Authorization in six cases (PUT with If-None-Match, LIST query, multipart start with an empty query value, a part, virtual-hosted style, a session token in another region); key file rules; no secret anywhere in the database; the copy job carrying only where and the key name; a 200 KB copy in one PUT and a 20 MB copy in three parts, both read back with the SDK; `-2` on a clash; a name planted between the look and the write refused with 412 and the copy taking `-2`, the other object untouched; a wrong checksum refused and the object removed; re-checks finding a changed object corrupt and a removed one missing; wrong, unprotected and missing keys and an unreachable store as retries with the reason, then the same copy going through; a target holding copies keeping its bucket and prefix. It fails at HEAD (`s3Target.ts` is new). Around it 19/19 suites, 495 assertions: mirror-archive 72/72 (its kind check now asks for an unknown kind), mirror-archive-rules 45/45, site-jobs 67/67, sites, site-watchfolders, site-watchfolder-files, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, agent 19/19, retention-bin 41/41, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, ingest-core 97/97, migration-safety 60/60, listStandard, listQuery, s3Retention 8/8, uploads 49/49.
- **Not here**: a run on the Mac against a real MinIO or another store with a real agent process. MinIO's own downloads are archived (dl.min.io answers 410) and the container has no Docker daemon, so moto stood in. On the Mac: `docker run -p 9000:9000 minio/minio server /data` (or the store you have), make a bucket and keys, put them in `~/.volt-agent/archive-keys.json` (chmod 600), create a target with `kind: s3`, copy an asset, and the object should appear under the prefix and the copy show `verified`.
- **Not done**: H3-6 (recordings, clips and packaged outputs as copy sources, on recording close and on a schedule, mirror mode, consolidated recordings, the console). H3-5 still needs Christian's input (see its row).

### C1 Rule-based collections (8 Nov 2026, v0.59.0)

Collections are the one editorial grouping. A collection's members are what its rules match, plus what a person added, minus what a person took out. Design: Publishing Policies doc, "Collections, packages and apps".

- **Where**: migrations `0138_rule_collections.sql` and `0139_rule_collections_index.sql`; `volt-app/src/services/collectionRules.ts` (catalogue, operators, validation, compile to one SQL query, full and incremental matching, queue and stale passes, preview); `modules/collections.ts` (rules, order, recheck, exclusions, fields and preview routes; items carry `source`); passes on the control plane in `services/orchestrator/index.ts` (queue every 5 s, full re-check every 10 min for collections a day old or stale); `modules/rights.ts` marks rule collections stale when a provider changes. Console: `volt-web/src/pages/vod/CollectionRules.tsx` (condition builder, live count, order, left-out list) in `pages/Collections.tsx`. Guide `docs-site/docs/guides/collections.md`; docs seed adds the rule collection "Short and new".
- **How it works**: a vod_assets trigger queues an asset when a column a rule can read changes (only once some rule collection exists); the queue is drained in SKIP LOCKED batches, so several control plane tasks never clash; a full match runs under a per-collection advisory lock. Rules referring to a field that has since gone leave the members as they are and log it, rather than emptying the collection.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device. `test/collection-rules.check.ts` 67/67: every operator of every field type against known answers, groups, refusals, members and exclusions, re-matching on change through the trigger and queue, the stale and nightly passes, ordering, tenancy; scale on 100,000 assets: full match 0.1 s, repeat 0.06 s, live count 0.2 s, a 500-asset batch 16 ms. collections 33/33, rights-profiles 75/75, fast-lineup 68/68, migration-safety 60/60, docs-coverage 333/333. Full run 279/306; the other 27 fail the same on HEAD (encoder tools, GPU, browser codecs, missing siblings). Browser check of the Rules card, groups and the left-out list; screenshots retaken by `scripts/screenshots.mjs`.
- **Not done**: access rules per app and region on a collection, packages and View as (C2); collections as FAST pools and app catalogue rows (C3); sharing the condition builder with P3's metadata rules.

What: with the Mirror and Archive add-on, a copy rule can now archive the recordings themselves to storage at the customer's site, in the format Christian set: one file per programme when the channel has a guide, one per hour without, from the top rendition joined without re-encoding, with a metadata sidecar, named `Title-Channel-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`. ## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 2b-6: guided ad playlists and the FAST stitcher under enforce (build queue P2-2f-3; v0.103.0)

What: the guided on-demand ad playlists (`/vod-ads/g/...`, one answer for every viewer, cached at the CDN) and the pods a stitched FAST session puts into a viewer's breaks (conditioned creatives, house spots, the slate) named titles' segments by plain address, so under `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` guided ads and stitched FAST ads did not play. Both now carry the shared token (v0.101.0).

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/vodAds/index.ts`: `guidedEdgeToken` (the title folder's shared token, scope `guided:<base version>`, no country), `guidedVersions` (the current version and every earlier one whose token is still valid, each with its token; the token is hashed into the version, so it changes on rotation), `guidedVersion` (the current one, for the play gate), `guidedMaster` and `guidedPlaylist` take the token and tokenise every address in the title's folder (`tokeniseHls`). `modules/vodAds.ts` `guidedAsset`: the playlist routes accept any listed version and use its own token, so a CDN copy and a player's later rendition switch read the same token. `services/edgeTokens.ts` `playlistEdgeTokens` takes an `overlapSec` (never below the default, never past 24 h). `modules/fast.ts`: `podMasters`, `podTokeniser` (the channel's shared token, scope `fast:<channel>`, for every title in the breaks a request stitches, asked on each refresh like the playout's), `podSegments` puts it in front of each pod segment and init (before the slate's `#loopN` marker).
- **Choices made in the build**: a guided playlist is read once and played for the whole title, unlike a FAST window read every few seconds, so its token's overlap is the usual hour plus the title's length: a viewer who starts a second before a rotation still has a valid token at the last segment. Earlier versions keep answering with their own token while it is valid instead of 404, so a rendition switch late in a long title still works. The stitcher uses the shared token rather than a per-viewer one: it needs no viewer claims, matches the playout, and pods read the same for every viewer of a channel-scoped decision. A withdrawn title is left out (`playlistEdgeTokens`), so its addresses stay plain and are refused; a withdrawn guided title's playlist answers 404 as before.
- **Checked**: typecheck clean on the device (volt-app). New `test/edge-shared-playlists.check.ts` **37/37** (5 fail and it stops at HEAD): the longer overlap; on the real API under enforce, the guided master routes every rendition (subtitles too) through the guided route, every content segment, init and subtitle file through one shared token (this title only, scope `guided:`, no country, a period plus the overlap plus the title's length), the same text on every request with `public, max-age=300`, segments play (from another country too), plain and other titles' folders refused, the pre-roll's own tokens still play; a rotation gives a new version and token while the earlier version stays listed with its token until that token expires; tokens off plain as before, issue tokenised; a stitched FAST session's ad and house spot named through the channel's shared token (both titles, no country), playing, plain refused, the channel's own segments untouched, the same for a second viewer; withdrawn: guided segments already handed out answer 403 *No longer available*, the guided playlist 404, the stitched ad's segment 403, a new stitched playlist names the ad plain and the house spot's new token no longer names it. ## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 2b-6: the shared edge token (build queue P2-2f, first part; v0.101.0)

What: FAST channel playlists and guided on-demand ad playlists are written once for every viewer and cached at the CDN, so under `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` they cannot carry a viewer's token, and their plain content addresses are refused. Decided by Christian on 11 Nov 2026 (option B): a shared token per FAST channel or guided playlist version that opens only the titles it uses, rotating. This run builds the token and the checks at all three edges; the writers that put it in playlists are P2-2f-2.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/edgeTokens.ts`: `SharedClaims` (`k: "shared"`, `g` scope for logs, `A` the titles as 32 hex ids, `d: 0`, `n`, `e`; no country, country rules stay on the channel playlist and its rights variants), `sharedEdgeToken` (fixed clock: period start = `floor(now / period) * period`, expiry = start + period + overlap, defaults 3 h and 1 h, `EDGE_SHARED_TOKEN_PERIOD_SEC`, `EDGE_SHARED_TOKEN_OVERLAP_SEC`; a title revoked inside the period moves `n` to just after its revocation), `sharedEdgeTokens` (splits lists over 32 titles), `sharedRotation`, `sharedAssetOf`, `SHARED_PATH_RE`; `verifyEdgeToken` returns `shared` and the path's `asset`; `tokenPathMiddleware` checks the path's title against the revocation set. `deploy/edge/vod-token-viewer-request.js` (4.1 KB of the 10 KB) and `deploy/staging/edge-token.js`: the same branch, `r:<the path's title>`.
- **Choices made in the build**: ids without dashes keep 32 titles inside the 2048-character token the edges accept; the clock makes the API and any writer mint the same token with no shared state, so a cached playlist stays valid; a shared token opens encode folders only (`/vod/<id>/`, `/clips/vod/<id>/`), not clip output folders or ad creatives; a refused shared token is not counted as a viewer's country demand.
- **Checked**: typecheck clean on the device (volt-app; no volt-web change). New `test/edge-shared-tokens.check.ts` **41/41** (fails at HEAD: no `sharedEdgeToken`): the claims, the clock and its settings, the same token across a period, the cap and the split, the revocation shift; the TypeScript reference, the real CloudFront Function and the real njs on the same cases (named titles open under both folder kinds with the plain path passed on, any country, other titles, clip folders, ad folders and `..` refused, a widened title list refused, through a rotation with both tokens playing at the switch, a copied address refused at period plus overlap, a revoked title refused while the other plays, republished plays); the API under enforce over HTTP. Around it: edge-tokens 67/67, edge-tokens-enforce 38/38, edge-token-free 49/49, edge-clip-outputs 25/25, edge-rights 38/38, edge-variants 62/62, edge-variant-manifests 73/73, edge-viewas 5/5, rights-demand 40/40, vod-ads 91/91, vod-ads-sgai 51/51, vod-ads-enforce 37/37, vod-ads-catchup 47/47, vod-ads-vmap 56/56, share-links 34/34. cdn-logs 67/68 and fast-access 45/50 fail the same with HEAD's edgeTokens.ts (known since P2-2b; the FAST rights routes of c606d370); vod-ads-skins does not load in the container (no `@svta/cml-cmcd`).
- **Guides**: none yet; nothing a customer sees changes until the playlists carry the token (P2-2f-2 updates the play gate and FAST guides).

## 2026-11-11 - Mirror and Archive: mirror mode (build queue H3-10; v0.99.0)

What: a copy rule can keep a storage target in step with Volt, deletes included (decided by Christian, 11 Nov 2026). The grace period starts only when the asset (or a sidecar) leaves Volt for good, out of the restore bin; 7 days by default, per target, 0 to 90. Nothing is deleted from an archive-mode copy, and a restore cancels anything waiting.

Agent 0.13.0 `runDelete` (`volt-agent/src/siteArchive.ts`): read back, delete only what matches, a changed file kept (`changed_kept`), a package all or nothing over its listed files with folders removed only once empty, stores GET then DELETE. Console: `StorageArchive.tsx` (Mirror deletes wait (days), a Mirror deletes column, copy rows with the delete date and Removed), `RuleEditor.tsx` (Keep: Archive / Mirror per target, with the target's period).
- **Checked**: typechecks clean (volt-app, volt-web, volt-agent; on the device and in the container). ## 2026-11-11 - Mirror and Archive: recordings wait for their archive file (build queue H3-5c; v0.98.0)

What: (3) of H3-5, decided by Christian on 11 Nov 2026. For a channel or event whose copy rule archives recordings, retention's delete rung (the ladder's, or an expiry set by hand) no longer removes a recording until every consolidated archive file covering it is verified at the rule's targets: never the only copy. The recording, the Storage retention list and the recording page say why it waits.

- **Where**: migration `0167_recording_archive_wait.sql` (add only: `recordings.archive_wait_code`, `archive_wait`, `archive_checked_at`; `archive_recording_cursors.archived_from`); new `volt-app/src/services/archive/recordingWait.ts` (`recordingArchiveWait`: the licence and the stream's copy rule, the part of the recording after `archived_from`, the pass's `done_until`, the `archive.recording` jobs and the `archive_copies` rows overlapping it; codes `not_made`, `making`, `make_failed`, `no_file`, `copying`, `copy_bad`, each with a sentence; an error judging it keeps the recording); `services/media/retention.ts` (judged once per recording per sweep before the manual expiry and the rule's delete rung, `waitingArchive` in `SweepResult`, a dry run counts without writing); `services/archive/recordings.ts` (the pass sets `archived_from` the first time it hands a stream on); `modules/storage.ts` (`archiveWait` on `/api/storage/expiring`); `services/orchestrator/service.ts` (the sweep log line); `volt-web/src/pages/StorageRetention.tsx` (**Waiting for its archive copy** and the reason), `RecordingDetail.tsx` and `api.ts`.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/retention-recordings-archive.check.ts` **22/22** (real fMP4 runs on disk, the HTTP app): with an account copy rule archiving recordings and a 2-day delete rung, a 5-day-old recording waits with no file (`no_file`), while its file is made (`making`), when making it failed (with the job's error), while its copy is copied (naming the file and Recordings NAS), when the copy is failed, missing or corrupt (`copy_bad`), and while the pass has not reached its end (`not_made`); the retention list and `GET /api/recordings/:id` carry the reason; verified, it is deleted and the reason clears; an expiry set by hand waits and then goes; a recording from before `archived_from` goes at once; an account without Mirror and Archive deletes as before; a rule archiving originals only holds nothing; a dry run counts the wait and writes nothing; `recordingPass` records `archived_from` on its first pass. Around it, unchanged: mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, mirror-archive-clips 37/37, -packages 51/51, -rules 45/45, -sidecars 36/36, mirror-archive and -s3 ok, media-lifecycle 121/121, analytics-retention 33/33, recordings-counts 29/29, recordings-sources 14/14, recordings.check.sh 35/35 alone, stuck-recording 29/29, s3Retention 8/8, vod-lifecycle-api 42/42, retention-originals-restore 31/31, retention-originals-reencode 31/31, site-jobs 67/67, workflows 72/72. retention-bin passes 41/41 or 39/41 from run to run, the same at HEAD (six alternating runs; the sprite sheet flake noted on 7 Nov); retention-originals stops at its channel fixture in the container, the same at HEAD. ## 2026-11-11 - Mirror and Archive: a re-encode brings the original back by itself (build queue H3-5d; v0.97.0)

What: when an encode or a FAST channel's Prepare needs an asset's original and a retention rule has taken it out of the cloud (it is in the archive only), Volt starts the restore itself, or joins the one on its way, and runs that work once the original lands. Before, the encode failed with *the source file is not there* and the channel said *no source*, as if no original had been kept.

- **Where**: migration `0166_original_restore_then.sql` (add only: a jsonb column with a default); `volt-app/src/services/archive/originals.ts` (`restoreThen`, `restoreForJob`, `runAfterRestore`, `originalReturnOf`; `judgeRestore` takes the waiting list in the same statement that points the asset at the original); `services/fast/prepare.ts` (readiness says the original is in the archive and Prepare brings it back first, then *preparing* while it comes back for that channel; Prepare calls `restoreThen` and says why when it cannot); `services/ingest/pipeline.ts` (`queueEncodeFor`, never for an asset encoded already); `services/jobs/handlers/vodEncode.ts` and `vodLadder.ts` (a missing source in the archive starts the restore, and the job is queued again once it lands); `modules/vod.ts` (`original_return` on `GET /api/vod/:id`); `volt-web/src/pages/vod/EncodePlace.tsx`, `libraryApi.ts`, `Vod.tsx` (**Original** in the asset's Facts). Once the original lands, channels with a ladder are prepared before those without, because an encode in flight reads as preparing on every channel.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/retention-originals-reencode.check.ts` **31/31** (fails at HEAD), with the real agent code and a folder as the NAS: four originals copied and taken out; readiness on a FAST channel without a ladder and on one whose HD ladder the title is not packaged to says Prepare brings the original back (an asset with no original anywhere is still no source); Prepare starts ONE restore for both channels and queues no encode, the asset reads preparing, a second Prepare does nothing; a copy marked missing is skipped with why and gets no restore; an encode job that finds the original gone gets *it is being brought back* and starts the restore; `queueEncodeFor` starts none for an asset encoded already and one for an asset never encoded; `GET /api/vod/:id` says it is coming back from Archive NAS and what waits; after the agent's pass the film is back with the same bytes, its Movies encode and HD packaging are queued from the restored address, the other encodes are queued again and the asset follows its encode, and `original_return` is null. Around it, unchanged: retention-originals-restore 31/31, retention-bin 41/41, media-lifecycle 121/121, analytics-retention 33/33, the seven mirror-archive suites (37, 51, 34, 45, 36 and two ok), site-jobs 67/67, site-ladder-variant, site-reencode and site-fallback ok, fast-brand 35/35, fast-lineup 68/68, fast-ads 48/48, fast-ad-schedule 34/34, fast-beacons 52/52, fast-geo-rights 19/19, fast-geo-dash 9/9, uploads 49/49, ingest-core 97/97, watchfolder-video 70/70, watchfolder-video-landing 45/45, workflows 72/72. Not runnable in the container and identical at HEAD: fast-ladder (53 passed, then stops at its encoder script), retention-originals (44 passed, then its channel fixture breaks the owner check here), site-encode, site-encode-worker and site-encode-placement (no volt-encoder), fast-graphics, fast.check.sh, fast-access, trickplay-fast-dash and docs-seed-fast (sample media, H.264). Browser (Chromium, docs seed with an archived original): the asset drawer's Facts show **Original**: *Coming back from the archive at Archive NAS. The encode and preparing for AdminCo FM follow once it lands.*, and with no restore *In the archive at Archive NAS since ...*; no console errors. Docs coverage 63/63, scrub clean.
- **Guides**: Storage (a re-encode brings it back), Copies of your media at your own sites (step 5, a troubleshooting row), Media Library (Facts, a troubleshooting row), FAST (Media on Overview); RETENTION.md; HYBRID_SITES.md Phase 3l. No screenshot shows an archived original, so none was retaken.
- **Christian on the Mac**: after the H3-5 run (an original out of the cloud, its copy on the NAS folder, agent 0.12.0 running), put the title in a FAST channel's run order and choose Prepare: the original comes back into `clips/vod-src` and the channel's encode starts by itself.
- **Not done**: recordings waiting for their archive file (H3-5c).

## 2026-11-11 - Mirror and Archive: restore an original to the cloud (build queue H3-5b; v0.96.0)

What: an original a retention rule took out of the cloud comes back from its archive copy. **Restore** (Storage, Mirror and Archive, Originals out of the cloud, or the API) queues a job at the copy's site; a server there reads the copy, checks it is still the file that was archived and uploads it to the original's old address; Volt reads back what arrived and only when it matches the archived checksum does the asset use it again. A restored original's retention clock restarts at the restore, so it is not taken out again before the re-encode it came back for.

- **Where**: migration `0165_original_restore.sql` (add only); `volt-app/src/services/archive/originals.ts` (`requestRestore`, `judgeRestore`, `restoreFailed`, the clock from `original_restored_at`, `restorable`/`restoring`/`restoreError` on the status); `services/archive/archive.ts` (`ARCHIVE_RESTORE` in the licensed job types, judged and failed through originals.ts, never touching the copy row); `modules/archive.ts` (`POST /api/archive/originals/:assetId/restore`, restore state in the list, overlay entry); `services/workflows/map.ts`; `volt-agent/src/siteArchive.ts` (`runRestore`), agent 0.12.0; `volt-web/src/pages/StorageArchive.tsx` (**Restore**, **Being restored**, the last failure).
- **Checked**: `retention-originals-restore` **31/31** (new; with the real agent code and a folder as the NAS: two originals copied and taken out, restore refused for one still in the cloud, without the licence and for another account, queued once in the copy's site with one upload held to the archived checksum, an agent before 0.12.0 leasing nothing, the original back at its address with the same bytes and the row cleared, an audit entry, out of the list, the next pass keeping it with the due date 30 days after the restore, a copy changed at the NAS refused by the agent with the reason on the asset and the copy row left alone, a missing copy refused with 409, the cloud's read-back refusing and removing other bytes, refusing nothing there and a result without a checksum). Around it, unchanged: retention-originals 51/51, the seven mirror-archive suites, site-jobs 67/67, sites ok, workflows 72/72, workflow-report-sites 35/35, retention-bin 41/41, media-lifecycle 121/121, s3Retention 8/8. site-encode, site-encode-worker and site-encode-placement could not run in the container (volt-encoder was not copied), unrelated. Typechecks clean (volt-app, volt-web on the device; volt-agent in the container); docs coverage 359/359. Browser (Chromium, docs seed): Restore on the removed original queues the job and the row says Being restored. Screenshot `storage-mirror-archive` retaken and looked at.
- **Guides**: Storage (Bring an original back, the API), Copies of your media at your own sites (step 5, two troubleshooting rows, the API); RETENTION.md; HYBRID_SITES.md Phase 3k; API reference.
- **Christian on the Mac**: after the H3-5 run (an original out of the cloud, its copy on the NAS folder), choose Restore with agent 0.12.0 running: the file is back in `clips/vod-src` and the row leaves the list. Against MinIO (store target) the same with `archive-keys.json` in place.
- **Not done**: recordings waiting for their archive file (H3-5c); a re-encode starting the restore by itself (H3-5d).

## 2026-11-11 - Mirror and Archive: originals out of the cloud once archived (build queue H3-5, (1) and (2); v0.95.0)

What: decided by Christian on 11 Nov 2026. With Mirror and Archive, the account's retention rule can remove each asset's original file from the cloud a number of days after it landed, and only once a verified archive copy of that very file is held at one of the account's storage targets. Only the original goes; the renditions, images and metadata stay, so the title keeps playing. While the copy is missing, failed, corrupt, of an earlier file, still being made, or a job is reading the original, it stays, and the asset and the console say why. Account rules can now also cover uploads and watch folder files: new rules do, existing ones keep their reach until an administrator ticks the option.

- **Where**: migration `0164_retention_originals.sql` (add only); `volt-app/src/services/archive/originals.ts` (`judgeOriginal`, `sweepOriginals`, `originalStatus`, `originalRuleFor`); `services/media/retention.ts` (`saveRule` takes `originalAfterDays` and `includeUploads` and no longer resets `applies_to`/`enabled` when they are not sent; `ruleFor` `viaOwnAccount`; the sweep reaches uploads by their own account only through a rule that includes them); `modules/storage.ts` (the two fields, the licence check, originals in the dry run); `modules/archive.ts` (`GET /api/archive/originals`, `/originals/:assetId`, overlay entries); `services/orchestrator/service.ts` (the hourly pass); `volt-web/src/pages/Storage.tsx` and `StorageArchive.tsx`; docs seed (a rule at 60 days, one original removed and one waiting).
- **Checked**: `retention-originals` **51/51** (new; every verdict, the rung refused without the licence, kept on a save that does not mention it, a due original with no copy waiting with why, the file removed once verified and the address kept, an audit entry, a corrupt copy, a job and a second copy in progress keeping it, a file outside the originals folder put back, reach through old and new account rules and a channel rule, the dry run, the list and status routes, switching it off). Around it, unchanged: media-lifecycle 121/121, retention-bin 41/41, s3Retention 8/8, analytics-retention 33/33, vod-lifecycle-api 42/42, the seven mirror-archive suites (37, 51, 34, 45, 36 and two ok), recordings-counts 29/29, recordings-sources 14/14, stuck-recording 29/29, recordings.check.sh 35/35 alone (it fails half its checks only when run alongside the others, as before). Typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web); docs coverage 359/359 on the device. Browser (Chromium, docs seed): the ladder editor shows both options, Save sends `includeUploads` and `originalAfterDays` and the rule is stored; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Screenshots `storage` and `storage-mirror-archive` retaken by the script and looked at. docs-samples and docs-seed-fast could not run in the container (their sample files and media were not copied), unrelated to this change.
- **Guides**: Storage (the two options, Take originals out of the cloud once they are archived, the dry run, the API), Copies of your media at your own sites (step 5, a troubleshooting row, the API); RETENTION.md; HYBRID_SITES.md Phase 3j; API overview for Mirror and Archive.
- **Christian on the Mac**: with a site agent and a NAS folder, a copy rule for originals and the account rule's Originals rung at 0 days: upload a file, wait for its copy to show Verified, and after the next retention pass (hourly) the file is gone from `clips/vod-src` while the title still plays; Storage, Mirror and Archive lists it as In the archive only.
- **Not done**: restore to the cloud for a re-encode (H3-5b); recordings whose delete waits for their consolidated archive file (H3-5c). Until H3-5b a re-encode of such an asset answers as for an asset with no original.

## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 2b-5: on-demand ad sessions under enforce (build queue P2-2e; v0.94.0)

What: under `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` a stitched ad session's playlists named the title's segments, and each ad's and house spot's (a conditioned creative is an asset under `/clips/vod/` or `/vod/` too), by plain address, so on-demand ads did not play under enforce. A stitched session belongs to one viewer, so its addresses now carry that viewer's tokens, one per folder.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/modules/apps.ts`: the play gate reads the title's token spec once (country, policy class, the title's window, the rule's lifetime) and passes it to `adsForPlay`, so a session carries what the title's own token would. `services/vodAds/index.ts`: `openSession` keeps the claims in the session's `ctx.edge` (`c`, `k`, `n`, `e`; `sessionEdgeOf`, no migration: `ctx` is jsonb); `folderToken()` signs a token for one asset's folder from those same claims (the title's with its class and the play attempt's sid, an ad's with class open), so every playlist reads the same on each request; `tokeniseHls()` and `tokeniseMpd()` put the token in front of every address in the folder (segments, `EXT-X-MAP`, `URI=`, `BaseURL`, `sourceURL`, `media`); `sessionMaster`, `sessionMedia`, `sessionMpd`, `adMaster`, `adMedia` and `segmentRedirect` use them. Folders the edge does not guard (`/clips/ads/`) are left plain. `modules/vodAds.ts`: a session with claims whose title was revoked after it opened answers 403 *No longer available* on its playlists, segment route and timeline. Guided sessions' ads are tokenised the same way (fresh claims, the viewer's country); the shared guided content playlist is P2-2f. Mode off: nothing kept, addresses as before.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/vod-ads-enforce.check.ts` **37/37** (fails at HEAD: no `folderToken`): the claims in each folder's token, ad folders through the CDN, `/clips/ads/` untouched, the tokenisers; on the real API under enforce, a stitched session's HLS (30 content segments, the title's, creative's and house promo's init segments all tokenised, nothing plain left, the playlist identical on a second request) and DASH (content BaseURL and the ads' init segments tokenised) play end to end: content, init, creative and house segments 200 through their tokens, 403 from another country, by plain address and with one title's token on another folder; guided ads tokenised and playing; withdrawn: the session's master, playlist, MPD, segment route and timeline 403 with the edge's words and the content tokens 403; issue tokenised with plain still playing; off as before. Neighbours unchanged: vod-ads 91/91, vod-ads-sgai 51/51, vod-ads-vmap 56/56, vod-ads-catchup 47/47, vod-ads-skins 40/40, edge-tokens 67/67, edge-tokens-enforce 38/38, edge-token-free 49/49, edge-clip-outputs 25/25, publishing-delivery 47/47, apps 8/8, player-api 32/32, share-links 34/34, access-control 17/17, geo-access 15/15, cmcd 73/73, docs-samples 119/119, docs-coverage ok, migration-safety 60/60. Docs build clean (with the untracked fonts staged), scrub clean, coverage 63/63.
- **Guides**: The play gate (new Ad sessions under step 7), On-demand ads (ads keep playing where tokens are enforced).
- **Christian on the Mac**: with `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` and the staging nginx in front, play a title with a stitched pre-roll in the Volt player (HLS and DASH) and in Safari; the ads and the title play, and a copied segment address from the network panel answers 403 without its `/t/` prefix.
- **Not done**: FAST channel playlists and the guided content playlist under enforce (P2-2f, a rotating shared token). The edge reads no country for a session opened without one (`c` empty), as for the gate's own tokens.

## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 2b-4: clip output folders under enforce (build queue P2-2d; v0.93.0)

What: under `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` plain `/vod/` and `/clips/vod/` addresses were refused, but a clip cut from a recording keeps its files in a folder of its own, `/clips/<asset id>/` (`hls/`, `clip.mp4`, `clip.mp3`), and those still played by plain address. Now they are refused the same way at the API, the staging nginx and CloudFront, and play through a token.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/edgeTokens.ts`: `PLAIN_VOD_RE` and `plainAssetId()` take `/clips/<uuid>/` too, new `PLAIN_CLIPS_RE` for what the API serves; `tokenPathMiddleware()` refuses a plain clip folder under enforce unless the title is token-free (P2-2c). `deploy/edge/vod-plain-refuse.js`: guards `/clips/<uuid>/` and passes paths under `/clips/` that are not titles (ad creatives) unchanged, as the reference does. `deploy/terraform/edge_tokens.tf`: enforce adds a `/clips/*` behaviour after `/clips/vod/*`, before `*.m3u8`. `deploy/staging/edge-token.js` and `nginx.conf.template`: the plain location covers clip folders; its regex is quoted (an unquoted `{36}` stops nginx loading the file, found by running it). `modules/recordings.ts` `exportLinks()`: a recording export's download link and its `.m4a` alternative carry one token (no country, 4 hours) with `expiresAt`; plain when tokens are off.
- **Already tokenised, checked here**: the play gate, the console's preview and share pages give a clip a token for its folder through `tokenPrefix`. Server-side readers (FAST's `readKey`, the ad stitcher's `assetHls`, the archive's package copier, skip markers, social's own signed copy) read the disk or the bucket, never the public path.
- **Also in this release**: migration 0161 (Loops & Fillers, unshipped) built two indexes inside its transaction, which held the P2-2c release. They moved to `0163_filler_indexes.sql` (`-- volt:no-transaction`, `CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS`), so a database that already ran the old 0161 just records 0163 (checked: an upgraded database applied 0163 only and has both indexes; a fresh one builds them in 0163). `check-migrations` ok, migration-safety 60/60.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app). New `test/edge-clip-outputs.check.ts` **25/25** (9 fail at HEAD): the reference, the njs and the CloudFront function on 11 paths; Terraform and the nginx location; plain clip manifest, playlist, segment and MP4 refused at the API with the edge's words and no-store; ad creatives served; the gate's token plays (manifest, playlist, segment), refused from another country and for another clip's folder; the console preview (HLS and MP4); the recording export link; a token-free clip plain; withdrawn revokes the old token; issue and off as before. `terraform test` **25/25** (two new assertions in `edge_tokens.tftest.hcl`, failing with HEAD's `edge_tokens.tf`), fmt and validate clean. Staging nginx 1.24 + njs with the rendered template in front of the API: **16/16** (enforce: plain clip manifest, segment and MP4 403 with no upstream, plain encode 403, gate token through the edge 200 in AU and 403 in JP, another folder 403, token-free clip 200 from JP, ad creative 200; issue: plain clip 200). Neighbours: edge-tokens 67/67, edge-tokens-enforce 38/38 (reads the quoted location), edge-token-free 49/49, edge-rights 38/38, edge-variants 62/62, edge-variant-manifests 73/73, edge-viewas 5/5, share-links 34/34, public-status 71/71, block-message 39/39, publishing-delivery 47/47, downloads 15/15, clips-store 28/28, clip-provenance ok, scheduled-clips 15/15, recordings 35/35, recordings-counts 29/29, recordings-sources 14/14, vod-ads 91/91, vod-ads-sgai 70/70, vod-ads-vmap 56/56, vod-ads-catchup 47/47, vod-ads-skins 40/40, cmcd 73/73, rights-demand 40/40, vod-lifecycle-api 42/42, vod-markers 40/40, mirror-archive-clips 37/37, mirror-archive-packages 51/51, mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, filler-library 33/33, filler-store 36/36, filler-thumbs 36/36, filler-prep 15/15, apps 8/8, access-control 17/17, player-api 32/32, engineering-guides 36/36 on the device. The same on HEAD: cdn-logs 67/68 (counts 6 CloudFront behaviours since P2-2b's refuse behaviour, which has no real-time log), clips-off-disk (a slate must now be approved, LF1) and filler-preview (no packager in the container) fail identically with and without this change. Stitched ad sessions are per viewer and can carry the viewer's token (P2-2e); FAST and guided ad playlists are shared by every viewer and cached, so they need a decision (P2-2f). schema.org `contentUrl` (`services/schemaOrg.ts`) also names plain addresses.

## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 2b-3: token-free playback (build queue P2-2c, (c); v0.93.0)

What: under `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` every on-demand title played only through a play token. For content anyone may watch a token adds nothing and stops plain addresses being shared, embedded or cached. A publishing rule can now ask for **token-free playback**: the gate hands out the plain addresses and the edges let them through, but only where nothing about the viewer decides.

- **Where**: `protection.tokenFree` (`services/publishingRules.ts` type, `SECTION_FIELDS`, baseline false, lockable; zod in `modules/publishingRules.ts`). `services/deliveryPolicy.ts`: `tokenFreeBlockers(settings, { rights, accountGeo, collectionAccess })` (pure: geo or a country access rule, the account's geo, rights limiting territories or apps, paid or entitled access, paywall, premium rule, members or viewer group rule, a collection asking for a sign-in or package, DRM, referrers, concurrent streams) and `tokenFreeNow(assetId)` (Available, stamped, no blocker; reads only cached rows, so warm it reads nothing). `services/publishingPolicies.ts`: conflict `token_free_conditions` (block, naming the reasons and their fields; per title in the impact with the title's rights and collections, now selected). `modules/apps.ts`: the gate gives plain addresses when the title is token-free now and the viewer was let in openly (policy class open), the edge cache capped at the window end. `services/edgeTokens.ts`: `openKey`, `plainAssetId`, `plainPathVerdict` (the reference), `syncEdgeOpen` (every minute from the orchestrator beside `syncEdgeTokens`: `f:<id>` = window end or 0, kept in `edge_open_assets`, migration 0162, so stale keys are found and removed), and `tokenPathMiddleware` lets a plain `/clips/vod/<id>/` through for a token-free title. Edges: `deploy/edge/vod-plain-refuse.js` reads the KVS (Terraform `templatefile` and `key_value_store_associations` in `edge_tokens.tf`), `deploy/staging/edge-token.js` `plainVerdict(uri, mode, store, now)`. Console: Token-free playback in Protection (`volt-web/src/publishing/PolicyFields.tsx`, `policyApi.ts`).
- **Choices made in the build**: a rule asking for it is refused rather than warned, as the item says, also when the condition is inherited (turn it off on that rung or remove the condition); a stamp that gains a condition later is ignored rather than trusted, decided at the gate, the API and in the sync from the same function. Per-app publication and the WAF profile are not blockers: neither is enforced at the edge for tokens either. Share pages and console previews keep their tokens.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/edge-token-free.check.ts` **49/49** (fails at HEAD). Neighbours: edge-tokens 67/67, edge-tokens-enforce 38/38, publishing-delivery 47/47, publishing-policies 77/77, publishing-restamp 39/39, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, publishing-metadata-rules 54/54, public-status 71/71, block-message 39/39, access-rules 28/28, rights-profiles 75/75, rights-preview 50/50, rights-demand 40/40, collection-access 72/72, vod-lifecycle-api 42/42, share-links 34/34, apps 8/8, access-control 17/17, geo-access 15/15, saas 17/17, auth-boundary 23/23, player-api 32/32, edge-rights 38/38, cmcd 73/73, the five vod-ads suites, docs-coverage 359/359, docs-samples 119/119; migration-safety 59/60, docs.check and docs-seed-fast 14/20 fail the same without this change (the other session's 0161 indexes, routes and FAST work). `terraform test` 25/25 (a new assertion: the refuse function has the store), fmt and validate clean. Staging nginx 1.24 + njs, rendered template, API under enforce: 9/9. Browser `volt-web/scripts/check-token-free.mjs` 16/16 at 1440 and 390 px. Docs build, scrub and coverage clean.
- **Fixed on the way**: `edge-tokens-enforce` compared a token's end with a rounded window end and failed about half the time; it floors now, as the token does.

## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 2b-2: play tokens enforced (build queue P2-2b, (b) and the overlay; v0.92.0)

What: since P2 the play gate hands out on-demand addresses with a play token and both edges check it, but a plain address still played, because the console's previews and share pages used plain addresses and would have broken. Now those carry tokens too, and `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce` (Terraform `edge_tokens = "enforce"`) refuses plain on-demand addresses at CloudFront, at the staging nginx and at the API itself.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/edgeTokens.ts`: `edgeTokensMode()` knows `enforce`; `tokenisedUrls()` (one token for every file under the folder the manifests share); `revokedAtFor()` (the revocation set, cached 5 s); `tokenPathMiddleware()`, mounted before every `/clips` route in `server.ts`: `/t/<token>/clips/...` checked (signature, time, folder, the viewer's country from the trusted edge header, revocation) and served from the plain path with its query, and under enforce a plain `/clips/vod/` refused with *Not available* (the API serves no `/vod/` media and `/vod` is the console's page). `modules/vod.ts` `previewUrls()`: `preview` on `GET /api/vod/:id` (no country, not clamped to the window, so drafts and archived titles preview; the rights-class 4 hour lifetime). `services/shareLinks.ts` `shareMedia()`: share pages' on-demand addresses tokenised, bound to the viewer's country, ending at the earlier of the link's expiry and the title's effective window; `modules/shareLinks.ts` reads the country through `viewerGeo` (the staging edge sends X-Viewer-Country, never CloudFront's header, so share pages there had no country). `scale/config.ts`: `/t/` on the public API tier. Console: `pages/Vod.tsx` (Preview, the HLS and DASH links) and `SkipMarkers.tsx` use `preview`; `vite.config.ts` proxies `^/t/[^/]+/clips/`. Edges: `deploy/edge/vod-plain-refuse.js` (403 *Not available*, not cached); `deploy/terraform/edge_tokens.tf` (`enforce` allowed, `edge_tokens_mode`, the refuse function, `edge_plain_vod_paths`), `s3_cloudfront.tf` (the two behaviours after `/t/*`, before `*.m3u8`), `ecs_services.tf` (`VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS` is the mode), `scaling_alb.tf` (`/t/*` to the public tier); `deploy/staging/edge-token.js` (`plain`, `plainVerdict`), `nginx.conf.template` (`env VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS`, `$edge_plain`, a `/clips/vod/` location, checked `/t/` requests passed on with the token, which the API checks again), `docker-compose.yml` (the edge gets the mode). Overlay: the gate's 451 (`code`, `message`, `notify`) and teaser, `POST /api/public/availability/notify`, `GET /api/analytics/rights-demand`, `preview` on the asset.
- **Choices made in the build**: the API checks tokens itself instead of trusting a header from the edge, so the Mac with no edge, the staging stack and the ALB's own `/clips/*` route behave alike; the staging nginx therefore no longer strips the token. Admin preview tokens name no country and ignore the window, since the people working on a title must see it in any status; share tokens keep both limits.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/edge-tokens-enforce.check.ts` **38/38** (19 fail at HEAD). New `deploy/terraform/tests/edge_tokens.tftest.hcl` **4/4** (issue by default, enforce adds the behaviours before `*.m3u8` and tells the control plane, no store means off, an unknown mode refused); `terraform test` 25/25, `terraform validate` clean (Terraform 1.9.8, mocked providers). Neighbours: edge-tokens 67/67 (the nginx assertion now expects the token passed on), share-links 34/34, vod-ads 91/91, cmcd 73/73, graphics 120/120, public-status 71/71, vod-lifecycle-api 42/42, publishing-delivery 47/47, vod-library 70/70, rights-demand 40/40, block-message 39/39, qoe-reports 71/71, edge-variants 62/62, edge-variant-manifests 73/73, apps 8/8, saas 17/17, auth-boundary 23/23, access-control 17/17, collection-access 72/72, docs-samples 119/119, docs-coverage 359/359, docs ok, engineering-guides 36/36 on the device. Staging nginx 1.24 + njs (the rendered template) in front of the API: **13/13** (see the queue row). Console in Chromium under enforce at 1440 and 390 px: **8/8**. Scrub, coverage and docs build clean.
- **Guides**: The play gate (plain addresses refused where tokens are enforced), Share links (the video's country and end, a troubleshooting row), Deploying to AWS (`edge_tokens.tf`, when to switch to enforce), Running on the Mac (`VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS`).
- **Christian**: when you deploy, leave `edge_tokens` at `issue` first and switch to `enforce` a day later; on the Mac, `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=enforce bash run-on-mac.sh` to try it.
- **Not done**: token-free titles and plain clip output folders under enforce (P2-2c).

## 2026-11-11 - Publishing policies 1b: the admin API and the Availability tab (build queue P1-2; v0.91.0)

What: P1 computed and stored each title's public status and enforced it on every public route, but nobody could see it or act on it: the console still showed only the old availability state, and Listed from, Unlisted, Archive and Restore existed only as service functions. Now the status, its reason and its next moment are on the asset and in the library, the listing fields and archive and restore have routes and controls, and the Availability tab has a who-can-watch tester that gives the play gate's own answer.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/modules/vod.ts`: `statusFields()` on `GET /api/vod/:id` (computed at the moment of the request: `public_status`, `status_reason`, `status_until`, `editorial_state`, `effective_from`, `effective_until`, `publicly_visible`, `publicly_listed`); list fields `public_status`, `editorial_state`, `status_until`, `listed_from`, `unlisted`, `archived_at`, a `public_status` facet and the `expires_within_days` parameter (Available now, `status_until` within N days, counted from `now()` on the server so the page's query does not change with the clock); `PUT /:id/availability` (`listedFrom`, `unlisted`, audited); `POST /:id/archive` (409 when archived, audited with from, to and reason) and `POST /:id/unarchive` (`to: draft | published`; a refused publish leaves it a draft and answers 409 `not_publishable` with the reason; named unarchive because `/:id/restore` is the restore bin); `POST /:id/evaluate`. `volt-app/src/services/whoCanWatch.ts`: the public status from `computePublicStatus` at the asked moment, then the gate's `evaluateAccess` and the gate's 402, 403 or 451 reading (`classifyGate`). `services/availability.ts` `onDemandAccess` and `services/rights.ts` `evaluateAccess` take an optional `at` (the tester only; skips the stored-state checks, which describe now, and reads the rights at that moment). `services/publicStatus.ts` `setListing` drops the cached decision row (a Scheduled title that gains a teaser date keeps its status, so the refresh kept the old row and the gate kept answering 404). `auth/roles.ts`: archive and unarchive are Media library: Operate, evaluate is a read (View). `volt-web/src/pages/vod/Lifecycle.tsx` (+ `lifecycle.css`, `PublicStatus.tsx`): the status line, the five steps, the proportional bar with today marked, the dates, Listing (Manage), Archive with an in-place confirm, Restore as draft and Restore and publish (Operate), Who can watch (country, viewer, when). Library: a Public status column (the tag, the reason on hover), a Public status filter group with Expires in 7 days (`libraryQuery.ts` `pub`, `exp`). Overlay entries for the four new routes, `GET /api/vod/:id`, the list and the play gate's `scheduled` answer; routes and openapi regenerated. Docs seed: Station promo published with a window, for the screenshots.
- **Choices made in the build**: the tester names the viewer kinds the gate can tell apart without a real viewer (not signed in, signed in, signed in and has paid, the last as the gate's trusted `entitled`), and says under every answer what it cannot check (the player page's address for allowed referrer domains, a real viewer's purchases and groups). The library's Availability column became Public status (the old availability chips stay as a filter). Archive asks in place rather than in a browser dialog.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `test/vod-lifecycle-api.check.ts` **42/42** (0/4 at HEAD: no status on the asset or list, no routes): the status on the asset and in the list, filters, sort, facets and Expires in; Listed from making a teaser on the gate at once, Unlisted, back to the default, validation, audit; archive, twice, restore to draft and to published, a refused restore, the audit's from, to and reason; the tester against the real `POST /api/availability` for 13 cases (draft, in review, changes requested, rejected, processing, expired, archived, scheduled, teaser, available, unlisted, a region allow-list and a paid title) plus a rights-restricted title, all agreeing, the allowed country and a paying viewer, two other moments; tenancy (404 on every new route for another account) and a Viewer role (may test, may not archive, restore or change the listing). volt-web `library.check.tsx` **41/42** with five new checks (the one failure, "Created as", fails the same at HEAD, 36/37). Neighbours unchanged: public-status 71/71, rights-profiles 75/75, access-rules 28/28, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-delivery 47/47, publish-required-metadata 13/13, vod-library 70/70, vod-audio-asset 30/30, geo-rights 7/7, fast-geo-rights 19/19, access-control 17/17, auth-boundary 23/23, custom-role 31/31, server-setup 25/25, apps 8/8, saas 17/17, share-links 34/34, collection-access 72/72, collections 33/33, edge-tokens 67/67, vod-ads 91/91, docs-samples 119/119, docs-coverage 359/359, docs and docs-session ok. docs-seed-fast 14/20 fails the same with HEAD's seed (no packager in the container). Browser (Chromium, real API on a seeded database, vite): **32/32** at 1440 and 390 px: the column, chips and Expires in 7 days filtering, Archived from the URL, the tab's status, steps, bar and today, the tester now (200) and on a later date (404 expired), Unlisted saved, archive with its confirm, Restore and publish, a listed scheduled title as a teaser, no sideways scroll, no page errors. Scrub clean, coverage 63/63, docs build ok.
- **Guides**: Media Library: the Public status filter, See what viewers get and when (the statuses, the timeline), Listing, Archive and restore, Who can watch, the access table, three troubleshooting rows and the API rows; screenshots `media-library` (retaken) and `media-library-availability` (new) by the script on the docs seed.
- **Christian**: nothing needed.
- **Not done**: the tester does not take an app (the API does: `appId`); the list hides nothing by default (archived titles show with their tag, as before).

## 2026-11-11 - Hybrid Sites: where an asset is encoded, on the asset page and in the Media Library (build queue H4-8, (a); v0.90.0)

What: a title from an Encode at site watchfolder read only "Building" while a server at the customer's site encoded it, which can take hours, and one that fell back to the cloud never said why its original left the site. Both facts were in the asset's ingest record (`ingest.siteSource`, `ingest.siteFallback`) but nothing showed them. Now the Media Library and the asset page say where it is encoded, which site, and why it moved.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/sites/encodePlace.ts` (`encodePlaceOf`: `at` site or cloud, `state` site, fallback_waiting, fallback_cloud or fallback_failed, `siteId`, `siteName`, `reason`, `error`; `SITE_NAME_SQL` reads the name for the asset's own account only); `volt-app/src/modules/vod.ts` (`encode_place` on `GET /api/vod/:id` and on every row of `GET /api/vod`, the helper column dropped). Console: `volt-web/src/pages/vod/EncodePlace.tsx` (the tag, the sentence, the progress words), used by `pages/Vod.tsx` (the list row, the status row and an **Encoded** row in Facts, the Encode tab), `pages/vod/LibraryCells.tsx` (the grid's encode line), `pages/vod/AssetStatus.tsx` (the status panel's sentence) and `pages/vod/AssetPanels.tsx` (`EncodePanel` takes the line); two icons in `pages/vod/icons.tsx`. No migration; no new route (the field is added to existing answers, so no overlay entry).
- **Choices made in the build**: the place is derived on the server from the ingest record rather than read from it in the browser, so the list and the page say the same thing and another account's site id never turns into a name. The tag shows only while the title is being made (at a site, the wait is the question); afterwards the Encoded row in Facts and the Encode tab keep the answer. "Building" is the console's word for Processing.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `test/encode-place.check.ts` **23/23** with the real site agent and API: registered at a site (name, list row with its queued encode, no helper column), an ordinary upload null, a renamed site, another account's site never named and its asset unreadable, a fallback waiting for the original with the reason, then encoded in the cloud still saying why, an original that could not be sent with the error; with `modules/vod.ts` at HEAD 12 fail. Unchanged: site-encode, site-encode-placement, site-encode-worker, site-fallback, site-jobs 67/67, site-ladder-variant, site-reencode, site-registration-sidecar, site-watchfolders, site-watchfolder-files, vod-library 70/70, listStandard, media-library 43/43, asset-readiness 25/25, collection-access 72/72, find-details 34/34, find-details-page 31/31, library suites. vod-library-pure 169/170 fails on the content-type list (`services/contentTypes.ts`, not touched here); site-agent-api and engineering-guides need `docs/engineering`, not copied into the container. Browser in Chromium **22/22** at 1440 and 390 px against the real API (the row reads Encoding at site 42% with At site naming the site, the asset page's status panel and Facts, a fallen back title's reason and the Encode tab, nothing sideways, no page errors; screenshots looked at). docs-coverage 357/357, scrub clean, docs build clean (the one broken anchor reported is on the FAST from the library workflow page, not touched here).
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites (Encoding at the site: the labels, the new screenshot `hybrid-sites-at-site` by the script on the docs seed, which gains a title being encoded at the site; the fallback steps and a troubleshooting row), Media Library (two troubleshooting rows), Watchfolders (the Processing row now reads Building with At site).
- **Christian on the Mac (H4-9)**: `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE=volt-encoder:<tag> volt-agent site run` with Docker; drop a file on an Encode at site folder: the row reads Queued at site, then Encoding at site with a percentage and the At site tag, it plays from the local origin, the source and its `.json` sidecar stay in the folder and the sidecar is not taken in again; a FAST channel with a ladder gets the title's ladder variant from the site; kill the agent mid-encode (lease reaped after about two minutes, retried). On AWS staging the same into the bucket. GPU on NVIDIA hardware.
- **Not done**: those runs (H4-9).

Now any content type with the general finder also takes a link: the title's own page on a studio, broadcaster, news or podcast site is read for what it publishes about itself and shown first among the results, to tick and apply like a Wikidata item.

## 2026-11-11 - Find details for every content type (build queue F1; v0.88.0)

What: only songs could be looked up ("Find song details"). Now every asset has **Find details…** on its Metadata tab. Songs keep the song service, a Music video uses it too, and every other content type, including one an account builds later, gets a general finder that searches Wikidata (Christian's choice, 8 Nov: free and with no key) and fills the type's fields through their schema.org names.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/media/finders.ts` (new: `finderFor`, the one place a type names its finder, by standard key: `song` and `music-video` use the song service, everything else `general`; `titleTerm`, the title field's value by guide slot `title`, else schema.org `name`, else the asset title). `volt-app/src/services/media/wikidataLookup.ts` (new: `findOnWikidata`, a search then the items with their statements in one call and the names of what they point at in one more, one call at a time with a short gap, a timeout, a User-Agent from `FIND_DETAILS_UA`; Wikidata properties mapped to schema.org names (director P57, cast P161, genre P136, release P577, language P364/P407, series P179, image P18 and others); items of the kind the type describes first (`likely`); `mapToFields` shapes each value to the field: tags a list, an enum one of its options, a number, a date or a year where the field allows one, never a guessed full date). `volt-app/src/modules/vodDetails.ts` (new, mounted in `vodImageRouter`): `GET /api/vod/:id/finder`, `POST /api/vod/:id/details/find` (writes nothing; 400 with no content type, 409 for a song-finder type, Wikidata down or busy said in `error`), `POST /api/vod/:id/details/use` (only the given fields, checked by `validateMetadata`, images copied into the Image Library, `metadata_provenance` with source, item, link, who, when and the value replaced, audited). `modules/vodImage.ts`: the song finder fills a Music video through its own fields and keeps its type (`contentType.now.keep`, and `/song/use` ignores `setSong` for one); the image view returns `finder` and the song for a music video. Console: `volt-web/src/pages/vod/DetailsFinder.tsx` (new), **Find details…** in `pages/Vod.tsx` (AssetMetadata, works on the saved type, so it waits for unsaved changes), `SongFinder.tsx` (no Song offered and the cover not ticked for a Music video), `AssetImage.tsx` (Find song details shown for a music video). New `test/find-details.check.ts` **34/34** against a stand-in Wikidata and MusicBrainz (fails at HEAD: the modules do not exist): which finder per type, the term, times by precision, mapping by schema.org name per field type, a Movie's results with the film first, nothing saved by a search, only ticked values written with provenance and the replaced value, refusals (unknown field, bad value, nothing ticked, bad item, another account), a type the account built, no type, a Song refused, Wikidata down (503) and busy (429), a Music video filled by the song service keeping its type, label and frame. Browser `volt-web/test/find-details.browser.mjs` **20/20** at 1440 and 390 px on the docs seed (a Movie gets the general finder with its title; films first; the same title shown as =, empty fields ticked; a corrected value kept; Cast unticked stays empty; provenance saved; a Song and a Music video open the song finder, no Song offered and the cover not ticked for the video; no page errors, no sideways page scroll). docs-coverage 353/353, scrub clean, docs build clean apart from the existing broken anchor on /workflows/fast-from-library. Real Wikidata answered the first call from the container and then 429 for the shared address, so the checks used stand-ins.
