## 2026-11-10 - Publishing policies 3c: the metadata-rule rung (build queue P3-3; v0.85.0)

What: a title got its policy from the account, its content type, and the channel, event or station it came from, but nothing could say "every EPL match is geo-locked to AU and NZ" whatever its source. Now an account keeps an ordered list of metadata rules, each a set of conditions on a title's metadata, tags, collections or source and the policy it chooses. The first rule a title meets applies, and it sits where Christian decided on 11 Nov: account < content type < owner < metadata rule < asset.

- **Where**: `volt-app/migrations/0160_publishing_metadata_rules.sql` (add only: the `publishing_metadata_rules` table with position, conditions, settings, locks, preset and on or off; a BEFORE UPDATE trigger on `vod_assets` that marks a published title for the restamp sweep when something a rule can read changes, and one on `collection_items` for going into or out of a collection, both only once some account has a metadata rule); `src/services/publishingMetadataRules.ts` (new: the fields a rule can use, which are the collection rule fields plus **Collection** and minus what a policy decides (availability, published, available from); every rule of the account compiled into ONE query with a CASE answering the first rule each title meets, for one title or a batch; a rule whose field has gone is passed over); `src/services/publishingRules.ts` (the `metadata` rung in `RUNG_ORDER` and `resolveForAsset`); `src/services/publishingPolicies.ts` (`resolveWith` takes the matched rule; `policyImpact` measures a metadata change over the whole library with the rules as they would be after it); `src/services/availability.ts` (`restampStale` matches each batch in one query per account; `markPolicyStale` for a metadata change); `src/modules/publishingRules.ts` (`/api/publishing-rules/metadata-rules`: list with the titles each governs, fields, create, change with `?dryRun=1`, order, test, remove; conflicts, the account's locks, 422 and 409 as on every rung; audited); console `volt-web/src/publishing/MetadataRules.tsx` (new: the list with drag and arrows to reorder, the condition builder of collections, the policy editor for the rule, the test box), `RuleEditor.tsx` (the metadata rung in the ladder; an item's own editor names the rule that applies to it), `pages/Publishing.tsx`, `pages/vod/CollectionRules.tsx` (the builder exported), `publishing/policyApi.ts`, `policy.css`; `scripts/seed-docs.ts` (two rules for the guide).
- **Choices made in the build**: a table of its own rather than a fourth `scope_kind` in `publishing_rules`, because a release running beside this one lists every row there as a channel or event rule. Matching is SQL, as for collections, and never per request: it is part of the stamp a published title carries, so the play gate still reads no extra row. A metadata rule is checked against the account default's locks when saved (a type's or owner's lock is reported per title at resolve, as for any rung). The policy of a rule is edited with the same editor as every rung; name, conditions and on or off are saved from the rule's header, each keeping the rest. Fields a policy decides cannot be conditions, so a rule cannot change the answer that chose it.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/publishing-metadata-rules.check.ts` **54/54** (fails at HEAD: no table, no routes), three runs in a row: the rung's place (beats the owner, loses to the title's override, a lock on the owner still holds, a lock on the rule binds the title), the migration and triggers, the field list, validation, first match in order, a new order changing the match, a disabled rule passed over, a match beating the channel, the account lock refused on save and ignored at resolve, the test box, tags and collection conditions, the dry run (2 titles, 1 published), a published title taking up a new rule, a metadata edit and a collection change through the sweep, removal handing titles on, tenancy, audit. Neighbours green: publishing suites 6/6 (273), collection suites 2/2 (136), edge-tokens 67/67, mirror-archive 7/7 (203), delivery 5/5 (143), public-status 71/71, rights 6/6 (229), edge-rights 38/38. Browser check against the real API and vite **14/14** at 1440 and 390 px: the list in order with conditions, what each sets, locks and title counts; an arrow and a drag reorder it; the test box names the rule, the later one also met, and the geo it gets; an open rule with its conditions and policy sections; a rule added through the form goes last and opens; no sideways scroll, no page errors. Docs coverage map 63/63, scrub clean. Not mine and failing in the copy: fast-access 45/50 (expects `GET /channels/:id/rights` refused for View, which a01dfb7a opened), docs-seed-fast 14/20 (fails the same with this item's seed change taken out; the FAST library files carry the other session's staged edits), docs-coverage 339/347 (the workflow diagrams under `static/img/flows` were not in the copy).

## 2026-11-10 - Collection access by app and region, packages (build queue C2, first part; v0.84.0)

What: collections were groupings only. The design (PUBLISHING_POLICIES.md, Collections, packages and apps) has them carry access by app and region, sell premium ones as packages priced per app, let a child only tighten its parent, and make the strictest collection a title is in win per app and region, with rights capping everything.

- **Where**: migrations `0158_collection_access.sql` (`collections.access`, `vod_assets.collection_access`, `collection_access_rechecks`, a `collection_items` trigger) and `0159_collection_access_index.sql` (partial index, concurrent); `volt-app/src/services/collectionAccess.ts` (new: validation, a level's scope and region rules, `cellFor` most restrictive over every chain, `holdsPackage`, `collectionGate`, `looserThan` for a child, `rightsConflict` for a manual add, compiling and the queue pass, product sync); `services/availability.ts` `onDemandAccess` (the collection layer after the rights cap); `services/viewerPath.ts` (the column in the cached availability row); `services/accessRules.ts` (`Grants.collectionIds`); `modules/apps.ts` (`packages` on the 402); `modules/collections.ts` (`GET`/`PUT /:id/access`, `GET /:id/access/view-as`, the rights cap on `POST /:id/items`, a move compiles the subtree); `services/orchestrator/index.ts` (the 5 s pass).
- **Choices made in the build**: a collection's apps and territories are where it applies; outside them it asks nothing of a title (so a UK-only premium collection does not block the title elsewhere). Within them a region rule asks free, members or premium. A chain (a collection and its ancestors) applies only where every level applies and asks the highest of its levels; the cell is the highest of every chain, and every premium level there is a package that unlocks it. The title's own rules still apply on top: a collection only adds a requirement. An unknown country or app counts as inside every scope and as the strictest region. The record per title is the governing chains (only collections asking more than free), evaluated in memory at the gate, rather than a precomputed cell per app and territory group: a few chains of a few levels per title, so the gate stays at no database reads.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/collection-access.check.ts` **69/69** on a copy of HEAD plus this change (fails at HEAD: the service does not exist): validation, scope, most restrictive across collections, per-app prices, the unknown country, a child only tightening, the rights cap, then through the API and the gate: 402 with the package and the app's own products, a free collection not loosening it, inheritance and a parent's change reaching a child's title, a purchase and a viewer session unlocking it, the just-bought look-up, members 403, removal, a move, clearing and deleting compiling the title again, the rights refusing a manual add and capping the gate, View as for each viewer, product key clash 409, tenancy, and a warm gate answering 402 and 200 with 0 database reads. Docs build and scrub clean (two broken anchors in the FAST workflow page are from before).
- **Guides**: Collections (what it is for, a new Access by app and region section with the three routes and an example, How it connects, Who can do it, four troubleshooting rows); the play gate's 402 row (`packages`).
- **Christian to confirm**: the reading of a collection's apps and territories as where it applies (above); and that a title whose own rule is premium and which a collection also makes premium needs both (the rule's product and one of the packages).

## 2026-11-10 - Publishing policies 3d-2: protection and distribution where titles are played (build queue P3-4b, first part; v0.83.0)

What: a rule's Playback token lifetime, Allowed referrer domains, MP4 download and Share links were saved, locked, checked and stamped on published titles, but nothing that plays or hands out a title read them: a player on any site played a title whose rule named two, and a Premium title's MP4 could be downloaded or shared. Now the play gate, the edge token, Downloads and share links read them from each title's stamp.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/deliveryPolicy.ts` (new: `deliveryPolicyOf` reads the delivery fields of a stamp with the baseline under anything missing; `deliveryPolicyFor` uses the stamp in the cached availability row for a published title and the rules as they resolve for a draft; `domainMatches`, `hostNameOf`, `referrerRefusal`); `src/modules/apps.ts` (the play gate: referrer check before the teaser, 403 `mode: blocked`, `code: referrer`, filed in the gate log as an app restriction; a GET answer checked against a Referer is not shared at the edge; the rule's lifetime passed to the edge token); `src/services/edgeTokens.ts` (`lifetimeSec`, capped at `POLICY_MAX_TOKEN_LIFETIME_SEC`, a day; `maxTokenLifetimeSec` at least a day so a revocation outlives the longest token a rule may ask for); `src/modules/downloads.ts` (the MP4 link refused with 403 `mp4_off`, the list marks it `allowed: false` with the reason; MP3 and AAC untouched); `src/services/shareLinks.ts`, `src/modules/shareLinks.ts`, `src/modules/sharePage.ts` (making a link refused with 403 `sharing_off`, a link made before answers 410 `sharing_off` and the page says "Sharing has been turned off for this title.", the hi-res download refused with 403 `mp4_off`, the metadata file still offered).
- **Choices made in the build**: read from the stamp, never the rules, so the warm play gate stays at zero database reads and a change arrives through the P3-4 restamp within a minute. A request that names no page (no Origin, no Referer) is a phone, TV or server app and is not embedded anywhere, so it passes; a web app always plays from a page, so one naming none is refused, as the app's own allow-list already does. The referrer check comes before the Scheduled teaser so a site that may not embed a title learns nothing about it. MP4 download covers the MP4 leaving Volt, including staff Downloads: a clear MP4 of a title the rule protects is the copy the rule exists to stop; the audio files are not the MP4. A draft is judged by its rules as they resolve (a database read on a console path only).
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web) and on a copy of HEAD plus this change. New `test/publishing-delivery.check.ts` **47/47** (fails at HEAD: `services/deliveryPolicy` does not exist): the baseline and stamped fields, domain matching and the refusals, the token lifetime (the rule's, the default, capped at a day, clamped by the window), a revocation kept a day; over HTTP with edge tokens on: a listed site plays with a 10-minute token, a wildcard name plays, another site 403 `referrer` naming it with no token or delivery, a web app with no page refused, a phone app plays, a Referer counts, GET shared for an Origin and `private, no-store` for a Referer, a type with no rule plays anywhere with the platform lifetime; share links refused for the title and its draft with nothing saved; Downloads MP4 refused, MP3 handed out, the draft's MP4 refused, the list marked, no download audited; a News rule change taken up by its published title, then its site, 20-minute tokens, MP4 refused in Downloads and on the share link's hi-res while metadata and opening still work, then sharing off and the old link 410 with the page words; a stamp from before the sections keeps the baseline; a warm gate with allowed and refused answers makes 0 database reads. ## 2026-11-10 - Publishing policies 3d: a rule change reaches published titles (build queue P3-4, first part; v0.82.0)

What: publish() stamps the resolved rules on a title and the play gate reads that stamp, so a changed rule reached a published title only when someone published it again; Before you save could not say what a change would do to titles already out. Now a save marks the published titles the rung governs and they take the new rules up within about a minute, without being published again and without posting to social accounts again; the dry run counts the published titles that take it up and those that stop being playable at once.

- **Where**: `volt-app/migrations/0154_policy_restamp.sql` (add only: nullable `vod_assets.policy_stale_at`) and `0155_policy_restamp_index.sql` (partial index, concurrently, `-- volt:no-transaction`); `src/services/availability.ts` (`publish` keeps `publishedAt` on the stamp; pure `restampPublished`: the rules as they stand, with the targets and DRM the title has and the window counted from when it was published, capped by the rights window, state scheduled, available or expired; `markPolicyStale`: one UPDATE over the rung's published titles; `restampStale`: claims 500 with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, reads the account's rules once per batch, writes only what changed, closes publications as the sweep does when the window now ends, drops the play gate's cached row and refreshes the public status, which revokes edge tokens when a title stops being playable; `restampFor`: batches within a time budget; `sweepAvailability` runs it with 20 s each minute); `src/services/publishingPolicies.ts` (`Impact.publishedChanged`, `Impact.unavailableNow` by the same window arithmetic); `src/modules/publishingRules.ts` (PUT and DELETE mark and start the first batches without holding the response; `restamp.marked` in the PUT answer); `volt-web/src/RuleEditor.tsx` and `publishing/policyApi.ts` (Before you save: "N published titles take this up within a minute" and, in amber, "N become unavailable now").
- **Choices made in the build**: the jobs queue was not used because registering a handler means `src/index.ts`, which carries another session's staged edits; a marked column claimed with SKIP LOCKED gives the same batching and spreads over API tasks. A restamp keeps the title's publishing points (an act with its own publication rows; new points apply at the next publish, so nothing is posted again) and its DRM (the files' protection until P4 repackages; a stamp from before the section is clear, as encodes are). Relative windows count from `publishedAt`, or `available_from` for titles stamped before it was kept. A title withdrawn or archived after it was marked is left as the person left it. A save that changes no published title marks nothing.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web) and on a copy of HEAD plus this change. New `test/publishing-restamp.check.ts` **39/39** (fails at HEAD: `restampPublished` does not exist): the pure restamp (targets, DRM, anchor, 30 days from publish, a window now in the past, a later start, the rights cap, an old stamp); over HTTP, an account geo change taken up by three published titles 80 ms after the response with the play gate refusing the US and allowing Australia, the social target kept and no publication made again, the draft, the withdrawn title and another account untouched; a one-day window on a title published 3 days ago counted as unavailable now by the dry run, then expired with publications removed and an edge revocation written; DRM kept on the stamp; removing a rung; the sweep; a withdraw after marking wins; 3,001 titles marked in one statement (166 to 187 ms), two workers taking 1,000 different titles, the rest in 5 batches of 500 (7.3 s for 2,001), one sweep tick taking all 3,001 up again (12.3 s). Browser check `volt-web/test/publishing-policies.browser.mjs` now **35/35** (5 new) on the docs seed plus its seed at 1440 and 390 px: the take-up line, both published titles carrying the type's geo after the save, "2 become unavailable now" equal to the API's dry run, nothing saved by the preview. Neighbours on the same copy: publishing-policies 77/77, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, publish-required-metadata, public-status 71/71, edge-tokens 67/67, access-rules 28/28, access-control 17/17, block-message 39/39, rights-profiles 75/75, collection-rules 67/67, collections 33/33, share-links 34/34, geo-rights 7/7, apps 8/8, auth-boundary 23/23, vodOwnership, social 53/53, social-x 46/46, social-api 20/20, social-instagram 56/56, social-threads 68/68, migration-safety 60/60, the seven mirror-archive suites, docs-coverage 343/343, docs-samples 119/119, docs-session, guide-icon 23/23; docs scrub clean. docs-seed-fast 14/20 and docs.check (openapi version line in an archive copy) fail the same at HEAD here; the docs site build needs the brand fonts, which live outside the repo, so it was not built here.

Screenshots: publishing-policy-check retaken by the script and unchanged (the docs seed has no published titles, so the new lines do not show there).

## 2026-11-10 - Publishing policies 3b: the policy console (build queue P3-2, first part; v0.81.0)

What: P3 gave a publishing rule its protection, distribution and retention sections, locks, presets and conflict checks, but only through the API; the console still showed the ten old fields in one list. Now every rung is edited as the five sections of a policy, with a padlock per field, presets, and a check of the rule as it is changed that says what the save would do before it is made.

- **Where**: `volt-web/src/RuleEditor.tsx` (the editor used on Publishing Rules, Content Types, a stream's Access tab and an asset: sections, per-field inheritance including section fields rolled back from the server's resolution, locks from beneath, own locks and preset saved with the settings, the dry run 450 ms after a change, the confirmation on 409); `volt-web/src/publishing/PolicyFields.tsx` (new: the sections and their fields, dotted ids for section fields, the section controls, the padlock and section icons), `policyApi.ts` (new: presets, rule, dry run and save with locks and preset, the refusal reader), `policy.css` (new: rows by grid areas, so the value drops under the name on a phone); `pages/Publishing.tsx` (lists stack under 640 px, rows name fields, locks and preset); `volt-app/src/services/publishingPolicies.ts` (`stableJson` for the changed count); `volt-app/scripts/seed-docs.ts` (the account default locks DRM, for the guide's picture).

## 2026-11-10 - Publishing policies 3: sections, locks, presets and conflicts (build queue P3, first part; v0.80.0)

What: a publishing rule could say where a title goes, when and to whom, but not how it is protected, what else may be done with it or how long it is kept; nothing stopped a content type from undoing what the account had decided; and a rule that made no sense (DRM with MP4 download) saved quietly. Now a rule has protection, distribution and retention sections, can lock fields for the rules beneath it, can start from one of five presets, and is checked for conflicts, with its impact counted, before it is saved.

- **Where**: `volt-app/migrations/0153_publishing_policy_sections.sql` (add only: `publishing_rules.locked_fields`, `name`, `preset_key`); `src/services/publishingRules.ts` (the three sections in `RuleSettings` and `BASELINE`, `SECTION_FIELDS`, `LOCKABLE_FIELDS`, `POLICY_SECTIONS`; `resolveLadder` merges sections field by field with `fieldSources`, applies locks from the rung that sets them and reports what was ignored); `src/services/publishingPolicies.ts` (new: `PRESETS`, `policyConflicts`, `lockConflicts`, `policyImpact`, `resolveWith`); `src/modules/publishingRules.ts` (the section schemas, `GET /presets`, `GET /fields`, `?dryRun=1`, 422 `policy_conflict`, 409 `confirm_repackage`, locks, name and preset on PUT and in the audit, `/resolve` with conflicts); `docs/openapi-overlay.json`, `API_ROUTES.md`, `api-routes.json`, `openapi.json` (committed on HEAD's files only).
- **Choices made in the build**: the new sections ride the existing `settings` JSONB and the existing four rungs, so nothing about the play path changes; section fields resolve one by one because the design resolves each field separately and a type that sets DRM must not reset the WAF profile. A lock is final from the rung that sets it (the highest lock wins), and what a more specific rung says is ignored rather than an error at resolve time, so a lock added later never breaks a title; saving a rung that changes a locked field is refused. Presets carry no locks and no countries: a preset never guesses a territory. Only what a save introduces is charged to it: a problem a title already had is not a reason to refuse. Titles with the same content type and owner and no override resolve once between them, which is why a dry run over 5,004 titles takes under 0.1 s. A DRM change counts as a repackage only on published titles, and the save waits for `confirm: true`.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). ## 2026-11-10 - Publishing policies 2d: the Rights demand report in the console (build queue P2-4; v0.79.0)

Now **Analytics, Rights demand** shows them: the period and kind, the totals, a world map shaded by blocked viewers (or plays), the daily trend, the countries and titles ranked, a title's window, rights dates and territories with a Licensed column per country, the window opening and closing marked on the trend, the other refusals, and the CSV.

Blocked viewers and plays share one trend axis because they are the same kind of daily count and the comparison is the point; today is dashed. Filters live in the URL so a view can be shared. ## 2026-11-09 - Publishing policies 2c: refusals counted and the Rights demand API (build queue P2-3, first part; v0.78.0)

What: nothing counted who the play gate or the CDN turned away, so a customer could not see that a title had thousands of viewers waiting in a country it is not licensed for. Now every gate answer and every token the CDN refuses for the wrong country is counted per day, title, country and reason, with unique viewers as hashes, and `GET /api/analytics/rights-demand` reports it beside the plays where the title is licensed.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/availabilityDemand.ts` (new: `recordDemand` adds to an in-memory counter, `flushDemand` writes once a minute; the two rollup tables made in the analytics database on first use; `edgeDemandOf` for CDN lines; `rightsDemandReport` and `rightsDemandCsv`); `modules/apps.ts` (`timedDecide` counts every answer; the gate notes the signed-in viewer and, for an on-demand title answered 404, its status, so expired and restricted titles count as demand after the window); `services/qoe/gateLog.ts` (`GateNote.viewer`, `GateNote.vodStatus`); `services/qoe/cdnLogs.ts` (a 403 on a tokenised manifest checked with `verifyEdgeToken`: a country refusal is geo demand, weighted by the sampling rate); `modules/analytics.ts` (`GET /api/analytics/rights-demand`, titles named per account, each on-demand title's window from `publicDecision` and `rightsForAssetId`).
- **Choices made in the build**: counted in each API task's memory and added to the rollups with `ON CONFLICT ... +`, not through Redis: any number of tasks can write their own minute, the database sees one statement per task per minute, and the gate pays a map lookup instead of a Redis round trip. The rollups stay in Postgres even with `QOE_STORE=clickhouse` (a few rows per title, country and day). The viewer hash is keyed per account with the server secret and is the same every day, so unique viewers over a period are exact; no address is stored. Plays are counted (the gate's allowed answers), not kept by viewer. Edge refusals other than the country one are not demand (expired links, bad tokens, segments); a withdrawn title is counted by the gate. Answers the CDN serves from its cache (the anonymous GET gate) never reach Volt, so a cached refusal is counted once per cache period, not per viewer.
- **Checked**: typecheck clean on the device (volt-app). New `test/rights-demand.check.ts` **40/40**: 300 refusals make no statement and no row until the flush, then 2 statements and 4 rows; geo, rights, plays and demand after the window; unknown and other accounts' ids not counted; 5 unique viewers from 3 sessions, a signed-in viewer with two sessions and the anonymous GET; hashes only; edge: two manifest 403s from Japan on an Australian token logged at 50 % count as 4 requests and 1 viewer, a segment, an expired link and a 200 not; flushes add up; the report's totals, countries, titles with windows and territories, licensed flags, trend, other refusals, filters, an empty period, 400 and 401, CSV, another account sees nothing; off switch. With HEAD's gate and CDN reader, 7 fail and it stops. Neighbours on a copy of HEAD plus this change: cdn-logs 68/68 (its CDN behaviour count updated to 5 for P2's `/t/*`), qoe-reports 71/71, qoe-reports-more 70/70, qoe-sid-route 29/29, block-message 39/39, public-status 71/71, edge-tokens 67/67, access-control 17/17, access-rules 28/28, geo-access 15/15, geo-rights 7/7, cmcd 73/73, fast-access 50/50, player-api 32/32, share-links 34/34, rights-profiles 75/75, rights-preview 50/50, the five vod-ads suites 304/304, migration-safety 60/60, docs.check ok, docs-coverage 335/335, docs-samples 119/119, docs build and scrub clean. scale-check: its other 17 checks pass every run; the warm-start transaction count (91 to 95 against a limit of 25) missed in 3 of 6 runs and misses the same way on HEAD (2 of 4 runs): background timers, not this path.
- **Guides**: developer guide Playback reporting (section 5, Rights demand, with a tested sample; Journey to play is now section 6 and the Player SDK link follows it), Analytics and Rights (the API rows).
- **Christian on the Mac**: on the staging stack with tokens on, play a title from a blocked country (`X-Test-Country`) a few times, wait a minute, then `GET /api/analytics/rights-demand` with an account token. Edge refusals need CloudFront real-time logs: checked on AWS staging.
- **Not done**: P2-4 (the report in the console: map, ranked table, trend, drill down, windows overlay, CSV button, guide and screenshots, the openapi overlay entry). Drill down by series, content type and rights holder needs those in the rollups or a join at report time (P2-4). Live blackouts are counted (channels and events refused by the gate) but not shown apart.

Now every refusal says what to show, viewers outside a title's territories see the account's own block message with an optional Notify me, and the CDN's refusals say why in the same words.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/blockMessage.ts` (new: the account's block message and Notify me, cached on the viewer path; notify requests and the per-title summary); `modules/apps.ts` (the gate's 451 body with `code`, `message`, `notify` and `meta`; 403 Scheduled with `until`, `message` and `notify`; which refusal from `classifyGate`, so a title's own paid or entitled access is 402; POST sends refusals that have a mode instead of throwing them; `POST /api/availability/notify`); `modules/rights.ts` (`GET`/`PUT /api/rights/settings` carry `blockMessage`, `blockMessageDefault`, `notifyMe`, `notifyRequests`; `defaultProfileId` optional on PUT; audited); `services/edgeTokens.ts` (`EDGE_REFUSAL_TEXT`); migration `0152_block_message_and_notify.sql` (`accounts.block_message` nullable, `accounts.block_notify` default false, `availability_notify` table; add only); `deploy/staging/edge-token.js` (`refusal`, `words`), `nginx.conf.template` (`js_set $edge_refusal`, `return 403 $edge_refusal`, text/plain); `volt-player/src/sdk/resolver.ts` (the player's locked screen shows the gate's `message`); `volt-web/src/pages/Rights.tsx` (Where a title cannot play, below the profiles), `rights/rightsApi.ts`, `rights/rights.css`; `scripts/seed-docs.ts` (a message, Notify me on and eight waiting viewers for the guide).

## 2026-11-09 - Publishing policies 2: play tokens at the edge (build queue P2, first part; v0.76.0)

What: the play gate decided who may play an on-demand title, but the URLs it handed out were the plain CDN paths, so a copied URL played for anyone, anywhere, after the window closed and after a title was withdrawn. Now the gate's on-demand URLs carry a short signed token in their path and the edge checks it on every manifest and segment request.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/edgeTokens.ts` (new: mint, the reference check, the folder a token opens, the URL with the token, the policy class, the revocation set, the sync, a file store for staging); `modules/apps.ts` (the play gate tokenises on-demand `delivery` when `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS` is on; ad sessions keep their own addresses); `services/publicStatus.ts` (a title leaving Available writes a revocation); `services/orchestrator/index.ts` (the edge sync also writes the token key `k:pt` and `r:<asset id>` revocations, to the KVS or, with `EDGE_STORE_FILE`, to a file); migration `0151_edge_revocations.sql` (new table, add only); `deploy/edge/vod-token-viewer-request.js` (new CloudFront Function); `deploy/terraform/edge_tokens.tf` (new: `edge_tokens` variable, the function on the rights KVS, an origin request policy forwarding the viewer country), `s3_cloudfront.tf` (behaviours `/t/*.m3u8`, `/t/*.mpd` with the manifest cache policy and `/t/*` with the segment one, listed first), `ecs_services.tf` (`VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS`), `env-coverage.json` and `terraform.tfvars.example`; `deploy/staging/edge-token.js` (new njs), `nginx.conf.template` (njs loaded, `/t/<token>/clips/` checked and stripped, any other `/t/` 403), `docker-compose.yml` (tokens on, the shared volume read only in the edge).
- **How it works**: the token is `<base64url claims>.<first 32 hex of HMAC-SHA256>`, the same shape as the live rights-class claim. Claims: asset, the folder it opens (`/vod/<id>/` or `/clips/vod/<id>/`), country, policy class (open, members, premium, rights), DRM flag (0: on-demand packaging is clear today), sid, issued, expiry = the earlier of 4 hours (24 for open content; `EDGE_TOKEN_TTL_SEC`, `EDGE_TOKEN_OPEN_TTL_SEC`) and the end of the effective window. It sits in the path, so the relative segment names in every Volt manifest carry it with no player code, Safari's native HLS included; the edge strips it, so caches key on the plain path and viewers share segments. Refused with 403: bad signature, expired, more than a minute early, outside its folder (or `/../`), a known viewer country other than the token's, issued at or before the title's revocation time. No key in the store gives 503: without the key a forged "open" token would open anything. A revocation is kept as long as a token can live, then removed from the store and the table. A cached anonymous gate answer never outlives the token in it.
- **Choices made in the build**: tokens in the path rather than the query or cookies (no player changes, works in native players, shared cache); "issue" mode first (plain paths still play) because the console and share previews do not carry tokens yet, so refusing plain paths is P2-2; on AWS `edge_tokens = issue` by default, on a plain Mac run tokens stay off unless `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=issue`; the edge only checks the country when it knows it (an unknown country is not a mismatch); 4-hour lifetime until token renewal and the policy's lifetime (P3) exist.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app). New `edge-tokens.check.ts` **60/60**: claims, the window clamp, lifetimes, folders, URLs, classes; the TypeScript reference, the real CloudFront Function file and the real staging njs file give the same verdict on valid, tampered, re-signed with another key, claims changed, expired (and one second before), the window's end, early, another folder, climbing out, another country, no country, garbage, revoked and issued after the revocation, and no key; over HTTP: tokens off leaves URLs as they were, on gives both URLs one token for that folder, country, sid and window (POST and GET), a 20-minute window ends the token with it, withdrawing writes a revocation the sync puts in the store and the staging edge refuses the old token, republished plays, an expired revocation leaves store and table, a never-public title is not revoked, a warm gate with tokens on reads nothing. With the gate and status code from HEAD the suite fails (2 failures, then stops). **The staging edge for real**: nginx 1.24 with njs 0.8.2 in the container (the compose file uses nginx 1.27.5-alpine, which ships njs), the staging template rendered with local upstreams, the API behind it with `VOLT_EDGE_TOKENS=issue` and `EDGE_STORE_FILE`: the scheduler's own sync wrote the key; manifest, media playlist, segment and DASH 200 from AU and from no country; NZ 403; a token whose window ended 403; garbage, another title's folder, `../` and `/t/<token>/api/health` 403; the plain path still 200 (issue mode); withdrawn through the admin API: `r:<id>` in the store within 51 s, the old token 403 and the gate 404; republished: a new token 200, the old one still 403. Neighbours: public-status 71/71, edge-rights 38/38, edge-variant-manifests 73/73, edge-variants 62/62, edge-viewas 5/5, rights-preview 50/50 (49/50 once, then 50/50: timing), rights-profiles 75/75, access-rules 28/28, migration-safety 60/60, vod-ads 91/91, vod-ads-sgai 51/51, vod-ads-vmap 56/56, vod-ads-catchup 47/47, fast-access 50/50, share-links 34/34, player-api 32/32; scale-check all 17 checks with tokens on, twice; docs-coverage 333/333; docs build and scrub clean; `terraform fmt` and `validate` clean. terraform-env: 29 ok, 2 fail on five settings missing from env-coverage.json, three already at HEAD (SITE_STAGING_DIR, ARCHIVE_BUILD_DIR, SITE_PACKAGE_MAX_FILES) and two from the other session's uncommitted work (FRAME_FACES, VOLT_LIBRARY_TRASH_DAYS); this item's four new settings are covered. vod-ads-skins needs the player's npm packages, not installed in the container.
- **Guide**: the developer guide The play gate and playback tokens has a new step 7, On-demand addresses carry a token, and a troubleshooting row for a 403 from the CDN.
- **Christian**: on the Mac staging stack (`deploy/staging`, `docker compose up -d --build`): play an on-demand title through the edge, then try its URL with `X-Test-Country` set to another country (403), withdraw it and try again after a minute (403), republish (plays). On AWS staging after the next apply: the same with a VPN for the country; and please check in the CloudFront console that the viewer-country header reaches the function on the `/t/*` behaviours (the new origin request policy forwards it; the live rights function's manifest behaviours have no such policy, worth a look in the same run).
- **Not done**: P2-2 (the refusal answers and block message, notify-me, enforcing tokens once the console and share previews carry them, token-free), P2-3 (counting refusals and the Rights demand report). Ad sessions keep their own (per viewer) addresses, and share links and console previews hand out plain paths until P2-2.

Both now go through the viewer-path cache like the rest of the gate, so a warm gate for an on-demand title reads nothing from the database, with or without an ad rule.

A "no rule" answer is cached too, so turning a rule on is seen at once through the trigger. ## 2026-11-09 - Publishing policies 1: lifecycle and public status (build queue P1, first part; v0.75.0)

What: until now a title's public face was decided by several separate fields (published, the availability state, the window, the rights), and the play gate told any caller with a key that a draft or expired title existed (451 with a reason; the access check even said 200). Now Volt computes one public status per title, with a reason, and only Available titles (and teasers past their Listed from date) are public; everything else is answered as an id that never existed.

- **Where**: `PUBLISHING_POLICIES.md` (the design, saved from the design doc); migrations `0148_public_status.sql` (vod_assets: `listed_from`, `unlisted`, `archived_at`, `archived_by`, `public_status`, `status_reason`, `status_until`, `status_at`; add only) and `0149_public_status_index.sql` (concurrent); `volt-app/src/services/publicStatus.ts` (new); `services/availability.ts` (publish and withdraw store the status, the sweep runs `sweepPublicStatus`); `services/viewerPath.ts` (the cached decision row carries review, media, listing and archive); `modules/apps.ts` (the play gate and the access check); `modules/vodAds.ts` (guided playlists); `modules/rights.ts` (a profile change marks its assets for the sweep); guides `docs-site/docs/api/play-gate.md` and `guides/rights.md`; `volt-app/test/public-status.check.ts` (new).
- **How it works**: `computePublicStatus(row, rights, now)` is pure. The editorial state is derived (archived when `archived_at` is set; published for scheduled, available and expired, and the old draft rows with `published = true`; draft otherwise, withdrawn included, dates kept), so the stored status can never contradict it. A draft takes its review state; a published title is processing until its media is ready, expired after its window, restricted when the rights do not allow on demand, allow no territory, have ended or have not opened yet, scheduled before its window, else available. The effective window is the publish window inside the rights window; `status_until` is the next moment the answer can change. The gate computes it from the cached row and cached profile (no database read when warm) and caps its edge cache at that moment; lists use the stored columns through `publicListSql`, which is exact between sweeps. The sweep (once a minute) recomputes up to 500 rows that are due, never computed, or changed since (`updated_at > status_at`).
- **Choices made in the build**: a Scheduled title past its Listed from date answers 403 with `mode: scheduled`, `availableFrom` and `meta`, never a token (the design's "403 not yet available, with until"); a title whose media failed, is missing or unplayable counts as processing (not public); a territory allow-list is not a status (the gate refuses by country with 451, as before); share links are left as they are (they are reviewer links, decided on their own); deleted titles have no row, so they were already unknown.
- **Checked**: `public-status.check.ts` 64/64 (pure: every status, reasons, next moments, rights narrowing, one title over time, visible and listed; HTTP: every hidden status gives the same 404 and body as an unknown id on POST and GET `/api/availability`, `/api/public/availability` and `/api/access/check`; available and unlisted play; a teaser; publish, withdraw, archive and both restores; the sweep for an ending window, rows never computed and rows changed; a rights profile change; the catalogue SQL; warm gate reads). Against the code before this change 22 of them fail. Neighbour suites green (listed in BUILD_QUEUE.md P1); review-qc 49/51, rights-preview 49/50 and vod-library-pure 169/170 fail identically with these changes taken out. Typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web); docs coverage 333/333 and scrub clean on the device.
- **Christian**: nothing needed for this part. When P1-2 lands you can archive and restore titles and set Listed from in the console.
- **Not done**: P1-2 (the admin API: status in the asset and list routes, Listed from and Unlisted on the availability route, archive and restore, the who-can-watch tester; the Availability tab and library filter; the openapi overlay), all in files with another session's uncommitted edits. ## 2026-11-09 - Hybrid Sites: site stages on the Workflows map and report (build queue H5-2, (b); v0.73.0)

Site encodes were listed by job type under Other background work, and copies to the customer's storage were not counted at all. Both now have a stage in a fourth lane, **Your sites**, for the accounts licensed for them.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/workflows/map.ts` (lane `site`; stages `siteenc` "Encode at site", licence `hybrid_sites`, col 2 under the cloud Encode, and `archive` "Archive copies", licence `mirror_archive`, col 10 past Publish; `StageDef.licence`; arrows `rules -> siteenc -> qc` and `publish -> archive`; `JOB_STAGES` gains `site.vod-probe`, `site.vod-encode`, `archive.copy`, `archive.copy-folder`, `archive.verify`, `archive.verify-folder`, `archive.recording`; new `mapFor(licences)`); `src/modules/workflows.ts` (`GET /map` serves `mapFor` of the account's licences); `src/services/workflows/rollup.ts` (the job query also joins `archive_copies` by `payload.copyId` for `archive.*` jobs with no stream or asset; the report reads the account's own map, gives a stageless older row the stage its type has now, and puts the work of a stage the account no longer sees under other jobs).

## 2026-11-09 - Hybrid Sites: fall back to cloud encoding (build queue H5, (a); v0.72.0)

What: HYBRID_SITES.md "When the site is offline". An item in an Encode at site folder used to wait for the site for ever. A folder can now have a fallback time: when no server of the site has taken an item's encode within it, the site's agent sends the original and Volt encodes it in the cloud. Without one (the default) the item still waits and the original never leaves the customer's storage. H5 was split: the DRM key proxy, Workflows stages and the docs are H5-2.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteFallback.ts` (new: `sweepSiteFallbacks`, `pendingFallbacks`, `fallbackItemForUpload`, `landFallbackSource`, `fallbackFailed`, `fallbackMinutesOf`); migration `0147_site_encode_fallback.sql` (`watchfolders.fallback_minutes`, 5 to 10080 or null); `services/jobs/worker.ts` (the sweep runs with the reaper); `modules/siteTus.ts` (a `fallback 1` upload is opened for the registered item as a `site_file` session and landed by `landFallbackSource`); `modules/sites.ts` (`GET /api/site-agent/watchfolders/:id/fallbacks`, `POST .../fallbacks/:itemId`); `modules/watchfolders.ts` (`fallbackMinutes` on create and patch, on-prem folders only); `services/ingest/pipeline.ts` (`queueEncodeFor` queues nothing while the original is asked for); `services/sites/siteWatchfolders.ts` (`mediaTypeOf` exported); `volt-agent/src/siteWatch.ts` (step 4c, `FolderPass.fellBack`), agent 0.11.0; `volt-web/src/pages/Watchfolders.tsx` (**If the site does not encode it**).
- **Choices made in the build**: no fallback unless a person sets a time, because the design says the source leaves the customer's network only when a policy says so (Christian's answer was that failing over to the cloud is fine; the default is the conservative reading of the design). "Waited" counts from when the current job was queued or last returned by the reaper, so a running encode never falls back. Only the asset's own encode falls back; a FAST ladder variant made at a site waits (the asset already plays). The original keeps its place at the site (`ingest.siteSource`) and is kept in the cloud under `vod-src/site/<asset>/<name>` (the bucket on AWS through `landVodSource`), so later re-encodes run in the cloud. A file replaced at the site fails the asset (it is never encoded from other content); the folder takes the new content in as a new item. No new state column: the request lives in the asset's `ingest` JSON.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/site-fallback.check.ts` **45/45** (fails at HEAD: no module): the real agent and API; the fallback time's range and that only on-prem folders have one; none set, ten hours waiting changes nothing; with 15 minutes a 20-minute wait falls back, a 10-minute wait and a running encode stay, the job is cancelled with why, the asset and audit log record it, sweeping again does nothing, `queueEncodeFor` queues nothing meanwhile; another site sees nothing; an upload Volt did not ask for is refused; the agent sends it, byte for byte, as the asset's original, the cloud `vod.encode` is queued with it, no second asset, the original still at the site; a replaced file fails the asset with nothing kept; a deleted file is reported and fails it. Around it: site-encode-placement 42/42, site-registration-sidecar 34/34, site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, site-jobs 67/67, site-encode 66/66, site-encode-worker 35/35, site-reencode 21/21, site-ladder-variant 40/40, sites 88/88, watchfolder-api 26/26, watchfolder-defaults 82/82, jobs 43/43, tus 21/21, uploads 49/49, upload-staging 42/42, ingest-core 97/97; site-encode-bucket skipped (no moto in this container). Browser 9/9 in Chromium against the real API: the control shows only for Encode at site, defaults to wait, saves 30 minutes with its help text, is kept across a placement change, its row fits at 390 px, no page errors. Docs scrub clean on docs, coverage 62/62.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites (new "When the site does not encode it: fall back to the cloud", three troubleshooting rows), Watchfolders (the control, the API field, the Processing row). Screenshots not retaken: the watchfolder page screenshot predates the control.
- **Christian on the Mac**: an Encode at site folder with a fallback time of 15 minutes and the agent running without `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE`: after about 15 minutes the agent's log says the file was sent to Volt, the item encodes in the cloud and plays; the audit log has the fallback.
- **Not done (H5-2)**: the DRM key proxy, site stages on the Workflows map and W1 report, the guide and runbook pages; "encoded in the cloud because ..." on the asset page (with H4-8's label, same files). Seen in the browser check: the folder page's rules list is wider than a phone screen (it was before this change).

## 2026-11-09 - Hybrid Sites: the ingest sidecar of a registered original (build queue H4-7, (c); v0.71.0)

What: an upload gets its ingest sidecar (`<file>.json` or `.xml`, as the ingest rules say) written beside the original in the cloud. A file registered from an Encode at site folder stays at the site, so it got none. It now gets the same sidecar, written beside the original at the site by the agent.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/ingest/pipeline.ts` (`ingestLanded`: for a site source the payload is made, kept as `ingest.siteSidecar` `{ name, format, mediaType, sha256, text }`, at most `SITE_SIDECAR_MAX_BYTES` 256 KB; the payload names the original by its key at the site, `store: "site"`, `naming.renamed: false`); `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteWatchfolders.ts` (`registrationOf` answers `sidecar`; `isKnown` is also true for the exact sidecar of an asset registered from the folder); `volt-agent/src/siteWatch.ts` (`writeSidecar`, called after a registration; `FolderPass.sidecars`); agent 0.10.0.
- **Choices made in the build**: the sidecar goes beside the original at the site, as an upload's goes beside it in the cloud, because that is where the file is and where systems watching the folder look. The cloud makes the text (one payload builder) and the agent only writes it, under one allowed name and only when the text matches its checksum. A file of that name already at the site is the customer's and is never replaced. To keep the default companion rule from attaching Volt's own sidecar to its own video, the cloud answers `known` for its exact name and text (the agent's memory is only a shortcut); an edited sidecar is a new file. No migration: the sidecar lives in the asset's `ingest` JSON. Webhooks for site-kept originals now carry the site key and `store: "site"` instead of the guid name the file never had.
- **Checked**: volt-app typecheck clean on the device; volt-agent typecheck clean in the container. New `test/site-registration-sidecar.check.ts` **34/34** (with the two cloud files at HEAD, 14 fail): the real agent and API, a folder with the built-in default rules; the sidecar kept, written byte for byte through `.part`, the ingest payload with the asset and the original's checksum, named as at the site; the original untouched; the next pass skips it, and so does one with the agent's memory lost; `known` false for other content or another name; an edited sidecar taken by the companion rule; xml when the rules say xml, nothing when none (answer `sidecar: null`); registering again answers the same sidecar; a held duplicate's sidecar says duplicate; the agent keeps a customer's file, writes once, refuses another name and a checksum mismatch. Around it, in one run: 34/34 suites, 1198 assertions (site-encode-placement 42/42, site-encode 66/66, site-encode-worker 35/35, site-ladder-variant 40/40, site-reencode 21/21, site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, sites 88/88, site-jobs 67/67, ingest-core 97/97 and the other ingest suites, uploads 49/49, upload-staging 42/42, the watchfolder suites, filename-metadata 120/120); site-encode-bucket 34/34 with moto. Docs scrub clean, coverage 62/62.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites, Encode at site (the sidecar beside the original, not taken in again, never over your own file). HYBRID_SITES.md phase 4g; volt-agent README.
- **Christian on the Mac**: with the agent (0.10.0) running, drop a video on an Encode at site folder: `<name>.json` appears beside it within a pass, and the next passes do not list it in the folder's items.
- **Not done (H4-8)**: "at site" beside Processing on the asset page (its files carry another session's uncommitted edits), the Mac Docker and kill-the-agent runs, GPU on AWS staging. A sidecar whose registration answer was lost (the agent stopped between the call and the write) is not written later.

Its ladder variant (every rung in the 16:9 box at the ladder's frame rate) is now made at the site that keeps the original, and lands beside the asset exactly where `vod.ladder` puts one made in the cloud.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteEncode.ts` (`SiteEncodeRequest.variant`, `requestSiteEncode` variant branch keyed `site.vod-probe:<asset>:ladders/...`, `judgeVariantProbe`, `verifyPackage` counting the ladder's rungs, `finishVariant`, `siteEncodeAttemptFailed` failing only the variant's row); `volt-app/src/services/jobs/handlers/vodLadder.ts` (the planning pieces exported and shared: `setRow`, `variantLadder`, `ladderNotes`, `variantFolder`, `ladderEncoderSettings`; the cloud `vod.ladder` path behaves as before); `volt-app/src/services/fast/prepare.ts` (readiness, Prepare and in-flight for site variants).

## 2026-11-08 - Hybrid Sites: FAST re-encodes of a site-kept original go to its site (build queue H4-5, first part; v0.69.0)

What: an asset made by Encode at site keeps its original only at the site. When a FAST channel needs it encoded again (aligned for splicing, broadcast loudness, 1 s segments for a slate or loop), Volt used to say "no source" and leave it; it now sends the encode to the site that keeps the original. Also fixed: a site package that replaced an earlier one deleted the FAST ladder packages kept in the asset's folder.

- **Choices made in the build**: only the asset's own encode is redone at a site. A FAST ladder variant (`vod.ladder`) is a different encode (every rung, 16:9 box, the ladder's frame rate, its own folder and row), so making it at a site needs a variant mode in the site jobs; until then readiness says the site encodes it only to its own ladder and it airs on its own encode (H4-6). The asset reads Processing while the site works, as on first ingest, and keeps playing its current package until the new one is verified and swapped in.
