## 2026-11-08 - Hybrid Sites: the package straight into the bucket, large files streamed (build queue H4-4, (c) and (e); v0.68.0)

What: Encode at site now works on a Volt that keeps its media in the origin bucket (AWS). The site's server PUTs each file of the finished package straight to the bucket with a presigned URL; the cloud reads it back, checks every checksum, verifies the package and records it exactly as on the single Mac server. The agent streams large files instead of reading them into memory. The "at site" label on the asset page, re-encodes of a site-kept original and the Mac Docker runs are split to H4-5.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteEncode.ts` (the refusal `site_encode_needs_local_store` removed; `SITE_STAGING_PREFIX`, `stagingDirFor` naming `s3://<bucket>/site-staging/<asset>/<job>-a<n>` when `VOD_STORE` is s3, `localCopyFor`, `removeStaging`, `readBackFromBucket`; `declareOutputs` writes s3:// transfer URIs; `judgeEncode` reads back, hashes, verifies, then swaps in and finishes as before), `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteJobs.ts` (`transferUrl` signs `x-amz-checksum-sha256` for an agent that asks with `signedHeaders: true` and returns `headers`; `presignS3` takes signed headers kept out of the query string; `outputs` removes an earlier attempt's staging wherever it is), `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteWatchfolders.ts` (comment), `volt-agent/src/siteEncode.ts` (`STREAM_ABOVE_BYTES`, `VOLT_SITE_STREAM_ABOVE_MB`, headers from the answer sent as given, streamed PUT with Content-Length), `volt-agent/src/site.ts` and `package.json` (0.9.0), `deploy/terraform/s3_cloudfront.tf` (lifecycle rule `site-staging`, 3 days), `volt-app/test/site-encode-bucket.check.ts` (new), `volt-app/test/site-encode.check.ts` (the bucket refusal check now checks the staging place).
- **Choices made in the build**: the cloud reads the package back from the bucket and runs the unchanged `verifyPackage` and `finishEncode` on a local copy rather than verifying in place and copying inside the bucket. Same request cost (the copy to `vod/<id>/` is one PUT per file either way, and GETs in region are cheap and carry no transfer charge), one verification and one finish for both stores, and the checksums are proved by the cloud's own hash, not only by S3's header check. The package passes through the control plane's disk once, as a cloud encode's does today; reading it straight from the bucket belongs with 2.1a-4. The checksum header is only signed for an agent that says it sends it, so an older agent's uploads are not refused for a header it leaves off. The complete request holds while the package is read back (as it already held for local verification and the finish); for very long films a cloud verify job would free it (noted, not built).
- **Checked**: typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit` and volt-web `tsc -b` on the device, volt-agent `tsc --noEmit` in the container); `terraform fmt -check` and `terraform validate` clean (1.9.8, in the container). New `test/site-encode-bucket.check.ts` **34/34**, three runs, on Postgres 16 with moto 5.2.5 as the bucket: accepted with the bucket (was 409), probe and encode in one pass, `master_key` `vod/<id>/master.m3u8`, the package under its staging prefix, every PUT straight to the bucket and none through the API, each URL signing the file's SHA-256 and the agent sending it, no credential on any upload, every declared file under `vod/<id>/` at its size with its playlist type, staging and the read-back folder gone; bytes changed on the way caught by the read-back hash and a file never uploaded both refused with the staging emptied, the third attempt ready; files over the threshold streamed with their length to the bucket and to the API's transfer URL with VOD_STORE local, both verified; an agent without `signedHeaders` gets a host-only URL; the remover touches nothing outside one attempt's `site-staging/` prefix; the lifecycle rule. At HEAD it fails 3 and stops (the encode refused with 409). Unchanged: site-encode 63/63, site-encode-worker 35/35, site-encode-placement 42/42, site-jobs 67/67, sites 88/88, site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, mirror-archive 72/72, mirror-archive-s3 50/50 (moto with IAM auth), mirror-archive-packages 69/69, mirror-archive-clips 37/37, mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, mirror-archive-rules 45/45, mirror-archive-sidecars 36/36, jobs 43/43, bucket-tracks 28/28. Docs scrub clean, coverage 62 of 62.
- **Not checked here**: real S3 refusing a wrong checksum with BadDigest (moto does not check the header; the suite proves the cloud's own hash catches it), Docker and GPU.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites, "Encoding at the site" (how the finished streams reach Volt, what to allow through the firewall) and two troubleshooting rows (an upload refused with 403, a file that does not match its checksum). HYBRID_SITES.md phase 4d.
- **Christian**: on AWS staging, set an on-prem folder to Encode at site and drop a file with agent 0.9.0: the objects appear under `site-staging/` then `vod/<id>/`, the staging prefix empties, the asset plays from the CDN. On the Mac the H4-3 Docker run still applies.
- **Not done (H4-5)**: "at site" beside Processing on the asset page (its files carry another session's uncommitted edits), a re-encode or new ladder of a site-kept original sent to its site, the ingest sidecar for a registration, the Mac Docker and kill-the-agent runs, GPU on AWS staging; multipart upload for a package file over 5 GB (none today).

## 2026-11-08 - Hybrid Sites: Encode at site on on-prem watchfolders (build queue H4-3, first part; v0.67.0)

What: an on-prem watchfolder can now be set to Encode at site. Its videos and audio are not uploaded: the site agent registers each one with its facts, Volt runs the ingest rules on them, makes the Media Library item and has the site encode it; only the finished streams come to Volt and the original stays on the customer's storage. Images, captions, slates and loops in the same folder still upload. The bucket on AWS, large package files and the Mac Docker runs are split to H4-4.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteWatchfolders.ts` (`PLACEMENTS_BUILT` with `encode_site`, `registersAtSite`, `pendingRegistrations`, `registerSiteItem`, `sitePathOf`; `pendingUploads` and `itemForUpload` leave registered kinds out), `volt-app/src/modules/sites.ts` (`GET /api/site-agent/watchfolders/:id/registrations`, `POST .../registrations/:itemId`), `volt-app/src/services/ingest/pipeline.ts` (`LandedFile.atSite`, `SiteSource`; `queueEncodeFor` routes a site-kept original to `requestSiteEncode`), `volt-app/src/services/ingest/metadata.ts` (`extractFromFacts`, `probeFromSite`), `volt-app/src/modules/watchfolders.ts` (comment), `volt-agent/src/siteWatch.ts` (step 4b, `REGISTER_HEAD_BYTES`), `volt-agent/src/siteEncode.ts` (`siteProbe`), `volt-agent/src/site.ts`, `volt-web/src/pages/Watchfolders.tsx` (the option enabled, its help), `volt-app/test/site-encode-placement.check.ts` (new), `volt-app/test/site-watchfolders.check.ts` (three expectations that were "not yet").
- **How it runs**: each pass, for an Encode at site folder, the agent asks for accepted, unregistered video and audio, checks the file is still the size accepted, reads its first 192 KiB and, when an encoder is configured, probes it with the same runner the jobs use (two minutes at most; none: registered without a probe), and posts them. The cloud refuses changed content (409), makes the asset as an upload from the folder would (its ladder and rights profile), runs `ingestLanded` with `atSite` and then `requestSiteEncode` with the path at the site (`config.path` joined with the key, Windows paths kept as such). A duplicate or rejection holds as for an upload; the item closes `ingested` with a note either way.
- **Choices made in the build**: the original's place is kept in `ingest.siteSource` rather than a new column, so no migration; the asset's `source_key` stays empty, which keeps every cloud path that reads an original (encode, copy rules, retention) from trying to. A registration race keeps the first asset and removes the second. If the encode cannot be queued (site disabled, or a server whose packages go to a bucket, until H4-4) the asset fails with that reason rather than reading Processing for ever. The probe at registration duplicates the probe job's; kept so duration and picture size rules decide before the asset exists.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean (volt-app `tsc --noEmit` and volt-web `tsc -b` on the device, volt-agent `tsc --noEmit` in the container). New `test/site-encode-placement.check.ts` **42/42**, three runs, on Postgres 16 in the container: the real agent watch pass and encode worker through the real API with the real `vod_encoder.py` and ffmpeg (a stub writes Shaka's package): the placement offered and accepted, the video registered and not uploaded, no upload session, the asset with no cloud original and its site path, the facts (checksum, type sniffed, the 6 s 360-line probe), Processing, the probe job in the site's pool, the site worker then encoding it to ready with the original untouched, the image in the same folder uploaded, registering twice, changed size or content, another site's host, a head over the limit, a tus upload of a video refused, registration without a probe, an upload folder refusing registrations, a duplicate held then encoded at the site once kept, an ID3 title from the head, the probe mapping, POSIX and Windows paths. At HEAD it fails at the second check (422) and then stops (no `siteProbe`). Unchanged: site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, site-encode 63/63, site-encode-worker 35/35, sites 88/88, site-jobs 67/67, mirror-archive 72/72, mirror-archive-rules 45/45, and 23 ingest, upload and watchfolder suites (ingest-core, ingest-extract, ingest-s3, ingest-sources, uploads, upload-staging, upload-retry, watchfolder-video, watchfolder-video-landing and others) 1116 assertions, 0 failed. Browser (Chromium, the real API and console): the on-prem folder page offers Encode at site, saves it and shows its help, 4/4.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites, "Encoding at the site" (how registration works, what stays at the site); Watchfolders (the placement, two troubleshooting rows replacing the "arrives with site encoders" one, the API note). Scrub check clean, coverage 62 of 62.
- **Christian on the Mac**: set an on-prem folder to Encode at site, start the agent with `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE=volt-encoder:<tag> volt-agent site run`, drop a video: the folder's queue shows it registered (no upload), Docker encodes it, it plays from the local origin, the original stays in the folder.
- **Not done (H4-4)**: the package straight into the bucket on AWS, streaming large package files, the Mac Docker and kill-the-agent runs, GPU on AWS staging. Also open: "at site" beside Processing on the asset page (its files carry another session's uncommitted edits), a re-encode or new ladder of a site-kept original, the ingest sidecar for a registration.

## 2026-11-08 - Hybrid Sites: the site worker (build queue H4-2, first part; v0.66.0)

What: the site agent can now encode at the site. It takes the site's probe and encode jobs, runs the Volt encoder on the file where it sits, and uploads only the packaged streams, which the cloud checks before the asset plays (H4). A site server needs Docker and the encoder image name. Choosing Encode at site on an on-prem watchfolder, the bucket on AWS and the Mac Docker run are split to H4-3.

- **Where**: `volt-agent/src/siteEncode.ts` (new: `encodePass`, `encoderRunner`, `dockerArgs`), `volt-agent/src/site.ts` (the encode loop, agent 0.7.0), `volt-app/src/services/jobs/queue.ts` (`claimInPool` takes `{ gpu }`; `reapExpiredLeases` hands site encode jobs to `siteEncodeAttemptFailed`), `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteJobs.ts` (the lease reads the host's reported GPUs), `volt-app/test/site-encode-worker.check.ts` (new), `volt-agent/README.md`.
- **How it runs**: a probe job runs `vod_encoder.py` with `probe_only` and returns the probe; an encode job runs it with the cloud's settings and only this host's paths (`<scratch>/<job>-a<attempt>/{out,tmp,job.json}`). With `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE`: `docker run --rm --network none [--gpus all] -v <source folder>:/src:ro -v <scratch>:/work --entrypoint python3 <image> /app/bin/vod_encoder.py /work/job.json`. The encoder's `progress encode N%` lines become progress (encode is the first 80 %, upload the rest), reported at most every 10 s, which keeps the 120 s lease; a 409 or 404 on progress kills the encoder and nothing more is sent. Every file is hashed, declared 1000 per request (`append` after the first), uploaded through its own transfer URL without a credential (a presigned bucket URL would refuse one), then the job completes; a refused package is left to the cloud's retry.
- **Choices made in the build**: a missing file is permanent (`source_missing`) but a missing folder is a retry (`source_unavailable`: a share not mounted on the host that took the job); an encoder that gives no JSON answer is a retry (`encoder_failed`), while an answer that says it cannot read the file goes to the cloud, which fails the asset for good (H4). A GPU job is never encoded on the CPU: the lease does not hand it to a host that reported no NVIDIA GPU, and a host whose agent finds none hands it back (`no_gpu`). Scratch is removed when the job ends either way (verified: it is in the cloud; failed: the retry starts clean). Each file is read whole before its PUT (segments are small); streaming big files is listed under H4-3.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`, volt-agent `tsc --noEmit`). New `test/site-encode-worker.check.ts` **35/35**, three runs in a row, on Postgres 16 in the container: the real agent worker through the real API with the real `vod_encoder.py` and ffmpeg (a stub writes Shaka's package, as in site-encode): the Docker command and job file (through a stand-in for docker), a whole encode end to end (probe, plan, 14 files declared 4 per request, verified first time, asset ready, scratch gone), a refused package redone, GPU jobs only to GPU hosts and handed back by a host without one, missing file, unmounted share and a broken encoder, the lease reaped mid-encode (encoder stopped, job retried, then ready), a reaped lease dropping a half declaration, reaped on the last attempt failing the asset. With the two cloud changes taken out it fails 3 (32/35). Unchanged: site-encode 63/63, site-jobs 67/67, sites 88/88, site-watchfolders 67/67, site-watchfolder-files 30/30, mirror-archive 72/72, mirror-archive-s3 29/29, mirror-archive-clips 37/37, -packages 51/51, -recordings 34/34, -rules 45/45, -sidecars 36/36, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, encode-ladder 50/50, encode-store 24/24.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites: new "Encoding at the site" (preparing a server: Docker, `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE`, the scratch folder) and four troubleshooting rows. Scrub check clean, coverage 0 errors.
- **Christian on the Mac**: optional until H4-3 brings the watchfolder choice: build the encoder image and start the agent with `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE=volt-encoder:<tag> volt-agent site run` to check Docker starts it on macOS (the Mac's file sharing must include the drop folder and `~/.volt-agent`).
- **Not done (H4-3)**: Encode at site on on-prem watchfolders (registration without upload, the source as a site location), the package straight into the bucket on AWS, streaming large files, the Mac Docker and kill-the-agent runs, GPU on AWS staging.

## 2026-11-08 - Hybrid Sites: site encoding, the cloud side (build queue H4, first part; v0.65.0)

What: the control plane can now have a file encoded at a customer's site and take back only the packaged output, checked before the asset can play. The site worker itself, the Encode at site placement on on-prem watchfolders and the Mac Docker runs are split to H4-2, so nothing changes yet for customers.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/jobs/handlers/vodEncode.ts` (`planEncode`, `encoderJobFor`, `finishEncode`, `EncodePlan`; the handler calls them in the same order with the same log lines), `volt-app/src/services/sites/siteEncode.ts` (new: `requestSiteEncode`, `judgeSiteEncode`, `declareOutputs`, `verifyPackage`, `siteEncodeAttemptFailed`), `services/sites/siteJobs.ts` (site encode jobs judged in `complete`; `outputs`; failures fail the asset on the last attempt), `modules/sites.ts` (`POST /api/site-agent/jobs/:id/outputs`).
- **How it runs**: `site.vod-probe` in the site's pool: the worker probes with `vod_encoder.py` and returns the probe. The cloud fits the ladder with the cloud encode's own `planEncode` and queues `site.vod-encode` with the plan and the encoder job file minus its paths. The worker declares the package (relative paths, sizes, SHA-256; in parts with `append` so a long film fits the 1 MB body limit), each file becomes an upload transfer of that job into `clips/vod/<asset>.site-<job>-a<attempt>`, held to its checksum as it arrives. On complete the cloud verifies, swaps the folder in whole and runs `finishEncode` with no source: HLS and DASH, no MP4, stills and sprite sheets from the packaged master, QC and the follow-up jobs as after a cloud encode; `encode_result.encodedAt` names the site and job.
- **Choices made in the build**: verification lists every problem it finds (up to 50) so the job's error says what was wrong; it checks every declared file at its size, the master's rendition count against the plan (at least one for sound only), every playlist, map and segment named by the master and its media playlists, segment counts within one of the duration over the segment length, `#EXT-X-ENDLIST` on media playlists and a static MPD. A failed attempt discards its staging folder and clears its declaration, so the next attempt starts clean. With the origin bucket (AWS) a site encode is refused (`site_encode_needs_local_store`) rather than passing every byte through the API; going straight to the bucket is H4-2. No migration.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app `tsc --noEmit`, volt-web `tsc -b`). New `test/site-encode.check.ts` **63/63** on Postgres 16, playing the site worker through the real API with the real `vod_encoder.py` and ffmpeg (a stub writes Shaka's package in the container, which has no Shaka; on the Mac `PACKAGER_BIN=../packager-osx-arm64/packager` uses the real one): the plan matches the cloud's (no upscale, logged the same), the job file has no paths; licence, site, asset and store checks; no cloud worker takes a site job; probe, plan, encode; declaration rules (ways out, dot files, duplicates, bad checksums, another lease, a probe job); a missing segment, fewer renditions than planned, a live MPD and an unfinished playlist each refused, discarded and retried while the playing package stays untouched; the whole package swapped in with the asset ready, formats, encode result, size and length, every file byte for byte, nothing left behind, QC queued, the job's log; an unreadable source and a worker's permanent or last failure fail the asset with the reason. Fails at HEAD (no module). Unchanged: site-jobs 67/67, vod-audio-asset 30/30, vod-library 70/70, vod-source-store 30/30, job-log 10/10, frames 81/81, clips-off-disk 29/29, upload-retry 20/20, bucket-tracks 28/28, fast-ladder 56/56, watchfolder-video 70/70, watchfolder-video-landing 45/45, smart-render-frames 62/62, encode-ladder 50/50, encode-store 24/24, workflow-fixes 27/27, mirror-archive (all seven), sites, site-watchfolders, site-watchfolder-files. vod-library-pure 169/170: its content-type check fails without this change too (it imports none of these files).
- **Guides**: none changed: nothing a customer can see yet (the placement is still refused). HYBRID_SITES.md phase 4a; `api-routes.json` and `API_ROUTES.md` list the new agent route.
- **Christian on the Mac**: nothing to run for this part; the Docker worker run is H4-2.
- **Not done (H4-2)**: the agent's worker on the pinned encoder image (CPU and GPU builds), the Encode at site placement with registration without upload and the source as a site location, upload straight to the bucket on AWS, a lease reaped on the last attempt failing the asset, the Mac Docker and kill-the-worker runs, GPU on AWS staging.

## 2026-11-08 - Mirror and Archive: packages into S3-compatible stores (build queue H3-12; v0.64.0)

What: a copy rule's HLS/DASH package now goes to S3-compatible store targets (MinIO and similar) as well as folders: one folder of objects per package in the customer's bucket, every object read back.

- **Where**: `volt-agent/src/siteArchive.ts` (`runCopyFolderToStore`, `storeWrite`, `readStoreFolder`, `removeStorePackage`; `runVerifyFolder` and the refused-report cleanup handle stores), `volt-agent/src/s3Target.ts` (`topNamesLike`: one delimited listing, so a package of hundreds of objects counts as one name), `volt-app/src/services/archive/packages.ts` (`requestPackageCopy` accepts `s3`), `archive.ts` (comment only), `volt-web/src/RuleEditor.tsx` (the box offered on stores, the note says each file is its own object). No migration, no new job type, no API change: the cloud judges a store package exactly as a folder package (`judgeFolder`).
- **Choices made in the build**: a store has no rename, so the package cannot be built beside its name and moved in. Instead the free name is picked first (a name with ANY object under it is taken, so the whole package goes to `-2`, never mixed into someone else's folder), every object is written with `If-None-Match: *`, and on any failure every object this job wrote is removed, and nothing else. An object taken between the look and the write (412) stops the copy as `name_taken` rather than restarting inline, so the retry re-lists and picks a free name with the normal back-off. A re-check that finds none of the listed objects says `missing`, otherwise `corrupt` with the counts, as for folders.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web, volt-agent); mirror-archive-packages **69/69** (was 52; 18 new checks against a real S3 API, moto with IAM signatures, with the real site agent; the new store check fails at HEAD with `package_needs_folder_target`); mirror-archive-s3 50/50 against the same store; mirror-archive-console browser walk **75/75** (was 72); unchanged: mirror-archive ok, mirror-archive-clips 37/37, mirror-archive-sidecars 36/36, mirror-archive-rules 45/45, mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, site-jobs 67/67, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, listStandard ok, docs-samples 115/115, docs-coverage ok (62 of 62 sections), scrub clean, docs.check.sh ok. retention-bin is 39/41 or 41/41 run to run on HEAD alone too (the two sprite-sheet checks), not touched by this item.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites (What, skips), Publishing Rules (the HLS/DASH package on every target; screenshot `publishing-archive-copies` retaken by the script: the store's box no longer greyed), Storage; HYBRID_SITES.md phase 3i.
- **Christian on the Mac**: with a MinIO target (keys in `archive-keys.json` beside the agent's state), tick HLS/DASH package and publish an encoded item: `<Name>-package/` appears in the bucket under the target's folder with `master.m3u8`, `manifest.mpd` and the segments, and the copy shows verified.
- **Not done**: mirror mode (H3-10, needs input).

## 2026-11-08 - Mirror and Archive: packaged outputs as a copy source (build queue H3-11; v0.63.0)

What: with the Mirror and Archive add-on, a copy rule can now copy an item's HLS/DASH package, as Volt streams it, to a folder at the customer's site: one folder per item that plays as it is. Store targets are split to H3-12.

- **Size, checked first**: the 56 encoded assets of the Mac test library (`volt-app/clips/vod`, sources in `clips/vod-src`): package / original is 1.7 at the median, 0.1 to 5.9, with 11 to 460 files. HLS and DASH share the CMAF segments, so the second format adds only its manifest. The console says "usually about 1.7 times the original (0.1 to 6 times), a few hundred files" beside the box.
- **Cheaper of the two ways**: a folder job. A job per file would be a job row, a lease, a completion and a copy row per segment (hundreds per item, thousands for long ones) and a half-copied package would look half archived. One `archive.copy-folder` job lists every file as its own transfer; the agent still asks for one URL per object (presigned on S3, signed on the Mac), so nothing about the transfer security changed.
- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/archive/packages.ts` (new: `packageRootOf`, `planPackage` with a bucket listing on S3 deployments, `requestPackageCopy`, `packageFilesOf`), `services/archive/packageManifest.ts` (new, shared with the agent: layout and manifest checksums, `cleanPackagePath`), `services/archive/archive.ts` (job types `archive.copy-folder` and `archive.verify-folder`, `what: 'packages'` in `copyOnRule`, folder re-checks in `requestVerify`, `judgeFolder`), `sidecars.ts` (`COPY_WHATS`), `modules/publishingRules.ts` and `services/publishingRules.ts` (`packages` accepted), `volt-agent/src/siteArchive.ts` (`runCopyFolder`, `runVerifyFolder`, shared progress across files so the lease is kept), `volt-web/src/RuleEditor.tsx` (**HLS/DASH package** with its size note; greyed on a store target), `pages/StorageArchive.tsx` (a package copy says so, with its size). No migration: a package copy is an `archive_copies` row with `what` `package`; its file list stays in its copy job's payload, which re-checks read.
- **Choices made in the build**: left out of a package: the progressive MP4 (it is the original's or the clip's own copy source), FAST ladder variants (`ladders/`) and reframes (`reframe/`), which are outputs of their own, and the encoder's files. New job types rather than a new payload on `archive.copy`, so an agent from before this release (asking for `archive.copy` and `archive.verify` only) never leases a folder it would treat as a file. Folder name `<name>-package` with dots taken out, so a taken name becomes `-package-2`. A package for an S3-compatible store is refused when queued, so nothing waits on a job no agent can run.
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web, and volt-agent with volt-app's Node types). New `test/mirror-archive-packages.check.ts` **52/52** with the real site agent (fails at HEAD: no module): where a package is (encode folder, a clip's HLS folder, never another asset's, nothing that walks out); the two checksums (order-free, one file fewer or one changed differs); rules save `packages` alone and with the original, an unknown value refused; ingest queues ONE job for all 98 files of a test package, ladders, reframe, the MP4 and encoder files left out; an old agent's lease answers 204; the agent sees transfer names only; the folder at the target holds exactly the package byte for byte, the copy records size and a checksum matching the cloud's; nothing again on publish; re-checks: verified, one segment gone is corrupt ("1 file missing"), one changed is corrupt, the folder gone is missing, then copied afresh; a file gone from the source before the copy and a file changed under it are not archived and the agent removes its folder; the cloud's judgement refuses one file fewer, other sizes, other checksums and a folder outside the target; a clip's package named as clips are, without the clip's MP4; skips (not packaged, store target, no add-on); nightly; the bucket listing on S3. Browser walk `mirror-archive-console.browser.mjs` **72/72** (was 66) on the docs seed: the box greyed on the store with its reason, offered on the NAS, the size note when ticked, the field's explanation, saved as `["source","packages"]`, kept on reopening. Unchanged on HEAD plus this change: mirror-archive-clips 37/37, mirror-archive-sidecars 36/36, mirror-archive-rules 45/45, mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, mirror-archive ok, mirror-archive-s3 ok, site-jobs 67/67, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, clip-extract 33/33, clip-provenance ok, clip-smart-render 35/35, clips-off-disk 29/29, clips-store 28/28, scheduled-clips 15/15, listStandard ok, retention-bin 41/41, docs.check.sh ok. In the full working tree (with another session's uncommitted work) retention-bin is 39/41, the two sprite-sheet checks seen once in the H3-9 run; 41/41 on HEAD plus this change, twice. docs-coverage 333/333, docs-samples 91/91. docs-seed-fast 14/20 here, the same at HEAD (the FAST library is empty in the container), not touched.
- **Guides**: Hybrid Sites (What, skips), Publishing Rules (HLS/DASH package under Copy, the size, the API values; screenshot retaken by the script), Storage; API overview text for publishing rules; HYBRID_SITES.md phase 3h.
- **Christian on the Mac**: tick HLS/DASH package (When it is published) for a folder target and publish an encoded item: `<Name>-package/` appears at the folder with `master.m3u8` and `manifest.mpd`, and plays from there in a player pointed at the folder.
- **Not done**: packages into S3-compatible stores (H3-12); mirror mode (H3-10, needs input).

## 2026-11-07 - Mirror and Archive: clips as a copy source (build queue H3-9 (a); v0.62.0)

What: with the Mirror and Archive add-on, a copy rule can now copy clips cut from a recording (clips, full-event replays, exports) to the customer's storage: the clip's file exactly as Volt made it and a metadata file beside it. H3-9 was split: packaged outputs are H3-11.

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/archive/clips.ts` (new: `requestClipCopy`, `clipSourceUri`, `copyClipOnReady`); `services/archive/sidecars.ts` (`isClipAsset`, `clipFileOf`, `clipBaseName`, a `clip` block in the metadata file, `only` on `requestSidecarCopies`, a clip's sidecars named after the clip); `services/archive/archive.ts` (`what: 'clips'` in `copyOnRule`); `services/jobs/handlers/clipExtract.ts` (the `ingest` moment once the file exists, before auto-publish); `modules/publishingRules.ts` and `services/publishingRules.ts` (`clips` accepted); `volt-web/src/RuleEditor.tsx` (**Clips** beside Original and Sidecars), `pages/StorageArchive.tsx` (a clip copy says "Clip file, as made"). No migration: a clip copy is an `archive_copies` row with `what` `clip` under the existing unique index.
- **Choices made in the build**: a clip is named by the recordings rule (`Title-Channel-YYYYMMDD-HHMM` from where it starts, in the channel's zone), not by title alone, so two clips of the same title stay apart and sort beside the programme file they were cut from; the file copied is the single file clip.extract made (MP4, else M4A or MP3), never a re-encode; a clip made as HLS only has no single file and is skipped (its package belongs to H3-11); the clip's own file is never removed by the archive; on S3 with the local copy gone, the bucket copy is fetched and checked by size and download (as an S3 original is).
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/mirror-archive-clips.check.ts` **37/37** with the real site agent (fails at HEAD: no module): which file a clip is (MP4, M4A, none for HLS only, never an upload); rules save `clips` alone and with the others, an unknown value refused; ingest queues the clip and its metadata file, the agent copies both byte for byte, verified against the cloud's checksum, the metadata file names the clip's file and where it was cut from; nothing again on publish; names (same title apart by time, Berlin time for a Berlin channel, an audio clip as M4A); skips (an upload, a clip with no file, an unlicensed account); clip.extract's hook; a clip changed under the copy is not counted (checksum mismatch) and the agent removes what it wrote; nightly copies every clip the target lacks; clips with sidecars share one metadata file and name the poster after the clip; the S3 fallback. Browser walk `volt-web/test/mirror-archive-console.browser.mjs` **66/66** (was 60) on the docs seed: Clips offered, explained, saved in order, kept on reopening, alone allowed, the last kept. Unchanged: mirror-archive-sidecars 36/36, mirror-archive-rules 45/45, mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, mirror-archive, mirror-archive-s3, site-jobs 67/67, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, jobs 43/43, job-log 10/10, clip-extract 33/33, clip-provenance, clip-smart-render 35/35, clips-off-disk 29/29, clips-store 28/28, scheduled-clips 15/15, listStandard, retention-bin 41/41 (39/41 once in the batch, the two sprite-sheet checks, then 41/41 on both reruns; nothing in it was touched), docs coverage 62/62.

- **Christian on the Mac**: cut a clip from a channel whose rule ticks Clips (When it lands) on a folder target: `<Title>-<Channel>-<date>-<time>.mp4` and `.json` appear at the folder once the clip is made.
- **Not done**: packaged outputs (H3-11); mirror mode (H3-10, needs input); clips of uploaded assets do not exist in Volt (a clip is always cut from a recording).

## 2026-11-07 - Mirror and Archive: asset sidecars and nightly copies (build queue H3-8; v0.61.0)

What: with the Mirror and Archive add-on, a copy rule can now copy an asset's sidecars beside its original (a metadata file, the caption files as delivered, other files that came with it, the poster), and can copy nightly whatever it covers that a target does not hold yet, older assets included. H3-8 was split: clips and packaged outputs as copy sources are H3-9; mirror mode is H3-10 and needs Christian's grace period (HYBRID_SITES.md does not set one).

- **Where**: `volt-app/src/services/archive/sidecars.ts` (new: `baseNameFor`, `assetSidecar`, `planSidecars`, `requestSidecarCopies`, `scheduledSweep`); `services/archive/archive.ts` (`schedule` moment, `what` on a copy, `copyOnRule` copies sidecars, the built file released for any verified copy); migration `0142_archive_sweeps.sql` (new table, the nightly cursor per account); `modules/publishingRules.ts` and `services/publishingRules.ts` (`schedule`, `what`; "original only" stored unsaid); `services/orchestrator/index.ts` (nightly step every 30 minutes); `volt-web/src/RuleEditor.tsx` (**Nightly** on each target, **Copy: Original / Sidecars** on a target copied to, at least one kept ticked; the summary names them), `pages/StorageArchive.tsx` (a sidecar copy says what it is). No agent change.
- **Choices made in the build**: sidecars are named after the original's file name so a folder listing keeps them together; captions are copied as delivered (not the WebVTT Volt makes for players), since the archive should hold what the customer gave; a verified sidecar is not re-copied when metadata changes (archive mode keeps what it copied; following changes is mirror mode); "nightly" is once per UTC day of walking, and each copy it queues waits for the rule's off-peak window, so the copying itself happens at night in the customer's zone; the walk compares its cursor in SQL because a JavaScript date drops the database's microseconds (found by the suite: the last asset of each step came round again).
- **Checked**: typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). New `test/mirror-archive-sidecars.check.ts` **36/36** (fails at HEAD: no module): names; `what` and `schedule` saved and refused as they should be; on ingest the original and five sidecars (metadata, two caption files, an XML companion, the poster) are queued, copied by the real agent and verified, byte for byte, named after the original; the metadata file names the original and its checksum and lists the captions; the cloud's built files go and the original stays; nothing twice; sidecars alone on an asset's own rung copy no original, and a poster missing from the store is skipped while four still go; the nightly walk in bounded steps, every covered asset copied (older ones included), an opted-out asset and an unlicensed account left alone, nothing more that day, the next day only what is missing and held for the window, an ingest-only rule not run by it. `volt-web/test/mirror-archive-console.browser.mjs` **60/60** in Chromium against a fresh docs seed (was 53: Nightly, Original and Sidecars, the last box kept ticked, saved in order and still ticked after reopening). Around it: mirror-archive-rules 45/45, mirror-archive-recordings 34/34, mirror-archive ok, mirror-archive-s3 ok (no store), site-jobs 67/67, publishing-rules 28/28, publishing-stream-rules 28/28, jobs and job-log 110/110, listStandard ok, retention-bin 41/41; migration-safety 51/51 on the device (its database part needs `scripts/deploy` beside it and was not run in the container); `check-migrations` ok; docs coverage 62/62.
- **Guides**: Publishing Rules (Nightly, Original and Sidecars, the API keys; screenshot retaken by the script), Hybrid Sites (Copy by rule: What, Nightly, once per target), Storage; API overview text for publishing rules.
- **Christian on the Mac**: an upload with an SRT and a poster under a rule with Sidecars on, a folder as the NAS: the original, `<name>.json`, `<name>.en.srt` and `<name>.jpg` appear side by side; a Nightly rule on a second target picks up older assets the next half hour.
- **Not done**: clips and packaged outputs as copy sources (H3-9); mirror mode (H3-10, needs input: the grace period); on AWS the build folder is the task's disk, as for recordings (2.2).
